# OpenWhispr Technical Reference for AI Assistants

This document provides comprehensive technical details about the OpenWhispr project architecture for AI assistants working on the codebase.

## Response Style

Keep responses focused, brief, and concise. Keep disclaimers and caveats short, and spend most of the response on the main answer. When asked to explain something, give a high-level summary unless an in-depth explanation is specifically requested.

## Project Overview

OpenWhispr is an Electron-based desktop dictation application that uses whisper.cpp for speech-to-text transcription. It supports both local (privacy-focused) and cloud (OpenAI API) processing modes.

## Architecture Overview

### Core Technologies

- **Frontend**: React 19, TypeScript, Tailwind CSS v4, Vite
- **Desktop Framework**: Electron 41 with context isolation
- **Database**: better-sqlite3 for local transcription history
- **UI Components**: shadcn/ui with Radix primitives
- **Speech Processing**: whisper.cpp + NVIDIA Parakeet (via sherpa-onnx) + OpenAI API
- **Audio Processing**: FFmpeg (bundled via ffmpeg-static)
- **Node.js**: 24 (pinned in `.nvmrc` — CI uses Node 24, do NOT regenerate `package-lock.json` with a different major version)

### Key Architectural Decisions

1. **Dual Window Architecture**:
   - Main Window: Minimal overlay for dictation (draggable, always on top)
   - Control Panel: Full settings interface (normal window)
   - Both use same React codebase with URL-based routing

2. **Process Separation**:
   - Main Process: Electron main, IPC handlers, database operations
   - Renderer Process: React app with context isolation
   - Preload Script: Secure bridge between processes
   - ONNX Utility Process: hosts all `onnxruntime-node` inference (text embeddings, speaker embeddings, fbank). Lazy-spawned on first use via `src/helpers/onnxWorkerClient.js` → `src/workers/onnxWorker.js`. Native crashes (e.g., ORT `bad_alloc`) confine to the worker; main process rejects in-flight requests, waits out a backoff, and respawns the worker on the next request (never eagerly, so a crash can't leave an empty worker running). A request that times out kills the worker so it respawns the same way; the embedding clients reload their sessions on the new worker. Exits once idle with no session loaded (`releaseIfIdle`, called after semantic search releases its model and after `speakerEmbeddings.js` unloads the speaker model 5 minutes after its last extract). Each session's calls are serialized in the worker, so an unload never releases a session mid-inference. Stopped in `will-quit`.

3. **Audio Pipeline**:
   - MediaRecorder API → Blob → ArrayBuffer → IPC → File → whisper.cpp
   - Automatic cleanup of temporary files after processing

## File Structure and Responsibilities

### Main Process Files

- **main.js**: Application entry point, initializes all managers
- **preload.js**: Exposes safe IPC methods to renderer via window.api

### Native Resources (resources/)

The Windows helpers we own (`windows-*.c`: key listener, mic listener, process stats, text monitor, fast paste, system audio helper) call `FreeConsole()` at the start of `main` when stdout isn't a console (`GetFileType` not `FILE_TYPE_CHAR`: OpenWhispr starts them hidden with piped stdio), so Windows' `conhost.exe` for them (~8 MB each) exits at once while the pipes keep working; run by hand in a terminal they keep it and print. `SetConsoleCtrlHandler` still registers, it just never fires. They are never started `detached` on Windows: libuv puts non-detached children in a kill-on-close job, so they die with OpenWhispr, while a detached one survives a crash (a surviving key listener would keep its keyboard hook). Each compiles from source in `compile:native` (see Build Scripts).

- **windows-key-listener.c**: C source for Windows low-level keyboard hook (Push-to-Talk)
- **windows-mic-listener.c**: C source for WASAPI mic session monitor (event-driven mic detection)
- **windows-system-audio-helper.c**: C source for WASAPI process-loopback system audio capture (meeting transcription). Excludes OpenWhispr's own process tree, so it hears every app on every output device. Requires Windows 10 2004+; falls back to Chromium display-media loopback when unavailable. Mid-session, the helper emits a `capture_silent` warning when its own stream is silent while a render endpoint is metering output, checked every second for the whole session because Windows keeps some apps' streams out of process loopback (Teams call audio, #1265). Main then starts renderer loopback beside the helper, and `meetingSystemAudioHandover.js` gives loopback the system channel only after it hears audio the helper misses, so a false alarm never drops a working capture. Outputs 24 kHz mono s16le PCM on stdout, line-delimited JSON events on stderr (same protocol as linux-system-audio-helper)
- **macos-mic-listener.swift**: Swift source for the CoreAudio process-object microphone listener (event-driven mic detection); falls back to aggregate device activity and retries PID monitoring from its heartbeat
- **windows-process-stats.c**: C source for the process inventory's Windows numbers (`processInventory.js`). Every process in the tree under `--root <pid>`, walked recursively from one `NtQuerySystemInformation` listing (a child counts only if it started after its parent, so a reused parent PID never adopts a stranger; the helper and its own children are excluded): exe file name (never a path or command line), creation time (ms since epoch, matching Electron's `creationTime` floored), CPU seconds, working set, private usage, peak, handles, threads, GDI and USER objects (`GetGuiResources`); a value it can't read is `null` with an error code, never 0. `--gpu` adds graphics memory per process (dedicated, shared; summed over adapters) and the PC-wide count of PIDs holding dedicated memory, from PDH with English counter names (`\GPU Process Memory(*)`), and in `--stream` the busiest 3D engine's % per process (`\GPU Engine(*)\Utilization Percentage`, engines summed per adapter LUID, physical adapter and engine, only from collects ≥ 1 s apart). `--once` prints one JSON object; `--stream --interval <ms>` (≥ 1000, default 2000) one JSON line per interval from one PDH query until stdin closes or the root exits, keeping the tree's process handles between samples (an open handle keeps a PID from reuse; a process that exited is dropped at once) and walking the process list again every 5th sample for new children and thread counts. Normal priority on purpose: at below-normal a busy PC starved it past its deadline (12 s for a once-run under the test suite's load), when the numbers matter most. Exit codes: 2 bad arguments, 3 root not found, 4 listing failed (each with a `{"v":1,"error":"<code>"}` line). Measured on Windows 11 with ~850 processes: a once-run 36 ms wall (process creation included), ~27 ms CPU; with `--gpu` ~240 ms (PDH's first counter add is ~190 ms of CPU whatever the counter); a stream 0.2% of one core (2.7 ms a sample; listing every sample cost 0.75–0.9%); numbers equal Process Explorer's (working set, private, handles, threads, GDI/USER and graphics memory exact)
- **globe-listener.swift**: Swift source for macOS Globe/Fn key detection
- **macos-window-bounds.swift**: Swift source for the System Settings window reporter used by the onboarding permission guide. Prints the dialog's bounds, whether a sheet or authorization prompt is in front, which app owns the front window (`settings` / `self` / `other`, attributed by process because window owner names are localized) and whether System Settings is running. CoreGraphics window list only, so it needs neither Accessibility nor Screen Recording
- **bin/**: Directory for compiled native binaries (whisper-cpp, nircmd, key/mic listeners)

### Helper Modules (src/helpers/)

- **audioManager.js**: Handles audio device management
- **clipboard.js**: Cross-platform clipboard operations
  - macOS: AppleScript-based paste with accessibility permission check
  - macOS: when the clipboard is restored afterwards, `macos-fast-paste --await-read <ms> --session <id>` takes the transcript on stdin and puts it on the pasteboard as a promise (`NSPasteboardItemDataProvider`), marked `org.nspasteboard.TransientType` so clipboard managers skip it and kept off Universal Clipboard, then posts ⌘V. The restore waits for the target to actually read it (`PASTE_READ <ms>` → 50 ms later, never sooner than 450 ms after ⌘V), so a target that dequeues ⌘V late (a busy renderer, a stalled app) no longer pastes the user's old clipboard (#1740). Nothing read in 3 s (`PASTE_NOT_READ`) leaves the transcript itself and restores; `PASTE_REPLACED` (a copy in between) keeps the new copy. The item carries a `com.openwhispr.paste-session` tag, which the restore checks before the text, since reading the text would itself count as the read (the text check still covers an app that rewrote the transcript). `pasteText` settles at `PASTE_POSTED`; from then on the paste is never retried or handed to osascript, which would paste twice. Electron never writes the transcript itself on that path (only a failure before ⌘V puts it back for the fallback or the user), and the osascript fallback keeps the fixed 450 ms restore
  - macOS: when the frontmost app is a Chromium browser (Chrome, Brave, Edge, Arc, Dia, Vivaldi, Opera, Comet, Chromium; their channels and installed web apps match by bundle-id prefix, e.g. `com.google.Chrome.app.<id>`), `macos-fast-paste` types the transcript as keyboard unicode events posted to that browser's process instead of posting ⌘V. Chrome drops a synthetic ⌘V's insert at random, in waves: the page gets `paste` and `beforeinput` with the right text and nothing is inserted, with this helper and the osascript fallback alike. It types only what lands exactly as a paste would (`typingChunks`): one line of 2–2000 UTF-16 units with no control characters, since a typed line break is Return (submits forms, runs notebook cells and terminal commands) and a lone character reaches the page as a keypress (single-key site shortcuts); chunks are whole characters of at most 20 units, a lone last character joining the chunk before it, so the page gets text input rather than key presses. It also needs a plain keyboard layout, since an input method (Pinyin, Kotoeri) composes typed keys. Anything else keeps ⌘V. Posting to the process keeps a mid-typing app switch from redirecting the rest and keeps keyboard monitors (text expanders) from seeing the transcript. The transcript typed is the one given on stdin with `--await-read` (which never touches the pasteboard and reports `PASTE_TYPED`: nothing to restore), otherwise the clipboard text. The helper prints `PASTE_POSTED` (`PASTE_POSTED typed`) in every paste run, so `pasteMacOS` never retries or falls back once the transcript is going out. `--copy` keeps ⌘C
  - macOS: with `allowClipboardFallback` (Accessibility skipped in onboarding) and no permission, `_pasteText` leaves the transcript on the clipboard and returns `{ pasted: false, reason: "accessibility-missing" }`. `useAudioRecording` shows a "Copied, not pasted" card for it once per launch, until Don't show again sets `pastePermissionNoticeDismissed` in localStorage (#2606)
  - Windows: PowerShell SendKeys with nircmd.exe fallback
  - Linux: compositor-aware Wayland paste (Hyprland sendshortcut, wlroots wtype, GNOME/KDE portal keysyms) with native uinput/XTest and system-tool fallbacks. KDE's portal paste sends Shift+Insert to every window but Warp, which binds paste to Ctrl+Shift+V only: KWin resolves keysyms in the active layout only, so a Ctrl+V on a non-Latin layout arrives as Ctrl alone (#2055). The app runs on XWayland, which the compositor won't let read a native Wayland app's clipboard while a Wayland window is focused, so `_saveClipboard` falls back to `wl-paste --type text` when Electron reads nothing; otherwise the restore would wipe the user's clipboard. On KDE it also takes wl-paste's UTF-8 plain text whenever it differs from Electron's, since KWin can leave a stale X11 owner in place after a Wayland app copies, so a rich or image restore first writes the original's text (empty for an image) to Wayland, or the app's own transcript or selection sentinel left there would read as newer. A Wayland-only copy with no plain text (a screenshot copied in Spectacle) isn't saved, so a dictation replaces it with X11's older text
  - Linux: wlroots, Hyprland and niri (xwayland-satellite) hand XWayland's clipboard to a native Wayland app only while an X11 window has focus; Mutter, COSMIC and KWin 6.0.3+ do it themselves (KDE also writes the transcript and primary selection with `wl-copy`, and Settings asks for wl-clipboard there too, since `_saveClipboard` reads the Wayland clipboard there with `wl-paste`). There (`requiresWlCopy` in `linuxSession.js`; a leftover `SWAYSOCK` or Hyprland signature under another desktop doesn't count) the transcript reaches Wayland apps only through `wl-copy`, so `_pasteText` pastes only when wl-copy wrote both the Wayland clipboard (`_writeClipboardWayland`) and the primary selection that terminals paste on Shift+Insert (`_writePrimarySelection`). `wl-copy --primary` goes through data-control, which still takes a primary selection under niri `disable-primary` (checked on niri 26.04) and Hyprland `middle_click_paste = false` (its data-control code never reads that option). Otherwise it injects nothing and returns `reason: "wl-copy-missing"` (not installed) or `"wl-copy-failed"` (e.g. killed by its 200 ms timeout): the renderer shows the dictation-error pill (Retry writes and pastes the kept text again, never records), a selection edit reports `code: "wl_copy_missing"`/`"wl_copy_failed"`, and the paste-tools card and Settings → Wayland Paste Setup ask for wl-clipboard (#2621). There, a missing `wl-copy` is probed again rather than read from the 30 s command cache, so one installed after a held-back paste counts at once
  - Linux COSMIC: paste reads the focused app from cosmic-comp's `zcosmic_toplevel_info_v1` (`cosmicToplevel.js`, a minimal Wayland wire client, bound at version 1, which still sends app_id and state), never from xdotool, whose XWayland answer goes stale while a native Wayland window has focus. A known terminal is pasted through wtype (Ctrl+Shift+V; Shift+Insert for Konsole); everything else keeps uinput Shift+Insert, since COSMIC gives no PID to spot Electron apps hosting TUIs. Short terminal names (`st`) match whole words only, or every `com.system76.*` app id would read as a terminal
  - Linux: before any paste or selection-copy chord, `linux-fast-paste --capabilities --await-modifier-release <ms>` waits for physically held modifiers to be released (XKB state on X11, `EVIOCGKEY` on Wayland; `unknown` without `/dev/input` access pastes as before). A chord injected into held keys arrives as a different shortcut, and a virtual device cannot release another device's key (#2113). Still held after 1.5 s → not pasted, `reason: "modifiers-held"`, and the renderer shows the dictation-error pill with the transcript on the clipboard; a selection edit blocked the same way reports `code: "modifiers_held"` and gets the same pill. `ydotoold`'s virtual keyboard is ignored, since an interrupted chord can leave its modifier down, and an unreadable key state is logged once per session. The wait runs before `pasteLinux` detects the target window, and a selection copy that had to wait looks the target up again, since focus can move while a key is held: a fresh capture then runs the command on its own (`focus_moved`), and a revalidated session declines as `target_changed`. Retry on a modifiers-held pill pastes the kept transcript again rather than recording again (the macOS/Windows push force-stop pill keeps its record-again Retry, since neither platform waits for held keys), ignores clicks while its paste is pending, and never dismisses a newer pill. `useAudioRecording` keeps the pill in processing until the paste attempt settles (the audio manager settles before the paste starts), but never reports that to main, which drops dictation hotkeys while processing; a paste held back after the next dictation started leaves the transcript on the clipboard without a pill. The helper measures its wait on `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, since the JS watchdog kills it at timeout + 1 s
- **database.js**: SQLite operations for transcription history
  - Transcriptions, chats, dictionary words and snippets are personal in every space: `account_id` is stamped on write and on pull, and every read, delete by id, pending queue and pull merge is scoped. `activeAccountId` is the signed-in account and stamps every new row, tombstone, claim and counter; `_visibleAccountId()` is whose rows are in view: the signed-in account, or signed out the device's only recorded account (`device_accounts`, written when an account is set active, seeded at launch from the account ids already in the owned tables, cleared by account deletion; `hasOtherAccountRows` counts it, so an account that signed in and wrote nothing still makes the device shared). Reads, by-id edits and deletes, duplicate hiding and the recordings scope use `_ownedRowScope` (visible account); pending queues, client-id lookups and pull merges use `_syncRowScope` (signed-in account), so signed out on a sole device the account's rows stay visible and editable, and its edits wait for its next sign-in. A NULL row (signed out, or from before attribution) is visible to everyone; for words and snippets the account's own row hides a NULL twin with the same key (keys compare case-insensitively through `fold_key`, a function registered on the connection that folds as JS `toLowerCase` does, since SQLite's `lower()` folds only ASCII; the indexes keep `lower()` so an older build can still open the database). `legacy_unattributed` marks rows from before attribution: 1 until a cloud match claims it for an account, 2 once this device uploaded it for whichever account synced first, which proves nothing, so it stays NULL. At sign-in, the only account on a device (`hasOtherAccountRows` false) takes every legacy row no cloud holds (`claimLegacyRowsOfOnlyAccount`); one with a cloud id waits for its own cloud to prove it, and so does a word or trigger whose other casing has one. With another account on the device, a 404 parks a legacy row rather than re-creating it, and account deletion leaves it. Clear history, retention and single deletes (dictation, word, snippet, chat) stamp an unattributed row's tombstone with the signed-in account, so only its token pushes the delete and another account's cloud copy stays; signed out they stamp nobody. Push acks (`markTranscriptionSynced`, `markDictionaryEntrySynced`, `markSnippetSynced`, `markConversationSynced`) only reach an unattributed row or the account's own. A signed-out chat fork keeps its source chat's account. A dictation is stamped with whoever was active when it was spoken, not when it finished (`_accountSpokenUnder`), and a voice command from an account that has since left is dropped (`accountChangedSince`, true only when that account was a real one other than the active one, so a guest signing in mid-command keeps it). Signed-out rows that never sync (failed dictations, container chats) join the account at sign-in (`claimSignedOutLocalRows`). Hard-deleting the account's word or snippet beside a legacy copy with a cloud id leaves a synced tombstone that keeps hiding the copy, since it may be another account's
  - IPC writes from a sync pass are fenced to the sign-in that read the data (`isStaleSyncAck`, which refuses a missing generation, and one whose scoped account (`scopedAccountByGeneration`) is no longer the database's active account, so a session revoked under the same token can't store pulled rows with no owner; `db-clear-dictionary-cloud-id` and `db-clear-snippet-cloud-id` take the generation too); whole-list dictionary and snippet saves name the account the window's list belongs to, and a save main refuses as another account's makes the window take what SQLite holds. Renderers that follow the rows take their baseline from `get-database-account-scope` and `database-account-scope-changed` (`{ accountId, visibleAccountId }`, from `getAccountScope()`), not `get-active-account-scope`, which answers from the credential binding and reads signed out between a token rotation and the re-scope: the lists' account (`settingsStore`) follows `accountId`, and history (`transcriptionStore`) reloads only when `visibleAccountId` changes. In-flight recording updates (`updateTranscriptionStatus`, `updateTranscriptionAudio`) stay unscoped on purpose, and the IPCs a user reaches (`update-transcription-text`, `retry-transcription`, audio reads) check the row first; `retry-transcription` gives up when the account changes mid-retry, not when the token rotates. Recordings are per account too: Clear All Audio and the storage count skip another account's. Audio retention is device-wide like its setting, so a recording expires whoever owns it. The Insights summary follows the active account and shows only unattributed counters signed out. The Insights claim skips a counter whose dictation is another account's or, on a shared device, an unproven legacy row (`_claimableAnalyticsSql`)
  - `SyncService` keeps its pull cursors per account (`claimSyncCursors`, saved under `lastSyncedAt.byAccount.<id>`), so an account's first pass reads its whole history and a returning one still gets deletes. Cursors from before owners were recorded stay with the first account to sync, which runs one snapshot pull (`backfillUnownedCursors`) that also claims its legacy rows. The snapshot records each table as it finishes (`syncCursors.unownedClaimProgress`), so a retry repeats only the tables still outstanding, and one that keeps failing waits 5 minutes, doubling up to an hour. The note-feed replay counters are saved and restored with the cursors. `forgetAccount` drops every cursor, counter, claim marker and parked record that names a deleted account
  - A pass is pinned to the sign-in it started under (`passGeneration`, `passIsCurrent`): every stage boundary and dirty-flag loop ends the pass once the token generation changes, a same-account rotation included, so it can't finish as the next account or advance that account's cursors. The next trigger runs the rest
  - A delete the cloud didn't confirm (a 404, or a dictation the batch answer leaves out) ends only for the pass's own row, or an unattributed one on a device no other account has used (`openDeleteQueue`). Otherwise the tombstone may be another account's, so it stays and is parked for this account (`parkedDelete.<kind>.<account>`) until its owner signs in. A word or snippet whose PATCH 404s is never parked when it is the pass's own row
  - Sharing a personal word to a workspace list sends it as `{id, word}` only when it is the signed-in account's own row; an unattributed row carrying a cloud id goes as a plain word
  - A chat's cloud create inserts its messages again on a client id already in the cloud, and leaves that row's tombstone in place, so `SyncService` sends a chat's messages in a create only once. An edit during the create (`acknowledgeConversationCreate` outcome `changed`) links the returned copy and keeps the chat pending; a create that may already have landed (`cloud_create_attempted`) goes without messages. Both then read the copy and send only what it lacks (`sendMissingConversationContent`), so a retry repeats nothing. A message counts as sent while the copy holds one of its role in its place, whatever the text: a reply regenerated in place isn't sent again, and the cloud, which can't change a message, keeps the earlier text. Marking the chat synced takes the copy's `updated_at`, so the same pass's pull doesn't put that text back over this device's. A create answered with a tombstone gives the chat a new client id (`replaceConversationClientId`). In the pull, a tombstone deletes a live chat only when it is linked to that copy, and a cloud copy with fewer messages never replaces a chat. Pulled rows keep the cloud's ISO timestamps beside SQLite's format, so chat messages are read in `id` order and chat lists sort on `datetime(updated_at)`
- **noteUndo.js**: Undo for assistant `update_note` edits. The tool passes `{ undoable, expected, turn }` to `db-update-note`; inside one transaction main refuses the edit if a guarded field changed since the tool read the note, writes it, and journals the previous values in `assistant_note_undo` (one row per note, bound to the account and kept 10 minutes). An `AFTER UPDATE` trigger retires the row when any guarded field changes again (manual save, sync pull, delete, identity or folder change), treating `''` and NULL as equal because a pull stores an empty field as NULL; sync acknowledgements don't retire it. Edits of one note in the same chat turn (`turn` = the assistant message id) share one Undo; `update_note` runs one note's edits in turn, since the AI SDK runs a step's tool calls concurrently. `AssistantNoteUndoListener` (control panel and dictation window) offers each edit once, in the first focused window (`db-claim-note-undo`); typing in the open note retires it (`discardNoteUndo`). The trigger is recreated and a journal of another shape is dropped at launch; if setup fails, edits save without Undo
- **debugLogger.js**: The app's log (Settings › Storage › Logs; rules in `logPolicy.js`, IPC in `logIpc.js`, see section 11). The level comes from the setting (`OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` in `.env`: `off` / `warn` / `debug`, read without rewriting: `debug`/`trace` are Everything, unset/`info`/anything else Problems only) unless a `--log-level` flag or an `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` inherited from the parent process overrides it (`main.js` calls `captureInheritedEnv` before any `.env` loads and takes it out of `process.env`, so a `.env` rewrite never saves it as the setting); `refreshLogLevel()` re-reads and applies the settings live. Any level but Off writes one file per start, `debug-<ISO>.log` in `<userData>/logs` (`debug-<ISO>-text.log` while "Include dictated text" is on), whose first line is a one-line `System Info` header (version, `selfBuiltCommit`, the logging settings, what the start's housekeeping removed), written whatever the level; Off writes nothing new to files, and the console (where it's on) keeps warnings and errors. A file continues in a new one past 20 MB, when the text switch changes, and for a `-text` file once it's a day old. Retention: `-text` files go 3 days after they began, others after `OPENWHISPR_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS` (7/14/30/90; default 7 at Problems only and Off, 30 at Everything), then the oldest until the folder fits in 100 MB; never the file being written; run at start, at each new file, every 6 hours (an unref'd timer) and right after the retention shortens, logging off or not. The first start of this version treats older logs as holding text: those older than 3 days are deleted and the rest renamed `-text` (marker `.text-policy-1`; the count goes in the first file's header). Bounds: an entry over 16 KB is cut with a note; while more than 1 MB waits in the stream, entries are dropped and one line counts them once it drains; a stream error turns file logging off for the session with one `console.error`; `write()` never throws. `error()`/`fatal()` called as `(message, details, scope)` keep the details and scope (other calls are flattened as before). `clearLogs()` closes the file, deletes every `debug-*.log` it can, reports what it couldn't and reopens if logging is on. Tests: `test/helpers/debugLoggerRetention.test.js`, `debugLoggerConsolePolicy.test.js`, `logPolicy.test.js`, `logIpc.test.js`, `logTextCanary.test.js`, `logPreviewFields.test.js`
- **graphicsMode.js**: What Chromium draws with, for the log, System › Diagnostics and the start visuals line (section 11). `classifyGpuCompositing(getGPUFeatureStatus().gpu_compositing)`: `enabled*` is `hardware`, `disabled*`/`unavailable*` `software`, anything else or missing `unknown`; software is `requested` when a launch flag asks for it (`--disable-gpu`, `--disable-gpu-compositing` (Linux's NVIDIA rule appends it), `--use-angle=warp|swiftshader[-webgl]`, `--use-gl=swiftshader[-webgl]`; `requestedSoftwareSwitch`), else a `fallback` (Chromium gave up on the GPU: a driver refusing a D3D11 device leaves WARP, "Microsoft Basic Render Driver"). `createGraphicsModeMonitor` is created at the top of `main.js`, before app ready, and `attach`ed to the logger in `initializeCoreManagers`: until the GPU process reports, every feature reads `disabled_software` even on a working GPU (measured: `getGPUInfo("basic")` resolves within ~15 ms, before that), so nothing is classified before the first `gpu-info-update`, then 1 s for a burst to settle; one "Graphics mode" line (`gpu` scope; mode, reason, `requestedBy`, `gpuCompositing`, `rasterization`, and `glRenderer`/`displayType` from `getGPUInfo("complete")` within 5 s; Linux adds `disabledForNvidia`), info for hardware or requested, warn for a fallback. Every later `gpu-info-update` burst and a GPU `child-process-gone` ("GPU process gone", warn; one before the logger was up is logged on attach with `beforeLogging`) re-evaluate it; a change logs "Graphics mode changed" (with `from`) and is broadcast as `graphics-mode-changed` (and handed to the tray, `onGraphicsModeChanged`). No report within 15 s logs `unknown` with `gpuReported: false` rather than guessing. Tests: `test/helpers/graphicsMode.test.js`
- **dictationStartVisuals.js**: "Dictation start visuals", one main-process line per dictation start (section 11): `StartVisualsTracker` collects one press at a time (`begin` from `windowManager`, keyed by the press trace's `pressId`), the dictation window's `pillPainted`/`pillSettled` marks, the lifecycle's recording start and end, and the edge strip's show; writes once everything expected arrived (the pill always; once recording, the entrance's end unless another window runs it, and the strip unless it's off), or after 3 s, when the recording ended, or when the next press begins, with what arrived (`outcome`, `missing`). A press cancelled before anything showed writes nothing. Warn ("Slow dictation start visuals", `slow`) when the pill showed more than 500 ms after the key, the strip more than 1000 ms (a missing mark counts from when the line is written; the strip only once the recording started), or graphics is a software fallback. Never waits on anything and never throws. Tests: `test/helpers/dictationStartVisuals.test.js`, `windowManagerStartVisuals.test.js`, `test/utils/dictationStartVisualMarks.test.js`
- **buildInfo.js**: What the running app was built from, for System › This build (`get-build-info`). `scripts/afterPack.js` stamps `build-info.json` next to `app.asar` (macOS: the bundle's `Contents/Resources`) in every packaging (`build:*`, `pack`, `dist`, `selfhost.js ship`; one stamp per run, so a universal macOS build's halves match): `builtAt` (UTC ISO), `commit` (full SHA of HEAD in the folder being packaged, `packager.projectDir`, not the cwd), `dirty` (tracked changes: `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no`), `source` (`selfhost` when the package is `selfBuilt`, else `npm`) and `version`; without git those are null and the packaging goes on. git always runs through `gitEnv()` (agent-injected `GIT_CONFIG_*`, `GIT_EXEC_PATH`, `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT`, tokens and `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` stripped; `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`). Main reads the file once, on the first request (`parseBuildInfo`: anything malformed reads as unknown, null; a self-built package without one still names its `selfBuiltCommit`); a run from source (`!app.isPackaged`: selfhost Dev, `npm run dev`) asks git about the app path instead, once, with a 5 s timeout, and adds the commit subject. `channel` is `source`, `selfhost-stable` or `release`, from how the app runs, never `NODE_ENV` or `OPENWHISPR_CHANNEL` (selfhost Dev sets both to production). The answer adds `startedAt` (the main process's start, from `process.uptime()`), `uptimeSec`, the exe path with the user profile as `%USERPROFILE%` (`~` elsewhere) and the Electron, Chromium and Node versions. `settings/BuildInfoPanel.tsx` shows it at the top of System, above Updates, carrying the `system.updates.version` anchor (the version row moved there): version with a Self-built / Running from source / Latest / Update tag, built (local time and relative), commit (short SHA, uncommitted changes, subject), running since, install folder, engine; relative times tick at most once a minute while the page shows; Copy build info copies English lines with UTC times (`settings/buildInfoFormat.ts`). Updates always renders `system.updates.auto`: the toggle, or Not available with the reason (development, self-built, package manager). Tests: `test/helpers/buildInfo.test.js`, `test/scripts/afterPackBuildInfo.test.js`, `test/components/buildInfoPanel.test.js`
- **storageInventory.js**: Settings › Storage's inventory (`registerStorageInventoryIpc`, wired in `ipcHandlers.js`): fixed resource ids `recordings`, `logs`, `database` (the db file + `-wal` + `-shm`; `transcriptions-dev.db` in development), `settings` (`Local Storage` + `.env`), `secrets` (`secure-keys`), `models` (one part per kind through `modelDirUtils`, so relocated folders are followed: whisper, parakeet (Parakeet and Cohere), diarization, `models` (GGUF), plus the home cache's `embedding-models`), `searchIndex` (`qdrant-data`, `qdrant-data-dev` in development), `appCache` (`Cache`, `Code Cache`, `GPUCache`, `DawnGraphiteCache`, `DawnWebGPUCache`, `Shared Dictionary`: never Chromium's storage), `cliBridge` (`~/.openwhispr/cli-bridge.json`) and `selfhost` (Windows only, when `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenWhispr-selfhost` exists; never measured). Main owns every path (`defaultStorageResources`); IPC `storage-inventory({ refresh })` → `{ measuredAt, cached, totalBytes, items: [{ id, path, paths, target, exists, bytes, files, parts, clearable }], database: { counts }, recordingPace }`, paths masked with `buildInfo.maskUserProfile`; `storage-open(id)` (a folder opens, a file is shown in its folder; recordings, logs and models create theirs first; anything but a known id is refused); `storage-clear(id)` only for `recordings` (the fixed Clear all), `logs` (`debugLogger.clearLogs`) and `appCache` (`session.defaultSession.clearCache()` and `clearCodeCaches({})`, never `clearStorageData()`), anything else `not-clearable`. Sizes are walked asynchronously with at most 8 file operations at once (`createLimiter`), never following symbolic links or junctions (`lstat`), and cached for 60 s (a clear drops the cache; concurrent requests share one measurement). `countHistoryRecords(db)` counts the History database's rows (not deleted) in one query over the tables that exist; `readRecordingPace(db)` is dictations a day over the last 30 days of history (or since the first one in that window) and their average recorded length. Tests: `test/helpers/storageInventory.test.js`, `test/helpers/storageInventoryDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node)
- **processInventory.js**: Every process OpenWhispr runs and what it costs, for Settings › Data › Processes & resources, the "Resource snapshot" log line and Copy diagnostics; created in `initializeCoreManagers` (`createMainProcessInventory` in `main.js`, before any window, so `createWindowVisibilityTracker` sees each window's show and hide), started in `startApp` (first run 60 s later), stopped by the sidecar registry (`process-stats`) and in `will-quit`. One shared, single-flight collector: Electron's `app.getAppMetrics()` (KiB → bytes; private bytes Windows-only; a missing or zero value is `null`, never 0) merged with `windows-process-stats.exe`'s rows by `(pid, creation time)` (within 1 s; the helper enriches Electron's row with handles, threads, GDI/USER and graphics memory, adds programs Electron doesn't list, and a reused PID is another process, keyed `pid:createdAt`). Then `foldConsoleHosts` folds each console host (`conhost.exe`) whose parent is another process in the tree into that process's row, before roles, CPU, history, warnings and the report see the rows: the row keeps the parent's key, PID and exe (its history continues) and gets `includes` (the parent, then its console host: `key`, `pid`, `createdAt`, `exe`, `role` and every number, `cpuPct` and `cpuAvg` included); its in RAM, committed, peak, CPU seconds, handles, threads, GDI/USER and graphics memory and use are the parts' sums (of the known ones: null only when no part has it), its CPU % the parts' each against its own last reading, added (a console host appearing or unreadable is never a spike); the totals count every process, parts included, so they're the same folded or not. A console host whose parent isn't in the tree (its program just exited) keeps its own row (`consoleHost`). Our own helpers release their console, so what's left to fold is the third-party sidecars' and the Copilot runtime's (GitHub's SDK starts it). Roles come from `processRoles.js` (pure: Electron type and service name, the window a renderer hosts from `collectWindowRoles` (dictation window first, control panel, meeting prompt, permission guide, edge-strip windows via `recordingEdgeStrip.listWindows()`), or the helper's exe name; each role's i18n keys `settingsPage.processes.roles.<role>.{name,what,why.<variant>,goesAway.<variant>}` with the parameters main fills in: hidden since, the key listener's hotkey (`windowsKeyManager.listeners`), the Copilot runtime's idle stop (`getStatus().idleStopAt`), the strip's release; English texts in `test/fixtures/processRoles.en.json` for the page's locales). CPU is % of one core from each instance's cumulative CPU seconds over a monotonic clock (`100 × Δcpu ÷ Δwall`; Electron's `percentCPUUsage` divides by the core count and resets whenever anyone reads it); an instance's first sample is `null`. Sampling: a once-run every 5 minutes (deadline 2 s, output ≤ 256 KB, structured error codes only; `--gpu` only for the run that feeds the 30-minute line, since PDH costs ~200 ms of CPU, and later runs carry that reading with `gpuAt`); a stream (`--stream --interval 2000 --gpu`, watchdog 8 s, retries 2 s/10 s then rests 5 minutes after 3 failures) only while a subscribed renderer's window is visible and not minimized; off Windows or without the helper, Electron's numbers (every 2 s while subscribed). Never during a dictation: while `windowManager.isDictationBusy()` a run in flight is cancelled and its late result dropped, the stream stops (stdin closed) and the 5-minute run is deferred; the lifecycle reaches it through `onDictationStateChanged` (the tray's hook calls it too), and its only work then is `getAppMetrics()` at the recording's start and stop and at delivery: the last recording's CPU per process while recording and while processing and delivering (a process that appeared is measured over its own life and marked partial; one that exited is partial with no number), whether the edge strip's window was new, then graphics memory after delivery, only while the page is open (the resumed stream's first sample; with the page closed nothing runs and the status is `pageClosed`, since a `--gpu` run costs ~200 ms of CPU per dictation), "Recording cost" (debug). A 5-minute run a dictation cancels runs again once it's delivered. History: a point per process instance at most every 5 minutes, 24 hours in memory by age (≤ 200 instances), gaps left as gaps (no CPU % across 15 minutes), recordings marked. Logs (scope `resources`) only an allowlisted report (`buildReport`: roles, PIDs, numbers, enums; a folded row's parts as `includes`, each a report row of its own role, PID and numbers; never exe names, paths, command lines, window titles, hotkeys, raw helper errors or stderr, which is never read): "Resource snapshot" (info, one compact JSON line, `roles` one entry per row with the folded numbers and no parts, its shape unchanged by folding) after the first run and then every 30 minutes, "Resource warning" (warn: GDI or USER over 5,000 in a process, handles up more than 50% and 200 over 6 hours, everything's in-RAM sum over 2 GB; each at most once an hour per process). IPC (`registerProcessInventoryIpc`, from `ipcHandlers.js`): `process-inventory-get({ history })` → `{ snapshot, history, lastRecording, unavailable, paused, streaming, report }` without starting a run; `process-inventory-subscribe` / `-unsubscribe` (the sender's window; destroyed senders drop out), samples as `process-inventory-sample`. Copy diagnostics adds the report as a Processes table (`settings/processInventoryFormat.ts`; a folded row's parts on indented lines under it). Tests: `test/helpers/processRoles.test.js`, `processInventory.test.js`, `processInventoryService.test.js`, `processInventoryLogCanary.test.js`, `windowsProcessStatsHelper.test.js` (runs the built helper on Windows)
- **audioStorage.js**: Recording files (`<userData>/audio/OpenWhispr-<date>-<time>-<id>.webm`, legacy `<id>.webm`; `transcriptionIdFromAudioFile`). `deleteAllAudio({ exceptTranscriptionIds })` skips other accounts' recordings, counts only the files it actually deleted and returns `{ deleted, failed, deletedIds }`; `clearAllRecordings(databaseManager)` (behind `delete-all-audio` and `storage-clear("recordings")`) reads the other accounts' ids first (`getOtherAccountTranscriptionIds`; if it can't, it deletes nothing), so their recordings stay as with Clear history, then clears `has_audio` only for the rows whose files were deleted, so a file that couldn't be deleted keeps its row's flag, and returns `{ deleted, failed }`. `getStorageUsage` leaves the same files out. `cleanupExpiredAudio(days)` runs from main's retention sweep at `recordingSweepDays(audio, transcript)` (`utils/retentionPeriods.js`): the shorter of the two limits, so a file whose transcript expired goes even with recordings Off. Tests: `test/helpers/audioStorageClearAll.test.js`
- **localModelPolicyGate.js**: Main-process authorization for workspace allow lists of local models (`transcription`/`llm.allowedLocalModels`; `null` allows all, `[]` none). Every manager in `ipcHandlers.js`'s gate setup gets `localModelPolicy`; a new local engine or server must be added there, or it runs unenforced. `runLocalModelOperation` holds a lease across nested calls (`AsyncLocalStorage`), rechecked before network transfer, native load and inference, and when a result is delivered. Decisions reuse `policyRules.ts`. A held verdict answers at once and refreshes behind it, so a slow network never holds up dictation; only an identity with no verdict waits. A token change refuses new work until its verdict lands, while running work and loaded servers keep theirs (`retained`); `cancelJobs` ends work on an account switch or sign-out, and an unresolved verdict never stops anything. Under a guest or unmanaged verdict a blank model means the engine's default; only a managed policy refuses it. Refusals carry `POLICY_MODEL_BLOCKED`, `POLICY_LOCAL_MODE_BLOCKED`, `POLICY_APP_VERSION_BLOCKED` or `POLICY_UNRESOLVABLE` and are terminal: no retry, no fallback to another engine or the cloud. Transfers keep `createDownloadSignal`'s plain signal, which `bindLocalModelDownload` connects to the lease. In the renderer, `selectPolicyEffectiveSettings` leaves a blocked selection inert (`policyBlockedLocalScopes`) without touching the saved preference, and Local turned off moves the member to an allowed mode
- **providerHttpErrors.js**: Pure ESM classifier for BYOK provider failures (transcription and AI models), shared by main (require(esm)) and renderer. `providerHttpError({ provider, status, body, headers, model, surface, selfHosted })` builds an Error carrying `code` (`PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED`, `PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, …), `messageKey` (`providerErrors.*`), `messageParams`, `settingsTarget` (`speechToText`/`llms`, fixable errors only) and redacted `technicalDetails`; `asProviderError` classifies AI SDK, timeout (including a transcription request's `TRANSCRIPTION_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` marker) and network errors and leaves already-keyed errors alone. Raw provider bodies never reach UI text. Its English sentences (Error.message, what History rows and logs show) twin the locales; the Settings pointers name pages that exist ("Settings → AI Connections & Local Models"; Copilot's unsupported request: "Chat and the Voice Assistant in Settings"). `providerSettingsTargetFor(code, surface)` is the fixable-code rule alone, for a failed History row that kept only its code. Azure Speech (`providerId: "azure-speech"`) has its own, scoped normalization of `{"error":{"code","message","innerError":{"code"}}}`: a 404 is a wrong endpoint (`AZURE_SPEECH_ENDPOINT_INVALID`, `providerErrors.azureSpeech.endpointNotFound`, fixable), `AudioLengthLimitExceeded` is too long (`PROVIDER_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`), `InvalidModel`-family codes and regions without MAI ("Enhanced mode … not supported yet") are the model unavailable there (`PROVIDER_MODEL_NOT_FOUND`), `InvalidLocale`/`UnsupportedLanguageCode` the language (`PROVIDER_BAD_REQUEST` with Open Settings); 401/403, 429 and 5xx classify as for any provider; `apim-request-id` is kept as the request id. Rendered by `src/utils/describeProviderError.ts`
- **connectionChecks.js**: Settings › AI Connections' connection checks, main's half (the renderer's is `settings/connectionHealth.ts`). The renderer sends **check descriptors**, names only: `{ id, kind, capability: llm | stt | identity, route, slot, models, generation }` (the resolved base URL, the credential slot's name, the model ids active features use on it, and a fingerprint of route, slot, models, region and tenant). `validateDescriptor` refuses anything outside the kind's allowlist before a key is read (`CHECK_INVALID_ROUTE`): fixed provider routes (`FIXED_ROUTES`; OpenAI also its `OPENWHISPR_OPENAI_BASE_URL`/`OPENAI_BASE_URL` override), Azure Speech by `azureSpeechOrigin`, Corti's `https://auth.{eu|us}.corti.app/realms/<tenant>`, the custom connection by its canonical endpoint and feature endpoints and self-hosted servers through `validateEndpoint` (custom connection's validator); each kind's slot is fixed (`KIND_SLOTS`; a feature endpoint names one of `CUSTOM_CONNECTION_SLOTS`, a self-hosted server none). Main reads the key (`createSecretReader`; Corti's speech-to-text slot `CORTI_CLIENT_CREDENTIALS` is its client id and secret) and makes **one free request**, never text or audio:
  - OpenAI, Groq: `GET {base}/models` (Bearer), ids compared. Anthropic: `/v1/models` with `x-api-key` and `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`, models not compared (aliases vs dated ids). Gemini: `/v1beta/models?pageSize=1000` with `x-goog-api-key`, up to 5 pages (more is `truncated`), ids minus `models/`; its 400 `API_KEY_INVALID` is a rejected key. Mistral: `/v1/models`, its `aliases` count. Corti language models: `https://ai.eu.corti.app/v1/models` (Bearer), not compared. Deepgram: `/v1/projects` (`Token`), status only. xAI: `/v1/api-key`; a blocked or disabled key or team is rejected (`CHECK_KEY_BLOCKED`).
  - OpenRouter: `/api/v1/key` only, never `/credits` (that needs a management key): a management or provisioning key is rejected (`CHECK_MANAGEMENT_KEY`, "can't make requests"); `limit`/`limit_remaining` only when the key has a limit (`null` is unlimited; ≤ 0 left is `CHECK_LIMIT_REACHED`); models from the public `/api/v1/models`, without the key, and, when a model in use isn't listed there, its speech-to-text list (`/api/v1/models?output_modalities=transcription`, which is the only place OpenRouter lists them); a list that can't be read leaves its models unknown.
  - AssemblyAI: `GET /v2/transcript?limit=1`, status only, the body cancelled unread (it lists recordings' metadata). Azure Speech: `{origin}/speechtotext/models/base?api-version=2024-11-15&top=1` with `Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key` (proves the key and resource; MAI-Transcribe-2 is checked when you dictate). Tinfoil: `/v1/models` through the attested client (`tinfoilSecureFetch`), no key: reachability and its ids (`CHECK_NOT_VERIFIABLE`, `proof: "reachability"`).
  - Corti speech-to-text: the client-credentials token request through `cortiAuth.getCortiToken`, `fresh` on an explicit Check (its cache otherwise), so edited credentials or tenants are asked again; 400/401 is rejected.
  - The custom connection: `customConnectionIpc.runCustomEndpointCheck` (the dialog's Check connection, shared): `{languageBase}/models` (Bearer), then on Azure its deployments; deployment ids compared (ignoring case) only when listed in full, else `unknown` (unavailable or `truncated`: more than 200, or a page link); 401 rejected, 403 access denied, 429 rate limited, 5xx unreachable. The catalog is never returned.
  - A feature's custom endpoint: `GET {base}/models` with its slot's key (none when empty, `proof: "reachability"`); a self-hosted server: the same without a key, a 404 counting as reachable.
  - GitHub Copilot: automatically `GET https://api.github.com/user` with the stored token (identity: "Signed in", `proof: "identity"`); an explicit Check also `runtime.listModels({ refresh: true })` (45 s; starts the runtime) and compares the plan's ids (`proof: "service"`, "Copilot available"); its authentication failure calls `auth.reportTokenRejected`, as `copilot-list-models` does. OpenWhispr Cloud isn't here: the renderer holds the session (`connectionHealthStore`).
  - **Evidence**, not a verdict: `{ reachable, credentials: accepted | rejected | unknown, models: { [id]: offered | missing | unknown }, limit?, httpStatus?, code, proof, checkedAt, generation }`. Failures classify through `providerHttpError` (a 403 isn't always a bad key; a quota answer keeps the key accepted; 5xx and timeouts are unreachable; a 404 is `CHECK_ENDPOINT_NOT_FOUND`); bodies are read (bounded) only to classify and compare, never kept. `compareModels`: listed (or a listed alias) is offered; a truncated listing, a moving alias (`-latest`, preview, experimental), a dated variant either way or a routing variant (`model:free`) is unknown; only the rest is missing.
  - **Transport** (`createTransport`): the injected fetch (Electron's proxy-aware `net.fetch` on `session.fromPartition("ow-connection-checks")`, no cookies; `connectionChecksIpc.js`), `redirect: "manual"` and only same-origin hops followed (a credential-bearing request never follows another origin: `CHECK_REDIRECTED`), an 8 s timeout covering the body, at most 4 requests at once (Tinfoil's included), `assertCloudAllowed` on every URL (`CHECK_CLOUD_DISABLED`). Logs carry the kind, the code and the HTTP status only.
  - **Queue and cache** (`createConnectionChecks`): one queue deduplicating a descriptor already in flight (an explicit Check of Copilot or Corti's speech-to-text still runs its deeper check); results cached per descriptor id, fenced by the descriptor's generation and main's **credential generation** per slot (each source secret's counter, bumped by `noteSecretsChanged` from environment.js's `onSecretChanged`: every write, copy or removal, the Copilot sign-in and sign-out, Reset app data). A check whose generations changed while it ran is dropped (`{ id, generation, dropped: true }`); a key change invalidates its cached results (`{ id, invalidated: true }`; Reset app data, all), the Cloud switch those on its domains. `run({ descriptors, maxAgeMs, fresh })` answers young fenced results at once and queues the rest; no key → `API_KEY_MISSING` without a request
- **connectionChecksIpc.js**: `connection-checks-run` (`connectionChecks.run`) and the `connection-check-result` broadcast (`{ id, generation, result }`, `dropped` or `invalidated`: codes and evidence, never a key, body or model list); wires environment.js's `onSecretChanged` and the Cloud switch's `onCloudDisabledChange` into the queue. Its fetch is `session.fromPartition("ow-connection-checks").fetch` with `credentials: "omit"` (an in-memory session, created on first use; `session-created` installs the Cloud blocker on it). Registered in `ipcHandlers.js` beside the custom connection's IPC
- **connectionOutcomeTargets.js**: "Last recorded use" (stage B of the connection checks), pure ESM shared by main (require(esm)) and the renderer: which of stage A's descriptor ids a real request ran on. `llmOutcomeTarget({ provider, baseUrl, lanUrl, slot })` (`provider:<id>` with its `PROVIDER_KEY_SLOTS` slot, `cloud` for OpenWhispr Cloud, `copilot`, `corti:llm`, a feature's custom endpoint `endpoint:<hash>` of its route and key slot, a self-hosted server `selfHosted:<hash>`; local models and enterprise deployments none), `transcriptionOutcomeTarget(route, settings)` / `transcriptionOutcomeTag(route, settings, feature)` from a resolved transcription route (the custom and self-hosted URLs read from the settings fields stage A reads; Azure Speech `provider:azure-speech`, Corti `corti:stt`). The hashes and routes are stage A's own (`shortHash`, `endpointCheckRoute`, `endpointDescriptorId`, moved here from `connectionHealth.ts`, which re-exports them), never a URL. `descriptorSlot(id, slot)` is the slot an outcome is fenced and cleared by (only an `endpoint:` id takes the request's; undefined for anything stage A wouldn't build). Features: `OUTCOME_FEATURES` (`featureForScope`: the screen context model's scope is the assistant's). `connectionFailureCode(error)` keeps the connection's own answers only (`PROVIDER_*`, `AZURE_SPEECH_ENDPOINT_INVALID`, Cloud's `AUTH_EXPIRED`/`LIMIT_REACHED`/`SERVER_ERROR` as auth/quota/unavailable, an unclassified network error as `asProviderError` reads it) and drops cancels and yields (`isCancellation`: AbortError, `*_CANCELLED`, `YIELDED_TO_DICTATION`, status 499), a missing key, and anything already worded as something else (an output check such as `CLEANUP_OUTPUT_INVALID`, a truncation, a refusal); `requestOutcomeOfFailure` also reads `NO_SPEECH_DETECTED` and `EMPTY_TRANSCRIPT` as the service working
- **connectionOutcomes.js**: "Last recorded use", main's one writer, `recordConnectionOutcome({ descriptorId, slot, feature, ok, code, startedAt })`, which never throws into a request: refuses anything stage A couldn't show (id and slot by `descriptorSlot`, a feature from `OUTCOME_FEATURES`, an uppercase code), keeps each descriptor's last outcome and last failure (`{ feature, ok, code, at }`) in memory and in History's database (`connection_outcomes`, `database.js` `_initConnectionOutcomes`: created idempotently, local only, never synced, exported, indexed or counted, kept when History is off or cleared; a failure to store keeps it for the session) and broadcasts `connection-outcomes-changed` (`{ outcomes }`). Fenced by the same signal as the checks' credential generation (environment.js's `onSecretChanged`, the slot's sources as `connectionChecks.sourcesOf` expands them): an outcome whose request started before its slot's key last changed is dropped, and the change clears what was recorded under it (a replaced or removed key, a Copilot sign-out, Reset app data: all); Cloud's at sign-out (`ipcHandlers._handleAuthTokenChange` without a token → `noteCloudSignedOut`). Logs carry the feature, OK and the code. IPC (`registerConnectionOutcomesIpc`, beside the checks in `ipcHandlers.js`): `connection-outcomes-get`, `connection-outcome-record` (fire and forget, from the renderer's `utils/connectionOutcome.ts`)
- **azureSpeechTranscription.js**: Pure ESM (renderer, route resolver and main via require(esm)) for Azure Speech's fast transcription with MAI-Transcribe-2: `isAzureSpeechEndpoint` / `azureSpeechEndpointError` (https, the resource origin only: no path, query, fragment, port or credentials, on `<label>.cognitiveservices.azure.com` or `<region>.api.cognitive.microsoft.com`; also the same resource as Azure AI Foundry shows it, `https://<name>.services.ai.azure.com`, with or without a project's `/api/projects/<project>` (one segment, a trailing slash allowed), any other path, query, fragment, credentials or port there still `resourceOnly`; deliberately not `isAzureOpenAIEndpoint`, whose hosts differ and which picks OpenAI's `api-key` header; `urlUtils.ts` re-exports it), `azureSpeechOrigin` (the origin; a Foundry form **converted, not called**: `https://<name>.cognitiveservices.azure.com`, the same custom subdomain on the host Microsoft documents for fast transcription, so the key never goes to the `services.ai` host), `normalizeAzureSpeechEndpoint` (bare host → https, a valid one → its origin, a Foundry one converted), `buildAzureSpeechTranscribeUrl` / `isAzureSpeechTranscribeUrl` (`{origin}/speechtotext/transcriptions:transcribe?api-version=2025-10-15`, built from the converted origin even for a stored, unconverted value; what main checks before sending the key, which accepts only the documented URL, never the same path on `services.ai`), `resolveMaiLocale` (base codes as Microsoft's examples use, `no` → `nb`, `tl` → `fil`; "auto" and languages MAI doesn't list (`be`, `hr`, `mt`, `mi`, `sr`, `cy`) send no locale, and the dictation log says so), `buildAzureSpeechDefinition` (`locales` only when set, `enhancedMode: { enabled, model }`, `phraseList` only with phrases, at most 199 (200 is rejected); never `modelOptions`, so the verbatim default stays), `buildAzureSpeechRequest` (the `Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key` header, the `definition` field, the `audio` part name; null for an invalid endpoint), `parseAzureSpeechAnswer` (`combinedPhrases[].text` trimmed, empty ones skipped, joined with a space; "" for silence) and `isAzureSpeechAcceptedExtension` (WAV, MP3, FLAC). Constants: provider id `azure-speech`, default model `MAI-Transcribe-2`, 250 MB cap. Tests: `test/helpers/azureSpeechTranscription.test.js`
- **dictationTranscriptionRequest.js**: Live dictation's batch request in main (OpenAI-compatible endpoints and Azure Speech; `dictation-transcription-request` / `-cancel`): its own connection pool `ow-dictation-stt` (`connectionPool.js`), the deadline (`transcriptionRequestDeadline.js`), SSE reading (`transcriptionEventStream.js`), one retry on a reset pool, per-sender cancellation by request id; `runHistoryTranscriptionRequest` serves History's Re-transcribe (and Azure Speech uploads) on `ow-history-stt`. Each logical request (after its retry) records its "Last recorded use" (`connectionOutcomes.js`) under the tag it came with (the dictation window's `outcome`, or main's `transcriptionOutcomeTag`): OK, the HTTP status's code also when the IPC answers `success: true` around it, or the failure's; never a cancel. `transcriptionRequestFrom` allows two extensions only: the `Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key` header, which it refuses unless the endpoint is exactly a Speech resource's transcribe URL (`isAzureSpeechTranscribeUrl`) and then sends alone, and the audio part's name, `file` (default) or `audio`. The renderer side is `dictationTranscriptionClient.js`. See section 2
- **devServerManager.js**: Vite dev server integration
- **dockManager.js**: Single owner of the macOS Dock icon
  - The icon follows the control panel: it appears when the panel opens and goes away when the panel closes to the tray, so no other caller (in particular the dictation panel's hide path) can resurrect it
  - Every path that surfaces or hides the control panel reports that state explicitly: openers (tray, app menu, deep links, `activate`, second instance, first show) go through `windowManager.showControlPanel()`, hiding through `hideControlPanelToTray()`
  - `showControlPanel()` reports the Dock before restoring, showing and focusing the window: on an active app `dock.show()` activates the Dock and then re-activates the app, which macOS does not honour, so a window surfaced first is left behind the previously frontmost app
  - `showControlPanel()` calls `show()` even on an open panel, since on macOS that activates the app and `focus()` does not; openers that send the user to another app (meeting-prompt Join, the meeting hotkey, Upcoming Meetings' Join & transcribe) pass `{ activate: false }`, so an open panel updates behind the meeting instead of covering it. A hidden panel is still shown
  - `app.dock.show()`/`hide()` run only when the requested state differs from `app.dock.isVisible()`, so reopening an already open panel never triggers that Dock hand-off
  - Never derive that state from the window's `show`/`hide` events: on macOS those are occlusion events, so they also fire when the panel is merely covered by another window, minimized, or on another Space, and the icon flickers as the user switches windows
  - Decision logic lives in `dockPolicy.js` (pure, unit-tested in `test/helpers/dockPolicy.test.js`)
- **dragManager.js**: Window dragging functionality
- **environment.js**: Environment variable and OpenAI API management. `onSecretChanged(listener)` (static on the class, module-level like the write queue): told the names, never the values, of secrets whose value changed (`_saveKey`, `persistSecret`, the Copilot sign-in's save and clear, `clearAllPersistedData` with `all: true`); a save of the same value tells nothing. Connection checks fence their results by it
- **hotkeyManager.js**: Global hotkey registration and management
  - Named hotkey slots: `dictation`, `voiceAgent` (voice assistant — opens and listens in the agent window), `translation`, `meeting`, `review` (Review last dictation, see 13d: a tap slot with no default key, wired like `meeting` on every backend: `.env` `REVIEW_KEY`, `register-review-hotkey` in `main.js`, `LINUX_NATIVE_TAP_SLOTS`, the GNOME and Hyprland D-Bus method `ReviewLastDictation`, KDE by slot name, the native listener's dispatch)
  - Handles platform-specific defaults (GLOBE on macOS, Control+Super on Windows/Linux)
  - Auto-fallback to F8/F9 if default hotkey is unavailable
  - The dictation slot also carries the style hotkeys (Stage 4, see 13g): `styleKeys` is the committed hotkey → style id map, `slot.styleHotkeys` lists which registered keys are style keys, and `getStyleIdForHotkey` maps a press to its style
  - Notifies renderer via IPC when hotkey registration fails
  - Integrates with GnomeShortcutManager for GNOME Wayland support
  - Integrates with HyprlandShortcutManager for Hyprland Wayland support
  - Integrates with KDEShortcutManager for KDE Wayland support
  - Each press and release of a bound hotkey writes a debug-level `Hotkey event` line (`logHotkeyEvent`: source, action, key, owning slots, activation mode; key names only). Unbound keys and hotkey capture are skipped, since the macOS listener reports every right modifier and side mouse button. Every `globalShortcut` registration goes through `registerGlobalShortcut`, which logs a held key once and leaves keys the Windows/Linux listener watches to that listener; GNOME, KDE and Hyprland report through their `onHotkeyEvent` hook, and `handleMacPushModifierUp` logs the release of a macOS compound hold. The Windows/Linux listener's presses are logged from `main.js` before `nativeHotkeyDispatch` acts on them; a Windows press or release also carries the listener's timing (`KEY_DOWN <osEventMs> <hookMs>`), reported on the same line as `eventToHookMs` and `hookToMainMs` (`describeKeyTiming`), and the listener no longer logs each key itself
- **gnomeShortcut.js**: GNOME Wayland global shortcut integration
  - Uses D-Bus service to receive hotkey toggle commands
  - Registers shortcuts via gsettings (visible in GNOME Settings → Keyboard → Shortcuts)
  - Converts Electron hotkey format to GNOME keysym format
  - Only active on Linux + Wayland + GNOME desktop
  - D-Bus transport: `@homebridge/dbus-native` (pure JavaScript, no native addons)
- **hyprlandShortcut.js**: Hyprland Wayland global shortcut integration
  - Uses D-Bus service to receive hotkey toggle commands (same `com.openwhispr.App` service)
  - Registers shortcuts via `hyprctl keyword bind` (runtime keybinding)
  - Converts Electron hotkey format to Hyprland bind format (`MODS, key`)
  - Only active on Linux + Wayland + Hyprland (detected via `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE`)
  - D-Bus transport: `@homebridge/dbus-native` (pure JavaScript, no native addons)
- **kdeShortcut.js**: KDE Wayland global shortcut integration
  - Uses D-Bus to communicate with KGlobalAccel for global hotkey registration
  - Registers hotkeys via `setShortcut`/`doRegister` D-Bus calls on the KGlobalAccel interface
  - Listens for `globalShortcutPressed` signals to trigger callbacks
  - Converts Electron hotkey format to Qt key codes
  - Only active on Linux + KDE desktop (detected via `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`)
  - D-Bus transport: `@homebridge/dbus-native` (pure JavaScript, no native addons)
- **autoStart.js**: Single entry point for launch at login, used by `ipcHandlers.js` and `main.js`
  - Dispatches to `setLoginItemSettings()` on macOS/Windows and to `linuxAutostart.js` on Linux
  - `getAutoStartState()` returns `{ enabled, requiresApproval }`; `requiresApproval` is macOS-only and means SMAppService registered the item but the user has not allowed it under System Settings → General → Login Items yet
  - `wasLaunchedAtLoginHidden()` decides whether this launch should go straight to the tray
  - `syncAutoStartEntry()` runs from `initializeCoreManagers()` and repairs entries written by older builds
  - Decision logic lives in `autoStartPolicy.js` (pure, unit-tested in `test/helpers/autoStartPolicy.test.js`)
- **autoStartPolicy.js**: Electron-free launch-at-login and relaunch decisions
  - `HIDDEN_LAUNCH_FLAG` (`--hidden`) is how a login launch tells the app to start in the tray. Windows has no native equivalent (`openAsHidden` is macOS-only and a no-op on macOS 13+), so the flag rides on the login item's `args`; Linux puts it on the autostart entry's `Exec`; macOS uses `wasOpenedAtLogin` instead
  - On Windows, read the state from `executableWillLaunchAtLogin`, never from `openAtLogin`: `openAtLogin` only compares the `Run` value against the current executable and args and ignores the `StartupApproved` key that Task Manager and Settings write when a user disables a startup app
  - Reads and writes must pass identical `args`, or `openAtLogin` always reports false
  - `getRelaunchOptions()` and `getRelaunchWaiter()` shape the `relaunch-app` IPC that follows `cleanup-app` (Reset app data; Delete account with device erase): the relaunch drops `--hidden` and any cold-start deep link, and an AppImage is started again from its on-disk file (`$APPIMAGE`) by a detached waiter, since it runs from a FUSE mount that disappears when the app exits. The restart itself is `appRelaunch.js`
- **appRelaunch.js**: `relaunchApp({ app, updateManager, protocol })`, the one restart behind the `relaunch-app` IPC (Settings' Restart buttons) and the tray's Restart items, both through `ipcHandlers.relaunchApp()`: only quits under `npm run dev` (Vite dies with Electron, so a relaunched dev copy would have no renderer); on macOS with an update Squirrel already holds, hands the restart to the updater; otherwise defers the update install, relaunches (an AppImage through `getRelaunchWaiter`) and quits. Tests: `test/helpers/appRelaunch.test.js`, `relaunchAppIpc.test.js`
- **linuxAutostart.js**: Launch-at-login on Linux via an XDG autostart entry
  - `app.setLoginItemSettings()` is a no-op on Linux, so the entry is written directly to `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/autostart/open-whispr.desktop`, matching the executable name electron-builder packages under
  - `Exec` resolves from `$APPIMAGE` first: `process.execPath` is the ephemeral AppImage FUSE mount
  - `isAutostartEnabled()` honors `X-GNOME-Autostart-enabled=false` and `Hidden=true`, which GNOME Tweaks and KDE's autostart editor write in place instead of deleting the file
  - `Exec` carries `--hidden` (see `autoStartPolicy.js`), and `syncAutostartEntry()` compares against the full value including that flag — comparing against the bare path would make every launch look stale
  - `syncAutostartEntry()` runs from `autoStart.syncAutoStartEntry()` in `initializeCoreManagers()` and re-points a stale `Exec` after the executable moves (renamed or auto-updated AppImage); it never re-enables an entry the user disabled, and no-ops in development
  - Unit-tested in `test/helpers/linuxAutostart.test.js`
- **ipcHandlers.js**: Centralized IPC handler registration
- **windowsKeyManager.js**: Windows Push-to-Talk support with native key listener
  - Spawns native `windows-key-listener.exe` binary for low-level keyboard hooks
  - Supports compound hotkeys (e.g., `Ctrl+Shift+F11`, `CommandOrControl+Space`)
  - Emits `key-down` and `key-up` events for push-to-talk functionality
  - Graceful fallback if binary unavailable
- **meetingDetectionEngine.js**: Orchestrates meeting detection from all sources
  - Gates notifications during recording (tap-to-talk and push-to-talk)
  - Post-recording cooldown (2.5s) before showing queued notifications
  - Priority-based coalescing (process > audio) — one notification, not three
  - Both detectors start off and only run once the renderer has synced its saved notification preferences (`sync-notification-preferences`); they stop when meeting prompts are disabled and restart when re-enabled. The derivation lives in `meetingDetectionPreferencePolicy.js` (pure, unit-tested in `test/helpers/meetingDetectionPreferencePolicy.test.js`); `ipcHandlers.js` is a thin adapter over it
- **meetingProcessDetector.js**: Detects running meeting apps
  - macOS: Event-driven via `systemPreferences.subscribeWorkspaceNotification` (zero CPU)
  - Windows/Linux: Shared `processListCache` polling (30s interval)
  - Scans are start-generation guarded: a process-list read that completes after `stop()` or a newer `start()` is discarded, since the detector is now stopped and started at runtime (notification toggles, auto-end sessions)
- **audioActivityDetector.js**: Detects microphone usage for unscheduled meetings
  - macOS: Event-driven via `macos-mic-listener` binary (CoreAudio process objects; aggregate device activity prompts only while a known meeting app is running)
  - Windows: Event-driven via `windows-mic-listener.exe` (WASAPI sessions, self-PID exclusion)
  - Linux: Event-driven via `pactl subscribe` (PulseAudio source-output events)
  - Windows/Linux: Graceful fallback to polling if the native approach fails; macOS has no safe polling signal and respawns the listener with backoff instead
- **processListCache.js**: Shared singleton process list cache (5s TTL, `ps-list` npm)
- **meetingEchoLeakDetector.js**: Audio-layer echo analysis for meeting recordings — correlates each mic chunk against the recent system-audio tap (lag search 0–500 ms in 5 ms steps) and classifies it `clean_local` / `suspected_render_bleed` / `double_talk`; drives chunk muting and per-segment suppression flags. PCM-driven tests in `test/helpers/meetingEchoLeakDetector.test.js`
- **meetingMicGate.js**: Pure RMS/peak chunk stats + the meeting mic gate verdict (`send` / `zero` for streaming, `send` / `skip` for local) with the exported silence and bleed thresholds; `ipcHandlers.js` (`dispatchMeetingAudioBuffer`, `transcribeLocalMeetingChunk`) only applies the verdict. Unit-tested in `test/helpers/meetingMicGate.test.js`
- **meetingMicHoldback.js**: Pure holdback/retract policy for risky mic finals — pending-final partition, retract window, risky-profile classifier, text-layer duplicate check, racing-retract candidate selection, pending-overlap partition. `ipcHandlers.js` keeps thin adapters over its closure state (`meetingDiarizationSegments`, `meetingPendingMicFinals`, `hasNearbyTranscriptMatch`). Unit-tested in `test/helpers/meetingMicHoldback.test.js`
- **meetingFragmentFilter.js**: Drops meeting finals of four characters or fewer whose letters are in a script none of the meeting's languages use (the set language, or the spoken languages under auto); `ipcHandlers.js` applies it to streaming and local finals. Unit-tested in `test/helpers/meetingFragmentFilter.test.js`
- **googleCalendarManager.js**: Google Calendar sync (REST, OAuth via `googleCalendarOAuth.js`)
  - 10s socket timeout on API requests
  - Incremental sync via `syncToken`; full re-sync on 410 prunes stale events (note-linked rows retained)
  - Sync tokens pin the `timeMin`/`timeMax` window of the full sync that created them (incremental syncs never roll it forward), so tokens are discarded after 1 day to keep the 33-day lookahead covering the availability tool's 31-day horizon
- **microsoftCalendarManager.js**: Microsoft Calendar sync via Graph API (OAuth via `microsoftCalendarOAuth.js`)
  - `calendarView/delta` incremental sync over a 38-day window; delta token discarded after 7 days (Graph delta links never roll their window forward)
  - Delta can return recurring-series occurrences as bare stubs (no subject/attendees/meeting link); they're backfilled from their series master, one `GET /me/calendars/{calendarId}/events/{id}` per series (calendar-scoped: `/me/events/{id}` 404s for shared calendars). A failed backfill shortens the delta token TTL to 10 min so an early full sync retries instead of leaving untitled blocks
  - Full re-sync (410 or expired token) prunes stale events like Google
- **appleCalendarManager.js**: Apple Calendar (EventKit) via the `macos-calendar-listener` Swift helper — macOS only, snapshot-push over stdout, no tokens ("connected" = `apple_calendars` has rows). The helper takes `--lookback-seconds` (an old binary ignores it and keeps a ~2-hour lookback; rebuild with `npm run compile:calendar-listener`)
- **calendarCacheWindow.js**: `CALENDAR_LOOKBACK_DAYS` (3) — every provider keeps this much past history (Google `timeMin`, Microsoft `startDateTime`, the Apple helper flag) so notes can link recent meetings offline. Widening it needs a `user_version` token reset in `database.js` (v5 did it for 3 days)
- **calendarReminderScheduler.js**: Provider-agnostic meeting reminder scheduling over the shared `calendar_events` table (provider-scoped reset keys, so one provider's disconnect doesn't re-fire another's reminders)
- **calendarSyncInterval.js**: Shared interval runner for the REST providers — exponential backoff (2min → 4min → 8min → cap 30min on consecutive failures, reset on success) and 30s focus-sync throttle
- **oauthLoopbackFlow.js**: Shared PKCE auth-code flow over an ephemeral 127.0.0.1 server, used by both calendar OAuth helpers
- Events from all providers land in the shared `calendar_events` table with a `provider` column; queries suppress the Apple copy of a meeting when a REST row occupies the same time slot + title (Calendar.app mirrors the same accounts); all-day rows pair by local start and end days + title, since REST stores plain dates and the Apple helper local instants
- The note header's date/attendees dropdown (`NoteParticipants.tsx` + `NoteEventPicker.tsx`) links a note to a stored event (`getLinkableCalendarEvents`, local only): linking merges the event's guests into `participants` in the same write, leaving out rooms and declined guests and leaving attendees already on the note as they are, and tags the ones it adds `addedByEvent: <event id>` (meeting detection tags the attendees it copies onto new notes the same way, via `eventAttendees.js`); the user's own `self` row (every calendar sync stores `self` as the user, Google's shared calendars included) is kept whatever they answered and never tagged, since they recorded the note whichever event it links. Removing or switching the event drops exactly the tagged ones, and speaker counts skip `resource` attendees; Join only opens the link; `web_link` (Google `htmlLink`, Outlook `webLink`, none for Apple) backs "Open in calendar". An unlink is stored as `calendar_event_id = ""`, never NULL (`updateNote` converts an explicit NULL on a linked note), because the cloud and `upsertNoteFromCloud` COALESCE a NULL as "unchanged"
- **menuManager.js**: Application menu management
- **toneAppCategories.js**: Tone & Style's app classifier (pure, `test/helpers/toneAppCategories.test.js`): an app identity, window title and page host map to `personal`, `work`, `email` or `other` (see Tone & Style)
- **tray.js**: System tray icon and menu. "Review last dictation" runs the review hotkey's action (`windowManager.reviewLastDictation`, refused the same way during a dictation) and shows that hotkey when one is set: as the item's accelerator text when a menu can show it (`registerAccelerator: false`: the hotkey manager owns the key), in its label otherwise (modifier-only, a side-specific modifier); `register-review-hotkey` rebuilds the menu. "Restart OpenWhispr" (`tray.restart`) sits right above Quit. While the graphics mode (`windowManager.graphicsMode.getState()`, read whenever the menu or icon is built) is a software **fallback** (`isSoftwareFallback`; never a launch flag's software mode or unknown), the menu starts with "⚠ Graphics acceleration is off" (`tray.graphicsFallback.title`; opens Settings at `system#system.diagnostics.graphics` through `windowManager.openSettings`) and "Restart to use the graphics card" (`tray.graphicsFallback.restart`), then a separator; Windows menus can't color text, so the ⚠, the wording and the place are the hint. Both restarts are disabled while one would cut something off (`_restartBlocker`: `windowManager.isDictationBusy()`, a dictation recording, processing or still pasting and saving, or `ipcHandlers.isMeetingRecording()`, from a meeting transcription's start until its stop finished), the top one then reading "Restart after this recording" (`tray.graphicsFallback.restartAfterRecording`); the click checks again (a stale menu: "Tray restart skipped", info, with `busy`), then logs "Restarting via tray menu" (info, `item`, `graphics`) and runs `ipcHandlers.relaunchApp()`, the restart Settings uses (`appRelaunch.js`; `setRestartControls` from `main.js`). The menu rebuilds on the dictation lifecycle and delivery (`onDictationStateChanged`, which `setDictationDeliveryState` now calls on a change), a meeting's start and stop (`_setMeetingRecording`) and a graphics mode change (`onGraphicsModeChanged`, from `graphicsMode.attach`'s `onChange` in `main.js`). Tests: `test/helpers/trayQuickActions.test.js`, `trayRecordingIndicator.test.js`
- **whisper.js**: Local whisper.cpp integration and model management
- **parakeet.js**: NVIDIA Parakeet model management via sherpa-onnx
- **parakeetServer.js**: sherpa-onnx CLI wrapper for transcription
- **qdrantManager.js**: Qdrant vector DB sidecar process lifecycle (spawn, health check, shutdown)
- **semanticSearchLifecycle.js**: Single owner of the semantic search resources — starts Qdrant and the embedding model on demand, drains the SQLite change journal, and releases both after 5 minutes idle
- **llamaServer.js**: The one shared llama-server, holding one model. A start that needs a restart (another model, a bigger context, a different drafter) never cuts off work: it waits while `/slots` reports a slot processing (a read that times out counts as busy, since the server answers between batches; a failed read doesn't), while main has an inference in flight, or for 1 s after a caller was told the server is ready, since the renderer connects to the port itself. The wait stays outside `startupPromise`, so the running model keeps serving. `busyWaitMs` is the deadline (default 60 s; dictation 2 s via `processWithReasoningModel`; pre-warm 0), also covering a wait on another model's load, and running out throws `LOCAL_MODEL_BUSY` with the server untouched, so cleanup falls back to the raw transcript. A caller that gives up ends its wait without swapping its model in (`signal`, `LOCAL_MODEL_CANCELLED`): a note action's cancel reaches it through `localReasoningBridge.cancel`, and Esc in chat through `llama-server-cancel-start`.
