A policy revoke ends a waiting start the same way and stops only a server that is, or is becoming, the revoked model, never the one answering. A `/slots` read that a handoff or a main-side request overtook counts as busy. Explicit `stop()` calls don't wait. Local chat turns ask main for the server before every request (`localServerFetch.ts`), since a model swapped in during a tool step usually reuses the port
- **localEmbeddings.js**: Local text embedding via ONNX Runtime + all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384-dim vectors)
- **vectorIndex.js**: Qdrant collection management — upsert, delete, search
- **windowConfig.js**: Centralized window configuration
  - `WindowPositionUtil.getMainWindowPosition` places the dictation window by a `panelStartPosition` on a display: rows `top`, `middle` and `bottom`, columns `left`, `center`, `right` (bottom-center is stored as the legacy `center`). The top row anchors the window's top edge, so grown surfaces (menu, toasts, error card, the Live Transcript panel) open downward; the other rows anchor its bottom edge and open upward, the middle row putting that edge 32px below the work-area center, which centers the resting 40px pill over its 12px dock inset (`PILL_RESTING_CENTER_OFFSET`). `anchorToRestingBounds` derives a grown window's bounds from the resting ones
  - Top-row renderer mirror: the pill's top docks (`.voice-pill-position-top-*`) are spelled in the same `bottom`/`transform` coordinates as every other dock so the pill glides into a panel footer; `PillTooltip` and `PillCommandMenu` take `side="below"`; the dictation window's toast viewport reads the setting (`panelDocksAtTop`) to open below the pill; the resize mask has `top-left`/`top-center`/`top-right` anchors. The Live Transcript panel still keeps its pill in a bottom footer
- **floatingIconPlacement.js**: Pure ESM shared by main, renderer and tests: position parsing and normalizing, the display preference (`cursor`, `primary`, or an Electron display id) and `resolvePreferredDisplay`, which matches the id unless the remembered EDID label contradicts it, then a unique label (Windows display ids hash adapter + output and can change across reboots or driver resets), then falls back to the primary display without forgetting the preference
- **recordingIndicator.js**: Pure ESM for the floating icon's look while recording (Settings → Floating icon → While recording): styles `classic` / `tinted` / `solid` / `beacon` / `rec` (a near-black capsule with a blinking dot, the elapsed time and the waveform), eight curated colors or a custom `#rrggbb`, and the contrast rules. With "Color by mode" on, `resolveRecordingModeColor` gives voice assistant and translation recordings their own colors (default purple and cyan; dictation keeps the main one), and every surface follows it: the pill, the strip or frame, the tray dot, and the agent window's pill for the assistant's takes. `resolveAgentPillRecordingLook` gives that pill the style while a take records, plus REC's blinking dot and the elapsed time when the style is REC or "Show recording time" is on (section 17). Filled styles get white glyphs when white reaches 3:1, dark ones otherwise (one of the two always clears 3:1), and Tinted lightens or darkens its accent until it reaches 3:1 on the theme's surface. `recordingIndicatorCssVars` yields the `--recording-*` variables that `useRecordingIndicator` puts on an ancestor shared by the pill and the cancel skin. `resolveRecordingChromeStyle` styles only a pill that owns its own chrome while recording: never inside Live Transcript (integrated) or under the fused cancel skin, which redraws the style itself (`.liquid-cancel-skin[data-recording-style]` in `dictation-panel.css`). Beacon's halo and ripple stay inside the 12px dock inset and hold still under reduced motion. It also normalizes the edge strip settings and computes the strip's box (`resolveEdgeStripBounds`: the top of the display's full bounds, 8 DIP tall, short = 25% of the width clamped to 480–1400 DIP, negative origins kept), the elapsed readout (`formatPillElapsed`: `0:07` … `59:59`, then `1h02`, in a fixed 32px slot), the start time main accepts from the renderer (`resolveRecordingStartedAt`: within 10 s back and 1 s ahead, else main's own clock) and the reminder marks (`nextRecordingReminderDelay`: whole intervals from the start, marks missed while asleep skipped)
- **recordingEdgeStrip.js**: The optional glow at the screen edge while the microphone is live (Settings → Floating icon → Screen edge while recording): a strip along the top (short or full width, on the pill's display — read when it shows, so a drag takes it along — or all displays) or a frame (`frame`) around the work area of the pill's display only. One overlay window per display, created when it first shows and kept hidden between recordings until it has been idle for 5 minutes (each holds a renderer, ~20 MB private), or 30 s for a frame, whose work-area surface stays held while hidden (the GPU process grew by ~260 MB for one on a 5672 DIP display at 135%); a frame left behind on another monitor is destroyed at once, and turning the setting off destroys them all. Thin windows can't stand in for a frame: Windows clamps windows to at least 64 px in each direction, growing right and down, which pushed bottom and right edges off the display. The page is an encoded CSS-only `data:` URL with JavaScript off, loaded once per mode, and steady: a transparent window is recomposited on every animation frame, and three breathing strips cost the GPU process about a fifth of a core while recording. Everything draws in `--a`; a new color (a settings change, or a recording of another mode) is inserted as `:root{--a:…}` with `webContents.insertCSS` and the previous rule removed, so the page never reloads, and a hidden window waits for its color before showing. A recording's start flashes each strip once (a one-shot keyframes rule, 500 ms, removed when it ends; never for a preview, a refresh or a recolor), and a reminder swells a visible recording strip (1.2 s); the page's reduced-motion rule silences both. A recolor that fails reloads the page with the color baked in. Windows are transparent, unfocusable, click-through on every platform (`setIgnoreMouseEvents(true)`), content-protected (absent from screen shares, screenshots and screen context), shown with `showInactive` only after their page has loaded (the `loadURL` promise, never a `getURL()` comparison) and only if a recording or preview still wants them. A work-area frame didn't make Windows report a fullscreen app (`SHQueryUserNotificationState` stayed "accepts notifications"); unmeasured: its effect on video or games presenting underneath while recording, and an auto-hide taskbar, whose work area is the whole display. On Windows a strip's native window ends up 64 px tall at 135% whatever the requested 8 DIP; the rest is transparent and click-through. `windowManager.setDictationLifecycleState` drives it (every dictation kind; meetings are separate), fed by the dictation window's `dictationLifecycleStateChanged(state, attempt, details)` (an invoke: main answers whether it accepted the change, refusing any other window, an older attempt's report and a start while the agent window records; `details` is `{ inputKind, startedAt }`, the mode for the colors and the recording's start). The agent window's voice takes (section 17) don't report it, so they show no strip, tray dot or reminders. Placements and drags re-place it, and the topology reconcile drops windows of displays that are gone. Because the renderer reports the lifecycle, main resets it to idle itself when the dictation renderer crashes, reloads or closes (`_registerDictationLifecycleReset`), so neither the strip nor the recording gate can stay stuck, and closing the dictation window releases every strip window so none outlives it. Main keeps one start time per recording (`getRecordingStartedAt`, set on the transition into recording from the renderer's back-dated start) for the tray, the pill's elapsed time and the reminder clock: at each interval of "Remind me while recording" it pulses the strip and sends `recording-reminder` to the dictation window's pill, cancelled when the recording stops or the setting changes. The dictation window reports the color, strip, displays, tray, color-by-mode and reminder settings over `recording-indicator-changed` (main accepts it only from that window and keeps them, since the tray is created later); `preview-recording-edge-strip` carries its own settings, and a live recording keeps its strip over any preview. Once per recording start, when every targeted display shows its strip, it calls `onRecordingShown({ window, mode, displays, at })` (`window` is `new` when the show waited for a page load, else `reused`; never for a preview), and `stripModeForRecording()` says what a recording would show (`off` when unsupported), both for the start visuals line. Off on Linux Wayland, where windows can't be placed
- **trayRecordingBadge.js**: Pure helpers for the tray's dots: `drawRecordingBadge` draws a dot in a given color, in a dark ring, into a premultiplied BGRA bitmap, and `formatRecordingElapsed` gives the recording tooltip's `m:ss`. `tray.js` (`updateTrayIcon`, called on lifecycle, settings and graphics mode changes and once after the tray is created) decides the icon from both the recording and the graphics mode: the recording dot (Settings → Floating icon → Tray icon while recording) in the recording's mode color while it shows; otherwise, while graphics is a software fallback, an amber dot (`#f5a524`) whatever that setting says (it's a health warning), with "Graphics acceleration is off, animations may stutter. Right-click to restart." (`tray.graphicsFallbackTooltip`; macOS says Click, `tray.graphicsFallbackTooltipMac`) as the tooltip whenever the recording one isn't showing. So a recording with the setting on covers the amber dot and gives it back when it ends, and with the setting off the amber dot stays through it. It swaps in one badged bitmap drawn at 16 DIP × the primary display's scale (Windows turns a generated image into a single bitmap, so extra representations are ignored), cached per size and color (up to four) so the two dots take turns without redrawing, keeps the loaded `.ico` to restore, and ticks only the recording tooltip each second from the window manager's start time. Debug lines: "Tray recording badge shown/removed", "Tray graphics warning shown/removed". macOS keeps its template icon (it can't carry a color) and gets the tooltips and the menu items only
- **plasmaWorkAreas.js**: Per-monitor work areas on KDE Plasma. On X11 and XWayland, Chromium clips only the primary display with the single root `_NET_WORKAREA`, which KWin builds from every monitor's panels at once (#1110). This asks plasmashell over D-Bus (`evaluateScript`) for each screen's always-visible panels and rebuilds each display's `workArea` for the dictation pill, the agent window and dragging. On X11, Qt reports a screen's origin in device pixels and only its size scaled, so those origins are divided by the display scale before matching. It refreshes on display events and each time a dictation shows the pill, retrying after 1, 5 and 15 s when plasmashell isn't ready; a display event starts those retries over. When the result changes, an auto-placed pill re-anchors through the floating icon placement (its locked monitor and grid spot, at the exact base size); a dragged one stays put while it stays on its display. The startup correction applies even while onboarding shows. With a single display, or outside Linux + KDE, Chromium's work area is used as is
- **windowManager.js**: Window creation and lifecycle management
  - Dictation window placement: one owner, through the native geometry queue (`_enqueueMainWindowMutation`), so a placement can never be overwritten by a resize waiting on the renderer's resize mask. `_commitPlacement` puts the pill at its configured spot on a display and records the resting bounds a grown surface returns to. At fractional scaling (135%) each `setBounds` rounds and grows the window a pixel or two, so placement and the BASE restore always set the exact BASE box instead of feeding the reported size back
  - When it runs: at a dictation start (`showDictationPanel({ reposition: true })`, on the locked display or the one the user works on — the cursor's on Windows/Linux, the target app's window on macOS), on a settings change, and after display changes, sleep/wake and unlock (debounced, then re-checked at bounded intervals, since Windows relocates windows off displays it turns off and never moves them back). A start on a locked display shows a hidden pill only after it moved, and a hide in the meantime wins
  - A drag (`stopWindowDrag` with movement) keeps the pill where the user put it; with a locked display only while it stays on that display. Settings changes and Settings → "Show me" (`previewFloatingIconPlacement`, held visible by the renderer for 2s against auto-hide) reset it
- **agentWindowManager.js**: The assistant's own window (`?agent=true`, see §17): created hidden once the normal app is up and kept alive while hidden, shown bottom-center on the display the press came from, sized per layout, given the keyboard per platform, and the owner of the agent's voice state that keeps dictation off the microphone. Unit-tested in `test/helpers/agentWindowManager.test.js`
- **agentWindowPlacement.js**: Electron-free geometry for the agent window: the layout sizes and the bottom-center anchor, lifted above a center-docked dictation pill (above the whole dictation window on Windows, where it takes every click) unless there's no room for the agent above the pill within the lower 40%. The modal header drags through the shared `DragManager`, keeping the dock in the centered lower 40% with room to expand upward; placement is remembered per display while the app runs, including dictation clearance on reopen.
- **cliBridge.js**: Loopback HTTP server on ports 8200–8219, bearer-token auth (token at `~/.openwhispr/cli-bridge.json`), 127.0.0.1-only. Used by the unified CLI to talk to a running desktop app. `POST /v1/transcribe` takes a file **path** (never audio) and runs the user's downloaded local model through `IPCHandlers.transcribeLocalFile`, approving the path with `approveAudioPath` first; `GET /v1/transcribe/models` lists local models with download state and the app's default (`localTranscriptionModels.js`, read from the `.env` pre-warm values). Both go through `localModelPolicyGate.js`: the list leaves out what the workspace refuses, and a refused transcription is 403 `forbidden` with the refusal's message, never a switch to another model.
- **copilot/**: GitHub Copilot as a language-model provider for dictation cleanup, note formatting and translation only, over the official Copilot SDK (`@github/copilot-sdk`, pinned to an exact version)
  - `copilotRuntime.js` owns the SDK client and its bundled `copilot-runtime` process: one client per sign-in, started on first use and stopped after 10 idle minutes, on sign-out and on quit (sidecar `copilot`; the SDK's stop is raced against 4 s, then `forceStop`, to fit the 8 s quit deadline). Models come from `client.rpc.models.list`, since `listModels()` caches until disconnect
  - Prewarm: a recording's start sends `copilot-prewarm` when the dictation's cleanup or translation can run on Copilot (`audioManager.beginCopilotPrewarm`, called beside `beginStepHistoryRun` in `useAudioRecording`, never awaited; `copilotPrewarmWanted` reads the policy-effective settings with the pipeline's resolvers: never a voice assistant recording; a translation recording when the translation scope or the chain's cleanup step (cleanup on) sends to Copilot; otherwise when the cleanup scope sends to Copilot in providers mode and cleanup is on or the recording has a style). `runtime.prewarm()` starts the runtime and fetches the catalog unless a fresh one is known, then lets the idle timer run; it is bounded (30 s), never throws, does nothing signed out, and logs one debug line, "Copilot runtime prewarmed" (`coldStart`, `startMs`, `catalog`: `fresh`/`fetched`/`shared`/`failed`, `catalogMs`) or "Copilot runtime prewarm failed" (`code`). A wrong guess only keeps the runtime up for its idle time. Re-transcribe, Re-run cleanup and Prompt Studio never prewarm
  - Catalog: kept per sign-in (token generation) across idle restarts, dropped on a token change or sign-out. One fetch in flight per runtime, shared by the prewarm, requests and `copilot-list-models`; it holds its own lease on the runtime (the idle stop waits for it), gives up after 15 s, and stores its list only while it is still its runtime's flight on the current sign-in (a lost or stopped runtime's fetch is dropped). A request uses a catalog younger than 10 minutes as is; an older one that lists the model is used at once and refreshed after the request; otherwise it waits for a fetch (raced with its deadline and cancel). Only a fresh catalog refuses a model it lacks; a failed fetch keeps the known catalog and lets the request go ahead unchecked
  - Each request is a one-shot session:
    - Copilot's own prompt is replaced and the session has no tools (`availableTools: []`) and `infiniteSessions` off.
    - The runtime's built-in GitHub MCP server is turned off (`disabledMcpServers`, `BUILTIN_MCP_SERVERS`): with no tools it only added a network round trip before every model call (0.6–10 s measured). A server that still connects is counted as `mcpConnected` in the request's timings.
    - Its session filesystem is in memory (`memorySessionFs.js`), so the runtime never writes a transcript to disk.
    - The model id is checked against the catalog first, the session is created with `allowedModels: [model]`, and a `session.model_change` (seen through `createSession`'s `onEvent`, so a swap during creation counts too) aborts it before or during the send, because the runtime silently swaps an unknown model.
    - Thinking off sends the model's lowest effort from the catalog; from a stale catalog that is best-effort: a refusal before any output that may be about the effort refreshes the catalog and retries once with the fresh lowest effort, within the same deadline, and never without an effort.
    - An `onUserPromptTransformed` hook returns the exact prompt; otherwise the runtime prefixes `<current_datetime>`.
    - A truncated reply (`finishReason` `length`) is refused when `requireCompleteOutput` is set.
    - `session.error` events are captured for classification (`copilotErrors.js`), since `sendAndWait` flattens errors to a plain `Error`.
    - It returns `timings` (`coldStart`, `acquireMs`, `catalog`: `fresh`/`stale`/`fetched`/`shared`/`refreshed`/`failed`, `catalogMs`, `sessionMs`, `sendMs`, `closeMs`, `effortRetried` when it retried, `mcpConnected` when an MCP server connected) and `usage` (from `assistant.usage`, finite numbers only: `modelMs`, `ttftMs`, `inputTokens`, `outputTokens`, `cacheReadTokens`, `cacheWriteTokens`); a failure carries the timings so far as `error.copilotTimings`.
  - The runtime's working directory (`userData/copilot-runtime`) is created before every start: a missing one makes the stdio spawn fail silently, surfacing only as "Cannot call write after a stream was destroyed". The runtime gets an allowlisted environment, because `process.env` holds every decrypted API key, plus `COPILOT_GITHUB_TELEMETRY_DISABLED=true`. The Copilot modules redact tokens in their own logs.
  - `copilotAuth.js`: GitHub device flow through `connectors/githubDeviceFlow.js` (`net.fetch`, no session cookies). Every attempt has a flow id; cancel and retry name it, and a reply for a replaced attempt is dropped. A new token is checked against Copilot on its own runtime before it replaces the saved one. The panel (`CopilotSignIn.tsx`) offers Copy code, Copy link and Open in browser, and never opens the browser by itself. The OAuth App client id is `COPILOT_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` in `src/config/copilot.js` (`GITHUB_COPILOT_CLIENT_ID` overrides it). The token is `GITHUB_COPILOT_TOKEN`, a main-only secret that no `get-*` IPC returns; the login is `GITHUB_COPILOT_LOGIN`
  - `copilotIpc.js`: `copilot-auth-status`, `copilot-auth-start`, `copilot-auth-cancel`, `copilot-auth-retry`, `copilot-sign-out`, `copilot-list-models`, `copilot-prewarm` (fire and forget; preload `prewarmCopilot`), `process-copilot-reasoning`; status changes are broadcast as `copilot-auth-changed`. "Copilot request finished" (debug) logs `model`, `scope`, `effort`, `finishReason`, `ms`, `steps` (the timings) and `usage`; a request of 6000 ms or more also warns "Slow Copilot request" with the same fields, so it shows under Problems only and in Diagnostics' recent problems; "Copilot request failed" (warn) adds the partial `steps`. Numbers and step names only, never prompt or reply text; nothing new crosses IPC. AI Connections' check (`connectionChecks.js`) reads the token itself: `GET api.github.com/user` automatically ("Signed in"), plus `runtime.listModels({ refresh: true })` on an explicit Check ("Copilot available")
  - Scope gate (`copilotRequestRefusal` in `src/config/copilot.js`): chat, the voice assistant (`dictationAgent`, selection edits included), `requiresAgent` and image requests are refused in main and in the renderer's `processText`. Chat routing fails closed (`isChatUnsupportedProvider` in `chatRouting.js`), and the Copilot tab is hidden on those scopes
  - Renderer side:
    - `inferenceProviders/copilot.ts`; `copilotStore.ts` holds the auth status.
    - `copilotModels.ts` and `copilotModelCache.ts` hold the signed-in plan's live list (cached for an hour), laid over the catalog's `copilot` entry as an overlay (`setCopilotModelOverlay` in `cloudModelCatalog.ts`, cleared on sign-out), with the shipped list in `models/forkCatalogProviders.js` as the fallback (section 8).
    - `reconcileSelectedModels` repoints any scope that resolves to Copilot in providers mode with a model the plan doesn't serve, including note formatting inheriting cleanup's provider while keeping its own model. It runs after a fetch and on every settings change, against a list fetched within the hour.
    - Copilot ids that collide with OpenAI or Anthropic ids (`gpt-6-astra`, `claude-sonnet-5`, …) resolve per provider, since `getCloudModel` is strict when given one.
  - Packaging: `electron-builder.json` asar-unpacks `@github/copilot-sdk-*`, and `afterPack.js` (`verifyCopilotRuntime`) fails a build without the runtime. The win32-x64 runtime package is about 152 MB unpacked
- **openWhisprCloudGate.js**: The "OpenWhispr account & Cloud" switch (Settings → Privacy & Data; on by default). Off, nothing reaches OpenWhispr's servers (`openwhispr.com`, `gizmovoice.ai` and their subdomains, plus an overridden API or auth origin) and no account or Cloud surface shows
  - Main owns it: `OPENWHISPR_CLOUD_DISABLED=true` in the userData `.env`, read before any request. `set-openwhispr-cloud-disabled` blocks at once, refuses while a bearer token is stored (`SIGNED_IN`), clears OpenWhispr cookies locally and resolves only after the `.env` write (`PERSIST_FAILED` otherwise). Preload reads the value once with `sendSync`; `openwhispr-cloud-disabled-changed` updates `openWhisprCloudStore` in every window, and `lib/auth.ts` re-runs the session check on each change
  - Chromium traffic: one `session.webRequest.onBeforeRequest` listener per session (the app's only one), installed through `app.on("session-created")` before any session exists and reading the live switch. Its filter names `http`, `https`, `ws` and `wss` explicitly: a `*://` filter does not match WebSockets. Each cancel is logged once as `[cloud-off] blocked …`
  - Node traffic the session hook can't see goes through `assertCloudAllowed`: every `ws` streaming client dials through `guardedWebSocket.js`, `urlAudioDownloader` checks each hop, and `externalUrlOpener` refuses OpenWhispr links in the system browser
  - Nothing tries in the first place: `getApiUrl()` answers `""`, the stt/note-recording config handlers answer `CLOUD_DISABLED` without logging, `authContextFetch` answers `get-session` with a local `200 null` (Better Auth's signed-out shape) and rejects other auth calls, `requestSignIn` does nothing, and no sign-in token can be installed (OAuth deep link, auth bridge, `auth-set-token`)
  - Hidden while off: the Account group's Settings pages (Profile, Plans & Billing and the Workspace pages, with the nav's workspace picker; Settings opens on Dictation › Recognition, `settings/settingsPages.ts`), the `openwhispr` mode in every mode picker (`usePolicyModeOptions`; a stored Cloud selection stays visible but unavailable; the language-model picker lists Cloud unavailable, "Turned off in Privacy & Data", with a link there), the sidebar account row and upgrade cards, the account-only Integrations panes (`visibleIntegrationsSections`), the Insights leaderboard, Insights sync, leaderboard and usage-analytics rows in Privacy & Data (its Model list updates row stays: the signed catalog comes from GitHub, not OpenWhispr's servers), Upload's Cloud offers, Support's Docs and contact items, the reauthentication screen and onboarding's sign-in and Cloud setup. The assistant prompt names `cloudOff` instead of asking the user to sign in
  - Google and Microsoft calendar sign-in always answer from a local page (`calendarResultPage`), never the hosted `openwhispr.com/auth/desktop-callback`
- **connectors/**: Agent connectors
  - `connectorManager.js` owns every outside action: `prepare` → ApprovalCard → `commit` for anything other people see, `runDirect` for private drafts. The model can never reach `commit`
  - Three action kinds per action, fixed in code: `approval` (`prepare` → card → `commit`), `direct` (`runDirect`) and `query` (a read for the model, e.g. an issue search). `connectorManager.query` runs `prepare`'s gates (policy, action kind, account, the bound login), also refuses a login whose `ownerAccountId` is another account (`account_changed`), and writes no receipt; `queryResult.js` caps what reaches the model (20 flat items, 16 fields, strings cut at 1,000 characters, `truncated` on any cut) and strips characters that hide text (controls, DEL and C1, bidi overrides, zero-width spaces, tag characters; ZWNJ and ZWJ stay). `runQueryAction` marks the items `untrusted`, with guidance never to follow instructions in them, and takes the attendee fence's tag name out of them (`withoutAttendeesFence`); the chat keeps no copy of a search's items in the saved (and synced) conversation (`isQueryResultData`)
  - Registration is by list: `createConnectors.js` (`CONNECTOR_FACTORIES`, one `build<Name>Connector(deps)` per provider module) in main, `connectorToolModules.ts` (`CONNECTOR_TOOL_MODULES`, registered by `readyConnectorIds()`) for tools, and `connectorRows.tsx` (`CONNECTOR_ROWS`) for Settings rows. Each tool carries its own `promptInstruction` and `connectorId`; the connector rules join the prompt when any connector tool is offered
  - `runApprovalAction` caps a turn at 5 approval cards across connectors (`approval_card` turn slot, given back when no card appears). Cards lay out fields by `verbKey`: `email` (To, Cc when it has one, Subject, a read-only From), `slackPost` (a read-only Channel and As), `issue` (title and description) and `comment`; a sent commit may name what it created (`resultLabel`, shown as "Created ENG-124"). Every editable field is edited in place while the card can still send (no Edit toggle), the message scrolls inside the card with the destination and actions fixed, and Copy copies the message. Sending and Sent keep the message on the card for the session
  - A failed send keeps the tool waiting: the card is editable again with Retry, which prepares the action again on the current login (`requestApproval`'s `reprepare`, since main has finished the failed one) and waits for a fresh Send under the same send claim; Cancel, the turn ending or the TTL report the failure with the user's edits, also after Retry asked to connect, until the card is prepared again; a card that never failed reports the edits with its cancel or `not_sent` too. `unknown` never offers Retry. Send while the connector is disconnected or needs a reconnect, or a commit that finds its login gone (`not_found`, `connection_changed`, `reconnect_needed`), asks to connect on the card (`reconnect`), never settling it: after the real connect (skipped when the service is already connected, say by another card's Yes) the action is prepared again and always waits for a fresh Send, even when the account, workspace and destination labels are unchanged; if those labels changed, the card says who it now sends as. A login replaced while the connector stays ready is prepared again on its own, at most once per Send, then waits for a fresh Send. Retry with the service disconnected asks to connect first, a re-prepare that finds it not connected asks the same way, and one that needs the user to say where (an archived channel) settles with that question for the model, with what the user wrote on the card (the Slack picker sends that text, and whatever comes of it carries the text back to the model); a turn stopped during Send prepares nothing new. Only the latest connect started from the card counts, and a refused one is worded with the connector's own Settings copy. Main's commit guards are unchanged
  - `pendingActions.js`: pending → committing → sent/failed/unknown; actions are bound to the connection (account, workspace, `generation`) they were prepared on and to the OpenWhispr account that prepared them (another account's commit is withdrawn as `account_changed`)
  - `connector_actions` (SQLite) is the durable receipt: destination labels, states and links only, never content. Each row is stamped with the account bound to the credential the action ran under (`accountScopeBinding`, read before and after the policy wait; with no account, `prepare` and `runDirect` refuse as `receipt_unavailable`; a `commit` under a changed or missing account is withdrawn as `not_sent`/`account_changed`), so the `connector-recent-actions` history IPC lists only that account's rows and account deletion removes them. Interrupted rows are reconciled on launch (committing → unknown; rows with no account are deleted), expired pending actions are recorded as `expired`, a direct run the connector stopped (`not_sent`) is `cancelled`, and a direct run that throws or returns a malformed result is `unknown` (it may have happened), never `failed`. Every connector result is whitelisted and type-checked per state before it is recorded or reaches the renderer. A cancel (Esc) reaches a direct run until its side effect through `runtime.signal`, checked right before the compose window opens
  - Policy fails closed (`connectorPolicy.js`'s `connectorPolicyState` classifies the snapshot; `connectorIpc.js`'s `createConnectorPolicyResolver` enforces the 1.5s deadline): only a successful, well-formed snapshot allows; unresolvable, any error code, or >1.5s with no verdict already held for the account (`workspacePolicyManager.peekPolicy`) → unavailable; org switch `features.connectorsEnabled`, and `agentEnabled: false` turns connectors off too
  - `emailConnector.js` opens Gmail/Outlook/mailto compose windows (`emailCompose.js`, shared ESM); a body that won't fit the compose URL (2,000 chars; 6,000 for Gmail on macOS/Linux, since Windows caps opened URLs near 2,081) goes to the clipboard after the OS accepts the link (on Linux a `mailto` is refused first when `xdg-mime` reports no default — empty output with exit 0, or exit 4 on KDE 5 — except under Flatpak, where the portal picks the handler), then the subject too if it's still too long; a recipient list that alone doesn't fit is refused. `email_draft` opens at most 3 drafts per turn, and only one of them may use the clipboard (`ToolExecutionContext.claimTurnSlot`; a draft that didn't open gives its slots back, and the compose preview is built before any slot is claimed). Subject and body go through `toWellFormed()`, the body's Markdown (bold, headings, links; never inside code, a URL, `x**2` or a "# of" or "# 1" count) is made plain first in the renderer (`plainTextBody`, so the card shows what is sent), the renderer NFC-normalises addresses (`bareEmailAddress`), and a recipient with a non-ASCII domain is shown with its punycode form (`recipientLabel`) to the model, in the tool step and in receipts. Automatic picks Send from chat (Gmail) when the Gmail connector is connected (including one that needs reconnecting; in a chat that can ask the user, `email_draft` then asks to connect it on its own card, `connectFirst`, before the email card, and elsewhere points to Settings), then the Gmail compose link when a Google calendar is connected, otherwise the Microsoft tenant rule as today: Outlook work vs Outlook.com from the Microsoft account's tenant id (`tid`, consumer tenant `9188040d-…`), falling back to the email domain for accounts connected before it was stored (`resolveEmailDraftTarget` in `src/utils/emailDraftTarget.ts`)
  - Gmail send (`gmailMime.js`, `gmailApi.js`, `gmailAuth.js`, `gmailConnector.js`) is an approval connector: with Gmail connected (Settings → Integrations → Connectors), `email_draft` shows an editable card and `commit` sends through the Gmail API. The only Gmail scope is `gmail.send` (plus `openid email`); a grant without it, or without a verified Google address (`email_verified`), is revoked, and a refresh is bound to the card's login (single-flight per OpenWhispr account and login). A login records the OAuth client that issued it (`clientId`). A refresh Google refuses is never reported as a network error: `invalid_grant`, another OAuth refusal (not `invalid_client`/`unauthorized_client`/`deleted_client`, build faults that are `gmail_unavailable`), or a login issued to a client this build no longer uses flags the login for reconnecting, and a Workspace admin block (`admin_policy_enforced`, `org_internal`) is `domain_policy`. Google's `/revoke` ends the user's whole grant to a Cloud project, so Disconnect (and a refused grant) skips it when a connected Google Calendar account with the same address is on the same project as the login's own client (`sharesCalendarGrant`, by the client id's project number) and only deletes the local tokens; the disconnect result then carries `grantKept` and the Settings row says Google still lists the app until the calendar is disconnected too. The shared-grant check reads the calendar accounts only once the projects match. A revoke is skipped the same way when another OpenWhispr account on the device has a Gmail login for the same Google address on the same project (`readAllAccounts`), since it holds the same grant. Connecting a different Google (or Slack) account over an existing login revokes the replaced one (`loginKey`); reconnecting the same account doesn't, since Google's revoke would end the new login's grant too. The Google client is `GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`/`GMAIL_CLIENT_SECRET` when both are set, else the calendar pair (`GOOGLE_CALENDAR_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`); with neither, Gmail reports `configured: false` and its row hides. The card blocks Send on an empty To, a bad address, more than 50 recipients, a subject over 250 characters or a body over 512 KB as sent (UTF-8, CRLF; `MAX_EMAIL_BODY_BYTES`, `emailBodyBytes`), a size that with the largest headers the other limits allow stays under the 1 MB raw cap (`src/utils/emailApprovalFields.ts`, limits shared from `emailCompose.js`; To/Cc accept `,` `;` and the Arabic and CJK commas, and a pasted `Name <address>` — including Outlook's `Last, First <address>` — becomes its bare address, with the resolved addresses shown under the field); main re-validates the same limits at Send (addresses as dot-atoms of at most 64 bytes without `=?`, ≤ 50 recipients, subject ≤ 250 characters without line breaks, body ≤ 512 KB, raw message ≤ 1 MB — all checked before any token refresh) and refuses CR/LF in every header. 5xx, a timeout after writing, and a 200 without a message id are `unknown` (link to the Sent folder; no retry); a 401 is refreshed and retried once under the same login; a 429 asking for ≤ 5 s is retried once; a 413 is `too_long`. A connector card whose preview shows a field the action doesn't declare editable is refused as malformed, so a typo in a declaration can't silently drop the user's edit. Release rule: a release build falls back to the calendar's Google client, so `gmail.send` must pass Google verification before a release ships it; develop against a separate Testing project with only `gmail.send` (`GMAIL_CLIENT_ID`/`_SECRET`), never the production calendar project
  - Approval actions must declare their editable fields (`actions.<action>.editable`: `addresses`, `line`, `text`; a missing declaration or an unknown type throws when the manager is created); a preview field must be declared and hold its declared type, or there's no card; `sanitizeEdits` keeps exactly those, and a declared field of the wrong type withdraws the card as `not_sent`/`invalid_edit` rather than sending the prepared value. Previews may carry `fields`, which the approval store edits and the tool result reports as `final` (`finalText` for a card without fields) on a sent, failed or unknown Send alike. `connectorErrorText()` words card and tool-step errors per connector (`connectors.<scope>.errors.<connectorId>.<code>` before the shared key)
  - Reset app data calls `connectorManager.revokeAllStored()` before deleting `userData/connectors`: it revokes every login on the device, for every account, even with no one signed in (Settings signs out before `cleanup-app`), and Gmail even on a grant shared with the calendar. Deleting an account uses `disconnectAll()` for the signed-in account's logins; a Gmail grant shared with a calendar is kept, unless the user also erases the device (`delete-account-data`'s `{ erasingDevice }`), since `cleanup-app` then finds no Gmail login left to revoke. Signing out keeps logins (every action is refused while signed out)
  - `contactSearch.js` (`find_contact`) searches the meetings nearest to now on selected calendars (`SELECTED_CALENDAR_EVENT_FILTER`; for Google only primary calendars, since a colleague's shared calendar isn't the user's meetings, which also leaves out the user's own secondary Google calendars; shared Microsoft or Apple-mirrored calendars still count; `calendar_events` only spans the sync window; cancelled and declined meetings don't count) plus the `contacts` table, most recently synced first, behind the same org policy check as the actions. Excluded: the user's calendar accounts and their Microsoft addresses (`/me` `mail`, `userPrincipalName` and `proxyAddresses`, stored in `microsoft_calendar_tokens.own_addresses` by `fetchCalendars`, which also purges them from `contacts`), `self` attendees, an Apple organizer who is the current user, any address flagged `resource` in any stored event (rows with unreadable attendee JSON are skipped) (rooms, flagged by all three calendar managers) and `*.calendar.google.com` addresses; the calendar managers also keep the user and rooms out of `contacts` and purge those addresses when they see them again. Sources are many-to-many (`contact_sources`: `google:<account>`, `microsoft:<account>`, `apple`, `manual`): disconnecting an account removes its source rows and deletes only contacts no other source still has; the lookup reads contacts with a `manual` or connected-account source (rows from before sources existed are left out; note-participant autocomplete still reads every row). Picking an existing contact in a note never adds `manual`, so it can't make a synced contact permanent
  - Slack (`slackConnector.js`): PKCE user token through `oauthLoopbackFlow.js`. Slack only distributes apps with HTTPS redirect URLs, so it redirects to the openwhispr.com relay (`openwhispr-website`, `app/auth/slack/callback/route.ts`), which forwards to the loopback server by the port in `state` (`v1.<port>.<nonce>`). The loopback server shows a local result page, and the flow waits 5 minutes. `SLACK_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` points a dev build at a website preview. Rotating tokens refresh single-flight, only for the login a pending action is bound to, and are saved before use; transient refresh errors never disconnect (`slackAuth.js`). Channels the user is in and people are cached 10 min; a list cut off at the page cap resolves only an exact @username, an exact #channel, an email, or an id or link, since those are unique in a workspace (a channel id or link still has to be among the channels read) (`slackDirectory.js`, `targetResolution.js`). A name that matches several returns them as `choices` (a #channel, or an @username, matched exactly before any name so it finds exactly that person, labelled with the username since display names repeat; main keeps at most five), which `slack_send_message` offers on a question card once; an answer typed instead is tried as the destination. Whatever comes of the picker (still unclear, sent, failed, or dismissed), and a channel gone by Send, goes back to the model with the text the user left on the card (`finalText`). In the chat, an open picker shows in place of the settled answer card it shares a key with. A destination can also be an exact id or a Slack link to the conversation: `C…`/`G…` among the user's channels, `U…`/`W…` through `users.info`, and `D…` only once a Send opened that DM with a person in this run of the app, labelled with that person (naming who a DM is with takes `im:read`, which the app doesn't ask for, so any other DM id is asked about). Ids are nine or more characters with a digit, and a bare one can be a name too (`DESIGN2025`): a `C`/`G`/`U`/`W` id is tried as a name when nothing has that id, and a `D…` is a DM only when no name matches. A link may be on any `*.slack.com` host (Enterprise Grid and Slack Connect links aren't on the workspace's domain), since the conversation is pinned by its id; a `cid` that names another conversation makes it no link. An optional `thread` (a link to a message in the thread; a reply's `?thread_ts=` wins; without one, a destination that links to a message is the thread) posts with `thread_ts`, and the card's note says which thread by when it started ("Reply in thread started …", from `thread_ts` through i18next's `datetime` formatter, so the copy in every locale keeps that placeholder). The thread must be in the destination's conversation (checked at prepare, or for a person after `conversations.open` at Send), or nothing is sent (`thread_mismatch`); a reply's result link carries the `thread_ts` Slack reports back (`?thread_ts=`, so a later reply stays in the thread; a message Slack didn't thread gets a plain link), and main reports `outsideThread` when a reply meant for a thread went out as a new message: the card and the tool step say so (`connectors.approval.slack.sentOutsideThread`), and the model hears `repliedInThread: false`. `conversations.open` runs only at Send. Disconnect revokes the refresh and access tokens. Failures follow `SLACK_PRE_SEND_REJECTIONS` (`slackApi.js`); everything else is `unknown`
  - Linear (`linearApi.js`, `linearAuth.js`, `linearTeams.js`, `linearConnector.js`, tools in `linearTools.ts`): PKCE login with no client secret (`LINEAR_CLIENT_ID`), scopes `read`, `issues:create`, `comments:create`. Linear matches a registered redirect exactly, port included, so it redirects to the openwhispr.com relay (`openwhispr-website`, `app/auth/linear/callback/route.ts`), which forwards to the loopback server by the port in `state`; `LINEAR_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` points a dev build at a website preview (https only). `linear_search_issues` is a query action (≤ 10 compact results, untrusted); `linear_create_issue` and `linear_comment` go through the issue/comment cards. Teams and projects are cached 10 minutes per login (`linearTeams.js`); a name that matches nothing in a list older than 30 s reads it once more, and a project the team doesn't have is a question, never dropped. Lengths count characters, as the card does. Links in results and receipts keep no title slug (`linear.app/<urlKey>/issue/<KEY>`). A create and a comment each carry a client UUID `id`, made at prepare, so an uncertain one is looked up once: found is `sent`; a comment the lookup misses stays `unknown`. Connecting another Linear user or workspace over a login revokes the replaced one (`loginKey`). A GraphQL error inside a 200 is `failed` only for a listed rejection code (`LINEAR_PRE_SEND_REJECTIONS`) with no data beside it; Linear reports a missing entity as `INPUT_ERROR` "Entity not found", and a repeated create id ("conflict on insert") is not a refusal
  - GitHub (`githubApi.js`, `githubDeviceFlow.js`, `githubAuth.js`, `githubInstallations.js`, `githubConnector.js`, tools in `githubTools.ts`): a GitHub App with the device flow and no client secret (`GITHUB_APP_CLIENT_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_SLUG`; release builds write them to `.env` from the `GH_APP_CLIENT_ID`/`GH_APP_SLUG` secrets, since secret names can't start with `GITHUB_`; the row hides unless both are set); permissions Issues RW, Pull requests RW, Metadata R, so nothing can read code. The 8-hour token refreshes without a secret and the rotated refresh token is saved before use. Everything is limited to the repositories the App is installed on (`githubInstallations.js`, 60 s cache per login, one read at a time shared by everyone asking, and a read in flight when the list is cleared is never cached; a repo missing from a list older than 5 s is looked for in a fresh one, so installing the App from a "not installed" answer works at once). A bare repo name matches exactly first, then loosely through `resolveTarget` ("open whispr" for `open-whispr`), but only in a complete list; a miss in a list cut at 1,000 repositories per installation is `repo_unlisted` (the App may be installed after all), not `not_installed`; the row's **Choose/Manage repositories** opens the install page (`manageUrl`). The status never waits on GitHub: it reports the last repository count read and reads again in the background (reusing a list younger than 5 s), announcing a changed count, the first read for a login even when it failed, or a login the read found needing a reconnect, through `notifyStatusChanged`; until that first read finishes (after a connect, and at launch) the status carries `workspaceLabelPending: true`, a field no other connector sends (the manager asks every connector at once, so a GitHub read would hold up the others). Search result titles and snippets are redacted of email addresses over a bounded window only, since the redaction pattern is quadratic on long runs of word characters. `github_search_issues` is a query action; a GitHub App user token must search issues and pull requests apart (`is:issue`/`is:pull-request`, or a 422), so `type: "any"` runs both and merges them newest first. The user's own `repo:`/`org:`/`user:` qualifiers are removed (in groups too, with any operator they leave behind), and so are their own `is:`/`type:` issue or pull-request qualifiers, since the tool's `type` wins; a quoted phrase is left as written, and a quote left open or a parenthesis without its pair is dropped, since either would take in the qualifiers added after the words; a state the query names itself (`is:closed`, …) replaces the default `state:open`, and an invalid `state` or `type` is refused rather than defaulted. GitHub's 256-character limit counts only the words, so the `repo:` list is bounded by the size of `q` as sent in the query string (`MAX_SEARCH_QUERY_LENGTH`). GitHub answers 422 for a `repo:` the user can no longer search (unticked, deleted, access lost), so a 422 re-reads the installed repos fresh and searches once more. Suspended installations are skipped. `github_create_issue` and `github_comment` go through the issue/comment cards, with a live "This will notify @…" note while the card is pending (`githubMentions.ts`, `liveCardNotes.ts`), and a sent result tells the model who was notified (`notified`, from the card's final fields when edited, else the call's own). `github_comment` takes `owner/repo#N`, a github.com link, or `repo#N`/`#N`, whose repo is found among the installed ones (a question when ambiguous). A new issue needs a repo with issues on (archived and issues-off repos aren't offered as candidates), and more than 10 labels is refused rather than trimmed. A 401 on a token another request already refreshed reuses that token (`refreshRejected`), since every GitHub refresh ends the previous one. Connect shows the device code in the row (sent over the `connector-connect-progress` broadcast) with its own Cancel (`connectInRow`); `connector-cancel-connect` stops it, even while it still waits on the policy check (`pendingConnects` in `connectorIpc.js`), as do leaving Settings, the org turning connectors off, an account switch or a sign-out in main (`connectorManager.accountChanged`). A device code that can't be asked for is `network` (no answer, a timeout, a 408 or 5xx) or `rate_limited` (a 429), not `token_exchange_failed`, since the user never saw a code. The device poll keeps going through network failures until the code expires and treats a bare 429 like `slow_down`; the `/user` read after approval is retried once. A refresh answered with 408 or `temporarily_unavailable`/`server_error` keeps the login and is tried once more; a 429 keeps it without an immediate retry (`rate_limited`), and a refusal of the OAuth client itself (`incorrect_client_credentials`, `device_flow_disabled`, `unauthorized_client`, `invalid_client`: a build with a wrong client id) is `not_configured` and never flags the login. An archived repo takes no new issue or comment, and a locked conversation only takes a comment from someone with push access. Writes have no idempotency key, so anything uncertain after the send is final `unknown`. Only Reset app data and account deletion (the manager's `removingAll`) end the tokens, through GitHub's unauthenticated `POST /credentials/revoke` (an authenticated call is refused). That endpoint is meant for exposed credentials and emails the user, so Disconnect, a reconnect and a connect that can't be saved only delete the login locally; the App's authorization stays on github.com until the user removes it there
  - Token handling shared by the OAuth connectors (`boundLogin.js`): reads, the reconnect flag, saving a refreshed token and one refresh at a time, only ever for the login an action is bound to. Gmail's and Linear's clients share one timed POST and its body helpers (`providerHttp.js`); GitHub's uses the body helpers
  - Logins: one encrypted file per OpenWhispr account and connector under `userData/connectors/` (atomic writes, `0o700`, `connectorCredentials.js`). Every write names the account and the generation it started from, so an OAuth round trip, refresh or disconnect that outlives a reconnect or account switch writes nothing. Bindings carry `ownerAccountId`; receipts carry `account_id`. Signed out → every connector action is refused (`signed_out`). Pending actions also expire in main (`sweepExpired`, every minute)
- **skills/**: The assistant Skills library (Integrations → Skills), in the open Agent Skills format: a folder holding `SKILL.md` (a YAML header with `name` and `description`, parsed by `js-yaml` with `CORE_SCHEMA`) plus any `references/`, `scripts/` and `assets/`
  - `skillFormat.js` holds the pure rules: limits (25 MB and 500 files per skill), header checks (a problem refuses a skill, a warning only flags it; SKILL.md must be UTF-8 text), slugs from titles, building `SKILL.md`, and the content hash
  - `skillArchive.js` reads `.zip` and `.skill` uploads with `unzipper`. The end record's claimed entry count is checked before the archive is opened, and a zip64 archive isn't opened at all; every path is checked as the folder will be written (no `..`, absolute or drive paths, backslashes, links, or Windows device names from `windowsReservedName.js`, which the notes mirror shares); the bytes read are capped and checked against each entry's CRC-32; and entries the listing gives no size, or whose compressed sizes add up past the file, are refused
  - `skillLibrary.js` (`SkillLibrary`) owns `userData/skills/<name>/`, the only copy of a skill's content. The SQLite `skills` table keeps each skill's on/off state, content hash and when the assistant last used it (`markUsed`, stamped by `load_skill`), keyed by folder name; a scan lists a folder it finds with no row as off and drops a row whose folder is gone. A write goes to `.staging-<uuid>` and is swapped in by rename; a replace first moves the old copy to `.replaced-<uuid>-<name>` and renames it to `.trash-<uuid>` once the new copy is in; when the library opens it puts a `.replaced-` copy back if its new copy never arrived and deletes the other leftovers. A rename Windows refuses for a moment (antivirus, the indexer) is tried again for about half a second. Created, uploaded and added skills start on; a replaced skill keeps its switch
  - Uploads cross IPC as bytes: `skills-preview-upload` checks them and keeps each one that can install under a one-time token until `skills-commit-upload` installs it, `skills-discard-uploads` lets it go, or the window that previewed it closes, crashes or reloads
  - `skillsIpc.js` registers the `skills-*` channels (list, get, read-file, set-enabled, uninstall, reveal, preview-upload, discard-uploads, commit-upload, create, add-recommended, and for the assistant read-for-model, check-proposal, save-proposal, apply-update, undo, mark-used), checks every argument before the library runs, and broadcasts `skills-changed` after each write. A read, check or use stamp that fails answers as one that found nothing rather than throwing. `recommended/<id>/SKILL.md` are the starter skills; their title and description are written in the UI language when added. `builtin/skill-creator/SKILL.md` is the creator the assistant follows to draft a skill (Create with assistant), read for the model but never listed. Reset app data deletes `userData/skills` and `userData/skill-versions`
  - The assistant (`src/utils/skillContext.ts`, `src/services/tools/skillTools.ts`): each turn lists the switched-on, valid skills in the system prompt, most recently used first, fenced as data (`<skill_catalog>`, each description cut to 300 characters) and cut to `SKILL_CATALOG_MAX_TOKENS` (2,000 tokens hosted, 330 local). `load_skill` reads one through `skills-read-for-model`, fenced as data (`<skill_content>`; a fence tag inside skill text, in any case, spacing or full-width form, with unseen characters (zero-width, soft hyphen, joiners, combining marks) inside it or look-alike brackets and dashes, is escaped, so it can't end the block or open another) and cut to `SKILL_CONTEXT_MAX_TOKENS`; a skill attached to the turn (Try now, the chat chip) is read the same way, and one that can't be used (switched off, broken or unreadable) is left out with a note telling the model to say so (`skillNotUsedNote`), since its chip still shows. A first attached skill cut to fit counts as loaded, since `load_skill` has no more room to give it. Skills the user took off a chat leave its catalog and the `load_skill`/`update_skill` choices too (the creator still hears of them); `propose_skill` is offered only when the user has a skill or the creator is attached. A small local model without tools gets one skill picked from the request (`localSkillPick.ts`: it needs two words in common with the skill, since one word names a topic and a pick stays with the chat; Latin words under three letters, common function words in the app's languages that put spaces between words, and one- or two-kana runs never count). A chat's attached skill and the skills the user removed come back with the conversation (`chat/chatSkillMemory.ts`, localStorage on this computer)
  - Saving from chat: `propose_skill` and `update_skill` only show approval cards (`chat/SkillApprovalCard.tsx`, `stores/skillApprovalStore.ts`), and nothing is written before Save. `skills-check-proposal` checks a draft first, so the model can fix it; `skills-save-proposal` writes a new skill or updates the one already showing that title, keeping its other files and header lines as `update_skill` does; `skills-apply-update` changes a switched-on, valid skill's description, instructions or files and keeps every other header line byte for byte. A card that changes a skill carries the skill's hash from its check (`baseHash`), and a save after the skill changed since is refused (`error: "changed"`, the card's `outdated` state), as is one checked as new after a skill took its title, or made for a skill that's gone since. Old versions are pruned only after a new one is complete, and a version copy cut short is removed. A save that replaces a skill keeps the previous copy under `userData/skill-versions/<name>/<id>/` (the five newest, each with a `.json` note), so the card's Undo (`skills-undo`) can put it back while nothing has changed it since (a kept copy that can't be read fails retryably rather than reading as a later change); a note that can't be written after the swap leaves that save without Undo rather than failing it. Uninstall removes a skill's versions, and when the library opens it removes those of a skill that's gone. A card's tool returns its outcome as a record (`SkillCardRecord`: status, kind, folder, title, `versionId`), saved with the chat as the call's metadata, so a reopened chat rebuilds the card from it (`SkillCardSummary`, Undo included; Undo state is kept per version in the store, `undos`) and later turns' `[Tools used: …]` trace says whether it was saved. The saved call keeps no arguments (`withoutSkillCardDrafts` in `useChatPersistence` and `chatPersistence.ts`), since they hold the whole proposed skill and the chat syncs
- **postMigrationDetector.js**: Detects users returning from the pre-Gizmo bundle ID via a `.bundle-migrated` sentinel in userData; consumed by `ipcHandlers.js` to drive the `PostMigrationOnboarding` modal
- **permissionGuideManager.js**: macOS onboarding permission overlay — a frameless always-on-top window anchored to the bottom of the System Settings window (placement in `permissionGuidePlacement.js`, pure), polled one read at a time via `settingsWindowState.js` (exec of `macos-window-bounds`). Steps aside for authorization prompts, other apps and a dialog on another Space; closes when the dialog closes; never subscribes to the control panel's `hide` (occlusion fires it), so `hideControlPanelToTray()` closes it explicitly. Renderer side: `permissionGuideController.ts` (pure state machine, unit-tested) + `usePermissionGuide.ts`
- **computerProfile.js**: Reads the computer for onboarding's local setup in the main process, cached (`get-computer-profile`): model name (never the hostname or serial), chip or processor, installed memory, graphics, Parakeet support and every preferred system language as tags. Each read fails soft with a default, the GPU read is capped at 3 s, and nothing is logged or sent. Renderer side: `src/hooks/useComputerProfile.ts`
- **localSetupSpace.js**: Free disk space for local setup (`get-local-setup-space`) plus the unpack factors the downloads reserve (shared with `whisper.js`, `parakeet.js` and `gpuBinaryManager.js`). A folder that doesn't exist yet reads from its nearest parent; a failed read counts as room to spare, as the downloads' own check does. Renderer side: `useLocalSetupSpace.ts`
- **templatePrompts.js**: Pure compiler from a note template or action to the system prompt the model receives (see Note Templates and Actions). Shared by `actionProcessingStore.ts`, the note chat, and the live canary's note probe

### React Components (src/components/)

- **App.jsx**: Main dictation interface with recording states
- **ControlPanel.tsx**: Settings, history, model management UI
- **OnboardingFlow.tsx**: First-time setup wizard over a versioned, route-based step machine (`src/components/onboarding/flow.ts`; session persisted in `onboardingSessionV2`). Routes vary by auth path (account vs guest), policy (agent allowed), and setup mode (cloud / BYOK / local / enterprise); step components live in `src/components/onboarding/`
- **agent/**: The agent window's renderer (`AgentWindow.tsx`, see §17): the pill, the typing layout and the modal over one running chat (`useAgentChat`), voice takes (`useAgentVoice`), the shell's state reducer (`agentWindowState.ts`), progress rows, response actions and the selection toolbar
- **onboarding/LocalSetupStep.tsx**: The "We recommend these settings" screen, the one step of the Local route. `src/config/localSetup.ts` (pure) picks from `src/models/localSetupTable.json` (data only, keyed by machine type; Macs by chip): dictation by language and allow-list, cleanup and the assistant by machine, hiding rows the policy doesn't approve. A row too slow for the computer reads as "Not enough memory" with no figures and shows no switch; one short of memory shows the figures. `useLocalSetupDownloads.ts` and `localSetupDownloadState.ts` run the downloads one at a time, and on an NVIDIA PC the Vulkan pack (`graphicsPack`) rides with the first text row but still downloads if that row fails. Nothing is written before Proceed, which opens once dictation is on disk: `localSetupSettings.ts` writes every switched-off, unavailable or hidden scope as mode `local` with no model, never `openwhispr`, and rows still downloading stay off until their model lands. `BackgroundModelDownloadTray.tsx` carries on after Proceed and across a quit: it resumes a pending row (`pendingLocalModels.ts`) unless Settings has since answered its scopes or policy denies it, stops retrying by itself after 3 failures (the row keeps a Retry), and fetches a pack still on its way. Under `AppRouter`, a policy refetch for the same account keeps a download-owning step mounted (`keepsOnboardingStepThroughPolicyRefetch`)
- **PostMigrationOnboarding.tsx**: One-time modal for users returning from the pre-Gizmo bundle ID; reuses `PermissionsSection` to walk through re-granting Microphone, Accessibility, and System Audio. Triggered by `postMigrationDetector.js` (see Helper Modules)
- **SettingsPage.tsx**: Comprehensive settings interface
- **Settings layer** (`settings/SettingsLayer.tsx`, rendered by `SettingsModal.tsx`): Settings is a full-window layer over the Control Panel, not a dialog or a view. `ControlPanel` renders it after `<main>`, as a sibling of the sidebar container and `<main>`, which get `inert` + `aria-hidden` while it's open, so the view underneath (a chat stream, a download, an open note) keeps running. Shell: an icon rail of the app's views (`useControlPanelNavItems`; picking one leaves Settings), its own top bar (breadcrumb "Settings › <parent or group> › <page>", Ctrl+K button, drag region and `WindowControls`), the Settings nav ("Back to <view>" first, `navTop` slot for search) and one content scroller (920 px reading width; a page marked `wide` in `settingsPages.ts` — Overview, Styles, Hotkeys, Processes & resources — gets up to 1240 px, search results keep 920; `SettingsLayoutProvider` is compact only below 640 px of content)
  - **Pages** (`settings/settingsPages.ts`, React-free; icons by name in `settingsPageIcons.ts`): a typed `SettingsPageId` per page and the nav tree `SETTINGS_NAV`: groups → pages, or a parent whose pages list under it while it's open (Account: Profile, Plans & Billing, Workspace › General, People, Teamspaces, Developer · Features: Dictation › Overview, Recognition, Cleanup, Tone & Style, Styles, Output; Voice Assistant; Translation; Chat; Notes & Uploads › Live Recording, File Transcription, Note Formatting · Setup: AI Connections & Local Models; App & Controls › Appearance & Language, Microphone & Sounds, Notifications, Floating Icon, Startup, Note Files & Import, Hotkeys · Data: Privacy & Data, Storage, Processes & resources, System · Elsewhere: Dictionary & Glossary, Integrations, which leave Settings). A parent opens its first page (Dictation → Overview); nav buttons carry `data-settings-nav="<id>"`, the page `aria-current="page"`; below 900 px of layer the nav shows icons, a parent's pages as indented icons. A parent stays open until you close it (`SettingsLayer.tsx`), so leaving it never folds rows away under the pointer: opening a page elsewhere moves only the highlight; going into a parent (a click, search, `openSettings` with a destination, a link from another page, any navigation or shown page inside it) opens it; a chevron beside its name, its own button (`data-settings-nav-toggle`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` on its pages' list, which is `hidden` while closed; named "Show/Hide <parent> pages", `settingsLayer.showPages` / `hidePages`; in the icon nav a small corner button), opens or closes it by hand without navigating. Closed around the page you're on, the parent row takes `aria-current="page"` and the highlight. The open parents are remembered in localStorage (`settings.navOpenParents`, a JSON array of parent ids; `readOpenNavParents` drops unknown ids and falls back to Dictation open, the others closed, when nothing usable is saved). Each page has i18n title/description keys and a `visible(ctx)` predicate: the Account group needs Cloud; the Workspace pages also a sign-in, People, Teamspaces and Developer a chosen workspace rather than Personal (`hasWorkspace`), and Developer the workspace's `manage_settings` (`canManageWorkspace`); both are unknown until the workspace list loads, so a destination naming one of those pages waits for it; Voice Assistant and Chat need `isAgentAllowed`; a hidden page shows its `fallback` (People, Teamspaces and Developer → General, General → Profile, the agent pages → Cleanup) or the default page (Profile with Cloud on, Recognition off), also when the switch flips while it's open, without forgetting what was asked for (`shownSettingsPage`). Who is signed in shows on the Profile page; the nav holds the workspace picker under "Back to <view>" (`WorkspaceSwitcher`, compact in the icon nav; signed in with Cloud on), which General's "Choose workspace" opens too
  - **Bodies** (`SettingsPage.tsx`, the one owner): `page` prop; each page's body mounts on its first visit and then stays mounted, hidden (`data-settings-page-id`), so editor drafts, downloads and listeners survive page switches. Pages are built from the old section bodies, not rewritten: the old Preferences section (`general`) wraps each block in `show("<blockId>")` and a page lists its blocks in `settingsPages.ts` (`blocks`; each block on exactly one page, `test/components/settingsPages.test.js` checks), the speech-to-text and language-model tab bodies render one per page with no tab strip, and section-level dialogs stay outside the pages. Every page has a header (`settings/SettingsPageHeader.tsx`); AI feature pages (Cleanup, Voice Assistant, Translation, Chat, Note Formatting) also show their on/off switch there (moved out of the body: `enableToggleInHeader` / `hideEnableToggle`, one control each) and pills: provider · model from `describeLlm` (search context), the prompt in use, and for Cleanup what else runs on its model (Styles, Note Formatting when it inherits; `featurePageStatus.ts`)
  - **Kept mounted**: mounted on first open, then hidden (`display: none` + `inert`) when closed, so prompt drafts, scroll position and downloads survive. `useSettingsVisible()` (`settings/useSettingsVisible.ts`, true outside Settings) pauses presentation polling while hidden and re-runs OS queries on reopen: GPU status (`ReasoningModelSelector`, `settings/WhisperGpuCard.tsx`), accessibility and paste tools (`usePermissions`'s `active` option), microphone devices, auto-start, ydotool and the recordings' size (Storage › Recordings, Privacy & Data's summary); This build's relative times stop ticking; Storage › Logs and System › Diagnostics read the log (`log-info`, `log-recent-problems`) only when their page shows while Settings is visible, after each action and on the `log-settings-changed` broadcast; Processes & resources subscribes to the live samples only while its page shows while Settings is visible. Downloads and test runs are never paused. `SettingsPage` wraps each page body in `PanelVisibleProvider` (`ui/usePanelVisible.ts`; true only for the page showing while Settings is visible), the fork's stand-in for upstream's `TabPanel`, so upstream's automatic credential checks (`useConnectionAutoCheck`, #2652, in `ProviderCheckField`, `OpenAICompatiblePanel` and the custom speech endpoint) run only on the page in view
  - **Requests** (`settings/useSettingsLayerController.ts`, `settings/settingsNavigation.ts`): every caller uses `openSettings(destination?)`. A named destination is a request with a new id and always navigates; a bare open (Ctrl+, the sidebar, the app menu) keeps the page Settings was on, or the default page on first open. Never key the layer: a remount loses drafts. `resolveSettingsDestination` accepts page ids, parent ids (`dictation`), `#settingId` and `<page>#<settingId>` (the registry's page wins; the name is the fallback), and every pre-page string: the nine old section ids (`general` → Appearance & Language), `SECTION_ALIASES` / `LEGACY_SUB_TAB` (`meetings` → Note Formatting, `transcription` → Recognition, `intelligence` → Cleanup, `dictationAgent` → Voice Assistant, `uploadTranscription` → File Transcription, …) and the provider-error targets `speechToText` / `llms` / `noteRecording` (a meeting's provider error, → Live Recording). An `open-settings-section` request naming `integrations.<section>` (`INTEGRATIONS_TARGET_PREFIX`) opens that Integrations section instead of Settings (`drainPendingSettingsSection`). The old saved tabs (`settings.speechToTextTab`, `settings.llmsTab`) are read once per window, mapped to pages and removed; a bare `llms` / `speechToText` uses its mapped page once (the navigation state carries what's left), then lands on Recognition / Cleanup. Only People has sub-tabs: members, invitations, guests, teams (`subTab`, re-applied on every navigation id; `PEOPLE_SUB_TABS`)
  - **Leaving** (`settings/settingsLayerPolicy.ts`): Radix portals popups and dialogs into the body, outside the layer, so every way out (Back, Esc, the rail, Ctrl+, which toggles, Open in History, a palette result) calls `requestSettingsClose` first: an open popup is closed instead (a synthetic Escape at the focused element for a Radix popup, at the expanded trigger for a hand-rolled dropdown such as `LanguageSelector`, which can be open while focus is elsewhere), a modal dialog or hotkey capture refuses. A palette result passes the palette (`data-command-palette`) as `ignore`, since it's closing in the same step
  - **Esc**: read in the window capture phase (before Radix's document listener closes the top popup), acted on in the bubble phase unless something handled it: capture or IME → nothing, an Esc a popup or dialog already handled, or one in a field marked `data-escape-clears` while it has something to clear (`ui/escapeClears.ts`; People's member search, which clears itself) → nothing, an open popup that didn't get it → close that popup, focused text field → blur, search query → clear (`searchActive`), else leave Settings
  - **Focus**: the layer root takes focus on open; on close, after `inert` is released, focus returns to what had it before (captured in `openSettings`), else the sidebar's current item, else it just leaves the layer
  - **One scroller**: the content pane is the only vertical scroller in Settings, so the wheel always moves the page. Editors use `ui/AutoGrowTextarea.tsx` (as tall as its text, never scrolls inside; re-measured on value, width and font changes and when shown again after updating while hidden, never stored as 0; the page follows the caret); long read-only text uses `ui/ClampedText.tsx` (clamped with a fade, "Show all" only when it overflows); long model lists (`ui/SearchableModelList.tsx`) show a page of models at a time with "Show more". No capped `max-h-* overflow-y-auto` boxes or fixed-height textareas in Settings bodies; dialogs and popups keep their own scroll (`test/components/settingsOneScroller.test.js` holds the allow-list)
  - **Search** (`settings/search/`): a field on top of the Settings nav (Ctrl+F, or `/` outside a text field; `isSettingsSearchShortcut`) whose results replace the page while the query isn't empty (the page stays mounted, hidden), grouped by top-level nav item (Dictation, Voice Assistant, …) in nav order with breadcrumbs from the nav tree ("Dictation › Cleanup"), best match highlighted; ↑/↓ move, Enter opens, Esc clears. `registry.ts` holds one descriptor per setting row or block (~150): a stable scope-qualified id (`dictation.cleanup.model`, `hotkeys.dictation`), its `location` (`{ page, subTab? }`, data: moving a setting moves only this), the `anchor` it lands on, title/description/block i18n keys (the row's own), English `aliases` and localized `aliasKeys`, plus a page descriptor per page (titled as the nav shows it) and per parent (`page.*`), and the Dictionary and Integrations pages (`elsewhere.*`, which leave Settings)
    - **Anchors**: every setting row or block carries `data-setting-id` (passed through `SettingsPanelRow`, `SettingsRow`, `SettingsPanel`, `PermissionCard`; `InferenceConfigEditor` adds `<id>.thinking` / `<id>.keepLoaded` / `<id>.reasoningEffort` under its own; the last is upstream's per-scope reasoning effort (#2694), shown when the picked model offers effort levels). Every new setting row gets a `data-setting-id` and a descriptor: `test/components/settingsSearchRegistry.test.js` fails on an anchor without a descriptor, a descriptor without a rendered anchor, an unknown page, a page without its descriptor, a destination that no longer resolves, or a key missing from any of the 11 locales
    - **Availability**: `available(ctx)` returns true, a reason key with the prerequisite's descriptor id (Cloud off → account, billing, workspace, insights; a workspace's own pages and rows without a chosen workspace → `workspaceOnly`, pointing to General (`workspace.general`); Developer's and invitations' rows without `manage_settings` / `manage_members` → `managerOnly`, from the search context's `hasWorkspace`, `canManageWorkspace` and `canManageMembers` (`useSettingsSearchIndex`, the chosen workspace's); agent policy off; macOS / Linux Wayland only; a parent toggle off, e.g. cleanup off for the cleanup model; local-model-only rows), or null (not listed: the sign-in card when signed in). The result shows the reason and opens the prerequisite instead; search never changes a setting. A row that renders only in some states the context can't see (activation mode under a native shortcut, system audio) has a `fallbackAnchor`: a stable parent it lands on when the row isn't there. A row that only sums up settings kept on another page and links there (Privacy & Data's Recordings and Logs, System's Diagnostics) is marked `summary` and ranks below any setting whose title has the word, so "recordings" or "logs" opens the setting, not the summary
    - **Values** are an allowlist (`SettingsSearchValueKey`, built in `searchContext.ts` from policy-effective settings): provider · model per LLM scope via `selectResolvedLLMConfig` (mode, provider and model only), the speech-to-text engine per context, every hotkey (style keys included), activation mode, theme, UI and transcription language, assistant name, translation targets. Never API keys, tokens or endpoint URLs (`settingsSearchContext.test.js` checks)
    - **Landing**: `openSettings("#<settingId>")` (or `"<page>#<settingId>"`; the registry's page wins, the name is the fallback for unknown ids) and in-Settings results go through `SettingsModal`'s navigation with a `settingId`; `revealSetting` then waits up to 2 s for the first visible anchor on the setting's page (pages mount lazily; an anchor on another kept-mounted page is skipped), opens any `reveal.expand` block while the anchor isn't visible (one already showing, e.g. a connection's card outside the fold, opens nothing), scrolls it to the center (top when taller than the view) and sets `data-search-flash` for 1.6 s (`index.css`; held still under reduced motion). When nothing in Settings has focus (a Ctrl+K result) the landed row takes it (`focusLandedSetting`: `tabindex="-1"` until it blurs; the layer root instead when the row is a control). Pages navigate without a flash
    - **Ctrl+K**: `CommandSearch` shows a Settings group with the top 5 matches from the same index (first while Settings is open, last otherwise); a result calls `openSettings("#id")`, or leaves Settings for an app page. Its `onCloseAutoFocus` keeps Radix from returning focus to what had it before (often inert under Settings): `focusSettingsAfterPalette` puts it on the flashed row, else the layer root
  - **AI Connections & Local Models** (`settings/AiConnectionsPage.tsx`, page `connections`): every shared credential and download in one place. The model pickers on feature pages (language models and speech-to-text) take no keys and link here; the GPU pickers and keep-loaded switch also stay on their feature pages, all editing the same store fields
    - **Connections**: grouped by state (`settings/connectionGroups.ts`, pure) over the 12 shared API-key providers (`settings/connectionProviders.ts`), GitHub Copilot, OpenWhispr Cloud and the custom connection. Once any connection has an active use: **In use** (at least one active usage, with or without a key, which then shows "Not connected": full cards, most active uses first, ties in today's order Copilot, providers, Cloud, custom), **Saved, not in use** (a saved credential or Azure Speech's endpoint, a Copilot sign-in, Cloud on and signed in, or the custom connection: one row each), and the rest folded under **Add a provider (N)** (rows; never the custom connection, which shows "Add custom connection" below the fold while there's none). While nothing is in use (a first run) it shows "Get started": Copilot, the `common` starters (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google Gemini, Groq), any provider with a saved credential and Cloud while it's on as cards, the rest (and Cloud while off) folded under "More providers (N)", then the custom connection's card or the add button. A visit is the page showing (`SettingsPage` passes `showing`) while Settings is visible; its first render takes the groups and closes the fold in render, not an effect (`stepVisit`), so a search reveal opening the fold right after isn't undone, and they hold for the visit (`freezeGroups`: a known connection keeps its group and place, a custom connection saved since takes its live group, a removed one drops out), so adding or removing a key moves nothing until the next visit. Until secrets have hydrated (`secretsHydrated`, set in `initializeSettings`) and Copilot's status has loaded (`useCopilotStore.loaded`; the section calls `syncCopilotAuthStatus` on mount) nothing is frozen and the groups follow the live state.
