Rows (`settings/ConnectionRow.tsx`): a single-key provider shows its `ApiKeyInput` (masked key and Replace, or "Paste your API key") with Remove or "Get your API key"; Corti, Azure Speech, Copilot, Cloud and the custom connection show a one-line status and Manage, Set up or Sign in, which opens the card's own body under the row (`ProviderCredentials`, `CopilotSignIn`, Cloud's text and link, the custom connection's `ConnectionPanel`); **Azure Speech** (MAI-Transcribe-2, speech-to-text only) holds its key (`AZURE_SPEECH_API_KEY` in `BYOK_API_KEYS`, encrypted like every key) and its resource endpoint, the plain setting `azureSpeechEndpoint` (localStorage; the provider definition's optional `endpoint` field): the field is written as typed, tidied on blur (`normalizeAzureSpeechEndpoint`: a Foundry endpoint, `https://<name>.services.ai.azure.com` or a project's `…/api/projects/<project>`, is shown and stored as `https://<name>.cognitiveservices.azure.com` once left; a value saved as typed before that still routes there, `azureSpeechTranscription.js`) and says what's wrong once left (`connections.azureSpeech.endpointErrors.*`; the host error names `.cognitiveservices.azure.com`, `.services.ai.azure.com` and `.api.cognitive.microsoft.com`), under it the help "Paste the endpoint and a key from your Speech resource's Keys and Endpoint page. A Foundry endpoint (.services.ai.azure.com, a project's too) works as well: it's saved as the same resource's .cognitiveservices.azure.com address.", and its status reads "Key saved" only with a key and a valid endpoint (`isConnectionReady`), "Endpoint needed" with a key alone; search finds it by "foundry", "azure ai foundry", "services.ai" and "services.ai.azure.com" too (`PROVIDER_ALIASES` in `settings/search/registry.ts`); Cloud while off shows "Off" with its Privacy & Data link instead; off usages show as dimmed chips under the line. Keys come from the store (hydrated by the `get-*-key` IPCs) and are written through the store's debounced secret setters; they show only masked (`utils/maskApiKey.ts`, which re-exports upstream's `utils/maskKey.ts`, the same function). `ApiKeyInput`'s `replace` mode starts from an empty field and treats an empty draft as cancel, so the saved key never enters an input; Remove asks first and names what uses the key. A provider policy forbids everywhere shows "Managed by your organization" and no editor. Anchors sit on whichever card or row renders the connection (`connections.<provider>`, camelCase for a hyphenated id: `connections.azureSpeech`, via `connectionAnchor`; `connections.copilot`, `connections.cloud`; `connections.custom` on the card, row, add button or policy note), and `connections.more` on the fold in both layouts; every provider, Copilot and Cloud descriptor lists `reveal.expand: ["connections.more"]`, which opens the fold only when it holds the target. `ManageKeysLink` under a feature's model picker (and beside a key field in `ReasoningModelSelector`, onboarding's) opens `connections#connections.<provider>` (hidden outside Settings, e.g. onboarding)
    - **Used by** (`settings/connectionUsage.ts`, pure): which feature runs on which connection, from the request resolvers: policy-effective settings, `selectResolvedLLMConfig` per scope (managed enterprise and note formatting's fallback included), `resolveDictationAgentInference` / `resolveChatStreamingInference` (the assistant answers through Chat while its own pick can't run), `resolveDictationAgentVisionInference` (the screen-context model, API-key providers only, once picked), the meeting and upload transcription selectors and managed STT. Each use is configured, inherited (note formatting, live recording and uploads borrowing values, Styles on Cleanup's model even with Cleanup off), fallback, or off (switched off, policy, Cloud off, signed out, Copilot on chat/assistant, managed setup unavailable); off uses are dimmed and never block deleting a model. The result holds ids, model ids and endpoint hosts only (`endpointHost`), never a key or a full URL; a custom endpoint also names the store field of its URL and its key slot (`customEndpoint`). Chips open the feature's model setting (`USAGE_EDIT_DESTINATIONS`)
    - **Connection checks** (stage A of "check that every AI connection works"; main's half `helpers/connectionChecks.js`): the page checks every saved connection it lists and flags those that don't work. Free requests only (a model list, an account or key-info route), never text or audio.
      - **Descriptors** (`settings/connectionHealth.ts`, pure, `buildCheckPlan`): one per thing to verify, owned by a card or row (`CheckTarget.owner`: a connection id, or `endpoint:<feature>`): each provider with a saved credential (in use or not; Corti two, `corti:llm` with its API key and `corti:stt` with its client credentials, region and tenant in the route; Azure Speech only with a valid endpoint, else "endpoint needed"), Copilot while signed in, OpenWhispr Cloud while on and signed in (`kind: "cloud"`: the renderer's own, never sent to main), the custom connection (its canonical endpoint and `CUSTOM_CONNECTION_API_KEY`; a feature on it whose slot holds another key isn't vouched for: matched by endpoint and the same key, never by host alone) and the Self-hosted & custom endpoint rows it doesn't cover (`endpointCheckRoute`: Azure as its resource's v1 base; rows on one server with one slot share a descriptor). Models are the active usages' (`FeatureUsage.model`, set for providers and Copilot; custom and self-hosted carry theirs; OpenRouter's key serves its speech-to-text too, so its speech features' models are compared, against its transcription list); off features' models aren't compared. The generation (`g<hash>`) is route, slot and sorted models, so endpoint, model, region and tenant edits change it; a key change doesn't (main's credential generation fences that). Policy-forbidden providers, Copilot, Cloud or custom endpoints aren't probed (`skipped: managed`: "Managed by your organization · not checked here", also on the Enterprise section's managed card). Descriptors carry names only (`connectionHealth.test.js` checks no key reaches one)
      - **Store** (`stores/connectionHealthStore.ts`): results by descriptor id with the generation they answer (a view shows only its current generation's), `pending` by id → awaited generation, `epoch` bumped by main's `invalidated`; `runConnectionChecks(targets, { fresh, maxAgeMs })` flushes the targets' pending debounced key saves first (`flushPendingSecretSave`, `CheckTarget.secretFields`), skips (automatic runs) a target with a young current result or one already pending, sends the rest (deduplicated by id) and applies main's immediate answers; streamed answers for a generation no longer awaited are ignored, `dropped` clears the spinner and keeps the previous result. Cloud's check is the app's session check: its result is "Signed in" while Cloud is on and signed in (`proof: "session"`), and its Check calls `lib/auth.ts`'s `requestSessionCheck` (the session atom's refetch; `setCloudSessionCheck` in tests); a sign-in change or the Cloud switch forgets it (`forgetConnectionResults`). Nothing is persisted
      - **When**: on showing the page while Settings is visible (`showing`; and when its descriptors change meanwhile, or main invalidated a result), every saved connection with `maxAgeMs` 10 minutes (`CONNECTION_CHECK_MAX_AGE_MS`), so showing it again within ten minutes sends nothing; a card's or row's **Check** and the summary's **Check all** (fresh: main's cache skipped); and right after a key is saved on the page (a credential field changing, 400 ms later, fresh for that card). Never in the background or at app start
      - **States** (`stateOf`, worst first per card): Key rejected, Key can't make requests (OpenRouter management key), Can't reach (network, timeout, 5xx), Endpoint not found, A model isn't available, Spending limit reached, Out of credit, Rate limited, Access denied, Unexpected answer (red for the first four, amber for the rest), Not checked (with its reason), and Key works / Signed in (Copilot's identity, Cloud) / Copilot available (Copilot's Check) / Reachable · key not verifiable (Tinfoil) / Reachable (self-hosted without a key)
      - **UI** (`ConnectionCheckParts.tsx`: components; `connectionCheckContext.ts`: the page's context, `useOwnerHealth`, `useHealthNow`, ages via `formatAgo`; `connectionCheckControls.ts`: `useHealthControls`, the parts ready to place): the summary above Connections (`connections.status`: "Checking N connections…" with a spinner, then "All N connections work" / "N connections have a problem" / "… need attention" with counts, when it was checked, the free-check note and **Check all**, disabled while checks run; red when a connection a feature uses fails, amber for any other problem; search finds it as "Connection status" by "check connection", "connection status", "key rejected", "check all", …); on each card and row a pill with its age ("Key works · 2 minutes ago") and **Check** (`data-connection-check`; the custom connection's Check connection is this one, `data-custom-connection-check`), the previous result kept with a small spinner while a check runs, and until a first answer the card's own status (Key saved, Signed in as …) beside Check; a model chip gets ✓/✕ (`data-model-check`) when its model was compared; a note on what a working check proved (Copilot's sign-in only, Tinfoil, Azure Speech's model, an OpenRouter key's spending limit); and on a problem a box with each step (✓/✕, the host, the HTTP status; Corti's halves prefixed), what it affects ("Affected: Cleanup, Chat." or nothing affected) and actions: Replace key (`ApiKeyInput`'s `editRequest`, the row's Manage, the connection's Replace key dialog), Get a key, Remove, Choose a model for <feature>, Edit endpoint (Azure Speech's field, the connection's dialog, a row's feature page), Sign in again, Check again
      - **Flags elsewhere**: the Settings menu's dot on `connections` (`SettingsNavItem.flag`, `data-settings-nav-flag`, from `connectionsFlag`): red only for an active usage's own failure (its connection rejected, unreachable, endpoint not found or a management key, or its own model missing), amber for rate limits, spending limits and out of credit on a connection a feature uses; a connection nothing uses, or a model only an off feature uses, flags nothing. The model pickers: `pickerHealthMap` (by connection, and `corti:llm`/`corti:stt`) is published to the store with the flag (`publishConnectionHealth`) and shown as advisory metadata (`llmOptionAdvice`, `sttOptionAdvice`, `ModelPicker`'s `advice`): "Groq's key was rejected (checked 3 minutes ago)" with Fix on AI Connections beside each available option of that connection (a missing model on its own option only), and on the button when it's the pick without a warning of its own; never a `pickAvailability` reason, so the option stays pickable. Flags hold until a newer result
      - **Last recorded use** (stage B; main's half `helpers/connectionOutcomes.js`, the mapping `helpers/connectionOutcomeTargets.js`): real requests record how they ended, once, at the provider boundary and after their retries, with the feature that sent them, by the same descriptor ids. Each card and row with a record shows "Last recorded use: <feature>, OK · <time>" or "…, failed, <reason> · <time>" (`LastUseLine` in `ConnectionCheckParts.tsx`, `data-connection-last-use="ok|failed"`; red when failed), and under an OK the last failure ("Last failure: <feature>, <reason> · <time>", `data-connection-last-failure`); a local time, the date too when it isn't today; reasons in the checks' words (`connections.lastUse.reasons.*`: key rejected, rate limited, out of credit, can't reach, …; Copilot and Cloud "sign-in no longer valid", Cloud's credit "word limit reached"). A card reads its descriptors' newest (`ownerLastUse`: Corti's two halves; the custom connection's `CheckTarget.outcomeIds`, its same-key features' own `endpoint:` ids, where their requests record); a descriptor with no record shows no line. The page reads main's record when it shows (`loadConnectionOutcomes`, `connection-outcomes-get`) and follows `connection-outcomes-changed`; `useHealthControls` returns the line as `lastUse` (null without a record), placed by `ConnectionCard`, `ConnectionRow` (`lastUse`), the endpoint rows and the custom connection's card and row
        - **Recorded** (first coverage): the language-model handlers under `ReasoningService.processText` (at `handler.call`, before the reply's checks: a repeated transcript still records OK; Styles' requests say `connectionFeature: "styles"`, `dictationStyles.ts`; the screen context model's `connectionScope: "dictationAgentVision"` names whose custom key it reads; both stripped before any provider); chat streaming's provider finish and error (`chat/chatTurnEngine.ts`, which `useChatStreaming.ts` wraps: Chat's, or the assistant's from the agent window, its vision override and Chat fallback included; never a delivery's failure after the finish); main's batch transcription request (live dictation, `outcome` tag on `dictation-transcription-request`; Re-transcribe, `retry-transcription`; Azure Speech uploads, `transcribe-audio-file-byok` as File Transcription); and OpenWhispr Cloud's transcription (`cloud-transcribe`, and Re-transcribe on Cloud). Prompt Studio and the test set count: they run through `processText`
        - **Not yet**: the Gemini, Tinfoil, Corti, Mistral and xAI transcription proxies, streaming speech-to-text and other uploads; local models and enterprise deployments have no stage A descriptor
        - **Not recorded**: cancels and yields, a missing key or sign-in (nothing sent), local checks of a reply (a repeated transcript, a truncation, an empty answer), tool and delivery failures. Outcomes are codes and times: never a key, URL, body, prompt or dictated text (`connectionOutcomes.test.js`' canary)
        - Tests: `test/helpers/connectionOutcomeTargets.test.js` (requests to descriptor ids, fence slots, what isn't recorded), `connectionOutcomes.test.js` (the writer: last outcome and failure, refusals, fencing, clearing, a broken store, the privacy canary, IPC), `connectionOutcomesDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node), `dictationTranscriptionOutcome.test.js`, `useChatStreamingConnectionOutcome.test.js`, `test/services/reasoningServiceConnectionOutcome.test.js`, `test/components/connectionHealth.test.js` (the ids the cards read), `connectionLastUseRender.test.js` (happy-dom)
    - **Custom connection** (`settings/CustomConnectionCard.tsx`; pure logic in `config/customConnection.js`, ESM like `config/copilot.js` so main requires it): one Azure OpenAI or OpenAI-compatible endpoint with one key, one of the Connections: a card while in use (on a first run, after the fold), else a row under Saved, not in use whose Manage opens the card's content, and "Add custom connection" below the fold while there's none (anchor `connections.custom` on whichever renders; where policy allows the `custom` provider for no scope it says so and offers no setup). Setting `customConnection` (localStorage JSON `{ name, endpoint, models: { language, speech } }`, `"null"` when removed so the storage listener carries it; malformed reads as null) and the fixed secret `CUSTOM_CONNECTION_API_KEY` (`SECRET_KEYS`: encryption, plaintext fallback, Reset app data), hydrated by `get-custom-connection-key` into `customConnectionApiKey` and shown masked only; there is no renderer save IPC. `validateEndpoint`: Azure hosts (as `isAzureOpenAIEndpoint`) take the resource origin, optionally `/openai` or `/openai/v1`, and refuse any other path (Foundry projects, operation URLs: "paste the resource endpoint from Keys and Endpoint"); language models then use `{origin}/openai/v1`, speech-to-text `{origin}` (the transcription route builds the deployment URL). Other hosts take an OpenAI-compatible base (https unless private, no credentials, query or fragment), used as is by both. "Used by" is derived, never stored: `featuresUsingConnection` matches each custom usage's `customEndpoint` (from `connectionUsage.ts`: the store field its URL comes from, inherited ones included, and the key slot its request reads) by host on Azure, by normalized base otherwise. Main IPC (`helpers/customConnectionIpc.js`): `custom-connection-copy-key` (Save as connection), `-save-key` (with `applyTo` slots), `-apply-key`, `-compare` (booleans only), `-delete-key`, `-check`; slots are a fixed map (`CUSTOM_CONNECTION_SLOTS`: the speech-to-text, cleanup and per-scope custom keys) and unknown names are refused; writes are serialized and awaited (`environment.persistSecret`), then bump `credentialGeneration` and send `api-key-updated` to every window, the sender included, whose store re-reads them without saving. The renderer first cancels a slot's pending debounced save (`cancelPendingSecretSave`) and flushes one it reads from (`flushPendingSecretSave`). The add/edit dialog's Check connection runs in main behind `assertCloudAllowed` (`runCustomEndpointCheck`, shared with the connection checks): `GET {languageBase}/models` (401/403 → key rejected, else reachability), then on Azure `GET {origin}/openai/deployments` (optional: failure is "deployments unavailable"; found ones join `models` by `classifyDeployment` when the dialog saves). The card's Check is the page's connection check (Connection checks below: compared deployment ids, never the catalog, so it no longer adds found deployments to `models`). The card shows name, kind, host, its check's pill and Check, a ⋯ menu (Rename; Edit endpoint, which rewrites the URL field of every feature using it through the existing setters; Replace key, which lists and updates their slots; Remove, refused while an active feature uses it, listing them with links), the masked key, Used by chips (a slot holding another key shows "different key · Update", which applies the connection's key) and its models plus those its features use. The model pickers list it as its own group (Model picker and Speech-to-text picker below), whose picks write through to each feature's own fields; `reloadSecretFromMain` (settingsStore) is how the store reads a slot back from main, for the `api-key-updated` refresh and for a picker's pick
    - **Self-hosted & custom endpoints**: read-only, per feature: host and model of each LLM `custom` provider (per-scope URL and key) and `self-hosted` server, and the speech-to-text custom (one shared `customTranscriptionApiKey`) and self-hosted endpoints, each with "Edit in <feature>". While there is no custom connection, a custom row offers "Save as connection" (its Azure origin or base, and that feature's key copied in main), or says why its endpoint can't be one; rows the connection covers move to its card
    - **Local models**: downloaded Whisper, sherpa-onnx (Parakeet, Cohere) and GGUF models with kind, size on disk and Used by, through the same list IPCs and `useModelDownload` hooks as the pickers (download, cancel, delete with the existing confirm; a model with an active use can't be deleted here), the built-in search embedding model (Note search), "Keep model loaded" and both `GpuDeviceSelector`s (`settings/GpuDeviceSelector.tsx`; a pick in one copy updates the others, on Recognition and Cleanup too)
    - **Enterprise**: the managed deployments per feature (read-only, "Managed by your organization") or errors, personal Bedrock/Azure/Vertex setups by use, else "Not set up" with a link to Cleanup's model setting; configuration stays in the feature editors
    - **Links inside Settings** (`settings/settingsLink.ts`): `SettingsModal` provides `useOpenSettingsLink()`, which resolves a destination like `openSettings` and lands on its setting; `SettingsLink` (`settings/ManageKeysLink.tsx`) renders one, and nothing outside Settings
  - **Model picker** (`settings/modelPicker/`): each language-model feature (Cleanup, Voice Assistant and its Screen Context Model, Translation, Note Formatting, Chat) picks a connection and a model in one combobox in `InferenceConfigEditor`, which keeps the managed enterprise card and errors, the "Use managed" banner, Disable thinking and Keep model loaded (same visibility rules, same anchors). A legacy model (`"legacy": true`, #2958) is listed only while it is the selection, marked Legacy and not pickable again (`isListedModel`; a downloaded legacy local model isn't listed here, it is deleted on AI Connections › Local models). Disable thinking keeps the fork's provider-aware model lookup rather than upstream's `offersThinkingToggle`, so a Copilot model id can't match another provider's entry. Onboarding keeps `ReasoningModelSelector`. Speech-to-text has its own picker and adapter (below); `ModelPicker` is generic over the option type (`PickerOptionBase`), so both share it
    - **`ModelPicker.tsx`**: a Radix popover (so Settings' Esc and close rules see it) whose button shows logo · model, then connection · account, with a warning when the pick can't run (Cloud off, no key, Copilot signed out or unsupported), else what its connection's last check on AI Connections found (`advice`, advisory: see Connection checks). The menu has a filter field (type to filter, ↑/↓, Enter; Esc closes only the menu) and groups per connection; unavailable entries stay listed and focusable with their reason, can't be picked, and their link (or Enter) opens where it's fixed. Note formatting, while any field borrows Cleanup's value, shows those fields one by one ("inherited from Cleanup") with Override, which opens the menu. A group may carry a badge beside its name (the custom connection's kind), and an entry that opens a field under the picker (`releasesFocus`) keeps focus there when the menu closes
    - **Options** (`options.ts`, pure; its context from `useLlmPickerContext.ts`: key presence only, Copilot's and Tinfoil's live lists only while the menu is open, downloaded local models): `buildModelPickerOptions(scope, ctx)` → OpenWhispr Cloud ("Automatic", no model list), GitHub Copilot (its live models), each API-key provider (connected ones first; OpenRouter shows the current model and "Search OpenRouter models…", which opens `SearchableModelList` under the picker), Custom endpoint, downloaded local models plus "Download more models", Self-hosted, Enterprise (and the organization's deployment while the setup mode is manual). Filters as before: `allowedModes` (the Screen Context Model offers API-key providers only) and policy (`filterModeOptionsByPolicy` with `llmPolicyProviderIds`, `isProviderAllowedByPolicy`) hide entries; Cloud off ("Turned off in Privacy & Data" → `privacyData#privacy.cloud`), signed out ("Sign in" → `requestSignIn`), Copilot on chat or the assistant ("Copilot serves Cleanup, Styles, Translation and Note Formatting only"), Copilot signed out or still checking ("Sign in on AI Connections" → `connections#connections.copilot`) and a provider without a key ("Add an API key" → `connections#connections.<provider>`) list unavailable. Keys are entered only on AI Connections; Custom and Self-hosted keep their per-feature URL, model and key (`ApiKeyInput` `replace` mode) under the picker. The custom connection (`connectionOptions.ts`; its context from `usePickerConnection.ts`) is a group after Copilot, named after it with its kind badge, wherever the Custom endpoint is offered (the same mode, policy and `allowedModes` checks, so the Screen Context Model has it too): its remembered `models.language`, the models its features run (`featuresUsingConnection`, read only while the menu is open; "Used by <feature>" under another feature's), the feature's own, an OpenAI-compatible connection's `{languageBase}/models` (`utils/openAiCompatibleModels.ts`, shared with `OpenAICompatiblePanel`; never Azure's regional catalog), then "Enter a deployment name…" ("…a model name…" when OpenAI-compatible), which opens `ConnectionModelField` under the picker (Enter picks, Esc or Cancel closes, focus returns to the button). Without a key its entries are unavailable ("Add an API key" → `connections#connections.custom`). A feature whose resolved custom URL is the connection's (`usesConnectionEndpoint`: the Azure host, else the base; inherited URLs too) selects that entry, and its button reads "<model> · <name> · Azure OpenAI"; the Custom endpoint entry, its fields and warnings stay as they were
    - **Selection adapter** (`selectLlm.ts`): every pick goes through `commitLlmSelection`, which revalidates it against the live context (`pickAvailability`: policy, Cloud switch and sign-in, Copilot scope and sign-in, saved keys, downloaded models) and refuses with a toast if it became invalid, then writes mode, cloud mode, provider and model in one `setResolvedLLMConfig` call. Mode and cloud mode are written only when the pick changes the mode shown, and a mode-only pick (Cloud, Self-hosted, Enterprise) clears a provider that mode can't use, as the mode cards did; Cloud while signed out calls `requestSignIn` and writes nothing; Custom and OpenRouter commit only when a model is picked in their fields; the managed entry sets `enterpriseSetupMode` to managed (a pick that a managed deployment would otherwise override sets manual; otherwise the global setup mode is left alone); `onModeChange` (Cleanup's toast) fires only on a mode change. Opening or browsing writes nothing, so note formatting's inherited fields and an unset Screen Context Model stay unset until picked. A model on the custom connection goes through `commitLlmConnectionSelection` (`useLlmConnectionSelection`), in this order: revalidated as any pick; `applyConnectionKey` (`connectionPick.ts`: `cancelPendingSecretSave`, the awaited `customConnectionApplyKey` into the scope's own slot, `LLM_KEY_SLOTS`, then `reloadSecretFromMain`, so this window never pairs the new URL with the old key; a failure refuses with a toast and writes nothing); the scope's `cloudBaseUrl` (the connection's `languageBase`, `{origin}/openai/v1` on Azure); the usual custom pick; then the model joins `customConnection.models` (`mergeConnectionModels`). `test/components/modelPickerSelectLlm.test.js` checks every scope × pick against the old editor's writes and every request route, and that a connection pick leaves exactly the store keys, persisted copies and routes of a hand-entered custom pick with the same URL, model and key, with nothing written before main's copy
  - **Speech-to-text picker** (`settings/TranscriptionConfigEditor.tsx`; options `settings/modelPicker/sttOptions.ts`, context `useSttPickerContext.ts`, adapter `selectStt.ts`): Recognition (`TranscriptionSection` in `SettingsPage`, which keeps the managed enterprise card and errors, the "Use managed" banner, the live preview toggle and the transcription `GpuDeviceSelector`), Live Recording (`MeetingTranscriptionPanel`, with speaker detection and echo cancellation) and File Transcription (`UploadTranscriptionPanel`) each pick an engine in one `ModelPicker`; the transcription language and Whisper VAD stay below on their pages with the old visibility. Onboarding has its own steps (`ProviderSetupStep`) and never used these editors. Upstream's `TranscriptionModelPicker.tsx` is gone; what v1.11.0 changed in it lives here: **enterprise audio** (#2989): the picker offers enterprise when a managed deployment exists (realtime for Live Recording, batch for File Transcription), and picking it only switches the shared setup to managed (`selectStt`), each surface keeping its own mode for "Use personal setup"; while managed, Live Recording and File Transcription show `settings/ManagedTranscriptionCard.tsx` instead of the picker, and `EnterpriseAudioSettings` (the organization's audio gateway setup) sits on Recognition (anchor `dictation.stt.enterpriseAudio`), Live Recording and File Transcription. The **custom endpoint** fields are `settings/SttCustomEndpointFields.tsx` (URL, model, key) with upstream's automatic check (`ProviderCheckField`, #2652); provider keys are checked on AI Connections. **Legacy** cloud models (#2958) are listed only as the selection, never pickable again (`models.legacyHint`). The Whisper GPU card (`settings/WhisperGpuCard.tsx`) names the verified backend (CUDA or Vulkan, #1982) and its failure reason
    - **Options** (`buildSttPickerOptions(surface, ctx)`, pure): OpenWhispr Cloud, each speech-to-text provider with its models (connected first), Custom endpoint (plus, while its URL is set, the model this surface last used there, `transcriptionModelByProvider["<context>:custom"]`, as a one-pick way back), downloaded local models only (Whisper, Oruk/NVIDIA, Cohere with its one-language note) and "Download more models", Self-hosted, Enterprise. Filters as the old mode cards and tabs: policy (`filterModeOptionsByPolicy` per surface catalog, `isProviderAllowedByPolicy`), enterprise only under a managed policy and never on Live Recording, which lists only `getMeetingStreamingTranscriptionProviders` with their streaming models, no custom endpoint and Self-hosted as "not available yet"; File Transcription hides `STREAMING_ONLY_PROVIDERS`. OpenRouter's speech-to-text (#2105, #3016) is its own provider group with the models each surface allows (`sttSurfaceProviders`): Recognition leaves out the upload-only ones (Deepgram Nova-3 through OpenRouter, which gets no dictionary; `isUploadOnlyTranscriptionModel`), File Transcription lists them, and a vendor-prefixed id outside the shortlist stays usable as the current selection (`acceptsUnlistedTranscriptionModel`); each entry carries its vendor's icon (`openrouter:<vendor>`). Cloud off, signed out, missing credentials (Corti needs both client credentials; Azure Speech its key and a valid endpoint, "Needs its endpoint and key" with "Set up on AI Connections") and sherpa models this macOS can't run list unavailable with a link. The button warns when the selection can't run there (`sttSelectionIssue`: a non-streaming provider or model on Live Recording, a live-only provider on uploads, an upload-only model on Recognition, no key, a local engine with no model on disk, counting the engine's default when none is set). The custom connection is a group right after OpenWhispr Cloud on Recognition and File Transcription (never Live Recording, which has no custom lane), built as on language models from its speech models. Speech-to-text has one custom key (`CUSTOM_TRANSCRIPTION_API_KEY`, also sent by an authenticating self-hosted Orukeet server, `getCustomTranscriptionKey`): while the menu is open main says whether it already is the connection's (`customConnectionCompare`, a boolean; `compareSharedSttKey`); if it isn't, the entries are unavailable ("Speech-to-text uses one custom key, and <feature> uses a different one") whenever `sttSharedKeyConflict` finds something that would still send it elsewhere after the pick: another surface's custom endpoint (its own URL, or Recognition's it borrows) or a self-hosted Orukeet server
    - **Under the picker**: the custom endpoint's URL (written as typed, normalized on blur), the shared `customTranscriptionApiKey` (replace-only, Remove) and model (commits through the adapter); the self-hosted URL and model (Recognition's and uploads' own keys); Corti's data region and tenant; Tinfoil's transport note; Cohere's language note; Whisper's GPU pack card (`settings/WhisperGpuCard.tsx`) while a Whisper model is selected. Live Recording and File Transcription list what they borrow from Recognition one row each, with Override (`sttInheritedFields`: in local mode their own engine, then Recognition's model or the engine's default; otherwise provider, endpoint host and model, or uploads' default after a live-only Recognition provider), then the button's warning with "Choose a model" (the picker) and its fix link (`connections#connections.localModels` for a missing local model)
    - **Selection adapter** (`commitSttSelection`): revalidates the pick (`sttPickAvailability`), then makes the old handlers' writes through the same setters in the same order: on a mode change, what that surface's mode card wrote (mode, the legacy `*UseLocalWhisper` flag, dictation also via `updateTranscriptionSettings`, cloud mode, `enterpriseTranscriptionSetupMode` managed for enterprise); a cloud model, `switchCloudTranscriptionProvider` (remembers the outgoing model) when the stored provider differs, then the model; a local model, the local provider and that engine's model key unless already selected. Cloud while signed out calls `requestSignIn` and writes nothing; Recognition's toast fires on mode changes only. Opening, browsing and inherited values write nothing (the old picker's open-time provider fallback and "first downloaded Whisper" writes are gone); local servers follow `syncStartupPreferences` as before. A model on the custom connection goes through `commitSttConnectionSelection` (`useSttConnectionSelection`, which asks main to compare again first): the shared slot's key via `applyConnectionKey`; the surface's URL setter with `connectionSpeechUrl` (the connection's origin, or a pasted Azure operation URL already on it unless it names another deployment); the usual custom pick, which remembers the outgoing provider's model in `transcriptionModelByProvider`; then the model joins the connection's speech models. A surface on the connection, its own or inherited (File Transcription borrowing Recognition's), selects that entry and shows "<model> · <name> · Azure OpenAI". `test/components/modelPickerSelectStt.test.js` replays the old handlers for every surface × pick × setup × policy and compares every store key, its persisted copy and every route, and the same for a connection pick against a hand-entered custom one on Recognition and File Transcription; `modelPickerConnection.test.js` covers both pickers' groups, the shared-key rule and the selected state
  - **Dictation overview** (page `overview`, Dictation's first; `settings/DictationOverview.tsx`, values from the pure `settings/dictationOverviewModel.ts`): read-only, writes nothing and never logs dictated text
    - **Strip** (`buildDictationPipeline`, explanatory, not a strict order): Recognition (`describeSttSelection` / `sttSelectionIssue` over Recognition's picker context, so it shows the picker's can't-run warning), Dictionary & Glossary (dictionary words, active glossary rules, snippets; opens the Dictionary page, leaving Settings via `onOpenElsewhere`), Cleanup (on/off, `describeLlm` of the policy-effective `dictationCleanup` route, `activePromptName`), Style (the dictation hotkey's style; styles on and with a key), Personality (planned, not a button), Output (automatic pasting, and auto-learn's mode, `autoLearnMode` from the settings store, 13h). Each node opens its page; 2, 3 or 6 columns by container width
    - **Last dictation**: the newest completed row in the transcription store, its steps read with `getTranscriptionSteps` (`history-get-steps`, as View steps) while Settings shows, again when the row's id or text changes; columns named by `ui/transcriptionStepLabels.ts` (shared with View steps), the glossary's fired rules struck/inserted from their saved offsets (plain corrected text when they don't line up), a no-op finalize left out, then "Pasted" (target app, total time). States: history off (link to `privacyData#privacy.history`, nothing read), none, loading, not captured, another sign-in's, load error. "Open in History" is `requestOpenHistoryItem`: the History view, selected on that row
    - **Hotkeys card**: the dictation hotkey (mode hidden where the Hotkeys page hides it) and the style keys, linking to Hotkeys
  - **Hotkeys** (wide): the activation mode above one table (`settings/HotkeyTable.tsx`: Action · Hotkey · How · a link to the feature; stacked below a narrow container) whose rows hold today's controls in `SettingsPage`: the dictation `HotkeyListInput` (Add another, Reset), each style (`ui/StyleHotkeysList.tsx`, read-only: its key or "Not set" / "Style off", refusals and the Ctrl+1 warning inline, "Edit style ›" = `requestStyleFocus` + navigate), Voice Assistant, Translation, Review Last Dictation (`hotkeys.review`: no default key, registered by main through `register-review-hotkey` like Meeting, its link leaves Settings for History, and every hotkey field's conflict check includes it, Style Studio's too), Meeting (+ layout select), Cancel (Esc, info only). Validation, conflict checks and anchors are unchanged (`hotkeys.cancel` is new)
  - **Storage** (page `storage`, Data, between Privacy & Data and Processes & resources; `settings/StoragePage.tsx`): what OpenWhispr keeps on this computer. In order: the size bar, **Recordings**, **Logs**, the **History database** and **Everything else** (the sub-bullets below). **Logs** (`settings/LogsSection.tsx`): the size and file count by the title; What to log (Off / Problems only / Everything, `system.developer.debug`), with the level in force and why when a `--log-level` flag or an inherited `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` overrides it (the select stays usable; the note says the override wins until removed); Keep logs for (7/14/30/90 days, `storage.logs.retention`); Include dictated text (`storage.logs.text`, with the 3-day note); Logs folder (size, the folder with the profile masked, the current file, Open folder, `system.developer.logs`) and Clear logs (`storage.logs.clear`; a destructive confirm first, then "Logs cleared" or the names of the files another instance kept); Recent problems (`storage.logs.problems`: the last 50 warnings and errors main read from the newest files, three shown until Show all: local time, level, area, first line); Copy diagnostics (`storage.logs.diagnostics`). Each setting is sent alone (`set-log-settings`), so an unset retention keeps following the level. `system.developer.debug` and `system.developer.logs` kept their ids when they moved from System. System's **Diagnostics** section (`settings/DiagnosticsSection.tsx`, anchor `system.diagnostics`) replaces the old Developer section: one line (level in force · kept N days · size · problems today), Manage in Storage (`storage#system.developer.debug`, shown inside Settings only), a Processes & resources row (`system.diagnostics.processes`, a search summary: in RAM · N processes · graphics memory from `process-inventory-get({ history: false })` when the page shows, "Not measured yet" before the first sample, and Open Processes & resources, inside Settings only) and Copy diagnostics. Above them, only while Chromium draws on the CPU, a Graphics row (`system.diagnostics.graphics`, search falls back to `system.diagnostics`): for a software fallback a warning ("Graphics acceleration is off…") with **Restart OpenWhispr** (the existing `relaunch-app`, which under `npm run dev` only quits, since Vite dies with Electron); for a launch flag's software mode a neutral "Drawing on the CPU (set by a launch flag)"; nothing for hardware or unknown (`graphicsNotice`, from `log-info`'s `graphics`, kept current by `graphics-mode-changed`). Copy diagnostics (`settings/CopyDiagnosticsDialog.tsx`, text from `settings/logSettingsFormat.ts`) previews exactly what it copies, then copies it: Stage 1's `buildInfoText`, the OS, the graphics mode (`Graphics: …` with `gpu_compositing` and `rasterization`, and `Graphics renderer: …` when known), the log level (in force and why), retention, the text switch, the masked folder, the size and counts of recent problems by area; never log lines, keys, dictated text or a user name. Hook: `settings/useLogInfo.ts`. Tests: `test/components/storageLogsSection.test.js`
    - **Size bar** (`settings/StorageInventorySections.tsx` `StorageOverview`, anchor `storage.inventory`; segments from `settings/storageFormat.ts` `storageBarSegments`): one segment per kind (recordings, local models, search index, app cache, logs, history database, and settings + keys + CLI bridge as "other"), a legend with sizes, the total and Refresh; "Measuring…" while main measures. The page reads the inventory (`settings/useStorageInventory.ts`, `storage-inventory`) only when it shows while Settings is visible, on Refresh (`{ refresh: true }`, past main's cache) and after each action; nothing polls
    - **Recordings** (`settings/RecordingsSection.tsx`): Keep recordings for (`privacy.audioRetention`, its id kept from Privacy & Data): Off, 1, 7, 14, 30, 60, 90 days, 6 months, 1 year (`utils/retentionPeriods.js` `recordingRetentionChoices`; an organization's cap disables longer options and an off-list value in force is listed); notes "Not saved while local history is off" and "Never kept longer than its transcript", plus the value in effect when the transcript limit is shorter (`effectiveRecordingRetentionDays`). Recording quality (`storage.recordings.quality`, setting `recordingQuality`): Smaller (32 kbps, recommended), Balanced (64 kbps), Original (default, no `audioBitsPerSecond`), each with KB a minute (~240 / 480 / 945) and an estimate for the retention in effect at the user's pace (main's `recordingPace`: dictations a day over the last 30 days of history, or since the first one in that window, × their average `audio_duration_ms`); no estimate while nothing is kept. Recordings folder (`privacy.audioStorage`, its id kept): size and file count (`get-audio-storage-usage`), the masked folder, Open folder (`storage.recordings.folder`, `open-recordings-folder`: main resolves the folder and creates it if missing) and Clear all recordings (asks first; another account's recordings stay, as with Clear history; `delete-all-audio` returns `{ deleted, failed }`, and the notice says what's left)
    - **History database** (`HistoryDatabaseSection`, anchor `storage.database`): the size of the db with its `-wal` and `-shm`, counts of dictations, step records, reviews, test cases, dictionary words, glossary rules and notes (rows not deleted, one query: `countHistoryRecords`), the masked file with Show in folder, and "Clear history in Privacy & Data" (`privacyData#privacy.history`); clearing stays there
    - **Everything else** (`EverythingElseSection`): one row per id with what it is, its masked path, size and actions: Settings (`storage.settings`: `Local Storage` + `.env`), API keys and sign-ins (`storage.secrets`), Local models (`storage.models`: size per kind, "Manage in AI Connections" → `connections#connections.localModels`, and the old System model cache action, Remove all models with today's confirm and `openwhispr-models-cleared` event, carrying `system.data.modelCache`, its id kept; once every model is gone it also clears local model selections and onboarding's pending local setup, `clearMissingLocalModelSelections` and `discardPendingLocalSetup`, #2979; under it, only while there are some, the retired models row: models the signed catalog no longer offers that are still on disk, never a speech model a feature still selects, with Remove, `listRetiredLocalModels` / `deleteRetiredLocalModels`, #3081), Search index (`storage.searchIndex`), App cache (`storage.appCache`, the only Clear here, asks first), Command-line bridge (`storage.cliBridge`) and, on Windows when it exists, Self-hosted builds (this fork's `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenWhispr-selfhost`, listed but never measured: its build worktree holds node_modules; no anchor). Each row has Copy path (the masked path) and Open folder / Show in folder (by id)
    - **Privacy & Data** (`settings/PrivacyStorageSummary.tsx`): the audio retention and storage rows left; two summary rows sit after Save discarded dictations, with their own anchors: Recordings (`privacy.recordingsSummary`: kept for the effective period, or Not saved · size) and Logs (`privacy.logsSummary`: level in force · size), each with Manage in Storage (`storage#privacy.audioRetention`, `storage#system.developer.debug`). Keep transcripts for has its own list: Forever (default), 1 year, 6 months, 90, 60, 30, 14, 7, 1 days (`transcriptRetentionChoices`)
    - **System › Reset app data**: only Reset is left under Data Management. Its row text, its confirm and account deletion's "erase this device" say what `cleanup-app` deletes, the search index included, and that logs stay (cleared in Storage › Logs); behavior unchanged. The self-hosted builds folder isn't mentioned there (it's listed on Storage, which says Reset leaves it alone)
    - Tests: `test/components/storagePage.test.js`, `test/helpers/storageInventory.test.js`, `test/helpers/storageInventoryDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node), `test/helpers/audioStorageClearAll.test.js`, `test/helpers/audioManagerRecordingQuality.test.js`, `test/utils/retentionPeriods.test.js`, `test/utils/recordingQuality.test.js`, `test/helpers/audioSegmentMerge.test.js`
  - **Processes & resources** (page `processes`, Data, between Storage and System, `wide`, icon Gauge; `settings/ProcessesPage.tsx`): every process OpenWhispr runs, what each one does and what it costs, from main's process inventory (`processInventory.js`). `settings/useProcessInventory.ts` subscribes (`process-inventory-subscribe`, samples every 2 s as `process-inventory-sample`) only while the page shows (`SettingsPage` passes `showing`) and Settings is visible, reads the 24-hour history (`process-inventory-get`) then and once a minute, and unsubscribes on hide and unmount; a reply that crosses a newer broadcast never takes the numbers back. Before the first sample "Measuring…" (never zeros); while `paused` (a dictation) "Paused while you dictate" with the last sample kept. In order:
    - **Status and notices** (in the `data.processes.summary` anchor): live, last sample at, or paused; one notice per `unavailable` entry: `windowsOnly` is the one note off Windows (committed memory, graphics memory and use, handles, GDI/USER, helper programs), and those columns, tiles, panels and the recording card's graphics column are left out rather than dashed; `helperMissing` / `helperFailed` (with the helper's error code) say what's missing while Electron's numbers still show; `gpuCounters`; `memory` (Linux: memory is unavailable, never 0)
    - **Tiles**: in RAM (the sum, a bar and legend by group like Storage's, N processes · committed), CPU now (% of one core), graphics memory (dedicated, with "Read at" when an earlier sample read it) and "processes holding graphics memory on this PC" (no limit marker) with the graphics mode badge
    - **Table** (`settings/ProcessTable.tsx`, anchor `data.processes.table`; `processesView.ts` `groupProcesses`): App, Windows, Chromium services, Helpers, then Not running now (`snapshot.notRunning`, each with when it starts); one row per instance (two key listeners are two rows): name, state pill (Hidden, Working, Stops in N min), program or Electron type · PID · hotkey, then "+ console host" when one is folded in; In RAM (with a bar), Committed, CPU, Graphics, Started. Sort buttons on In RAM, Committed, CPU and Graphics (`aria-sort`; a second click flips the order; unavailable values last); the row's name is a button with `aria-expanded` and the whole row toggles. Copy as text (`data.processes.copy`) copies `processesText` of a fresh `report`, as Copy diagnostics does (a folded row's parts on indented lines under it)
    - **Expanded row** (`settings/ProcessDetails.tsx`, layout "grouped panels"): What it does, Why it's running, When it goes away (the role keys with main's parameters, `processesText.ts` `aboutParams`: times in the UI language, the hotkey as `formatHotkeyLabel` shows it); for a folded row an **Includes** table under them (`data-process-includes`: each part's exe · PID, In RAM and Committed, the console host's line "Console host: Windows starts one for a command-line program, even a hidden one."); Memory (in RAM, committed, most in RAM, graphics card memory, a sparkline of the instance's history), CPU & graphics (now, during the last recording with partial marked, average since it started, total CPU time, graphics card use, sparkline), Windows objects (handles, GDI and USER "of 10,000", threads, the handle trend over the history and the leak note); the footer PID · type · Started · Running for, Copy (that row as Copy diagnostics' English table, `processRowText`, its parts on indented lines) and, on the graphics row, "Graphics in Diagnostics" (`system#system.diagnostics.graphics`, inside Settings only). Terms (in RAM = working set, committed = private bytes, …) explain themselves on hover and keyboard focus (`settings/ProcessHint.tsx` `Term`, `settings/useProcessHint.tsx`: a portaled tooltip, the text as the element's description); a `null` number shows "—" with why on hover (`Dash`, `processesText.ts` `missingReason`: first sample, helper not working, graphics not read yet, not available on this system)
    - **Last recording** (`settings/ProcessRecordingCard.tsx`, anchor `data.processes.lastRecording`): when, how long, which kind, the Screen edge setting and whether its window opened for it; CPU per process while recording and while processing and delivering (partial processes marked, an exited one never 0), graphics memory after delivery (said to be no peak) with the PC's count then, or why it wasn't read (when the page was closed the column is left out and the line says it's read only while the page is open). A processing phase under 0.2 s (main's CPU floor) says "Too short to measure" rather than unreadable, and the card waits for main's first answer instead of saying there was no recording
    - **Last 24 hours** (`settings/ProcessHistoryChart.tsx`, anchor `data.processes.history`; `processesView.ts` `buildHistoryChart`): a hand-drawn SVG stacked by group with a Memory / Graphics / CPU switch (`aria-pressed`; Graphics only on Windows), recordings marked along the top, gaps where nothing was measured (12 minutes; 40 for graphics, read every 30 minutes without the page open), the time axis flipped in RTL; its numbers (now, highest, average per group and in all) in a screen-reader table
    - **Footnote**: the terms (off Windows only those shown: `footnote.termsBasic`, and the chart's `history.noteBasic`) and that the table goes to the log every 30 minutes and into Copy diagnostics
    - Numbers in the UI language (`processInventoryFormat.ts` `formatProcessBytes` / `formatProcessPercent` / `formatProcessDuration` / `formatProcessTime`, `storageFormat.ts` `formatCount`); strings under `settingsPage.processes.*` (the role texts as in `test/fixtures/processRoles.en.json`) in all 11 locales. Tests: `test/components/processesPage.test.js`
- **WhisperModelPicker.tsx**: Model selection and download UI
- **ConnectorsSection.tsx**: Integrations → Connectors: the "Draft emails in" picker (Automatic shows what it resolved to; Send from chat (Gmail) says to connect Gmail until it can send, and the card's description follows where drafts actually go), a paid-plan upsell, "turned off by your organization" for a resolved org block and "not available right now" while the policy is loading, failed or requires a newer app
- **integrations/SkillsPane.tsx**: Integrations → Skills. Home (Installed, Recommended, search), Manage, Upload and Create swap in place behind a breadcrumb (`ui/Breadcrumb.tsx`); a skill's one pop-up is `skills/SkillDetailDialog.tsx`. The list lives in `stores/skillsStore.ts`, reloaded when the page opens, on `skills-changed`, and on window focus while the page shows
- **settings/WorkspaceTeamspacesSection.tsx**: Settings → Workspace → Teamspaces (the `workspaceTeamspaces` page; General, People and Developer are `WorkspaceGeneralSection.tsx`, `WorkspacePeopleSection.tsx` and `WorkspaceDeveloperSection.tsx`), the workspace's teamspace directory (`SpacesService.listInWorkspace`, via `useWorkspaceSpaces`): every teamspace, including ones the user isn't in, with its admins (the workspace admins when none is named), Join for an open one and a row menu for Settings and Delete teamspace; Create teamspace needs `workspaceCan(…, "create_spaces")`. Teamspaces carry `is_default` and `is_open`, set when creating and in the settings dialog (`SpaceSettingsDialog`, whose Members tab is `SpaceMembersPanel`: direct, invited and inherited rows, Add members). A default teamspace takes every new workspace member through the API's `workspace_members` INSERT trigger (`add_member_to_default_spaces`, migration 103), so invitations, join requests and SSO all land there and the desktop never adds anyone itself; turning Default on adds nobody already in the workspace. People → Add to teamspace (`workspace/AddToTeamspaceMenu.tsx`) reads `SpacesService.coverage` when it opens (how many of the selected people already have access to each teamspace) to draw none, some or all, then adds with one `addMembers` call per chosen teamspace. Deleting a teamspace is still the soft delete (`DELETE /api/spaces/:id` sets `deleted_at`, and its notes' share links stop resolving): there is no trash or purge in this release. The delete itself waits out the 10 seconds of its Undo toast (`holdSpaceDelete`, `heldSpaceDeletes.ts`, and the toast's `onSettled`): until then the teamspace is only out of this device's view (`hideSpaceInView`, the directory hook), so Undo has nothing to repair, here or on another member's device, whose next sync pass would otherwise purge it (chats scrubbed, unsynced notes forked into Personal). A failed delete brings it back with Try again, unsynced notes that do move to Personal are counted in a toast, and a quit inside the window sends the held delete, best effort and bounded at 1.5 s, except a quit to install an update, which main doesn't hold for (only the page's `beforeunload` tries), so being offline, a slow server, a force-quit or an update install can still leave the teamspace in place. Past the window only the API can restore it. Every directory field is optional, so an older API reads as absent. Tests: `test/components/spaceSettingsDialog.test.js`, `spaceMembersPanel.test.js`, `addToTeamspaceMenu.test.js`
- **ui/**: Reusable UI components (buttons, cards, inputs, etc.)
- **ui/DropZone.tsx** and **ui/LineNumberedTextarea.tsx**: the keyboard-reachable drop zone that Upload audio and Upload skill share, and a plain-text editor with a line-number gutter whose lines never wrap, so each number stays beside its line
- **ui/RichTextEditor.tsx**: Tiptap note editor. Note bodies (`content`, `enhanced_content`) are stored as Markdown via tiptap-markdown (`html: false`). NoteBottomBar publishes its height in `--bottom-overlay-height`; the editor reads it for its bottom scroll margin and threshold, its bottom padding and the line menu's flip padding, so the caret and the menu stay clear of the bar
  - `RichTextEditorExtensions.ts` holds the extension list (`RichTextEditor` adds the mention extension in front of it); order matters: at equal priority, later extensions' keymaps and clipboard props run first
  - `RichTextEditorTable.ts` keeps every table to what a GFM pipe table can store: header first row, one single-line paragraph per cell, no merged cells, no alignment or column widths. Pasted cells are flattened while parsing, a normalizer fixes what commands and pastes leave behind, and the table serializer escapes `|` in cells. Tests: `test/components/richTextEditor.test.js` (happy-dom)
  - Floating menus: `RichTextEditorFormatMenu.tsx` shows one formatting toolbar (marks, text style, lists, quote, insert table) above selected text (Tiptap `BubbleMenu`) and below the caret on an empty top-level line, flipping above when there's no room (`FloatingMenu`); `RichTextEditorTableMenu.tsx` is the table's `⋯` menu. They share `RichTextEditorMenus.ts`: a menu hides when focus leaves it, so dropdowns portal into the menu element itself, never into the editor's scroller (EditorContent moves the scroller's children when a note closes); `useHideOnFocusLeave` hides a menu when focus leaves from inside it or after a click on it, which Tiptap misses. The menus detach their element without unmounting it, so buttons use a native `title` rather than `<Tooltip>`, and a dropdown inside a menu is controlled and closed from the menu's `onHide` — otherwise it stays open over a scroll-locked page when the menu hides. Keep menu props stable, or each render dispatches an `updateOptions` transaction. The line menu is placed again in the frame after a transaction that scrolled the caret into view, since ProseMirror scrolls after the menu measured

### React Hooks (src/hooks/)

- **useAudioRecording.js**: MediaRecorder API wrapper with error handling. Owns the dictation window's global Esc through `helpers/dictationCancelHotkey.js` (held while recording, and for processing only after 8 s), the dictation error cards (persistent, Open in History, a late card of an older dictation never shown) and the error card's Retry re-send of a kept recording (see "Batch transcription request" in section 2)
- **useLiveTranscriptPanel.js**: Live transcript panel lifecycle (entrance choreography, buffered text scheduler, measure-then-reveal resize pipeline, preview IPC wiring)
- **useMainWindowSizeOwner.js**: Single owner of the main window size: the Live Transcript panel, then the ladder (error > menu > toast > pill > base), and the dictation-error pill handoff
- **useWindowResizeCompensation.js**: Masks split native setBounds frames via CSS-variable counter-translation
- **useMainProcessNotifications.tsx**: Main-process notifications (hotkey fallback, GPU fallback, learned corrections) as toasts
- **useClipboard.ts**: Clipboard operations hook
- **useDialogs.ts**: Electron dialog integration
- **useHotkey.js**: Hotkey state management
- **useLocalStorage.ts**: Type-safe localStorage wrapper
- **usePermissions.ts**: System permission checks and settings access
  - `openMicPrivacySettings()`: Opens OS microphone privacy settings
  - `openSoundInputSettings()`: Opens OS sound input device settings
  - `openAccessibilitySettings()`: Opens OS accessibility settings (macOS only)
- **useSettings.ts**: Application settings management
- **useWhisper.ts**: Whisper binary availability check

### Services

- **ReasoningService.ts**: AI processing for agent-addressed commands
  - Each streaming call takes the caller's `AbortSignal` and owns its controller and cloud request id, so streams run concurrently and starting or cancelling one never touches another. `cancelAllRequests()` (dictation cancel, in the dictation window's instance) aborts all of them. An aborted stream ends with `finishReason: "abort"`, which the chat engine saves as a stopped turn, never as an answer
  - Detects when user addresses their named agent and removes the agent name from final output
  - Provider implementations live in a registry at `src/services/ai/inferenceProviders/index.ts` covering 11 provider modules (`anthropic`, `copilot`, `corti`, `enterprise`, `gemini`, `groq`, `lan`, `local`, `openai`, `openwhispr`, `tinfoil`), each implementing the `InferenceProvider` interface from `types.ts`
  - Per-scope LLM config: 4 scopes (`dictationCleanup`, `dictationAgent`, `noteFormatting`, `chatIntelligence`) defined in `src/config/inferenceScopes.ts`
  - `selectResolvedLLMConfig(state, scope)` in `settingsStore.ts` resolves provider/model per scope with fallback chains

### whisper.cpp Integration

- **whisper.js**: Native binary wrapper for local transcription
  - Bundled binaries in `resources/bin/whisper-cpp-{platform}-{arch}`
  - Falls back to system installation (`brew install whisper-cpp`)
  - GGML model downloads from HuggingFace
  - Models stored in `~/.cache/openwhispr/whisper-models/`

### NVIDIA Parakeet Integration (via sherpa-onnx)

- **parakeet.js**: Model management for NVIDIA Parakeet ASR models
  - Uses sherpa-onnx runtime for cross-platform ONNX inference
  - Bundled binaries in `resources/bin/sherpa-onnx-{platform}-{arch}`
  - Windows: the bundled ONNX Runtime ships as `ow-onnxrt.dll`, not `onnxruntime.dll`. `scripts/download-sherpa-onnx.js` renames it and rewrites the import tables of every sherpa image (`scripts/lib/pe-imports.js`) because Windows 11 ships an older `onnxruntime.dll` in System32 and some loader configurations resolve the bare name to that copy (#2054). `afterPack.js` fails the Windows build if `onnxruntime.dll` is present or `ow-onnxrt.dll` is missing
  - Uses the upstream `-no-tts` archives and bundles only the three executables plus ONNX Runtime. The TTS builds statically link espeak-ng (GPL-3.0) into `sherpa-onnx-c-api`, and nothing in the app loads the sherpa C/C++ API libraries, so they are not shipped
  - INT8 quantized models for efficient CPU inference
  - Models stored in `~/.cache/openwhispr/parakeet-models/`
  - Server pre-warming on startup when `LOCAL_TRANSCRIPTION_PROVIDER=nvidia` is set
  - Provider preference persisted to `.env` via `saveAllKeysToEnvFile()` on server start/stop

- **Available Models**:
  - `parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3`: Multilingual (25 languages), ~680MB
  - `parakeet-unified-en-0.6b`: English-only, ~631MB, state-of-the-art EN accuracy (5.91% avg WER on Open ASR Leaderboard)
  - `parakeet-tdt-110m-en`: English-only, ~136MB, lightweight TDT transducer with punctuation for low-end machines
  - `nemotron-speech-streaming-en-0.6b`: English-only, ~632MB, cache-aware streaming FastConformer (`"runtime": "online"` in the registry)
  - `nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b`: Multilingual (15 transcription-ready languages, auto detection), ~650MB, cache-aware streaming FastConformer (`"runtime": "online"`)
  - `cohere-transcribe-03-2026`: Multilingual (14 languages, NO auto detection — one language per server start), ~1.7GB download / ~2.7GB on disk, Conformer encoder-decoder (`"modelType": "cohere-transcribe"`), most accurate local model (5.42% avg WER). Exposed as the `"cohere"` local provider in settings/UI but served by the same ParakeetManager/sherpa-onnx offline WS server; the dictation language is part of the server identity, so changing it restarts the server. Segments long audio at 30s (model max ~35s)

- **Runtimes**: Models are `offline` (default) or `online` per their registry `runtime` field. Offline models use the bundled `sherpa-onnx-ws-{platform}-{arch}` (offline websocket server); online models use `sherpa-onnx-online-ws-{platform}-{arch}` (online websocket server). Both are downloaded by `scripts/download-sherpa-onnx.js`. Partial/final JSON results are merged by `parakeetWsResult.js`.

- **Streaming Commit (online models)**: For online-runtime models, dictation streams worklet PCM to a persistent websocket stream during capture (regardless of the preview toggle) and commits the flushed text at stop as the final transcript — no second decode of the recording. The stop flush is truncation-aware (`finish()` extends its deadline while results keep arriving and flags `truncated`); anything but a clean flush falls back to the record-then-transcribe path (`transcribe-local-parakeet`), which for online models decodes the whole recording over a single stream (no 15s segmentation; segments only bound memory for very long files).

- **Live Transcription Preview**: When the preview toggle is on and an online-runtime model is selected, the preview shares the streaming-commit websocket: partial results update the preview window live (replacing text via `showTranscriptionPreview`). Offline models keep the 1.5s buffered-chunk path (appending via `appendTranscriptionPreview`). If the stream can't start or dies mid-dictation, the preview falls back to the chunked path and the final transcript falls back to the full decode. Tests: `test/helpers/parakeetOnlineStream.test.js` (mock websocket server).

- **Download URLs**: Models from sherpa-onnx ASR models release on GitHub

### Local Semantic Search (Qdrant + MiniLM)

Offline semantic search that finds notes by meaning, not just keywords. Used by the AI agent's `search_notes` tool. Its resources are lazy (#2143): Qdrant and the embedding model start on the first semantic search (or the first vector write while the index is active), the embedding model downloads on that first activation if missing, and everything is released after 5 minutes idle. While asleep, keyword FTS5 serves searches and note changes are journaled in SQLite.

**Architecture**:

- **Qdrant sidecar**: Rust binary spawned as child process (`qdrantManager.js`), port 6333–6350
- **Embedding model**: `all-MiniLM-L6-v2` via ONNX Runtime (`localEmbeddings.js`), 384-dim vectors
- **Vector index**: Qdrant collection management (`vectorIndex.js`), cosine distance
- **Lifecycle owner**: `semanticSearchLifecycle.js` — the only caller that starts or stops Qdrant, the embedding model and the collection
- **Hybrid search**: FTS5 + Qdrant in parallel → Reciprocal Rank Fusion (K=60) with 0.3 cosine score threshold

**Pipeline**:

1. App launches → nothing starts. The first `db-semantic-search-notes` call activates the lifecycle: embedding model downloaded if missing (~22MB) → Qdrant binary starts → collection ensured → the journal is drained. A cold search does not wait: it answers with FTS5 results while activation runs in the background. An existing collection serves searches while the journal drains; a newly created one waits until that activation's drain finishes. A journal row whose upsert keeps failing is parked after three failed attempts, so one bad note never blocks the rest of the index
2. Note create/update/delete → SQLite write → triggers journal the note id in `pending_vector_changes` → `IPCHandlers.notifyVectorChanges()` wakes an already-active index to drain the journal; an idle index drains it on its next activation
3. Agent searches → `db-semantic-search-notes` IPC → parallel FTS5 + vector search → RRF merge → ranked results
4. 5 minutes without a search or write → Qdrant is stopped and the embedding session released; FTS5 keeps serving

**Search fallback chain** (in `searchNotesTool.ts`): cloud search → local semantic → FTS5 keyword

**Storage**:

- Qdrant data: `~/.cache/openwhispr/qdrant-data/` (`qdrant-data-dev/` in development)
- Qdrant binary: `resources/bin/qdrant-{platform}-{arch}` (bundled — downloaded during `prebuild` / `predev:main`)
- Embedding model: `~/.cache/openwhispr/embedding-models/all-MiniLM-L6-v2/` (downloaded on the first semantic search)
- Change journal: `pending_vector_changes` table in the notes SQLite database, maintained by triggers

**Dependencies**: `@qdrant/js-client-rest`, `onnxruntime-node`

**Dev setup**: The Qdrant binary downloads automatically via `predev`/`prestart`. The embedding model downloads on the first semantic search. To manually download: `npm run download:qdrant` and `npm run download:embedding-model`.

### Build Scripts (scripts/)

- **download-whisper-cpp.js**: Downloads whisper.cpp binaries from GitHub releases
- **download-llama-server.js**: Downloads llama.cpp server for local LLM inference
- **download-nircmd.js**: Downloads nircmd.exe for Windows clipboard operations
- **download-windows-key-listener.js**: Downloads prebuilt Windows key listener binary
- **download-windows-mic-listener.js**: Downloads prebuilt Windows mic listener binary (the fallback of `build-windows-mic-listener.js`)
- **download-sherpa-onnx.js**: Downloads sherpa-onnx binaries for Parakeet support
- **download-qdrant.js**: Downloads Qdrant vector DB binary for local semantic search
- **download-minilm.js**: Downloads all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX model + tokenizer for local embeddings
- **download-brand-fonts.js**: Fetches the licensed Yowza font files from the private `OpenWhispr/brand-assets` release into the git-ignored `src/assets/fonts/yowza/`. Runs in `predev:main` and every `prebuild*` chain; skips (Noto Sans fallback) without repo access, fails only when `BRAND_FONTS_REQUIRED=1` (release CI)
- **sync-nucleo-icons.js**: Regenerates `src/components/icons/` from `nucleo-map.json` using the local Nucleo install (`~/.nucleo/skills`); only the icons the app uses are vendored
- **build-globe-listener.js**: Compiles macOS Globe key listener from Swift source
- **build-macos-mic-listener.js**: Compiles macOS mic listener from Swift source
- **build-macos-window-bounds.js**: Compiles the System Settings window reporter from Swift source
- **build-windows-key-listener.js**: Compiles the Windows key listener (MSVC, MinGW or clang) whenever `compile:native` runs on a Windows host, downloading the prebuilt binary when no compiler works; skips a binary newer than its source
- **build-windows-process-stats.js**: Compiles `resources/windows-process-stats.c` (MSVC, MinGW or clang; `pdh`, `psapi`, `user32`) whenever `compile:native` runs on a Windows host (`compile:winprocstats`); no prebuilt download, so `afterPack.js` fails a Windows package without it and selfhost's `COMPILED_HELPERS` requires it compiled; a no-op elsewhere
- **build-windows-mic-listener.js** / **build-windows-system-audio-helper.js**: Compile `resources/windows-mic-listener.c` (`ole32`, `oleaut32`, `user32`; `compile:winmic`) and `resources/windows-system-audio-helper.c` (`ole32`, `mmdevapi`; `compile:winsysaudio`) like the key listener: MSVC, MinGW or clang whenever `compile:native` runs on a Windows host, skipping a binary newer than its source, downloading the upstream prebuilt only when no compiler works, a no-op elsewhere. Both are in selfhost's `COMPILED_HELPERS`, as is `windows-fast-paste` (`build-windows-fast-paste.js` now compiles first and downloads as the fallback), so no fork edit to a `resources/*.c` helper is lost to a download (selfhost's `DOWNLOAD_FIRST_HELPER_SOURCES` is empty). Neither is required by `afterPack.js`: without them mic detection polls and meeting system audio uses Chromium loopback
- **verify-linux-sqlite.js**: Run by `afterPack.js` on Linux x64: fails the package when the SQLite addon needs a newer glibc or libstdc++ than RHEL 9's (GLIBC_2.34, GLIBCXX_3.4.29) or contains AVX/AVX2/BMI instructions, then runs a SQLite + FTS5 probe under `qemu-x86_64` on pre-AVX2 CPU models. CI must set `OPENWHISPR_VERIFY_SQLITE_BASELINE` (`1` checks, `0` skips) or the build fails; elsewhere the check is skipped unless it is `1`
- **run-electron.js**: Development script to launch Electron with proper environment
- **selfhost.js**: Windows self-hosting for this fork (`status`, `dev`, `stable`, `ship`, `stop`, `check`, `adopt-snapshot`, `init`; usage in the file header). Stable is a packaged build of a commit installed over the official app; Dev runs the working tree on the same data folder, with the log level from `.env` like Stable (Dev no longer forces debug; an `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` set in the calling shell passes through unchanged and overrides the setting, as any inherited value does). `stable` and `ship` start Stable by having Explorer open a shortcut to it (`launch-stable.lnk`), never as a child: an agent session's commands run in a kill-on-close job object that forbids breakaway, so a spawned Stable would die when that session or the app running it exits (Dev, a child of the shell, still does). `ship` builds in a dedicated worktree (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenWhispr-selfhost\build`) with `electron-builder.local-win.json`, which marks the package `selfBuilt` (so `src/updater.js` never touches the upstream feed) and records `selfBuiltCommit`. After installing, `ship` checks the installed `resources\build-info.json` (`core.shippedCommitCheck`): it must name the shipped commit and, when this ship's package (`dist\win-unpacked`) had one, carry the same `builtAt`; a missing one fails a ship of a commit that stamps, while a commit from before the stamp falls back to the log. The installed `selfBuiltCommit` check stays, and the new log's System Info line is now a secondary check (only a warning once `build-info.json` confirmed the commit; every log file starts with that line at any level but Off, and Off writes no file). A data guard refuses to run code on data that different code has migrated; an uncommitted Dev run counts as the commit its data code was later committed as (`git rev-list <writer>..<target> -- <data paths>`). Pure decisions live in `selfhost/core.js` (tests: `test/scripts/selfhostCore.test.js`)
- **lib/download-utils.js**: Shared utilities for downloading and extracting files
  - `fetchLatestRelease(repo, options)`: Fetches latest release from GitHub API
  - `downloadFile(url, dest)`: Downloads file with progress and retry logic
  - `extractZip(zipPath, destDir)`: Cross-platform zip extraction
  - `parseArgs()`: Parses CLI arguments for platform/arch targeting
  - Supports `GITHUB_TOKEN` for authenticated requests (higher rate limits)

## Key Implementation Details

### 1. FFmpeg Integration

FFmpeg is bundled with the app and doesn't require system installation:

```javascript
// FFmpeg is unpacked from ASAR to app.asar.unpacked/node_modules/ffmpeg-static/
```

### 2. Audio Recording Flow

1. User presses hotkey → MediaRecorder starts
2. Audio chunks collected in array
3. User presses hotkey again → Recording stops
4. Blob created from chunks → Converted to ArrayBuffer
5. Sent via IPC
6. Main process writes to temporary file
7. whisper.cpp processes file → Result sent back
8. Temporary file deleted

For offline local engines (whisper.cpp, Parakeet/Orukeet, Cohere) the renderer also keeps a 16 kHz mono PCM copy of the same stream from the moment the mic opens (`pcmTap.js`, pre-roll included, capped at 4 minutes) and sends that WAV instead of the WebM, so no FFmpeg decode runs after stop; the WebM still goes to history and remains the fallback for cloud providers and long recordings.

Recording quality (`recordingQuality`, Settings › Storage › Recordings; `utils/recordingQuality.js`): every dictation `MediaRecorder` is built by `createDictationRecorder` in `audioManager.js` (the pre-roll, the batch recorder, microphone recovery's replacement, the streaming fallback and its replacement) with `audioBitsPerSecond` 32000 (Smaller) or 64000 (Balanced), or no options at all for Original (Chromium's ~128 kbps, as before). Capture constraints don't change (stereo, #472). The quality is frozen per recording session in `_recordingQuality`: read when the mic is prepared for an adopted pre-roll (it already encodes), else at `startRecording` / `startStreamingRecording`; a change mid-recording applies to the next one. A recovered recording's segments merge at the session's quality (`mergeRecordedSegments(segments, quality)` → `merge-audio-segments(segments, { quality })` → `ffmpegUtils.mergeAudioSegments(segments, { bitrate })`: 32k for Smaller, 64k for Balanced and Original, 16 kHz mono as before); a cancelled recording's snapshot and the crash spool (`recordingSpool.js`) carry it too. Meetings, uploads and chat voice drafts (`useVoiceDraft.ts`) have their own recorders and are unaffected. Tests: `test/helpers/audioManagerRecordingQuality.test.js`, `test/helpers/audioSegmentMerge.test.js`

**Batch transcription request (OpenAI, Groq, custom endpoints, self-hosted servers, Azure Speech)**: dictation never hangs on a silent server.

- **Main sends it**: `processWithOpenAIAPI` still chooses the route, builds the prompt and keywords, converts to WAV and parses the answer, but hands the request (endpoint, `Authorization`/`api-key`/`Ocp-Apim-Subscription-Key` only, ordered form fields, the audio part's name, audio bytes, audio length, a request id, and its "Last recorded use" tag, `outcome`: descriptor id, slot name and feature, names only) to main through `dictationTranscriptionClient.js` → `dictation-transcription-request` (`helpers/dictationTranscriptionRequest.js`). Proxied providers (Mistral, Corti, Gemini, xAI, Tinfoil, managed Azure) and OpenWhispr Cloud keep their own paths.
- **Azure Speech** (MAI-Transcribe-2, route transport `azure-speech`, never an OpenAI-compatible endpoint): `transcribeWithAzureSpeech` converts the recording to 16 kHz mono WAV (`needsWavConversion` covers `azure-speech`; a failed conversion fails the dictation with `AZURE_SPEECH_CONVERSION_FAILED`, never uploading WebM), checks the 250 MB cap on what it sends, and sends the `audio` part and a `definition` (`azureSpeechTranscription.js`): `locales` from the translation-aware language (`resolveBatchRoute` passes `getEffectiveSttLanguage`, "auto" when none, so a translation from Auto never sends the preferred language), the dictionary as `phraseList` (the `phrases` hint channel, section 13). Main refuses the key for anything but the Speech resource's transcribe URL. The answer is `combinedPhrases[].text` joined; an empty one takes the usual "No text transcribed" path (`EMPTY_TRANSCRIPT`, see Empty dictations below), and there is no dictionary-echo check (a phrase list is a bias, and the check would discard a correctly heard one-word dictation equal to it). Errors go through `providerHttpError` with `providerId: "azure-speech"`. Tests: `test/helpers/audioManagerAzureSpeech.test.js`
- **Own connection pool**: `session.fromPartition("ow-dictation-stt")` (`connectionPool.js`, shared with the Cloud upload partitions), used for nothing else, so resetting it never touches chat, cleanup or another window. History's Re-transcribe of OpenAI, Groq, custom, self-hosted and Azure Speech rows sends through the same code on `ow-history-stt` (no cancel), so a live dictation's reset can't cut it; its Mistral and xAI re-sends keep `proxyFetch`. Azure Speech's Re-transcribe converts the stored WebM to WAV (a failed conversion is `AZURE_SPEECH_CONVERSION_FAILED`), uses main's key and the endpoint from the retry settings (`azureSpeechEndpoint`), the row's speech language (a translation's source language) and the dictionary as its phrase list, as live dictation sends it (13); its uploads (`transcribe-audio-file-byok`) send WAV, MP3 and FLAC as they are and convert anything else to WAV first, with the 250 MB cap on what's sent, no locale and no phrases, through the same request code and `ow-history-stt` (never the upload's own multipart builder, which names the part `file`); speaker labels stay the local diarizer's.
- **Deadline** (`transcriptionRequestDeadline.js`, pure ESM), per attempt, covering sending to the last byte: hosted 10 s + 0.5 × audio (retry 15 s + 1 × audio), local or self-hosted (`isPrivateHost` from `config/customConnection.js`, or the self-hosted route) 60 s + 3 × audio, plus the bytes sent at 64 KB/s, capped at 15 minutes. An unknown length is read from the bytes at 2 KB per second. A streamed (SSE) answer also times out after 15 s without data; `transcriptionEventStream.js` reads it in main, and `transcript.text.done` or `[DONE]` completes it even if the server keeps the stream open (the stream is then released). `OPENWHISPR_TEST_HOSTED_STT_DEADLINE=1` gives local servers the hosted budget, for live checks only.
- **One retry**: only when our own deadline fired before any answer (no body byte; for SSE, no transcript event). Never after an HTTP error (a stalled error body still returns its status), a started answer, a cancel or a second timeout. Main awaits `closeAllConnections()` on the pool before the retry (an HTTP/2 abort resets only its stream) and after a timeout it doesn't retry. Logs: endpoint, attempt, deadline, elapsed, bytes sent; never headers, fields or the answer.
- **Timeout vs cancel**: the deadline raises `TRANSCRIPTION_REQUEST_TIMEOUT`, classified in main by `asProviderError` as `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` ("… took too long to respond") before it crosses IPC; it never touches the cancellation generation, so it reaches the card, the keep-audio save and local fallback like any failure. A cancel (the window's `_activeTranscriptionAbortController` → `dictation-transcription-cancel` with the request id, per sender as `cloud-transcribe` does, or the window going away) aborts either attempt or the reset wait, replies `{ cancelled: true }`, and the renderer throws an `AbortError`. Main tells them apart by which signal fired, never by the error's name.
