- **Esc** (`helpers/dictationCancelHotkey.js`, one owner in `useAudioRecording`): held while recording, and while AudioManager's processing has run more than 8 s (not while pasting); a cycle token keeps an old timer or a late registration from taking a newer recording's Esc. Registered with `{ purpose: "processing" }` (`register-cancel-hotkey`), a press sends `cancel-processing-hotkey-pressed` to the window that registered it, which cancels processing only while it still holds Esc for it; `cancel-hotkey-pressed` stays the recording cancel that Fn and onboarding resets share. Esc belongs to the window that registered it (the dictation window, or the agent window during a take): another window's `unregister-cancel-hotkey` is refused, and the owner's reload, crash or close gives it back. Once registration succeeded the pill shows "Taking longer than usual · Esc to cancel" (`app.mic.slowProcessing`, `PillTooltip`'s `notice`, in place of the style badge; not while the Live Transcript panel is open).
- **Retry re-sends the recording**: any failure that kept its recording (`failedRecordingRetry.js`): timeouts, an unreachable provider, 5xx and rate limits, and auth, quota, configuration and model failures too, since the user may have fixed the cause meanwhile (Open Settings leaves the card up). Never for silence or no audio, a cancel, a voice-assistant recording, a selection edit (`selectionEditFatal`) or a delivery-only failure (`PASTE_FAILED`, `ACCESSIBILITY_PERMISSION_REQUIRED`; `NOT_RESENDABLE_ERROR_CODES`). The batch pipeline (`processAudio`) and the streaming paths whose failed-over upload or language re-transcription failed (`_failStreamingRecording`) keep the recording (blob, length, translation and style intent, the failed row's id from `saveFailedTranscription`'s `onRowSaved`) on the report as `keptRecording`, unless local history or recordings are off, plus the row's id as `historyRowId` (Open in History). The card's Retry then calls `audioManager.resendFailedRecording`: one at a time, only while the card is current and nothing records or processes; its card goes so the pill shows processing (cancellable as any). It captures the paste target afresh, begins the step-history run on the row (`step-history-begin-run` with `{ transcriptionId }`) and re-runs `processAudio` with the settings in force then. Success saves through `complete-failed-dictation-retry` (`stepHistory.completeFailedDictation`: text, steps, status, engine; `NOT_FOUND` saves a new row, `stale` writes nothing); a cause still there fails again with the same card for the same row, saving no second one. A newer card or recording makes the old Retry inert, and the toast's `onRemoved` releases the recording. Failures that kept nothing keep the record-again Retry.
- **Failure cards stay** (`useAudioRecording.showDictationError`, `CleanupFailureToastListener`): every failure card (transcription, cleanup, paste; every provider, enterprise and Cloud included) is the dictation-error presentation with `duration: 0` and a close button, until dismissed or the next dictation starts (its start dismisses dictation-error cards); "no audio" stays a short notice, or ends quietly (Empty dictations, below). Open Settings (`providerErrorActions`) and **Open in History** (`openInHistoryAction`) never dismiss the card. Open in History opens that dictation's own row: the failed row (`historyRowId`), or the row its save reports (`audioManager.saveTranscription`'s `onRowSaved`; the save starts before the paste, so a click waits on it), never "the newest": `history-open-item` from the dictation window → `dictationReview.openItem` (no review drafted, never refused as busy) → `windowManager.queueHistoryItemRequest` shows and focuses the control panel and holds `{ requestId, id }` until it asks (`get-pending-history-item-request` on mount and on `history-item-pending`) → `requestHistoryItem`. Each dictation (a recording or a re-send) bumps `dictationSeqRef`: a paste failure (`safePaste`'s `report`) or a cleanup card (`CleanupFailure.dictation.isCurrent`) of an older dictation that settles after a newer one started is never shown. The pill shows one card at a time, so a dictation's cleanup failure rides on its paste-failure card ("AI cleanup also failed: …", its Open Settings and Open in History); otherwise the cleanup card follows the delivery, whether a paste landed or nothing was pasted (auto-paste off, or a paste that showed no card): its second line says "pasted" or "Saved to History" from `CleanupFailure.dictation.pasted`, its reason from `utils/cleanupFailureCard.ts`. From upstream: no cleanup card during the onboarding demo or for a paste held behind a newer dictation; the cleanup-not-set-up warning (#2634: cleanup on but no model can run, or a missing key; `setupKey` on the failure, its Open Settings leads to the cleanup model) shows once (`markCleanupNoticeShown` once either card showed it); the listener reads `dictation.pasted`, falling back to upstream's top-level `pasted`. A failed row keeps the transcript a later step couldn't process (#2678, every path, streaming selection edits included: they now save a failed row and the card gets Open in History); failed rows aren't pushed to sync, the re-send's completed row is. Every toast in the app renders the same card (`ui/ToastCard.tsx`, #3051; `DictationErrorCard` only sizes the pill window around it), with actions only as structured `actions`; what stays the dictation error card's own is the behavior above (`presentation: "dictation-error"`). A toast's `onSettled` and `onRemoved` fire once, when it starts leaving (its timer, a close or a dismiss) or a newer dictation error replaces it; the kept recording behind a Retry is released then.
- **Empty dictations** (`utils/emptyDictation.js`, pure): a provider's successful answer with no text throws `emptyTranscriptError` (code `EMPTY_TRANSCRIPT`, the English "No text transcribed…" message kept for the logs): Azure Speech, the OpenAI-compatible / custom / self-hosted request, the proxies ("No text transcribed - <provider> response was empty") and the local engines' empty post-processing. HTTP errors, parse failures, timeouts and cancels are unchanged. `transcriptionFailureOutcome` reads it like `NO_SPEECH_DETECTED` and `DICTIONARY_ECHO`: `noAudio`, `keepAudio`, no report, so it never reaches `onError` or the persistent card; it skips the cloud↔local fallbacks as "No audio detected" does (Whisper invents words in silence); `NOT_RESENDABLE_ERROR_CODES` and `ANSWERED_CODES` (the connection worked) list it. History's Re-transcribe is main's own path: since upstream #2718 an empty answer from any engine there is "no speech" (`NO_SPEECH_DETECTED`, `notes.upload.noSpeechDetected`), and a partial result returns a `warning` the History toast adds. Every way a dictation ends with no text goes through `audioManager._endEmptyDictation` once, with the recording's length: a batch failure classified `noAudio` (`onNoAudio(emptyDictation)`), the speech gate's skip, a degenerate recording and any empty successful result, batch or streaming (`onTranscriptionComplete({ success: true, text: "", emptyDictation })`). `emptyDictationOutcome({ durationMs, thresholdSeconds })` with "End empty dictations quietly" (`quietEmptyDictationSeconds`, section 5) is `"quiet"` when the length is known and shorter, else `"notice"` (Off and an unknown length: always the notice; length alone never skips transcription, a one-word dictation can be 360 ms). **Quiet**: no card or toast, no failed row (`_saveQuietEmptyDictation` saves only what "Save discarded dictations" keeps of a cancelled recording, `shouldSaveDiscardedRecording`: off by default, nothing under 1 s; a re-send's row stays as it was), media resumes and the preview hides, and the pill plays the red spin-down: `useAudioRecording` bumps `nothingHeardSeq`, `useNothingHeardPill` holds the pill's `nothing-heard` state for `NOTHING_HEARD_TIMING.totalMs` (1.4 s; `voicePillPresentation.js`): the thinking ring (`processing-signal-glow`, kept active) turns the theme's `--color-destructive` over 250 ms (its four `--signal-*` colors are registered `@property`s so they tween), keeps spinning while `--signal-drag` takes the orbit's speed back, and fades by 1.1 s; with "Hide floating icon when idle" the pill fades out with it (`data-nothing-heard="fade"`, 1.05–1.4 s) and stays faded ("faded", at most 2 s) while App's auto-hide hides the window at once, so it never shows again first; otherwise it settles to idle. The auto-hide waits for the beat; a new press, recording or processing ends it at once. Reduced motion: no spin, no scale, a plain fade. Inside the Live Transcript panel the pill just returns to idle. The agent window's voice takes (section 17) read the same setting: a take shorter than it that heard nothing ends with the agent pill back to idle and no notice (an "Empty assistant take" info line); a longer one shows the window's "Didn't catch that" notice (`agentWindow.notices.nothingHeard`). **Notice** (longer, Off or unknown): the transient "We didn't catch that · Check your microphone and try again." (`showDictationError({ transient: true })`), and codes that keep audio still save the failed row and keep the recording, so Retry from History works. The onboarding demo hears an `error` event for both. One "Empty dictation" info line (scope `audio`) each: `durationMs`, `thresholdSeconds`, `outcome`, `source` (`provider`, `gate` or `result`), `code`, and the speech gate's `gateReason`, `peakRms`, `peakAmplitude` (4 places), `speechWindowCount`, `maxConsecutiveSpeechWindows` when it measured them; never text.
- Tests: `test/helpers/transcriptionRequestDeadline.test.js`, `dictationTranscriptionRequest.test.js`, `transcriptionEventStream.test.js`, `dictationCancelHotkey.test.js`, `failedRecordingRetry.test.js`, `audioManagerResendFailedRecording.test.js`, `audioManagerStreamingFinalization.test.js`, `audioManagerEmptyDictation.test.js`, `transcriptionFailureOutcome.test.js`, `useAudioRecordingResend.test.js`, `useAudioRecordingFailureCards.test.js`, `cleanupFailureToast.test.js`, `dictationReview.test.js`, `cancelProcessingHotkeyRouting.test.js`, `stepHistoryDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node), `test/utils/emptyDictation.test.js`, `test/hooks/useNothingHeardPill.test.js`, `test/stores/quietEmptyDictationSetting.test.js`, `voicePillStructure.test.js` (the spin-down's markup and CSS); audioManager suites reach main's handlers over a mocked fetch through `test/helpers/harness/dictationTranscriptionBridge.js`

### 3. Local Whisper Models (GGML format)

Models stored in `~/.cache/openwhispr/whisper-models/`:

- tiny: ~75MB (fastest, lowest quality)
- base: ~142MB (recommended balance)
- small: ~466MB (better quality)
- medium: ~1.5GB (high quality)
- large: ~3GB (best quality)
- turbo: ~1.6GB (fast with good quality)
- large-q5 / turbo-q5: ~1.1GB / ~574MB (the same models quantized to q5_0)

### 4. Database Schema

```sql
CREATE TABLE transcriptions (
  id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  timestamp DATETIME DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,
  original_text TEXT NOT NULL,
  processed_text TEXT,
  is_processed BOOLEAN DEFAULT 0,
  processing_method TEXT DEFAULT 'none',
  agent_name TEXT,
  error TEXT
);
```

Transcriptions, `agent_conversations`, `custom_dictionary` and `snippets` also carry `account_id` and `legacy_unattributed` (see `database.js` under Helper Modules).

The fork's own tables (history DB) follow the same rule:

- **History reads**: the History view (`getHistoryPage`, `getHistoryItem`, `getHistoryApps`, `getHistoryStepRows`, `getLastReviewableDictation`, `getHistoryRevision`, the dictionary filters and badges), reviews, steps, the export and the Dictionary's usage, occurrence, evidence and cleanup-set queries read only the rows `_historyScope()` shows (`_ownedRowScope("transcriptions")`: the account in view's own plus unattributed ones; signed out on a device only one account has used, that account stays in view). Steps, reviews, evidence and test cases are also scoped to step history's owner (`account:<id>` or `signed-out`, 13d), whose `<id>` is the same id stamped in `account_id`
- **Dictionary writes** follow upstream's per-account rule: `_liveDictionaryRows()` and `getDictionaryEntries()` read the words in view, so rename, merge, Tidy up and restore check uniqueness per account, and restored rows are stamped with the active account. `idx_custom_dictionary_account_word` (per account, tombstones included) still collides on a deleted row's spelling, so `_releaseDeletedDictionaryWord(key, accountId)` moves it aside within the account
- **Word-keyed tables** (`dictionary_pins`, `dictionary_categories`, `dictionary_suggestions`, `dictionary_word_evidence`) stay keyed by `word_key`, device-wide (13h): a pin or category is pruned only when no live word of any account has the key (`_liveDictionaryKeys`); reads and pin and category checks use the visible keys (`_visibleDictionaryKeys`). An account change broadcasts pins and categories, and `getDictionaryGeneration()` moves on (`_accountScopeEpoch`), so History's frozen dictionary matcher is rebuilt
- **Clearing**: Clear history, expiry and single deletes are upstream's account-scoped versions, deleting exactly the cleared rows' steps, reviews and evidence in the same transaction. Account deletion deletes the account's rows (their steps and reviews cascade) plus its owner's steps, reviews, evidence and test cases, then prunes pins and categories. Settings › Storage's Clear all recordings (`audioStorage.clearAllRecordings`) keeps other accounts' recordings
- **Migration order**: upstream's `_initPersonalRowAccounts()` (adds `account_id` and `legacy_unattributed`, rebuilds `custom_dictionary` without `UNIQUE(word)`) runs before the fork's `_init*` steps (glossary, step history, reviews, test cases, dictionary state, content revisions, connection outcomes), so the `custom_dictionary_generation_*` triggers the rebuild drops are recreated by `_initDictionaryContentRevision()`. `test/helpers/accountScopedForkDataMigration.test.js` builds a database with the fork's schema and rows from before account scoping and runs the merged migrations (Electron's Node, `REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1`)

### 5. Settings Storage

Settings stored in localStorage with these keys:

- `whisperModel`: Selected Whisper model
- `useLocalWhisper`: Boolean for local vs cloud
- `language`: Selected language code
- `agentName`: User's custom agent name
- `reasoningModel`: Selected AI model for processing
- `reasoningProvider`: AI provider (openai/anthropic/gemini/local)
- `hotkey`: Custom hotkey configuration
- `hasCompletedOnboarding`: Onboarding completion flag
- `customDictionary`: JSON array of words/phrases for improved transcription accuracy
- `autoLearnMode`: "Learn from your corrections" (Settings › Dictation › Output): `suggest` (the default: learned words wait in the Dictionary's suggestions), `add` (added directly, as before) or `off`; an unknown value reads as `suggest`. Written once from the old `autoLearnCorrections` switch (on → `suggest`, off → `off`; `migrateAutoLearnMode` at the settings store's load, only while the new key is absent; the old key stays, unread). The settings store owns it (other windows follow its `storage` event) and syncs it to main (`setAutoLearnMode` → `auto-learn-changed`) at `initializeSettings` and on change (13h)
- `cleanupDictionaryWords`: "Dictionary words in the cleanup prompt" (Settings › Dictation › Cleanup): `all` (the default, as before), `noEveryday` ("All words except everyday words": every dictionary word whose effective category isn't Everyday words; until the categories are ready it applies `all` and the scope says so), `typedPinnedMissed`, `typedPinned` or `none`; an unknown value reads as `all`. Only dictionary words are filtered; the glossary's canonical forms and snippet triggers always go (13h)
- `cleanupDictionaryGrouping`: "Group dictionary words by category" (Settings › Dictation › Cleanup, under the word set, `components/settings/CleanupDictionaryGroupingSetting.tsx`, `data-setting-id="dictation.cleanup.dictionaryGrouping"`, in settings search; mirrored in the Dictionary's "Where it goes" details; default off): the cleanup prompt keeps its dictionary sentence and then lists its words as "People: …; Work terms: …; Everyday words: …; Other: …" (13h). Not used with OpenWhispr Cloud, which gets the flat list. Other windows follow its `storage` event; turning it (or `noEveryday`) on loads the common-word list the categories read
- `dictionaryHintOrder`: "Dictionary order for speech-to-text" (Settings › Dictation › Recognition, `components/settings/DictionaryHintOrderSetting.tsx`, `data-setting-id="dictation.recognition.dictionaryOrder"`, in settings search; mirrored by the Dictionary page's "Order: by evidence ▾"): `evidence` (the default) or `added`; an unknown value reads as `evidence`. Orders the unpinned typed and auto-learned words in every speech-to-text hint list (13, `utils/dictionaryHintOrder.ts`); other windows follow its `storage` event
- `quietEmptyDictationSeconds`: "End empty dictations quietly" (Settings › Dictation › Recognition, the `quietEmptyDictation` block, `components/settings/QuietEmptyDictationSetting.tsx`, `data-setting-id="dictation.recognition.quietEmpty"`, in settings search): `0` (Off), `2`, `3` (the default), `5` or `10` seconds (`utils/emptyDictation.js`); an unknown value reads as `3`. A dictation shorter than this that comes back with no text ends quietly (the pill's red spin-down, no card, no History row); a longer one says "We didn't catch that" (section 2, Empty dictations). Read by the audio manager when a dictation ends empty; other windows follow its `storage` event
- `azureSpeechEndpoint`: Azure Speech's resource endpoint (AI Connections; a pasted Foundry `services.ai.azure.com` endpoint is stored as the resource's `cognitiveservices.azure.com` origin once the field is left), not a secret; its key is the secret `AZURE_SPEECH_API_KEY`
- `keepLocalModelLoaded`: Keeps the shared llama-server loaded instead of unloading it after 5 idle minutes (#1207; default off). Synced to main with `sync-startup-preferences`, outside its policy gate; `LlamaServerManager.setKeepResident` acts only on a change, since every window resyncs on load
- `toneStyleEnabled`, `toneStyles`: whether Tone & Style is on (the setup dialog's Finish, or Settings' Tones & voices switch) and the tone picked per kind of app (JSON, normalized by `normalizeToneStyles`)
- `emailDraftTarget`: Where email drafts open (`auto`, `gmail`, `gmailSend`, `outlookWork`, `outlookPersonal`, `mailto`); `gmailSend` sends through the Gmail connector's approval card ("Send from chat (Gmail)"); an unknown value reads as `auto`
- `panelStartPosition`: Where the floating dictation icon rests (`top-left`, `top-center`, `top-right`, `middle-left`, `middle-center`, `middle-right`, `bottom-left`, `center` = bottom-center, `bottom-right`); an unknown value reads as `bottom-right`
- `floatingIconDisplay` / `floatingIconDisplayLabel`: Which display shows it (`cursor`, `primary`, or an Electron display id plus that monitor's name). The dictation window reports both to main once at load, since main boots the window from the `.env` copies
- `recordingIndicatorStyle` / `recordingIndicatorColor`: How the floating icon looks while recording (`classic`, `tinted`, `solid`, `beacon`, `rec`; a curated color id or `#rrggbb`, default `red`); an unknown value reads as `classic` / `red`. Renderer-only: every window hosting a pill (the dictation window, and the agent window for its takes) follows changes through `useRecordingIndicator`, which listens for storage events itself because the agent window never runs `initializeSettings`
- `recordingEdgeStrip` / `recordingEdgeStripDisplays`: The glow at the screen edge while recording (`off`, `short`, `full`, `frame`; `pill` or `all` displays — a frame is always on the pill's display), in the recording color; an unknown value reads as `off` / `pill`
- `recordingTrayIndicator`: Badges the tray icon in the recording color and puts the recording time in its tooltip while recording (default off). The dictation window reports these three and the color to main whenever one changes, since storage events update its store without running the setters
- `recordingShowElapsed`: Adds the elapsed time after the waveform for any style (default off; REC always shows it). The floating pill then uses a wider footprint (`resolveRecordingLayout` / `resolvePillFootprint` in `voicePillPresentation.js`: 124px for REC, 136px for elapsed, both fitting the 184px usable window width with the hover cancel); pills inside a panel keep today's row
- `recordingColorByMode` / `recordingAssistantColor` / `recordingTranslationColor`: Color voice assistant and translation recordings in their own colors (default off; `purple` / `cyan`, same values as `recordingIndicatorColor`); the assistant's color shows on the agent window's pill while a take records
- `recordingReminderMinutes`: "Remind me while recording" (`0` = off, or 2, 3, 5, 10 minutes); an unknown value reads as off
- `recordingQuality`: How new dictations are recorded (`smaller` 32 kbps, `balanced` 64 kbps, `original` as before, the default; an unknown value reads as `original`), read by the audio manager when a recording session starts (section 2)
- `audioRetentionDays` / `transcriptRetentionDays`: Keep recordings for (0 = Off, 1, 7, 14, 30, 60, 90, 180, 365; capped by an organization's `audioRetentionMaxDays`) and Keep transcripts for (0 = Forever, the default, then 365 down to 1), separate lists in `utils/retentionPeriods.js`; a recording is never kept longer than its transcript (`effectiveRecordingRetentionDays` in the UI, `recordingSweepDays` in main's sweep)

Secret env vars (BYOK and per-scope custom API keys, Corti client credentials, enterprise cloud creds and the GitHub Copilot sign-in token — see `SECRET_KEYS` in `environment.js`) are encrypted at rest via Electron `safeStorage` and stored as per-key files under `userData/secure-keys/`. They are loaded into `process.env` at startup by `EnvironmentManager.init()`. Renderer reads them via IPC (`get-*-key`) and writes via debounced IPC (`save-*-key`), except `GITHUB_COPILOT_TOKEN`, which never leaves main. On Linux without a keyring, secrets fall back to plaintext.

Non-secret env vars persisted to `.env` (via `saveAllKeysToEnvFile()`):

- `LOCAL_TRANSCRIPTION_PROVIDER`: Transcription engine (`nvidia` for Parakeet)
- `PARAKEET_MODEL`: Selected Parakeet model name (e.g., `parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3`)
- `PANEL_START_POSITION`, `FLOATING_ICON_DISPLAY`, `FLOATING_ICON_DISPLAY_LABEL`: Copies of the floating icon placement settings, read before any window exists to place the dictation window at boot
- `OPENWHISPR_CLOUD_DISABLED`: The "OpenWhispr account & Cloud" switch (`true` = off). The only copy: main owns it (see `openWhisprCloudGate.js`)
- `GITHUB_COPILOT_LOGIN`: The GitHub login signed in to Copilot (its token is the secret `GITHUB_COPILOT_TOKEN`)
- `DICTATION_STYLE_KEYS`: The style hotkeys (13g), a JSON object of hotkey → style id (`#` escaped as `\u0023`, since unquoted values end at `#`). Main owns it and reads it at boot, before any window exists
- `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL`: The log level set on Storage › Logs: `off`, `warn` (Problems only) or `debug` (Everything); unset or `info` reads as Problems only (see section 11). The three log keys are written by Storage › Logs only (`set-log-settings`, `logIpc.js`) and re-written with every other `.env` save, so a settings change elsewhere never drops them (upstream #2585's guarantee); a failed write puts the previous values back
- `OPENWHISPR_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`: How many days logs are kept (7, 14, 30 or 90; unset: 7 at Problems only, 30 at Everything)
- `OPENWHISPR_LOG_TEXT`: "Include dictated text" (`true` = on; off by default)

### 6. Language Support

60 languages supported, defined in `src/config/languageRegistry.json` and read through
`src/utils/languageSupport.ts`:

- Each entry has a two-letter code, label, flag, and per-engine support flags
  (`whisper`, `parakeet`, `assemblyai`) — 59 Whisper, 26 Parakeet, 7 AssemblyAI
- "auto" is a 61st entry for automatic detection
- Passed to whisper.cpp via -l parameter

### 7. Agent Naming System

- User names their agent during onboarding (step 6/8)
- Name stored in localStorage and database
- ReasoningService detects "Hey [AgentName]" patterns
- Standalone wake-word commands go to the agent window (the address is stripped first, `stripAgentAddress`); a highlighted selection is edited in place by the dictation agent
- Supports multiple AI providers (all models defined in `src/models/modelRegistryData.json`, plus this fork's in `src/models/forkCatalogProviders.js`):
  - **OpenAI** (Responses API):
    - GPT-6 Astra (`gpt-6-astra`) - Most capable OpenAI model for coding, computer use, and research, 1M context
    - GPT-6.1 Sol (`gpt-6.1-sol`) - Near-Astra capability at lower cost, 1M context
    - GPT-6 Luna (`gpt-6-luna`) - Fastest, lowest-cost GPT-6, 1M context
    - GPT-5.6 Series (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`, `gpt-5.6-luna`) - Flagship, balanced, and fastest tiers
    - GPT-5.5 (`gpt-5.5`) - Frontier model for complex reasoning, 1M context
    - GPT-5.2 (`gpt-5.2`) - Strong reasoning model
    - GPT-5 Mini (`gpt-5-mini`), GPT-5 Nano (`gpt-5-nano`), GPT-4.1 and GPT-4.1 Mini (`gpt-4.1`, `gpt-4.1-mini`) - legacy. GPT-4.1 Nano was shut down on 2026-10-23; `retiredCloudModels.ts` moves it to GPT-6 Luna
    - GPT-5 and newer reject `temperature`, so their registry entries carry `supportsTemperature: false`
  - **Anthropic** (Via IPC bridge to avoid CORS):
    - Claude Fable 5.1 (`claude-fable-5-1`) - Most capable Claude model, Mythos-class, 1M context
    - Claude Fable 5 (`claude-fable-5`) - Previous Mythos-class flagship, 1M context
    - Claude Opus 5.5 (`claude-opus-5-5`) - Latest Opus model for complex reasoning, 1M context
    - Claude Sonnet 5.5 (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) - Latest Sonnet: fast and capable for everyday work, 1M context
    - Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) - Fastest, lowest-cost Claude model, 1M context
    - Claude Sonnet 5 (`claude-sonnet-5`) - Fast, capable agentic model at lower cost
    - Claude Sonnet 4.6 (`claude-sonnet-4-6`) - Balanced performance
    - Claude Haiku 4.5 (`claude-haiku-4-5`) - Fast with near-frontier intelligence
    - Claude Opus 5 (`claude-opus-5`) - Previous Opus flagship, 1M context
    - Claude Opus 4.8 (`claude-opus-4-8`) - Powerful Opus model tuned for honesty and reliability, 1M context
    - Claude Opus 4.7 (`claude-opus-4-7`) - Powerful Opus model, 1M context
    - Claude Opus 4.6 (`claude-opus-4-6`) - Previous Opus generation, 1M context
    - Claude Sonnet 4.5 (`claude-sonnet-4-5`) - Previous Sonnet generation (legacy)
    - Claude Opus 4.5 (`claude-opus-4-5`) - Earlier Opus model
    - Models from Opus 4.7 onward (including the 5.5 generation) reject `temperature` (`supportsTemperature: false`); the Anthropic IPC bridge sends no thinking parameters, so no Anthropic entry carries `supportsThinking`
  - **Google Gemini** (Direct API integration):
    - Gemini 3.6 Flash (`gemini-3.6-flash`) - Fast, high-capability Gemini model
    - Gemini 3.8 Flash (`gemini-3.8-flash`) - Most capable Flash model
    - Gemini 4 Argon (`gemini-4-argon`) - Google's frontier reasoning model
    - Gemini 3.5 Flash (`gemini-3.5-flash`) - legacy; Google now routes it to 3.6 Flash
    - Gemini 3.5 Flash Lite (`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`) - Fastest, most cost-effective 3.5 model
    - Gemini 3.1 Pro (`gemini-3.1-pro-preview`) - Next-gen flagship model for complex reasoning
    - 3.8 Flash, 4 Argon and 3.1 Pro reject the `minimal` thinking level, so suppressed thinking sends `low` (`gemini-no-minimal` in `modelFamilyConstraints.ts`, plus the native provider's per-model table)
    - Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite (`gemini-3.1-flash-lite`) - Frontier-class performance at low cost. It thinks, and requests that aren't streamed pin it to `minimal` unless a reasoning effort is picked; it has no `supportsThinking` flag, so Settings shows the effort picker rather than the thinking toggle
    - Gemini 3 Flash (`gemini-3-flash-preview`) - Ultra-fast, high-capability next-gen model
    - Gemini 2.5 Flash Lite (`gemini-2.5-flash-lite`) - Lowest latency and cost (retired for new API keys; kept for existing ones)
    - Gemma 4 (`gemma-4-31b-it`, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it`) - Google's open Gemma 4 models served through the Gemini API
  - **GitHub Copilot** (Copilot SDK runtime in main, see `copilot/`): the models on the signed-in user's Copilot plan, listed live; the `copilot` entry of `src/models/forkCatalogProviders.js` is the shipped fallback (Claude Haiku 5.5 `claude-haiku-5.5` default, `mai-code-2-flash`, `gpt-6-luna`, `claude-sonnet-5.5`). Cleanup, note formatting and translation only
  - **Local**: GGUF models via llama.cpp (Qwen, Llama, Mistral, GPT-OSS)

### 8. Model Registry Architecture

The desktop model catalog lives in the public **`OpenWhispr/model-registry`** repository. Add, retire or correct models there (its README and CONTRIBUTING explain the rules); merging publishes a signed catalog that installed apps pick up between releases. `src/models/modelRegistryData.json` is a vendored copy, not a file to edit: `npm run model-registry:verify` (part of `quality-check`) fails if it differs from what was synced. When a model also needs app code, add the data with `minAppVersion` first, then sync in the same desktop PR as the code:

```bash
npm run model-registry:sync -- --revision <commit on the registry's published branch>
```

(`--source <registry commit>` vendors an unpublished commit for review builds only; re-sync from its publication before release.) The mobile app keeps its own hand-updated copies (see below).

**Lifecycle:** `legacy: true` keeps a model working but hidden unless selected or (local) downloaded; `retired: true` with an optional `replacement` means it no longer works. Retired entries are stripped from `registryData` (what every picker and route reads) and kept in its retired index; `reconcileRetiredCloudModelSelections()` moves saved selections to the replacement (or the provider default) on startup and on every catalog update, with the retired-model toast, and `resolveRetiredCloudModel()` maps them at request time. Retired local models move the same way: Whisper and sherpa-onnx selections to the replacement or the engine's recommended model (`reconcileRetiredLocalSpeechSelections`), GGUF selections to a replacement on disk or to none (`reconcileRetiredLocalLlmSelections`), and Settings › Storage's retired models row removes their files (`retiredLocalModels.js`). Moves wait in localStorage (`retiredModelSwitchesQueue`) until the control panel has focus, since it is usually closed or in the tray when a catalog changes. While a catalog is pending (`isCatalogPending`, a cached catalog that can't be used yet or, with updates off, one newer than the bundle), an ID no catalog knows is left alone (`isAwaitingCatalog`): it may be one of that catalog's additions. Otherwise it is repointed to the provider's default with a notice. A verified publication no newer than the catalog in use clears pending and replaces the unusable cache. `registryDownloadTrust.js` checks downloads at runtime, since a stolen signing key skips registry CI: Hugging Face publishers must be on its list (copied from the registry's `trusted-publishers.json`), and a new or moved download must name its hash; a failing entry keeps its shipped copy or leaves. Every Parakeet archive gets the structural check (`validateModelArchive`), hashed or not. `npm run model-registry:release-check` runs in `release.yml` and fails unless the build pins a signed publication the registry still tests and lists under `compat/clients`. Every provider names `defaultModel`; list order is display only.

**Key files:**

- `src/models/modelRegistryData.json`, `registrySchema.cjs`, `registryEnvelope.cjs`, `registrySelection.js`, `registryMetadata.json` (and `registryBundle.json` once published) - vendored from the registry; never edit by hand
- `src/helpers/registryLoader.js` - main-process loader: applies signed publications through `acceptCatalog` (lenient), only when their `testedClientContracts` include this build's `clientContract`, never throws, keeps a backup cache, honours `OPENWHISPR_MODEL_REGISTRY_MODE=bundled` (Settings › Privacy & Data › Model list updates, shown with Cloud off too, since the catalog comes from GitHub). It starts from the bundled catalog and keeps every provider the bundle has (`acceptCatalog` maps over the bundle's providers)
- `src/models/forkCatalogProviders.js` - this fork's providers that no registry publication lists: GitHub Copilot (`FORK_CLOUD_PROVIDERS`, its shipped list; the signed-in plan's live list is an overlay, `setCopilotModelOverlay` in `cloudModelCatalog.ts`) and Azure Speech's `MAI-Transcribe-2` (`FORK_TRANSCRIPTION_PROVIDERS`). `registryData.js` adds them to the bundled catalog (`withForkProviders`, after upstream's `withAppTranscriptionProviders`, which adds OpenRouter's speech-to-text the same way), so `modelRegistryData.json` stays the registry's copy and a signed catalog can't drop them; a catalog that lists one keeps its own entry
- `src/models/registryData.js` - the per-process catalog store (`getRegistryData`, `subscribeRegistry`, `getRetiredModel`); `src/models/modelLifecycle.js` - retired/unknown helpers
- `src/helpers/installedModels.js` - remembers each downloaded local model so it keeps working without its catalog entry
- `openwhispr-mobile/src/config/providerCatalog.json` - Mobile's trimmed copy of the OpenAI and Groq entries. It, mobile's endpoint rules and its cleanup prompt (`openwhispr-mobile/src/config/prompts/defaultPrompts.json`) are copies, not shared code, so update them by hand when desktop's change (the catalog is covered in `openwhispr-mobile/CONTRIBUTING.md`)
- `src/models/ModelRegistry.ts` - TypeScript wrapper with helper methods; also derives
  `REASONING_PROVIDERS` (`buildReasoningProviders()`), consumed by the model pickers
- `src/models/providerDefaultModel.ts` - `pickProviderDefaultModel()`: the provider's `defaultModel`, else the first model
- `src/models/legacyModels.ts` - `isListedModel()`: pickers list a legacy entry only while it is selected
  (muted, marked Legacy and disabled) or, for a local model, while its file is still downloaded so
  it can be deleted. Speech-to-text pickers don't read the flag yet, so no Whisper or Parakeet
  entry may carry it (`test/helpers/legacyModels.test.js`).
- `src/config/retiredCloudModels.ts` - the built-in retirement table, for models retired before the
  registry existed. Mobile keeps its own copy (`openwhispr-mobile/src/lib/retiredModels.ts`)
- `src/helpers/modelManagerBridge.js` - Handles local model downloads
- Adding or removing a local model also means updating `lib/local-model-catalog.ts` in openwhispr-api and openwhispr-admin, or restricted workspaces can't approve it (the API's `npm run catalog:check` reports drift against desktop `main` daily)

**Local model features:**

- Each model has `hfRepo` for direct HuggingFace download URLs
- Chat formatting comes from the GGUF's embedded template (llama-server runs with `--jinja`); the registry carries no prompt templates
- Download URLs constructed as: `{baseUrl}/{hfRepo}/resolve/{revision or main}/{fileName}`; registry-added models are pinned to a commit and carry a SHA-256 the download is checked against

### 9. API Integrations and Updates

**OpenAI Responses API (September 2025)**:

- Migrated from Chat Completions to new Responses API
- Endpoint: `https://api.openai.com/v1/responses`
- Simplified request format with `input` array instead of `messages`
- New response format with `output` array containing typed items
- Automatic handling of GPT-5 and o-series model requirements
- No temperature parameter for newer models (GPT-5, o-series)

**Anthropic Integration**:

- Routes through IPC handler to avoid CORS issues in renderer process
- Uses main process for API calls with proper error handling
- Model IDs use alias format (e.g., `claude-sonnet-4-6` not date-suffixed versions)

**Gemini Integration**:

- Direct API calls from renderer process
- Increased token limits for Gemini 3.1 Pro (2000 minimum)
- Proper handling of thinking process in responses
- Error handling for MAX_TOKENS finish reason

**API Key Persistence**:

- All API keys now properly persist to `.env` file
- Keys stored in environment variables and reloaded on app start
- Centralized `saveAllKeysToEnvFile()` method ensures consistency

**Azure Speech (MAI-Transcribe-2)**:

- A speech-to-text provider (`azure-speech`, catalog model `MAI-Transcribe-2` from `src/models/forkCatalogProviders.js`, also `resolveByokModel`'s fallback for any id not starting `MAI-Transcribe-`) through Azure Speech's fast transcription (`POST {resource}/speechtotext/transcriptions:transcribe?api-version=2025-10-15`, public preview). Pure parts in `helpers/azureSpeechTranscription.js`
- The endpoint: a Speech resource's `https://<name>.cognitiveservices.azure.com` (Keys and Endpoint), a regional `https://<region>.api.cognitive.microsoft.com`, or the same AIServices resource as Azure AI Foundry shows it, `https://<name>.services.ai.azure.com` (also a Foundry project's `…/api/projects/<project>`), which is converted to `https://<name>.cognitiveservices.azure.com`: Microsoft documents fast transcription only on that host (both route the speech path on the resource; the documented one gets the key). The field shows the converted address once left; live dictation, Re-transcribe and uploads build the documented URL even from a stored, unconverted value, and main refuses the key for the `services.ai` host. Tests: `azureSpeechTranscription.test.js` (the forms, their conversion, rejected paths, main's URL check, regional hosts unchanged), `audioManagerAzureSpeech.test.js`, `retryTranscriptionHandler.test.js` and `dictationTranscriptionRequest.test.js` (a stored `services.ai` origin and project URL through live dictation, Re-transcribe and uploads, each sent to the `cognitiveservices` transcribe URL), `aiConnectionsPage.test.js` (a pasted project URL shown converted after leaving the field), `settingsSearchRegistry.test.js` (the Foundry aliases)
- Its own route transport (`resolveTranscriptionRoute` → `{ transport: "azure-speech", endpoint, model, language, locale, sizeCapBytes }`), so no caller treats it as OpenAI-compatible; a missing or invalid `azureSpeechEndpoint` fails closed (`AZURE_SPEECH_ENDPOINT_INVALID`, "Add your Azure Speech endpoint…", with Open Settings), never falling through. `byokFileSizeLimit("azure-speech")` is 250 MB, the one cap the route, dictation's converted-WAV check and the upload gates read
- Offered on Recognition and File Transcription (not Live Recording: not a streaming model); policy treats it as any BYOK provider. Onboarding's key step doesn't offer it (`onboardingTranscriptionProviders`): it takes no endpoint
- Dictation, Re-transcribe and uploads: section 2; the connection: AI Connections; errors: `providerHttpErrors.js`; the phrase list: section 13

### 10. System Settings Integration

The app can open OS-level settings for microphone permissions, sound input selection, and accessibility:

**IPC Handlers** (in `ipcHandlers.js`):

- `open-microphone-settings`: Opens microphone privacy settings
- `open-sound-input-settings`: Opens sound/audio input device settings
- `open-accessibility-settings`: Opens accessibility privacy settings (macOS only)
- `open-login-items-settings`: Opens the login/startup items pane (macOS Login Items, Windows Startup Apps)

**Platform-specific URLs**:

| Platform | Microphone Privacy                                                           | Sound Input                                                  | Accessibility                                                                   | Login Items                                                         |
| -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| macOS    | `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_Microphone` | `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.sound?input` | `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.preference.security?Privacy_Accessibility` | `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.LoginItems-Settings.extension` |
| Windows  | `ms-settings:privacy-microphone`                                             | `ms-settings:sound`                                          | N/A                                                                             | `ms-settings:startupapps`                                           |
| Linux    | Manual (no URL scheme)                                                       | Manual (e.g., pavucontrol)                                   | N/A                                                                             | N/A (XDG autostart entry, see `linuxAutostart.js`)                  |

**UI Component** (`MicPermissionWarning.tsx`):

- Shows platform-appropriate buttons and messages
- Linux only shows "Open Sound Settings" (no separate privacy settings)
- macOS/Windows show both sound and privacy buttons

### 11. Debug Mode

Set on Settings › Storage › Logs (`debugLogger.js`, `logPolicy.js`, `logIpc.js`; persisted in `.env` through the environment manager's save, in `PERSISTED_KEYS`):

- **Levels**: Off, Problems only (warn and above) and Everything (debug), stored as `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` = `off` / `warn` / `debug`. Read without rewriting `.env`: `debug`/`trace` are Everything; unset, `info` and anything else are Problems only. **Behavior change**: packaged builds used to persist nothing with debug off (not even errors); unset and `info` now keep warnings and errors (Problems only, 7 days)
- **Files**: any level but Off writes `<userData>/logs/debug-<ISO>.log`, one per start, starting with a one-line `System Info` header written whatever the level. Off writes nothing new; the console, where it's on, keeps warnings and errors. Packaged Windows keeps logger output off stdout/stderr unless launched with `--console-logs`, whatever the level
- **Overrides**: a `--log-level=<level>` flag, then an `OPENWHISPR_LOG_LEVEL` inherited from the parent process (captured at the top of `main.js`, before any `.env` load, and taken out of `process.env`), win over the setting; any level works there (`info`, `error`, `off`, …), and a file is written at any level but `off`. `log-info` reports the level in force and `overriddenBy` (`flag` / `env`); Storage › Logs says so under What to log. `LOG_LEVEL` no longer counts
- **Retention**: Keep logs for 7 / 14 / 30 / 90 days (`OPENWHISPR_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS`; unset: 7 at Problems only and Off, 30 at Everything), then the oldest files until the folder fits in 100 MB; a file over 20 MB continues in a new one. Pruned at start, at each new file, every 6 hours and right after the retention shortens, also while Off; never the file being written; only `debug-*.log` files
- **No dictated text by default**: logs carry `logText(value)` (`utils/logText.js`, shared by main and renderer): `{ chars, words }`, plus `text` (at most 80 characters) only while "Include dictated text" is on (`OPENWHISPR_LOG_TEXT=true`, off by default; the renderer learns it with the level from `get-log-state` and the `log-settings-changed` broadcast). Never logged: transcripts, cleanup input or output, prompts (dictionary prompts are a word count), model replies, provider bodies that echo the request (the error's message and the body's size instead), clipboard, selected text, field values, meeting segments, calendar titles, raw helper output (`textEditMonitor` stdout is its line kinds and size; whisper-server and sherpa-onnx stdout its size) and `JSON.parse` messages (they quote the text). Pipeline lines carry the dictation's ids where the code has them (`clientTranscriptionId`, the step-history `runId`, the History id in "Dictation saved")
- **Text files**: while the switch is on, the current file is `debug-<ISO>-text.log`; turning it on or off starts a new file, a `-text` file continues in a new one once it's a day old, and `-text` files are deleted 3 days after they began, whatever the retention. Logs from before this boundary are treated as text-bearing once, at the first start of this version: older than 3 days deleted, the rest renamed `-text` (marker `.text-policy-1`; the count is in the first header)
- **Bounds**: an entry over 16 KB is cut with a note; past 1 MB queued, entries are dropped and counted in one line once the stream drains; a write error (disk full, permissions) turns file logging off for the session with one `console.error`; logging never throws into callers
- **IPC** (`logIpc.js`): `log-info` (setting, level in force, `overriddenBy`, retention, text switch, total size, file count, current file name, the folder with the user profile masked, the OS, the graphics mode as `graphics`, null before main knows it), `set-log-settings({ level?, retentionDays?, includeText? })` (offered values only, else rejected before anything is written; saved, applied live, broadcast as `log-settings-changed`; returns `log-info`), `clear-logs` (`{ deleted, failed: [names] }`), `log-recent-problems` (the last 50 warn/error/fatal entries from the newest files, read from their ends within 1 MB: time, level, area, first line ≤ 200 characters), `open-logs-folder`, `get-log-level`, `get-log-state`. `get-debug-state` and `set-debug-logging` are gone with `DeveloperSection.tsx`. Main broadcasts `graphics-mode-changed` with the new mode (`useLogInfo` patches it in)
- **Graphics mode** (`graphicsMode.js`): one "Graphics mode" line (scope `gpu`) once the GPU process has reported: `mode` (`hardware`/`software`/`unknown`), `reason` (`requested` by a launch flag, `requestedBy` naming it, or `fallback`), `gpuCompositing`, `rasterization`, `glRenderer`, `displayType` (Linux adds `disabledForNvidia`; it replaced the Linux-only "Linux GPU compositing" line). Info, except a software **fallback**, which warns, so it shows at Problems only; "GPU process gone" and "Graphics mode changed" follow a mid-session loss. The System Info header can't carry it: it's written before the GPU process starts. A fallback also shows the warning with Restart OpenWhispr in Settings › System › Diagnostics, and in the tray: an amber dot on the icon (not macOS; a recording's dot covers it while it shows), the warning as the tooltip, and at the top of the menu "⚠ Graphics acceleration is off" (opens that Diagnostics row) and "Restart to use the graphics card", held as "Restart after this recording" while a dictation or a meeting recording runs (`tray.js`; "Restarting via tray menu" logs the mode). To see the warning on a development copy on Windows: `node scripts/run-electron.js --dev --use-gl=desktop` (an unsupported GL backend: the GPU process fails and Chromium falls back to WARP, unlike `--disable-gpu` or `--use-angle=warp`, which count as requested)
- **Dictation start visuals** (`dictationStartVisuals.js`): one main-process line per dictation start (a hold, `startWindowsPushToTalk`; a toggle's start, `_sendDictationToggle`, which now stamps its own press trace, `keyTimeSource: "main"`, and sends it with prepare and the toggle, so the renderer's "Dictation start trace" carries the same `pressId`; the macOS Globe and compound holds have no press trace and no line). Fields, ms after the key: `pillShownAfterKeyMs` (the first frame the dictation window painted after main's `dictation-start-visuals-probe`, sent once the pill is on screen and the press's messages went out: a double requestAnimationFrame, since `backgroundThrottling: false` keeps `visibilitychange` from firing), `pillWasVisible`, `pillSettledAfterKeyMs` (the frame where the listening entrance reached `settled`, `useDictationStartVisuals`), `recordingAfterKeyMs` (main heard the recording start), `frameShownAfterKeyMs`, `frameWindow` (`new`/`reused`/`none`) and `strip`, `graphics`/`graphicsReason`, `outcome`, `missing`. Written when everything expected arrived, or after 3 s with what arrived; never delays the recording; dropped when the press was cancelled before anything showed. Warn "Slow dictation start visuals" (`slow`) when the pill took over 500 ms, the strip over 1000 ms, or graphics fell back. It is separate from "Dictation start trace" (renderer, logged at recording start, before the strip shows)
- **Frame pacing doesn't see compositor problems**: in a software fallback the renderer's own frames stay on time (requestAnimationFrame steady at 120 Hz, no long animation frames) while the compositor stutters, so renderer-side frame sampling can't detect it; the graphics mode does
- **Processes & resources** (`processInventory.js`, scope `resources`; roles, PIDs and numbers only, never exe names, paths, command lines, titles, hotkeys or helper output):
  - "Resource snapshot" (info, so Everything only): one compact JSON line after the first sample (a minute after start) and every 30 minutes, from the last sample (the run that feeds it reads graphics memory; it never runs on its own): `ageSec`, `source`, totals (`processes`, `wsMB`, `privMB`, `cpuPct` over `cpuIntervalSec`, `gpuMB` with `gpuAgeSec`), `gpuProcesses` (processes on the PC holding graphics memory), `graphics` (mode), `helper` (and `helperError`), and per process `{ role, pid, wsMB, privMB, cpuAvg, cpuSec, gpuMB, handles, gdi, user }`. Consecutive lines' `cpuSec` give any interval's CPU
  - "Resource warning" (warn): `kind` `gdiObjects` / `userObjects` (over 5,000 of Windows' 10,000 per process), `handleGrowth` (more than 50% and 200 handles over 6 hours), `totalMemory` (in-RAM sum over 2 GB); each at most once an hour per process
  - "Recording cost" (debug, one compact JSON line per dictation, once its graphics memory after delivery is read or skipped): `recordingMs`, `processingMs`, `strip`, `frameWindow` (`new`/`reused`/`none`), per-role CPU % while recording (`recordingCpu`) and while processing and delivering (`processingCpu`), `partial` roles, `gpuAfterDelivery` (`ok`/`unavailable`/`cancelled`/`skipped`/`pageClosed`) and `gpuAfterDeliveryMB` per role
  - Copy diagnostics ends with a Processes section at any level: the same allowlisted report as a table (role, PID, in RAM, committed, CPU now/avg/time, graphics, handles, GDI, USER, threads, running), the sums and the graphics card's process count; "not sampled yet" in the first minute

### 12. Windows Push-to-Talk

Native Windows support for true push-to-talk functionality using low-level keyboard hooks:

**Architecture**:

- `resources/windows-key-listener.c`: Native C program using Windows `SetWindowsHookEx` for keyboard hooks
- `src/helpers/windowsKeyManager.js`: Node.js wrapper that spawns and manages the native binary
- Binary outputs `KEY_DOWN` and `KEY_UP` to stdout when target key is pressed/released
- `src/helpers/nativeHotkeyDispatch.js` routes each key's events to its slot; a style hotkey (13g) is a dictation key whose press names its style

**Compound Hotkey Support**:

- Parses hotkey strings like `CommandOrControl+Shift+F11`
- Maps modifiers: `CommandOrControl`/`Ctrl` → VK_CONTROL, `Alt`/`Option` → VK_MENU, `Shift` → VK_SHIFT
- Verifies all required modifiers are held before emitting key events

**Binary Distribution**:

- Compiled from source by `scripts/build-windows-key-listener.js`, part of `compile:native`, so every `prebuild*`, `predev:main` and `prestart` chain on a Windows host builds it; neither script runs on other hosts
- Started without a console host: `main` calls `FreeConsole()` when stdout isn't a console (it's piped by `windowsKeyManager.js`), so no `conhost.exe` runs beside each listener; run by hand in a terminal it keeps the console and prints. Never spawned `detached`: as a non-detached child it sits in libuv's kill-on-close job and dies with OpenWhispr, even on a crash, taking its keyboard hook with it
- Fallback: prebuilt binary from GitHub releases (`windows-key-listener-v*` tags) via `scripts/download-windows-key-listener.js`
- Release CI (`release.yml`, `build-and-notarize.yml`) compiles it with MSVC during `build:win`
- CI workflow: `.github/workflows/build-windows-key-listener.yml` rebuilds the prebuilt binary when `resources/windows-key-listener.c` changes on main
- `afterPack.js` fails a Windows package without the binary. A dev run without it starts with Hold unavailable (a saved Hold is reset to Tap at startup), modifier-only hotkeys refused, and `F8` as the default hotkey (`hotkeyManager.js`)

**IPC Events**:

- `windows-key-listener:key-down`: Fired when hotkey pressed (start recording)
- `windows-key-listener:key-up`: Fired when hotkey released (stop recording)

### 13. Custom Dictionary

Improve transcription accuracy for specific words, names, or technical terms:

**How it works**:

- User adds words/phrases through Settings → Custom Dictionary, or from a History review's fix (Dictionary word…, confirmed in a dialog; see 13d)
- Words stored as JSON array in localStorage (`customDictionary` key)
- On transcription, words are joined and passed as `prompt` parameter to Whisper
- Works with both local whisper.cpp and cloud OpenAI Whisper API
- Local sherpa-onnx models with a measured hotword score (only Orukeet so far, `src/utils/sherpaHotwords.js`) get up to 100 dictionary words as sherpa hotwords instead, personal words first; other Parakeet and Cohere models ignore the dictionary. The personal words go in stored order (without those hidden in every team list), then teamspaces', then the workspace's; pins and the evidence order don't apply, and the Dictionary's coverage shows that list (`sherpaHotwordCoverage` in `dictionary/useDictionaryCoverage.ts`)
- OpenRouter's speech-to-text drops `prompt`, so a request to its host (its own provider, or a custom endpoint there) carries no dictionary, script bias included, and runs no echo check (`dictionaryReachesTranscriptionModel` in `utils/dictionaryPromptCap.js`; `resolveHintTarget` and `sttHintFields.js` answer `none`). The Dictionary says so for OpenRouter and for local models, in "Where it goes" on the personal view and under the list on lists with teammates' words (`dictionary/speechDictionaryNotice.ts`)

**Implementation**:

- `src/hooks/useSettings.ts`: Manages `customDictionary` state
- `src/components/SettingsPage.tsx`: UI for adding/removing dictionary words
- `src/helpers/audioManager.js`: Reads dictionary and adds to transcription options
- `src/helpers/whisperServer.js`: Includes dictionary as `prompt` in API request

**Whisper Prompt Parameter**:

- Whisper uses the prompt as context/hints for transcription
- Words in the prompt are more likely to be recognized correctly
- Useful for: uncommon names, technical jargon, brand names, domain-specific terms

**One hint-list builder** (`src/utils/dictionaryHints.ts`, `buildDictionaryHints` + `resolveHintTarget`):

- Selects by priority within the provider's request budget: **pinned** words first (the Dictionary row's ☆, "always send first"; main's `dictionary_pins`, mirrored by `stores/dictionarySourcesStore.ts`, `pinnedWords` keyed by `dictionaryWordKey`, 13h) **in pin order** (`dictionary_pins.position`, which the Dictionary page's "Always sent first" sets; the store's `pinned` Set iterates in it; a pinned key without a live word is skipped), then typed (`manual`) words, then the glossary's canonical forms, then snippet triggers, then auto-learned words (`custom_dictionary.source`, mirrored by the same store). A pinned word leads whatever its source; `audioManager.getDictionaryHints` passes the pins to every live request.
- **Evidence order** (`dictionaryHintOrder`, section 5; `utils/dictionaryHintOrder.ts`; builder option `rankBy`, default `evidence`, with `scores: Map<dictionaryWordKey, { missedRows, written }>`): within the typed and auto-learned tiers only, words go by `missedRows` desc, then `written` desc, then stored order; a word with no score or 0/0 keeps its stored order after the scored ones. `added`, or no evidence among the scores, keeps stored order (the old output, byte for byte). The tiers never change places. The scores are the usage store's counts (13h, only while it has them): `hintScoreTuples` keeps words with evidence, best first, keys ≤ 100 characters, counts clamped to 1,000,000, ≤ 2,000; `hintScoresForDictionary` keeps the dictionary's own words, at most as many as it has; live, Re-transcribe and the page all go through these and `normalizeHintScores`, so they rank by the same numbers. The plan says whether evidence applied (`evidence`). Order only decides which words fit the cap (Azure Speech's phrase list has no per-phrase weight; a Whisper prompt is read from its tail).
- Whisper-family decoders keep only the prompt's **tail** (~223 tokens, `WHISPER_DECODER_PROMPT_CHARS`), so "tail" order emits the highest priority last: the tiers are reversed, and so are the entries of each **ranked** tier (the pins always; the typed and learned tiers when evidence order applies), so the top pin is the prompt's last word and several pins that overflow the window keep the top ones inside it. Unranked tiers (the glossary, snippets, a tier in stored order) are emitted as before. The budget still picks highest priority first, and `entries` (the coverage ranks) stay highest first. Keyword channels, Mistral's `contextBias` and the gpt-4o transcribers get "head" order.
- Azure Speech gets the `phrases` channel (`resolveHintTarget` for provider `azure-speech`): its `phraseList`, "head" order, counted in entries (`maxTerms` 199; 200 is rejected) rather than characters, every sent entry read. A phrase list is a bias, not a prompt the model continues, so that request has no dictionary-echo check.
- The request, the dictionary-echo check (`isDictionaryEcho(text, sent)` compares against what was actually sent) and the Dictionary page's coverage ("sent, likely read / likely not read / not sent") all read the same output: the page maps the current settings to Re-transcribe's hint route (`utils/sttHintSpec.ts`) and reads `buildSttHintFields`' `entries` for it (13h), with the same pins in pin order, the order setting and the scores (`useDictionaryCoverage`), so with 205 entries on Azure Speech it shows which 6 aren't sent, and Mistral's word cap and Gemini's and xAI's term cap cut where the request does. Never reorder the stored dictionary by deleting and re-adding words: it syncs to OpenWhispr Cloud.
- **Teammates' words** (upstream's shared team dictionary, #2855; `settings.sharedDictionaryHints`, tiers from `getDictionaryHintTiers` in `utils/snippets.ts`): live dictation adds them after the user's own plan as its lowest priority (`withSharedHints` in `audioManager.js`: space words, then workspace words, only in what the user's own words leave of the budget and never when that list was already cut; at most `MAX_SHARED_KEYTERMS` (100) words, a Whisper-style prompt only up to the decoder window (`sharedDictionaryPromptChars`), Cloud's Gemini route ≤ 100 terms (`PROMPT_HEAD_TERMS`), the keyterm caps per channel, Azure Speech's phrase list ≤ 100 of them). A tail prompt opens with them, a head-order list ends with them; the echo check compares against what was sent (`isDictionaryEcho(text, sent, personalPrompt)`), and upstream's strip of a dictionary list recited after the speech (#2586) runs on Cloud, proxied and BYOK, glossary spellings counting as dictionary terms. A moved personal word whose every shared copy the member hid (`suppressedPersonal`) is left out. Re-transcribe (`sttHintFields`) and the Dictionary page's coverage still send and show only the user's own words. `trimDictionaryPrompt` (`utils/dictionaryPromptCap.js`) stays for the fork's ordered hint strings beside upstream's `formatDictionaryPrompt` / `selectDictionaryPromptTiers`.
- **Re-transcribe sends it too** (`retry-transcription`): `ControlPanel.retryTranscription` sends `settings.dictionary` (`{ words, pinned (in pin order), glossaryTerms, snippets: [{ trigger }], chineseScriptPreference, hintOrder, scores: [[key, missedRows, written], …] }`) and `speechLanguage` (a translation row's source language, else the preferred language); `normalizeHintDictionary` validates it (`hintOrder` evidence unless `added`; `scores` through `normalizeHintScores`: at most as many as the words and 2,000, read in order, keys of 1–100 characters, two integers 0–1,000,000, a repeated key or 0/0 dropped, anything else dropped; an older window without them gets stored order) and `plan()` passes them on; main adds the learned words itself (`_learnedDictionaryWords`, from `custom_dictionary`) and builds each route's wire fields with `utils/sttHintFields.js` (`buildSttHintFields`, the same `buildDictionaryHints` / `resolveHintTarget` live dictation uses; its `ranking` says the order, whether evidence applied and how many scores came, for the log): OpenAI-compatible and self-hosted `prompt` and/or repeated `keywords[]` (no prompt for `orukeet-v0.1.0`), Azure Speech `phrases` (≤ 199, head order, no echo check), Mistral repeated `context_bias` (the script-biased head-order prompt's first 100 whitespace words), Gemini `keyterms` and xAI repeated `keyterm` (≤ 100 terms of ≤ 50 characters, no snippet triggers), Tinfoil `prompt`, managed Azure `prompt` (the executor's option), OpenWhispr Cloud `prompt` on the inline upload and every chunk, local Whisper `initialPrompt` (with live's echo recovery, `utils/localWhisperEchoRecovery.js`); Corti and sherpa nothing. A result that echoes what was sent (`isHintEcho`) fails as `DICTIONARY_ECHO` ("No audio detected") and writes nothing. A window without the field (older) sends nothing. Logs carry counts per channel only. Tests: `test/helpers/sttHintParity.test.js` (the live audioManager's request and the retry's, per route, byte for byte, plus a privacy canary; pin order and evidence order on head and tail routes, "as added", and 50 pins under Groq's cap, each against the page's coverage), `test/utils/sttHintFields.test.js`, `test/utils/dictionaryHints.test.js` (also "as added" against the old builder byte for byte, evidence ties and 0/0, tail emission within tiers, pins overflowing the window), `test/utils/dictionaryHintOrder.test.js` (the scores' bounds and validation)

### 13b. Glossary Corrections and the Post-Speech-to-Text Boundary

- **Rules** live in main's local-only `glossary_rules` table (history DB, `glossary_meta` holds the set revision and the one-time seed flag), never synced. `src/helpers/glossaryRules.js` validates and seeds; IPC `glossary-get-rules` / `glossary-save-rule` / `glossary-delete-rule`, broadcast `glossary-rules-updated`; the renderer mirrors them in `stores/glossaryStore.ts`. Editor: Dictionary page → Glossary tab (`GlossaryView.tsx`; the rule dialog is `GlossaryRuleDialog.tsx`, also opened by a History review's fixes, prefilled from selected words; see 13d).
- **Matching** (`src/utils/glossary.ts`, pure): `anywhere` spellings everywhere; `start` spellings only as the dictation's first word(s), optionally after a kept greeting, and only when the recording's target app (Windows `exeName`, case-insensitive) is on the rule's app list. Unknown app ⇒ `anywhere` only. Snippet trigger spans win. Only canonical forms join hint lists.
- **Target app**: `capture-dictation-target` (called from `performStartRecording` for Hold and Tap) freezes the start probe in `selectionManager.freezeRecordingTarget()`; the renderer fetches it once via `get-dictation-target-app` (`audioManager.beginRecordingTargetCapture`). `lastTarget` stays the paste path's.
- **Boundary** (`src/helpers/postTranscription.ts`, `runPostTranscription`): exactly once per ordinary dictation (batch incl. the no-model early return, Cloud, streaming, streaming→batch fallback, retry from history and Re-run cleanup, translation's source side); never meetings, note uploads, the CLI's raw transcribe or inside STT engines. Intent and routing read the raw transcript; models get the glossary-corrected working text; a failed model call keeps the corrected text; Chinese script last. Returns `{ text, trace }` (contract in the file header); the trace holds dictated text, so logs carry only rule ids and counts (`summarizeGlossaryTrace`). Retry from history and Re-run cleanup pass the dictation's captured target app, read from its stored steps' context (`services/historyRerun.ts`, `targetAppFromSteps`), so app-scoped rules apply as they did when it was dictated; a row without steps is an unknown app.
- **Auto-learn** never learns a glossary word (`_withoutGlossaryWords` in `ipcHandlers.js`), and Dictionary › Tidy up never suggests removing or merging away a glossary spelling (13h).
- **Cleanup reply check** (`utils/cleanupOutput.ts`, built-in cleanup prompt only): `finishCleanupOutput(input, reply)` is what every dictation cleanup path pastes (`ReasoningService.processText`, the three Cloud cleanup paths in `audioManager.js`, styled replies). A reply that repeats the transcript (≥ 85% shared words), then a "Cleaned:"-style label (cleaned, corrected, edited or final, optionally "version" / "transcript", in bold or as a heading, on its own line or leading the text), then the cleaned text keeps only the cleaned text; with nothing after the label it's rejected as `CLEANUP_OUTPUT_INVALID` (`cleanupDuplicated`). Everything else goes through `assertValidCleanupOutput` (a transcript said twice) unchanged.

### 13c. Prompt Library and the Cleanup Override

- **Authority**: `promptLibrary.<kind>` (kinds in `config/prompts/registry.ts`: `cleanup`, `dictationAgent`, `translate`, `chatAgent`) is one versioned record per kind, `{ version, activeId, entries: [{ id, name, text, updatedAt, origin }] }`; `activeId` is an entry id or `"default"` (the built-in prompt, never an entry, so it follows the UI language and app updates). Pure logic in `src/config/prompts/library.ts`.
- **Cache**: `customPrompt.<kind>` is written from the record in the same step (active entry's text, `""` for Default), so every reader (`resolvePrompt`, Cloud `customPrompt`, the cleanup validator gate, selection edits) is unchanged. Never write `customPrompt.*` directly, and never remove either key (the storage listener ignores removals): change prompts only through the store's `createPrompt` / `savePrompt` / `renamePrompt` / `deletePrompt` / `activatePrompt`, each one `updatePromptLibrary` read-modify-write that re-reads the record inside the write. Other windows refresh from the record's `storage` event (`applyPromptStorageChange`).
- **Migration** (startup, idempotent): a kind without a record gets one from its `customPrompt.<kind>` (non-empty ⇒ entry `migrated`, "My prompt", origin `migrated`, in use; empty ⇒ Default). The retired-default sweep (`sweepRetiredPromptEntries`) removes only `migrated` entries that byte-match a retired default; `user` entries are never deleted.
- **UI**: `usePromptLibraryEditor` (selection + local draft) and `PromptLibraryBar` (picker, actions, dialogs, "Use this prompt"), used by Prompt Studio and the Chat prompt editor. Drafts are never saved until Save / Save as new.
- **Cleanup override**: `ReasoningConfig.cleanupPrompt` (`inherit` | `builtin` | `text`) runs a candidate as a real cleanup request without touching settings; `processText` resolves it once into the request's provider context (`getSystemPrompt`, `getCleanupCustomPrompt`, `callChatCompletionsApi`) and strips it before any IPC. `services/cleanupWithPrompt.ts` (`runCleanupWithPrompt`, `captureCleanupPrompt`, `buildDictationCleanupRequest`) sends exactly what a dictation would (`helpers/dictationCleanupConfig.ts` is the dictation route's own cleanup config). Never pass a candidate cleanup prompt as `systemPrompt`: that switches off the wrapper, validation, cleanup sampling and Cloud prompt routing. Translation and assistant tests pass `resolvePromptText(kind, candidate, opts)` as their explicit system prompt.
- **Fixed prompts outside the library**: Tidy up's dictionary review (`config/prompts/dictionaryReview.ts`, 13h) and "Sort into categories"' category review (`config/prompts/dictionaryCategoryReview.ts`, 13h) are English, not library kinds and not editable; each goes as an explicit `systemPrompt` on the chosen model's scope, so Copilot serves it on the cleanup scope.
- **The grouped dictionary** (cleanup's "Group dictionary words by category", 13h): the request's one snapshot `ReasoningConfig.cleanupDictionary` (`{ words, scope, groups? }`, built once by `buildDictationCleanupConfig` / `selectCleanupDictionary`) or a test run's frozen `promptInputs` (`CleanupPromptInputs.groups`) reaches `processText`, which builds the grouped suffix inside the request's provider context (`getSystemPrompt` → `getCleanupSystemPrompt(…, groups)` → `appendDictionarySuffix(prompt, words, uiLanguage, groups)`: the unchanged `dictionarySuffix` sentence, then `People: …; Work terms: …; Everyday words: …; Other: …` with the labels `dictionaryGroupPeople` / `-Work` / `-Everyday` / `-Other` of the `prompts` namespace, empty groups left out) and strips the snapshot before any IPC; OpenWhispr Cloud's `customDictionary` is always the flat `words` (`ctx.getCustomDictionary`). Never a `systemPrompt` override. Without `groups` the prompt is byte for byte the flat one, so the default prompts and `CURRENT_DEFAULT_PROMPT_HASHES` are unchanged (the hashes cover `cleanupPrompt`, `fullPrompt` and `translatePrompt`, not the suffixes).

### 13d. Step History

- **What**: every stage of every dictation (`stt`, `glossary`, `cleanup`, `translation`, `agent`, `style`, `finalize`; `voice`, `edited` reserved) in main's local-only `transcription_steps` table (history DB), plus each resolved system prompt once in `prompt_versions` (keyed by sha256). Source: the boundary's trace (`postTranscription.ts`), which rides on the existing `db-save-transcription`; main writes the steps in the row's transaction, inside a savepoint so a failed step write never costs the dictation. Never synced, sent to the cloud or indexed, and never logged (log reasons and counts only).
- **Prompt reference**: `ReasoningConfig.onDispatch` also reports `prompt`: `{ source: "resolved", text, entryId }` is the system prompt exactly as sent (`providerContextFor` builds it once per request and every provider, including `callChatCompletionsApi`, reads that copy); OpenWhispr Cloud reports `{ source: "server" }` (it builds the prompt on the server; never reconstructed). `entryId` is the cleanup library entry in use (`activeEntryIdFor`), `"default"` for built-in.
- **Ownership** (`helpers/stepHistory.js`, `createStepHistory`): a trace belongs to `account:<id>` (the account binding connectors use, `accountScopeBinding`) or `signed-out`. The run begins when the recording starts (`step-history-begin-run`, `audioManager.beginStepHistoryRun`; retry: `retry-transcription` returns `stepRunId`) and its id travels as `trace.runId`. Main drops the steps if the owner changed, the run is unknown or already used, or the effective "keep local history" setting (`_retentionSettings`, off until synced) is off. Reads (`history-get-steps`) and the export are owner-scoped. History rows follow upstream's account rule (`account_id`, section 4): a row is the account's that spoke it, and History shows the account in view's rows plus unattributed ones; the owner's `<id>` is that same account id (`account:<id>` from `accountScopeBinding`, `ownerKeyFor`).
- **Lifecycle**: physical deletes (unsynced delete, expiry, clear-all, cloud deletion pulls via `hardDeleteTranscription`) cascade (`ON DELETE CASCADE`; `foreign_keys = ON` is restated after migrations). Tombstones (`deleted_at`) delete the row's steps explicitly in the same transaction (`_deleteTranscriptionSteps`). Clear history, expiry and single deletes touch only the rows in view (section 4), so only those rows' steps go. `deleteAccountData` deletes the account's rows (their steps cascade) and that owner's steps of rows that stay (unattributed ones on a shared device). A trigger deletes a prompt version when its last step goes, in any account. Text rewritten without a trace (`updateTranscriptionText`, a changed cloud copy) drops stale steps.
- **Retry**: `update-transcription-text` with `{ trace }` replaces the row's text and steps together (`completeRetry`); each retry bumps the row's in-memory run revision, so an overtaken completion returns `{ stale: true }` and writes nothing. Re-transcribe's own writes in main (`retry-transcription`: the new transcript, status, engine) go through `stepHistory.saveRetranscription`, guarded by the same revision (`currentRunCheck`, taken right after `beginRun`): once Re-run cleanup (or another Re-transcribe) has begun on the row, the overtaken one writes nothing and replies `{ stale: true }`, which the renderer leaves to the newer run. The dictation error card's re-send of a failed recording (section 2) begins its run on the failed row and completes it with `complete-failed-dictation-retry` (`completeFailedDictation`: `completeRetry`, then status `completed` and the engine; a row gone or tombstoned is `NOT_FOUND`, one completed meanwhile is `stale`).
- **Re-run cleanup** (History detail beside Re-transcribe, a failed step's card, Home's row menu; `ControlPanel.rerunCleanup` via `retryFromHistory` with `cleanupOnly`): everything after speech-to-text, with today's settings, on the row's stored `raw_text`; offered on a completed dictation with a raw transcript that wasn't an assistant command (trace route `agent` or the voice-agent intent), with or without its recording. It begins its run on the row first (`step-history-begin-run` with `{ transcriptionId }`), then reads the row and its steps, so an older Re-transcribe still running writes nothing after that read. `services/historyRerun.ts` (`rerunAfterSpeechToText`) is the one post-speech-to-text half Re-transcribe uses too: the boundary with the captured target app, the row's style (even switched off; a deleted one asks first and runs as Plain), translation chain or cleanup, then the snippet expansion a live dictation does (`expandSnippets` + `recordPastedText`), never a paste, then the `completeRetry` completion. Speech-to-text and its preflight never run: the trace's `stt` step is the stored one carried over (provider, model, the raw text) with `detail.reused: "stored"`, or `"unknown"` when the dictation had none; View steps and the History detail say "Reused" ("origin unknown").
- **Why a step failed**: the boundary's step recorder adds `detail.failure` to a failed step (`helpers/stepFailure.js`, `failureDescriptor`): the error's `code` when it's a plain identifier (else `"unknown"`), a `messageKey` from an allowlist of what error cards render (`FAILURE_MESSAGE_KEYS`: the provider errors', Copilot's, the cleanup-reply keys, Cloud's sign-in and the parameter-free Bedrock sentences), `messageParams` limited to `provider` and `model` (short strings) and a `settingsTarget` `isProviderSettingsTarget` accepts. The message, the provider's body, technical details and other params never enter the trace. Main's `normalizeTrace` re-validates it with the same allowlist and keeps it only on `failed` and `fallback` steps, and `detail.reused` only on `stt` steps. Steps stored before this show their code alone.
- **UI**: History row menu → View steps (`TranscriptionSteps.tsx`; "Steps not captured" for older or pulled rows; step names in `ui/transcriptionStepLabels.ts`, shared with Settings → Dictation → Overview's "Last dictation" card). History header → Export: main's warning and Save dialog (`history-export-dictations`), ownership re-checked after the dialog, JSON Lines (`export` header, `prompt` lines, one `dictation` line per completed row in view with this owner's steps). Snippets expand after the boundary, so `recordPastedText` ends the saved trace with what was pasted.
- **History view** (sidebar "History" after Home in `controlPanelNav.ts`, always available; `components/history/`: `HistoryBrowser` lays out `HistoryList` and `HistoryDetail` side by side from 900 px of view, below that the detail opens over the list with Back; side by side it also decides the detail's layout from the view's width, the list's state first: the list (380 px) folds to a 44 px rail with an expand button while a 👎 draft is open on a dictation the wide layout suits, when that leaves the detail 880 px, unless the user chose (the list header's collapse button, the rail's expand button; `listChoice`, kept in localStorage: expanding during a review keeps it shown for reviews, expanding otherwise goes back to folding by itself); then the detail is wide when it has 880 px beside the list as it is, so the detail's width never feeds back into the list's state; what it shows lives in `historyViewStore.ts`, so it survives switching views): main pages it (`helpers/historyBrowser.js` over `database.js`'s `getHistoryPage`, `getHistoryStepRows`, `getHistoryApps`, `getHistoryItem`). IPC `history-list` (`{ cursor, limit, query, filters }` → `{ rows, nextCursor }`: completed and failed rows, never discarded or deleted, ordered `COALESCE(timestamp, '') DESC, id DESC` with the cursor `{ timestamp, id }` compared by the same expression, since timestamps come as `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` or `…SS.SSSZ`; limit 1–100, 50 by default, a malformed cursor refused; lightweight rows with a 240-character preview cut in SQL; search is a parameterized `LIKE` on final and raw text with the user's `%`, `_` and `\` escaped and case folded by a JS SQL function, since SQLite folds ASCII only; filters `failed`, `cleanupChanged`, `app`, unknown ones ignored; `failed` is a failed row or one whose current owner's cleanup, style or translation step failed or fell back, one owner-scoped `EXISTS` on the steps' `(owner, transcription_id)` index (`HISTORY_STEP_FAILED`; a skipped or unchanged translation isn't one)), `history-apps` (the App filter's choices, most used first) and `history-get-item` (the full row plus `stepHistory.getSteps`; `NOT_FOUND` once deleted). Rows are the ones the account rule shows (`_historyScope()`, which is `_ownedRowScope("transcriptions")`: the account in view's own plus unattributed ones; signed out on a device only one account has used, that account stays in view), for the list, `history-get-item`, the App filter, the review hotkey's last dictation (`getLastReviewableDictation`), `historyRevision` and the dictionary filters and badges alike; the list reloads and the selection clears on `database-account-scope-changed`. Everything taken from steps (a row's `app` and `marks`, what each step after speech-to-text did: `changed`, `same` or its outcome; its `stepFailure` badge, "Cleanup failed", "Style not applied" or "Translation failed", from `failedStepKind`, the same rule as the filter; "Cleanup changed it"; the App filter; the steps) is the current owner's only, read for a page's ids in one query, and a row without them shows neither. What a step is compared with is one rule in `utils/stepChanges.js` (ESM, shared by main, the renderer and, as SQL, the filter): the output of the last earlier step that succeeded or was skipped.
