The list virtualizes (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, day groups by `formatDateGroup`), loads the next page near its end, refreshes the first page on `transcription-added` (keeping the rows loaded below it and the scroll position), re-reads one row on `transcription-updated` and drops it on `transcription-deleted`; ↑/↓ move the selection while the list has focus, Enter opens the detail on a narrow layout, nothing fires in the search field. The detail: final text, time, app ("app not recorded" otherwise), audio length and style; Copy, Re-transcribe (`ControlPanel.retryFromHistory` with the row's route kind, re-read on `onSettled`), Re-run cleanup (the same, `cleanupOnly`), Add to test set (`ui/historyActions.ts`, shared with Home's row), show audio in folder, Delete (Home's confirm); a failed row's banner shows what the row kept (its English message, its code as small print; a failed transcription has no step to carry a descriptor) and Open Settings when the code is one Settings fixes (`providerSettingsTargetFor`, Speech-to-Text); the player (`HistoryAudioPlayer.tsx` over one controller per shown row, `historyPlayer.ts`, kept outside the layout, so a layout change or a review opening keeps playback and the correction's caret): it reads `get-audio-buffer` on the first Play (or Ctrl+Space) into an object URL, stopped, revoked and its media keys let go on unmount (new selection, deletion, leaving the view), and stopped before the review hotkey's flow hides the panel; Play/Pause, −5 s/+5 s, a seek bar (`role="slider"`, `dir="ltr"`, click or drag, ←/→ ±5 s, Home/End, `aria-valuetext` "0:14 of 1:11", clamped to the recording), speed 0.75–1.5× (its button cycles) and "Back 2 s on resume" (on by default; only an ordinary resume after a pause, never a seek, word, mark or "From your cursor"); the speed, that toggle, the panes' split and the list choice are `historyViewStore`'s only preferences kept in localStorage; the media Play/Pause key through `navigator.mediaSession` once it has played; in the stacked layout it sticks to the top of the detail's scroller; the step strip, then one card per step diffed against its input (`utils/textDiff.ts`: word, punctuation and whitespace tokens so added line breaks show, case-only changes as their own kind, unspaced scripts per character, an LCS table capped at 4M cells, else plain text with a note; Changes / Text), glossary rules fired and `StepPrompt`; a failed step's card (`StepFailure`, shared with View steps) says why in the words its error card used (`describeStepFailure` over `detail.failure`), its code as small print, then the fix: Open Settings (`openProviderSettings`) when the failure names a section, and Re-run cleanup on a failed cleanup, style or translation step; a row without steps shows Heard against Final output with "Step details weren't recorded", never which step changed it. The detail has two layouts. **Stacked** (a narrow detail, or a failed, discarded, other-owner or no-raw-text row): the order above, the review form right under the header and player while a 👎 draft is open (a saved review stays after the steps). **Wide** (`HistoryWideLayout.tsx`; a completed row with text and something heard: the `stt` step's output, else the raw transcript): the header (meta at the start, actions at the end), the player, the step strip (it still jumps, opening the Steps tab at the card, and shows the flagged node as before), then two panes, each scrolling, split by a divider (`role="separator"` with `aria-valuenow`, dragged or ←/→, Home/End, 30–70%); the start pane's tabs (`ui/tabs`): **What speech heard** (`HistoryHeardText.tsx`: the heard text alone, **Changes after speech** (heard → final output) or, with a draft, **Speech → your correction** (debounced), always by the heard text's own offsets: changed heard words struck but selectable, added text marked `data-diff-mark`, so words selected there start the same fixes as in the speech-to-text card, with one selection for whichever surface is shown, cleared when the view or its text changes), **Steps** (the cards as before) and **Dictionary (N)** (left out only when aligned with no words, without a count when too long to compare; a removed active tab falls back to What speech heard); the end pane shows the final output ("sent to <app>", "Something wrong? Correct it here" with B, a saved review under it) or, with a draft, the review form filling it: "What should it have said?", the step chips on one line (still the picker: digits, and `chipsRef` the focus for B and the hotkey, marked focused only once focus took), the estimated-position line, the correction taking the free height, "From your cursor", the step's fixes box in its compact form, Note, Cancel, Save; draft store, revision checks and the hotkey flow as before. **Estimated times** (`utils/historyPlaybackTimes.ts`, pure): word timings aren't stored, so a place in the heard text is its character offset ÷ its length × the recording's length, called estimated wherever shown, and only with a finite known length (otherwise no click-to-play, sentence tint or ticks; the controls stay): a click (not a selection) on a heard word plays from 0.3 s before it; the sentence estimated to be playing is tinted (no word marker) and shown as the "Estimated position" line beside the correction (not a live region); faint ticks mark sentence starts; "From your cursor" (and Alt+Enter) plays the caret's sentence from its start, and amber marks on the seek bar show where the correction differs from the final output (a click plays from 1 s before), both placed only through a word both diffs matched (correction → final → heard, the nearest in the caret's own sentence, earlier ones first, never whitespace or punctuation): without one the button is disabled with why and the mark left out, and neither shows for a translation, an empty heard text or an empty correction; the heard ↔ final alignment is worked out once per row, the correction's as it settles. "Open in History" (`requestOpenHistoryItem`, from Home's row menu, Overview's Last dictation and a test case; a dictation error card's goes through main, section 2) leaves Settings, switches to History and selects the id (`requestHistoryItem`, no review drafted), read by id even outside the loaded pages or the current filter, which stays. **Reviews** (Stage 2): main's local-only `dictation_feedback` table (history DB, created idempotently at startup by `_initDictationFeedback`): `transcription_id` (FK, `ON DELETE CASCADE`), `owner`, `revision`, `verdict` (`good`/`bad`), `stage_kind` (a recorded step's kind, `delivery` or `unsure`) and `stage_seq` (the step's seq, null otherwise), `expected_text` (null when equal to the final text), `note` (one line, 500 characters), timestamps; one per `(owner, transcription_id)`. `helpers/dictationFeedback.js` (`createDictationFeedback`) validates every field and scopes every read and write to step history's owner; signed-out reviews are never adopted. `history-get-item` adds `revision` (`historyRevision`: a hash of the row's text, raw text, status and the owner's step ids), `owner` (an opaque tag, so drafts never cross sign-ins) and the owner's `feedback`; `history-feedback-save` (`{ id, revision, verdict, stage, expectedText, note }`) re-checks the revision in its transaction (`STALE`) and that a blamed step is one of the owner's steps of that row (`INVALID_STAGE`), and refuses with `HISTORY_OFF` unless history is durable for the owner, decided as for the test set (synced and on; for an account, the policy resolved and not forced off), keeping nothing for the session; `history-feedback-get` / `-delete`; `history-feedback-changed` (`{ id }`) to every window. In the transaction of the change: a retry, any text rewrite (`updateTranscriptionText`, a changed cloud copy, status included), a status change and step replacement delete the row's reviews; physical deletes cascade; tombstones, expiry and clear-all delete them with the mark; `deleteAccountData` deletes that owner's. Never synced, indexed, exported or logged (reasons and counts only). `history-list` rows gain `rating`, and the `flagged` filter lists the owner's `bad` ones. UI (`history/useHistoryReview.ts`, `history/HistoryReview.tsx`): 👍 / 👎 in the detail's header (👍 saves at once, again takes it back, and asks first when it would clear a saved step, correction or note); 👎 opens "What went wrong?" (a chip per recorded step by seq, a kind recorded twice shown twice, then Delivery and Not sure, digits 1–9 and 0; the correction starts as the final output, empty for a failed row; a note; the chosen step's fixes under the chips) as a draft in `historyViewStore`, keyed by owner and row with its revision: kept across rows and views, dropped with a notice when the revision changes, nothing written before Save; a saved review shows with Edit and Delete review. Keys while the History view has focus (never in a field, a select, under a dialog or while composing): G 👍, B 👎, 1–9/0 a chip, Ctrl+Enter saves (in the form's own fields too), Esc cancels (a field's Esc leaves the field first). The player's keys work anywhere in the view, the correction and note included, never while composing, under a dialog or in a select, list or menu, only with their exact modifiers (never AltGr) and not repeated for play or Alt+Enter: Ctrl+Space plays or pauses (an IME that switches on Ctrl+Space may keep it; the buttons and the media key still work), Alt+←/→ skip 5 s, Alt+↑/↓ change speed, Alt+Enter plays from the cursor; they're not remappable and never touch main's hotkeys. The list marks ratings and has a Flagged chip. **Review last dictation** (the `review` hotkey slot and the tray): `windowManager.reviewLastDictation` (not during onboarding or hotkey capture) refuses while `isDictationBusy()` (the lifecycle, or the dictation window's pastes and saves still in flight: `dictation-delivery-state` from `useAudioRecording`, reset with the lifecycle when that renderer crashes, reloads or closes) and the pill says "Finish this dictation first" (`review-dictation-busy`), touching nothing; otherwise `helpers/dictationReview.js` resolves `getLastReviewableDictation(owner)` (the newest listed row in History's order with the owner's steps whose trace route isn't `agent`; failed included) and `queueReviewRequest` holds `{ requestId, id }` until the control panel asks (`get-pending-review-request` on mount and on `review-dictation-pending`). The panel leaves Settings (its guard may refuse) and shows History on that row with 👎 preselected as an unsaved draft and the first chip focused, a banner, "Save & close" (saves, then `hide-control-panel` → `hideControlPanelToTray`) and Esc that discards (asking if changed) and hides; a changed draft on the shown row asks before switching and is kept; none found shows "Couldn't find your last dictation's details" with nothing selected. **Fixes** (Stage 3; `history/useHistoryFixes.ts`, `history/HistoryFixes.tsx`, pure logic in `utils/historyFixes.ts`): a 👎 review that names a step (the open draft's, else the saved review's) highlights that step's strip node and card (`data-blamed`: theme `destructive` border and background, "You flagged this step", the node's label "Go to <step>, the step you flagged"; Delivery highlights Final output, in a row without steps too) and shows one suggestion box, worded as a possibility, under the chips (or under the saved review). Speech-to-text or Glossary: "Glossary correction from selection…" and "Dictionary word…", from words selected in a step card's text at or before the blamed step (`selectionchange` shows them in a small floating toolbar too; the selection is read from the step's own output, leaving out a diff's deletions and ↵ marks, marked `data-diff-mark` in `HistoryDiffText`), plus "Edit rule" for each rule that fired (the glossary steps' `detail.rulesFired`). The glossary fix opens `GlossaryRuleDialog.tsx` (the Glossary tab's editor, with a `notice` slot; `glossaryRuleInput` lives in `glossaryStore`) prefilled with the selection as an `anywhere` spelling, apps empty, and as canonical the correction's words for it when `alignCorrection` (`textDiff`) finds a clean replacement (whole words, no changed run cut in two, more than a change of case), else empty; a spelling an existing rule already has opens that rule with a notice. The dictionary fix confirms one editable word (the aligned words, else the selection; "already in your dictionary" in any case) and adds it with `updateCustomDictionary` (source `manual`, synced like any word). Cleanup or Style: "Add to the test set…" pins with `pinHistoryTestCase` (main picks Cleanup or the row's style) and opens the case: input, final output and correction side by side, `suggestTestChecks`'s literal checks (each run of up to six words the correction adds → must contain, removes → must not contain; a single word takes its neighbor; none the other text already contains; at most three each, deduplicated, case kept) as editable, removable checkboxes (ticked for a new case; for an existing one its own checks ticked and the suggestions unticked), match case, the session-only notice (`storageNoticeKey`) when not durable, and a line that checks are literal; Save checks is its only write (`test-cases-update`, the case's label, target app and style checks passed back unchanged). Then "Open Prompt Studio" (`cleanup#dictation.cleanup.prompt`) or "Edit style" (`requestStyleFocus`, then `styles`). Delivery: "Copy text again". Not sure, translation, assistant and final touches: no box. Nothing is saved before the editor's own Save, no model is called, and no dictated or corrected text is logged.
- **Dictionary in History** (Dictionary v2 Stage A; the occurrence model and its ownership contract are 13h's "Where words appeared"): `history-get-item` adds `dictionary: [{ word, appearances: [{ kind, start, end, speechWrote, by }] }]` (the current dictionary's words in the row by the alignment, `historyItemDictionary`; `start`/`end` into the final text, into raw for `chg`), `dictionaryAligned` and `dictionaryGeneration`: shown with the row (the account rule), so no durability gate, but `by` only from the current owner's steps (another owner's row names none), and none for a translation or a row the owner's steps mark as the assistant's. `history-list` rows add `dictionaryFixes` (dictionary words the row-summary matcher finds in the final text and not the raw transcript; 0 for translations, rows without a raw transcript and the owner's assistant rows; `getHistoryDictionaryFixes` reads the page's rows in JS), the answer `generation`; filters `dictionaryFixed` (≥ 1 such word; translations and the owner's assistant rows left out) and `dictionaryWord` (a word, ≤ 500 characters, in the raw transcript or the final text; "Show in History"), both over the retained History rows in view, like search, both the row-summary matcher (never the alignment) as SQL functions (`history_dictionary_fixes`, `history_dictionary_word`, registered like `history_fold`) over the current dictionary and snippet expansions, built once per **dictionary generation** and frozen for it (`_historyDictionaryState`; each row's answer kept for that generation and History's content revision; an ASCII row without the word's first token is skipped untokenized). The generation is `dictionary_content_revision`, bumped by triggers on every `custom_dictionary` insert, word or `deleted_at` update and delete and every `snippets` insert, `replacement` or `deleted_at` update and delete (`_initDictionaryContentRevision`; sync bookkeeping doesn't bump it; without the table, a counter bumped when the words or expansions read differently), plus every account change this session (`_accountScopeEpoch`, since the words in view are the account's), so a switch rebuilds the matcher. A later page of a dictionary filter asked for with another generation answers `{ stale: true, rows: [], generation }`. `history-dictionary-fixes(ids)` (≤ 2,000) re-reads loaded rows' badges. About 60 ms cold over 10,000 rows × 250 words, a few ms for a later page. Renderer (`useHistoryList.ts`): later pages and `refreshRow` carry the first page's generation; `stale` starts over from the first page; on `dictionary-updated` or `snippets-updated` a dictionary filter starts over (the loaded rows may no longer match), otherwise the loaded rows stay and their badges are re-read; a first-page refresh under a dictionary filter whose generation moved starts over too. UI: a "📖 Dictionary fixed" chip (`historyViewStore` filter `dictionaryFixed`), the "Show in History" word as a removable chip (`dictionaryWord`; `showHistoryForDictionaryWord` clears the other criteria; All clears it), a "📖 n fixed" badge; the detail's final text underlines the words (`DictionaryFinalText`: green speech got it, amber fixed after speech; each focusable and described by its kind through hidden text outside the copied text), **Dictionary in this dictation** (`HistoryDictionary.tsx`, "your dictionary words, as the dictionary is now", the hidden assistant name left out; the stacked card and the wide layout's Dictionary tab): a summary ("11 dictionary words · 1 fixed after speech"), one wrapped row of chips (the word with ✓ speech got it and/or ↺ fixed or changed after speech, named with its status), a chip opening that word's details under the row, one at a time: its kinds, "Speech wrote X, fixed after speech" plus "by the glossary" / "by cleanup" / "by a style" when the steps show it and how many times speech wrote it (its variants), **Make a glossary rule** with the Dictionary panel's scope safety (`dictionary-speech-variants(word, { spelling })` for its `keptInFinal`), or "The glossary handles this", and **Open in Dictionary**; the cleanup and style steps' changes mark inserted dictionary words (`HistoryDiffText` `markWords`, amber underline, described as a dictionary word). The detail re-reads the row on `dictionary-updated`, `snippets-updated` and `dictionary-history-changed`. No "what was sent" line: it isn't recorded per dictation.

### 13e. Prompt Test Set

- **Cases** live in main's local-only `prompt_test_cases` table (history DB, so Reset app data deletes them with `transcriptions.db`): `id` (UUID), `owner`, `target` (`cleanup`, or a style's `style:<id>`, see 13f), `label`, `raw_text` (the input, **copied**), `reference_text` (what was pasted, never a pass/fail target), `target_app`, `must_contain` / `must_not_contain` (JSON arrays of literals), `match_case`, `style_checks` (style cases' counts), `source` (`history` | `test`), `source_transcription_id` (no foreign key: only for "Open in History"). Never synced, exported or indexed. Built-in starter cases (`config/promptTestStarters.ts`) are renderer constants; hiding them is a localStorage preference (`promptTestSet.hiddenStarters`).
- **Main owns them** (`helpers/promptTestCases.js`, `createPromptTestCases`), added from History's row menu (Add to test set, `TranscriptionItem.tsx`), a History review's fix (Add to the test set…, the same pin, then suggested checks saved with `test-cases-update` on confirm; see 13d) or Prompt Studio's single Test (Save as test case): the owner is step history's (`stepHistory.currentOwner()`, `account:<id>` or `signed-out`) and every read and write is scoped to it; signed-out cases are never adopted on sign-in; `deleteAccountData` deletes the account's cases in its transaction and `forgetOwner` its session copies. A pin from History reads `raw_text` and `text` from the row itself (the renderer only names the row; `getTranscriptionById` finds only a row the account rule shows, so another account's is `NOT_FOUND`), copies the target app from that owner's captured steps, and refuses a row without a raw transcript (`NO_RAW_TEXT`) or that isn't a completed dictation. IPC: `test-cases-list` / `-pin-history` / `-save` / `-update` / `-delete` / `-clear`; broadcast `prompt-test-cases-changed` (`{ owner, reason }`, also on an owner change from `set-active-account-scope` and the auth token).
- **Policy gate** (both entry points, decided in main from the retention sync): durable only while synced, `dataRetentionEnabled` is on and, for an account, `localHistoryPolicyResolved`; otherwise the case is kept in memory for the session (`reason`: `history-off`, `pending`, `unavailable`). `localHistoryForcedOff` (an organization's `always_off`, synced with the retention settings) allows no durable copy: `policyChanged()` moves the owner's stored cases into memory and off the disk.
- **Run** (`services/promptTestRunner.ts`, pure; `stores/promptTestSetStore.ts` wires it; UI `components/ui/PromptTestSet.tsx` in Prompt Studio → Test → Test set, cleanup kind only): every case through **A** (the prompt in use) and **B** (the selected prompt or its unsaved draft), 1–3 repetitions (default 2), one request at a time, A and B alternating. Capped at 50 cases / 300 requests. Captured once at start: A and B (`captureCleanupPrompt`), the request with its provider pinned (`pinDictationCleanupRequest`; the self-hosted URL can't be pinned, so `cleanupRouteKey` stops the run if it changes), the prompt's dictionary and languages (`captureCleanupPromptInputs` → `ReasoningConfig.promptInputs`, stripped in `processText`), and one glossary snapshot plus snippets; each input goes through `applyGlossary` with the case's target app (none = an unknown app). Every request ends `completed`, `error`, `cancelled` (Stop, account change) or `skipped` (the run stopped itself); auth/quota/configuration codes stop the run, rate limits and a busy local model get three bounded waits, three errors in a row stop it. Nothing is saved, no `transcriptions` rows or steps are written, and results stay in the window's memory; an owner change cancels the run and clears them.
- **Checks** (`utils/promptTestChecks.ts`): repetition (`assertValidCleanupOutput`; a validator rejection is a completed request with that check failed), literal must/must-not contain (case-insensitive unless `matchCase`), and advisory filler words (whole words, never a failure). An empty reply or a request error is an error outcome, not a failed check. The summary (`summarizeRun`) counts only cases where every run of A and B completed; partial runs say so.
- **Dictation comes first**: main forwards the lifecycle to the control panel (`dictation-lifecycle-changed`, `get-dictation-lifecycle`; `windowManager.isDictationActive`), and `services/dictationGate.ts` waits before every request while a dictation is prepared, recorded or processed, plus a 1.5 s grace for the paste. On the local model, test requests carry `yieldToDictation` and a `requestId`: `helpers/localRequestYield.js` refuses them during a dictation and aborts in-flight ones by id when one starts (freeing the bridge's single slot), returning `YIELDED_TO_DICTATION`, which the runner sends again afterwards.
- **Logs**: test requests (the set and the single Test) carry `ReasoningConfig.redactLogs`: `callChatCompletionsApi` drops its request body and response excerpts, and the local bridge and `runInference` drop their prompt and result previews (`textRedacted: true` instead). The store logs counts only.

### 13f. Dictation Styles

- **Record**: `dictationStyles` in localStorage is the one authority (`config/styles/library.ts`, pure): `{ version, dictationStyleId, seededStarters, styles: [{ id, name, emoji, instruction, enabled, runMode: "two-step" | "one-step", appGlossary, examples: [], useVoice: false, hotkey: null, origin, updatedAt }] }`. `examples` and `useVoice` are reserved (style from examples, voice profile): stored and carried, never shown. `hotkey` mirrors main's style hotkey binding (13g). Plain (`PLAIN_STYLE_ID = "plain"`) is built in, never an entry: cleanup only, and what the dictation hotkey does while `dictationStyleId` is `plain`. Every change is one `updateStyleLibrary` read-modify-write that re-reads the record inside the write; a change that makes the hotkey's style unusable (off, deleted, emptied) puts Plain back in the same write. `stores/styleStore.ts` mirrors it for the UI and follows other windows' `storage` events; what a recording or retry uses is read from the record itself (`resolveStyleForRecording`, `resolveStyleForRetry`). The starters (`config/styles/starters.ts`: 💬 Teams chat, 📋 Requirements, ✉️ Email) are written once, **off**, with names in the UI language (`ensureStyles` from the Styles section); `seededStarters` keeps a deleted starter from coming back.
- **Prompt** (`config/styles/restylePrompt.ts`): one function (`buildRestylePrompt`) assembles step two from the wrapper and safety rule, the fidelity rules, the voice block (empty for now), then the style; the fidelity rules outrank both. One step appends `buildOneStepStyleBlock` (style, then fidelity rules) to the cleanup prompt. The wording, the `<text>` / `<transcript>` messages and the starter instructions are the style lab's (round 4) and are pinned by `test/config/restylePrompt.test.js`; a dictated `</text>` is neutralized with a word joiner (`neutralizeDelimiters` / `restoreDelimiters`).
- **Requests** (`services/dictationStyles.ts`): both run on the `dictationCleanup` scope with the cleanup request's options (`buildDictationCleanupConfig`: thinking setting, sampling, `requireCompleteOutput`) and `styleOutputTokens` as the budget. Step two is a `systemPrompt` request (the restyle prompt). One step is the cleanup request itself with `ReasoningConfig.cleanupStyle` (`{ name, block }`): `processText` folds it into the request's provider context (the system prompt gets the block, `ctx.wrapCleanupTranscript` names the style in the closing line, Cloud's `customPrompt` is the template or the built-in prompt plus the block) and never forwards it; cleanup is never a `systemPrompt`. OpenWhispr Cloud returns no finish reason, so styled requests there ask for `STYLE_COMPLETION_MARKER` and a reply without it fails as truncated. An empty, cut-off or repeated reply is a failure.
- **Chain** (`helpers/styleChain.js`, pure; `runStyledDictation` wires it): two steps = cleanup with the prompt in use, then the restyle; one step = one request. Cleanup off: both modes restyle the working text in one request. A failed or empty restyle keeps the last good text (one step falls back to a plain cleanup); a failed cleanup restyles the working text; a reply that only moved line breaks is a change (no translation-style "unchanged" check). `strict` (test runs) rejects instead.
- **Dictation** (`audioManager.postTranscribe`): a recording's style is resolved and frozen in `performStartRecording` (`setRecordingStyle`), from the IPC payload's `styleId` (prepare, start and toggle carry one for Stage 4 keys; `winPushState` freezes it per push) or the hotkey's style; assistant and translation recordings never get one, a second press can't change it, cancel clears the badge and the next start resolves its own. In the boundary a styled recording is its own intent: `detectWakeWord` is skipped (so no assistant and no selection edit), `context.style` and `appScopedGlossary` reach `runPostTranscription`, and the path's `run` is replaced by `runStyleRoute`, so batch, committed streaming (Parakeet), streaming and the streaming→batch fallback each apply it exactly once. It runs whenever the cleanup scope has a model (`isStyleReachable`), cleanup switched on or off; no model keeps the corrected transcript with route `none`. Failures raise `onStyleFallback` (the "Couldn't apply <style>" toast in `useAudioRecording`). The Cloud and streaming paths pass `cleanupRoute` (Cloud's cleanup endpoint with their STT metadata), used only while Cloud is the cleanup provider.
- **Trace and history**: model calls are `cleanup` and `style` steps; a style step carries `detail: { styleId, runMode }` and its dispatched provider, model and prompt; `trace.route` is `style` and `trace.context.style` the recording's (`stepHistory.js` stores both). The row's `route_kind` is `style:<id>` (`styleRouteKind`), taken from the trace at save. History shows the style beside the time; View steps names the style and run mode. Retry from history (Re-transcribe and Re-run cleanup, both through `services/historyRerun.ts`) re-runs the row's style, even switched off; a deleted one asks first and retries as Plain (`retryFromHistory`). A failed style step keeps why in `detail.failure` (13d) beside `styleId` and `runMode`, and the History row says "Style not applied".
- **Pill**: `useAudioRecording.dictationStyle` (`{ id, name, emoji }`) drives `components/dictation/StyleBadge.tsx` beside the pill while it records and processes.
- **Auto-learn** is decided per paste in main: a styled result pastes with `suppressAutoLearn: true`, and `paste-text` then arms no edit monitoring and stops any running one (`_autoLearnPasteSeq` also cancels a monitor an earlier paste had only scheduled). The next plain paste arms it as usual (unless auto-learn is Off, 13h). Upstream's raw paste (`paste-text` with `raw: true`, pasted as given; no renderer sends it) is treated the same way: no edit monitoring, and a running one stops. The option never reaches the clipboard manager.
- **UI**: Settings → Dictation → Styles (`components/ui/StyleStudio.tsx`, a wide page): the "Style for <hotkey>" picker (Plain plus styles that are on), then master–detail (side by side from a 768 px container, stacked below): the list (Plain first, built in, "Cleanup only"; each row's emoji, name, run mode, keys — the dictation hotkey on the style it applies, the style's own key — and on/off switch; New style last) and the selected style's editor with Prompt Studio's View, Customize and Test. Selection, drafts and writes are as before (a row selects; the switch is `saveStyle(id, { enabled })`); `requestStyleFocus` selects the style on View, scrolls to the studio and focuses its row. Test runs one input in one step and in two steps with both timings, or the style's test set (`PromptTestSet` with `style`): A = one step, B = two steps (`startStyleTestRun`; both clean up with the captured prompt in use, `strict`).
- **Test set**: cases may target a style (`target` `style:<id>`; saved from a style's Test or pinned from a styled dictation), and carry `style_checks` (JSON `{ bulletsMin, bulletsMax, numberedMin, numberedMax }`, an additive column) checked by `countBulletLines` / `countNumberedItems`. `test-cases-clear` takes an optional target. The Requirements starter ships the fidelity cases (status update, negation, dates, commitments, a quoted instruction, a `</text>`), Teams and Email their own (`STYLE_STARTER_TEST_CASES`).

### 13g. Style Hotkeys

- **What**: each style may bind one key. Style keys are extra keys on the **dictation** slot, so they follow the activation mode (Hold or Tap) through the dictation hotkey's own path; on Windows the native listener runs one hook process per key (`getNativeListenerKeys` lists every key of the slot in Hold).
- **Authority**: main. It alone knows whether a key registered, and it needs the map at boot. The committed map is `hotkeyManager.styleKeys` (hotkey → style id) and `.env` `DICTATION_STYLE_KEYS` (`helpers/styleHotkeys.js` reads it in `initializeCoreManagers`, before any window exists; `parseStyleKeyMap` / `serializeStyleKeyMap` in `hotkeyList.js`). The style record's `hotkey` is a mirror for the UI, rewritten by every window from main's `style-hotkeys-changed` broadcast (`applyStyleHotkeyBindings`, not a user edit). A style that can no longer hold a key (deleted, switched off, emptied; here or in another window's `storage` event) makes `styleStore` send the eligible style ids (`style-hotkeys-sync-styles`) and main releases the rest. A rename changes nothing: bindings are by id.
- **One builder** (`buildDictationSlotKeys` in `hotkeyList.js`): the dictation key(s) first, then each style key; a key that repeats one already in the slot (`canonicalHotkey`: any spelling, alias or modifier order) is left out and reported, so the dictation key wins. Used by `setupShortcuts` for the dictation slot (startup, fallbacks, dictation hotkey changes; style keys are best effort there and never fail the dictation key, and `result.hotkeys` lists only the dictation keys), by `setStyleKey` (style edits), and by `refreshStyleKeyRegistration` (boot's `finishBoot`, activation-mode changes' `revalidate`, a failed save's `restoreStyleKeys`). The existing cross-slot check skips its own slot, so `setStyleKey` adds the in-slot duplicate check and `updateHotkey` refuses a dictation key that repeats a style key; other slots get "already a style hotkey".
- **Commit and rollback** (`setStyleKey`): validate (backend, duplicate, other slots), release the style's old key, register the new one; on failure re-register the old one and leave the map untouched. Only then is the map committed, then `.env` written (awaited); a failed write puts the previous map back, registrations included (`restoreStyleKeys`). The service (`createStyleHotkeys`) runs every change one at a time and broadcasts the result. IPC: `style-hotkeys-get` / `-set` / `-sync-styles`.
- **A press carries its style**: `getStyleIdForHotkey(key)` (only for keys registered as style keys) is passed by `nativeHotkeyDispatch.js` (the Windows/Linux listener's key-down: `startWindowsPushToTalk(key, timing, { styleId })` in Hold, `sendToggleDictation({ styleId })` in Tap) and by `createHotkeyCallback` (globalShortcut Tap). Hold freezes it in `winPushState`; overlapping presses, a short hold, a press during processing and a blocked start behave exactly as for the dictation key (`test/helpers/styleHotkeyPress.test.js`). A Hold whose key leaves the slot is reset.
- **Other platforms**: `getStyleHotkeyRefusal` refuses style keys on GNOME, KDE and Hyprland (one key per slot), macOS (Hold can't use a lone ordinary key; not built yet) and other Linux, with a visible reason: a new key is refused in Settings, and a committed key that isn't live stays listed with its reason (`getStyleKeyState`) and gets a startup toast (`finishBoot`), never dropped.
- **UI**: Style Studio's View and Customize tabs show `StyleHotkeyField` (the shared `HotkeyInput`, saved through main at once; conflict checks against every slot and the other style keys; a warning for common app shortcuts, `Ctrl+1`–`9` and `` Ctrl+Shift+` ``, `utils/styleHotkeys.ts`). Settings → Hotkeys lists them read-only as rows of its table (`StyleHotkeysList`: every style, its key or why none, a refusal's reason), each opening its style (`requestStyleFocus`, then `onNavigate("styles")`); every hotkey field there checks against the style keys by name.

### 13h. Dictionary

- **Page** (`DictionaryView.tsx`, merged with upstream's shared team dictionary, #2855): the page is upstream's (list picker `DictionaryListPicker`, add field `DictionaryAddField`, toolbar, `DictionaryWordList` rows, selection bar, `ShareWordsPopover`, locked/upsell states `SharedDictionaryLocked` with `onUpgrade` → Plans & Billing, actions in `useDictionaryActions`), plus the fork's Glossary tab and `onOpenSettings`. A list that holds only the user's own words (Personal; All words while it has no teammates' words, so also signed out, without Business, paused or with empty team lists) opens **Dictionary v2** (`dictionary/PersonalDictionaryView.tsx`: usage, pins, categories, Tidy up, suggestions, a word's detail, its own search and five sorts, and a "Share with…" button in its selection bar); the page's add field, bulk import, share popover, Clear all, Export and remove actions serve it, so bulk Remove also offers Undo. Lists with teammates' words (All words with shared ones, All shared, a team list, Hidden) are upstream's rows, where personal rows keep upstream's hover Share/Edit/Delete and checkboxes, and renaming a personal word there keeps its pin, source, date and category (`useDictionaryActions`' in-place rename). `dictionary/DictionaryWordList.tsx` is one module for both: `rows` renders upstream's list (`RowsWordList`, virtualized), `items` the fork's keyboard listbox with columns, pin sections and dragging (`ItemsWordList`). Upstream's prompt-length notice stays out; "Where it goes" replaces it. History's Open in Dictionary switches to Personal and the Dictionary tab. Import from Dragon (#2190, `helpers/dragonImport.js` `planDragonImport`, with a preview dialog) sits in the add field's import menu on both kinds of list and in the personal view's toolbar "Import ▾" (`importMenu`). The page is remounted whenever the personal lists start belonging to another account (`useListsOwnerRevision`, keyed by the lists owner), which closes any open editor, selection, word detail or Tidy up; `listsOwner` reaches `PersonalDictionaryView`, and a write opened under the old owner is refused (`updateCustomDictionary(changes, listsOwner)`, renames checked against `getListsOwnerRevision()`); the Tidy up and category review stores reset on an owner change (`subscribeListsOwner`). Upstream's notices on what dictation's speech model gets of the dictionary (`dictionary/speechDictionaryNotice.ts`: OpenRouter ignores it, a local model ignores it, Orukeet takes up to 100 hotwords) show in "Where it goes" on the personal view and under the list on lists with teammates' words. Tests: upstream's `test/components/dictionaryView.test.js` (with a `PersonalDictionaryView` stub) and the fork's Dictionary render tests
- **One identity** (`utils/dictionaryWordKey.js`, ESM shared by main and the renderer): trim, NFC, lowercase. Pins, suggestions, evidence and usage key words by it; the stored dictionary keeps its spelling.
- **Local state** (main's history DB, created idempotently by `_initDictionaryState`; never synced, exported, indexed or sent to a model, except the words' categories in a category review the user asks for; never logged, counts and reasons only). These tables are keyed by word (`word_key`), not by account, while the dictionary itself is per account (section 4): reads, pins and categories go by the words in view (`_visibleDictionaryKeys`), and a pin or stored category is pruned only once no live word of any account on the device has its key (`_liveDictionaryKeys`), so switching accounts never prunes another account's. An account change broadcasts the pins and categories with the new account's words (`_broadcastAccountScopedSettings`):
  - `dictionary_pins` (`word_key`, `pinned_at`, `position`): device-wide, by word. `position` is the pin order (0 first; gaps are fine; ties by `pinned_at`, then `word_key`), and `getDictionaryPins()` answers keys in it. **Migration** (`_migrateDictionaryPinPositions`, in `_initDictionaryState`, idempotent): the column is added when missing, then every pin without a position gets the next ones after any that exist, by `(pinned_at, word_key)`. Before this the pinned tier followed stored dictionary order, so an existing pin list can read in a different order once (by pin date). A new pin goes last (max + 1); unpinning leaves a gap. **`reorderDictionaryPins(keys)`** (IPC `dictionary-pins-reorder(keys[])`, broadcast `dictionary-pins-updated`; logs counts and codes only) takes exactly the current pins by key (none missing, repeated or unknown, so never more than the pin count), else refuses with `MISMATCH` and changes nothing; it sets positions 0..n−1 in one transaction and answers `pins` either way. Every pin mover keeps the place: `_moveDictionaryPin` (rename, a cloud pull rewriting a live row's word; onto a key pinned already, the higher place, the smaller number, wins: `_keepDictionaryPin`), `mergeDictionaryWords` (both pinned: the higher place wins), `restoreDictionaryWords` and Tidy up's paths (below). A pin needs its word in some account's dictionary, and follows it in the same transaction: `_pruneDictionaryPins` in `applyDictionaryChanges` (when it removed words), `setDictionary`, `hardDeleteDictionaryEntry` and `upsertDictionaryFromCloud`. IPC `dictionary-pins-get` / `dictionary-pin-set`, broadcast `dictionary-pins-updated`; the renderer's `reorderDictionaryPins` (`stores/dictionarySourcesStore.ts`) shows the new order at once and goes back to main's pins on a refusal (or the old order on a failure, unless something newer arrived); the hint tier is section 13.
  - `dictionary_categories` (`word_key` primary key, `category` `people` | `work` | `everyday`, `source` `user` | `model`, `updated_at`; both checked by the table): a word's **explicit** category (Dictionary v2 Stage C), the user's or a model proposal the user accepted; device-wide like the pins, never synced or exported, and sent nowhere but a category review's request (below). Only explicit choices are stored; the automatic one is computed in the renderer. It follows its word exactly like a pin, in the same transaction: `_pruneDictionaryPins` prunes categories too (`_pruneDictionaryCategories`: removal, Clear all, `setDictionary`, `hardDeleteDictionaryEntry`, `upsertDictionaryFromCloud`, Tidy up's paths), `_moveDictionaryCategory` beside `_moveDictionaryPin` (rename, a cloud pull rewriting a live row's word), `mergeDictionaryWords` through `_keepDictionaryCategory` (**precedence**: the user's choice beats the model's, which beats none; equal sources keep the target's, `into`'s). **`setDictionaryCategories(items)`** (IPC `dictionary-categories-set([{ word, category: "people" | "work" | "everyday" | null, source: "user" | "model" }])`, ≤ 10,000 items of words ≤ 500 characters at the IPC, broadcast `dictionary-categories-updated` with every stored choice when something changed; logs counts and codes only): one transaction, each item checked on its own (a word not in the dictionary `NOT_IN_DICTIONARY`, an unknown category `INVALID_CATEGORY` or source `INVALID_SOURCE`; only an explicit `null` removes the stored choice), more items than the dictionary has words refused whole (`TOO_MANY`); answers `{ success, changed, results: [{ ok, code? }], categories }`. `dictionary-categories-get` → `[{ key, category, source }]` by key. Rename, merge and Tidy up broadcast it with the pins (`broadcastDictionaryChange`); the renderer's store also re-reads it on `dictionary-updated`.
  - `dictionary_suggestions` (`word`, unique `word_key`, `times`, `status` `pending` | `dismissed` | `accepted`): device-wide. `recordDictionarySuggestions` skips words already in the dictionary and dismissed ones, bumps `times` on a pending one and reopens an accepted one whose word was removed since. Accept adds through `applyDictionaryChanges(…, "learned")` in one transaction (idempotent); dismiss is for good until "Clear dismissed". IPC `dictionary-suggestions-list` (`{ suggestions: [{ id, word, times, createdAt, updatedAt, evidence }], dismissed }`) / `-accept` / `-dismiss` / `-clear-dismissed` (ids), broadcast `dictionary-suggestions-updated` (`{ reason }`).
  - `dictionary_word_evidence` (`word_key`, `owner`, `transcription_id` FK `ON DELETE CASCADE`, `replaced` ≤ 200 characters, `app` exe name or null): what a learned word replaced, and where. Owner-scoped as step history (13d): written and read only for the current owner while local history is durable for it (`usageAvailability`, the test set's rule, 13e), at most 5 rows per word and owner (the oldest goes). It retires with History: physical deletes cascade; `clearTranscriptions`, expiry (`deleteTranscriptionsExpiredBefore`) and tombstones (`deleteTranscription` of a synced row) delete it in their transactions (`_deleteDictionaryWordEvidence`); `deleteAccountData` deletes that owner's. Accepting the suggestion keeps it (Stage 2 shows it on the word).
- **Auto-learn modes** (`autoLearnMode`, section 5; `utils/autoLearnMode.ts`, main's twin `helpers/autoLearnSetting.js`): main starts at Suggest but **unsynced**, and watches nothing until a window syncs (`auto-learn-changed`; the first sync always applies, later same-value ones are ignored, an older window's boolean maps true → Suggest). Off (`_stopAutoLearn`) bumps `_autoLearnPasteSeq` (a monitor scheduled in `paste-text`'s 500 ms start never starts), clears the start timer and the correction debounce, drops the pending edit and paste context and stops the monitor. `paste-text` captures the paste's context when monitoring starts (`{ owner, app: exeName, text, pastedAt }`); a `text-edited` event carries it only when its original text is that paste's. `_processCorrections` (glossary words never learned, 13b): Add → `applyDictionaryChanges({ add }, "learned")`, `dictionary-updated`, `corrections-learned` as before; Suggest → `_suggestCorrections`: the suggestions, then evidence (`_recordCorrectionEvidence`: the same owner as the paste and durable history, else none with a reason; `findEvidenceTranscription` finds the owner's newest completed, non-deleted row whose final text is the pasted text, saved in the 10 minutes before the paste or the minute after, with that owner's steps), `dictionary-suggestions-updated` and `corrections-suggested` (`[{ id, word }]`: the dictation panel's toast with Review, which opens the control panel on the Dictionary through `open-dictionary-suggestions` → `windowManager.queueDictionarySuggestionsRequest` / `get-pending-dictionary-suggestions-request`, and Add). An owner change (`set-active-account-scope`, the auth token) drops the watched paste's context (`_dictionaryOwnerMaybeChanged`), as does clearing History.
- **Suggestions UI**: `components/DictionarySuggestions.tsx` at the top of the Dictionary page (each word, how often it was learned, its evidence while history holds it: "Changed from “X” in App"; Add, Dismiss, Add all), with "Learn from your corrections" mirrored (`AutoLearnModeSetting compact`) and "Forget N dismissed words" (`-clear-dismissed`); with nothing waiting it's just that line.
- **Rows** (`getDictionaryEntries`, IPC `db-get-dictionary-entries`): the words in view (the account rule, as `getDictionary`), `{ word, source, createdAt, updatedAt }` in stored order (`created_at` as stored, SQLite's UTC format or a pull's ISO). `stores/dictionarySourcesStore.ts` mirrors them as `entries` beside `learned` and `pinned`, re-read when the word list changes and on `dictionary-updated` (a source change leaves the words as they were).
- **Rename and merge** (`database.js`, IPC `dictionary-rename` / `dictionary-merge`, both broadcasting `dictionary-updated` and `dictionary-pins-updated`; renderer `settingsStore.renameDictionaryWord` / `mergeDictionaryWords`, which adopt the stored list and push to OpenWhispr Cloud): `renameDictionaryWord(from, to)` updates the word in place in one transaction: the row keeps its id, source, `created_at`, `client_dict_id` and `cloud_id`, and is marked for sync as an edit (`sync_status = 'pending'`, `updated_at`), so a synced word pushes as a PATCH of the same entry; its pin with its place (`_moveDictionaryPin`), evidence (`_moveDictionaryEvidence`, still ≤ 5 per word and owner) and accepted suggestion (`_moveDictionarySuggestion`; a pending one for the new spelling is settled as accepted) move to the new key. A case-only rename keeps the key, so only the word changes. Another live word in view with the new key (`_liveDictionaryRows()`: the account's own or an unattributed one) refuses it (`{ code: "EXISTS", existing }`; the page then offers a merge); a deleted row of the same account still holding the spelling until its deletion is pushed (`idx_custom_dictionary_account_word` is unique per account, tombstones included) has its word moved aside (`_releaseDeletedDictionaryWord(key, accountId)`; the push deletes by `cloud_id`). `mergeDictionaryWords(from, into)` removes `from` (hard, or a tombstone when synced) and keeps `into` with its id and date: typed wins (a learned `into` becomes typed, marked for sync), a pin on either survives on `into` with its place (both pinned: the higher place, the smaller number, wins), `from`'s evidence moves to `into`'s key; `from`'s suggestion row stays as a removal leaves it. Words are matched by exact spelling first, then by key, so legacy case-variant rows can be told apart. The page's inline edit (double-click, the pencil, F2) and the detail's Edit both rename; nothing removes and re-adds.
- **Mark typed** (`setDictionarySource(words, "manual")`, IPC `dictionary-set-source`, broadcast `dictionary-updated`; renderer `settingsStore.markDictionaryWordsTyped`): learned rows become typed, marked for sync. Only `manual`: a pull never demotes a typed row, so a demotion couldn't sync.
- **Per word, pace and cleanup sets** (`dictionaryUsage.js`): the token index (`buildUsageIndex`) and the row matcher (`createUsageMatcher`) are shared by the totals, `countWordUsageByWeek` (IPC `dictionary-word-usage(word)` → `{ available, days, weeks: [{ start, end, heard, written, missedRows, both, changed }] }`, 13 weeks ending now, anchored once per computation (`usageWeekBounds`: each week `[start, end)`, the last ending at `now`, the oldest starting where the 90-day window does), `both` speech got it and `changed` changed after speech that week; the chart's tooltip and its click filter read these same bounds; cached for the last 16 words by owner, day, key, expansions and content revision) and `dictationPace` (`dictionary-usage` adds `pace: { perDay }`: dictations a day over the last 30 days, or since the oldest row when History is younger; the usage store keeps `perDay`). IPC `dictionary-cleanup-scope-counts` → `{ available, days, sets: [{ applied, dictations, avgWords }] }`: the owner's `ok` cleanup and one-step style steps of the last 90 days with `detail.dictionaryScope`, grouped by the set applied (`getDictionaryCleanupScopeCounts`). IPC `dictionary-word-evidence(word)` → this owner's evidence for the word while history is durable. All four use the usage rule (`usageAvailability`); counts only, except the evidence the word detail shows, and nothing is logged but counts and codes.
- **Coverage and the cut line** (`utils/sttHintSpec.ts`, `utils/sttHintFields.js`): `resolveSttHintSpec({ settings, managed, providers })` resolves the transcription route as Re-transcribe does (no policy floor) and maps it in `retry-transcription`'s branch order: managed Azure → `managed` (a managed error → `none`), a self-hosted server → `openai-compatible` (with the route's endpoint and model), local → `local-whisper` (sherpa → `none`), OpenWhispr Cloud → `cloud` (also for a BYOK error route, unless self-hosted), Tinfoil, Mistral, Gemini, xAI → their routes, Corti → `none`, Azure Speech → `azure-speech`, OpenAI, Groq and custom → `openai-compatible`; a streaming-only provider (Deepgram, AssemblyAI) has no batch route → `none` (streaming dictation's own keyterm list isn't described). `buildSttHintFields` returns `entries` (`{ term, source, rank, coverage }`, every term in priority order) read off the fields it built: a prompt route's term is sent when the prompt it carries (after the script bias, cut at a comma) holds all of it, and read when it starts in the last `WHISPER_DECODER_PROMPT_CHARS` of a tail-ordered prompt; gpt-transcribe's when every keyword it splits into is a `keywords[]` field or in the overflow prompt; Mistral's when all its words are in the first 100 `context_bias` words; Gemini's and xAI's when it's among the 100 keyterms; Azure Speech's when it's among the 199 phrases; nothing for `none` or Orukeet, and nothing for an OpenRouter endpoint (its speech-to-text drops `prompt`). For a local sherpa model with hotwords (Orukeet, spec `none`), `useDictionaryCoverage` reads the hotword list instead (`sherpaHotwordCoverage`: a term is sent when all of it is among the up to 100 hotwords, ranked by its place there, `terms` as the unit). `budget` is `{ unit: chars | keywords | phrases | words | terms | null, used, limit, readChars }`; `counts` follow the entries. Send order lists likely read, then sent but likely not read, then not sent, each by rank; dividers go before the first unread ("The speech model stops reading here") and the first unsent ("Not sent beyond here") where something precedes them (`utils/dictionaryList.ts`).
- **Similar words** (`utils/dictionarySimilar.ts`, pure, shared with Tidy up): `similarWordsFor(word, words)` and `similarWordGroups(words)`: spelling variants (`spellingKey`: case, hyphens, spaces, dots and underscores removed), plural pairs (s, es, ies; a singular of 3+ letters) and near spellings (`editDistance` with a neighbour swap as one edit: ≤ 2 from 5 letters, ≤ 1 below), with the reason.
- **The page** (`DictionaryView.tsx`, `components/dictionary/`; Dictionary | Snippets | Glossary tabs, the add field, import and export, the hidden assistant name, the empty state and the Clear all / Remove all auto-learned confirms kept): the suggestions line, then **Where it goes** (`DictionaryWhereItGoes.tsx`): a strip of speech-to-text (setup · model, used of the limit, how many aren't sent, "Whisper reads the last ≈ 550 characters", Send order ›), the cleanup prompt (≈ tokens of the current set, the set, ≈ tokens a month from `perDay` × 30) and chat and the assistant (≈ tokens of the full list), opening into details with the mirrored `CleanupDictionaryWordsSetting` (every set's size) and the cleanup-set comparison; every estimate says ≈ (characters ÷ 4) with the plural its number needs: one plural key, `dictionary.tokens.approx_*` (and `approxCompact_*` for the monthly figure, `{{count, number(notation: compact; maximumFractionDigits: 1)}}`), selected by `count` = the raw estimate and shown through i18next's number formatter (`{{count, number}}`), rendered by `approxTokens(t, n, { compact })` (`dictionary/dictionaryLabels.ts`); every sentence carrying an estimate (Where it goes, the cleanup sets' "N words · ≈ …", both reviews' "One request, ≈ …", the categories' word-set note) takes it as `{{tokensText}}`. Each locale has its plural forms (Arabic all six, Russian one/few/many/other, Japanese and Chinese one/other alike), and French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese also `_many` for whole millions, which i18next would otherwise show in English. The toolbar: a word filter, a Category chip row and "🗂 Sort into categories" (Categories, below), chips with counts (All, Typed, Auto-learned, Pinned, Not sent, Speech misses it: `isSpeechMissed`, only with counts, Never used: in neither the raw transcript nor the final text in 90 days, only with counts, Added this week), a sort (send order, recently added, A–Z, most used, least used: by `written`, then `bothRows`; the usage ones need counts; the keys stay `mostHeard` / `leastHeard`), Import and Export. The list (`DictionaryWordList.tsx`): a multi-select listbox (`aria-activedescendant`; ↑/↓, Home/End, Space selects, Shift extends, Ctrl+A, Enter opens, F2 edits, Delete asks to remove, C opens the focused row's category menu, Esc closes the detail or clears the selection; the keys are spoken with it through `aria-describedby`, `dictionary.list.keyboardHelp`, pins or not, and named in `aria-keyshortcuts`), rows memoized and laid out only on screen (`content-visibility`), the search deferred; each row: checkbox (Shift-click selects a range), ☆ pin, the word, source, "Used · 90 days" (`UsageCell`: "405 · speech 256 · fixed 149" with a two-tone bar, green speech got it and amber fixed after speech, "not used", "—" without counts; its title adds changed after speech; the column header's ⓘ, `DictionaryUsageExplainer`, a Radix popover the keyboard reaches, explains used / speech got it / fixed after speech / changed after speech and the old heard and written), "added 3d", the coverage dot with "Sent · #n" / "Likely not read · #n" / "Not sent". **Pin order** (Dictionary v2 Stage B; `viewDictionaryList` answers `pinnedCount` only in send order with no filter and no search, else null and the pins show inline as before): the card opens with "📌 Always sent first" (what it does, "+ Pin a word", which takes the keyboard to the first unpinned word; pinning stays the ☆), the listbox's first group is the pins in pin order (each with `pinRank` #1, #2…, a ⋮⋮ handle and the usual columns; nothing pinned: one line saying what pinning does), then the group "Everything else" under its header: how it's ordered ("Ordered automatically: words speech misses most first, never-used words last." / "In the order they were added." / evidence chosen without counts yet) and "Order: by evidence ▾" (`DictionaryHintOrderMenu`, a radio menu writing `dictionaryHintOrder`), then the rest in send order with the cut-line dividers (pins above count as what precedes them). One listbox throughout, so ↑/↓, ranges and Ctrl+A cross both. A pinned row moves by HTML5 drag and drop (`draggable`, a drop line above or below the row by the pointer's half, `dragover` / `drop`), by Alt+↑/↓ (Alt+Home: to the top) on the focused pinned row, or by its ⋯ menu (Move to top, up, down; Edit spelling); each is one `reorderDictionaryPins` call with the full key order (a hidden pinned assistant name keeps its slot), optimistic, rolled back on a refusal with a toast, and announced in a polite live region ("Moved X to position N"). The bulk bar: Remove (asks), Mark typed, Pin, Unpin, Set category, Clear selection. **Word detail** (`DictionaryWordDetail.tsx`): beside the list from 900 px of content width, over it below (the list inert, focus moved in); Esc or ✕ closes it and focus returns to the row, which the detail follows while it's open beside the list. It shows source, pin and date; **Used in the last 90 days** ("405 dictations · speech got it 256 · fixed after speech 149 · changed after speech 2", the ⓘ) with the week chart (`DictionaryUsageHistogram`, bounds in `usageWeeks.ts`: stacked green / amber / red bars, each a button named with its week and numbers; hover or keyboard focus shows a tooltip with the range from the week's own `[start, end)`, used, speech got it, fixed and changed; a click filters "Where it appeared" to that week, shown there as a removable chip; unavailable says why); **Speech writes it as** (`DictionarySpeechVariants`); **Where it appeared** (`DictionaryWordOccurrences`); where it goes (speech model with its rank and, for a pin, "Always sent first · #2", whether cleanup's set includes it and why, chat always), what it replaced (this owner's evidence, each with Open in History, `requestOpenHistoryItem`), similar words (each opens), and Pin/Unpin, Mark typed, Edit spelling, Make a glossary rule (`GlossaryRuleDialog` with the word as canonical, or the rule that already writes it) and Remove. Everything it reads from History is read again on History, dictionary and snippet changes, and dropped at once on `dictionary-history-changed` (owner or policy), its sections remounted; answers to an older request are dropped. "Open in Dictionary" (History's card, `utils/openDictionaryWord.ts`: `requestOpenDictionaryWord` → ControlPanel leaves Settings, `setDictionaryWordRequest`, the Dictionary view) opens the word's panel once the word, by key, is in the list.
- **Where words appeared** (Dictionary v2 Stage A; `helpers/dictionaryOccurrences.js`, ESM, pure core plus main's `createDictionaryOccurrencesService`): two layers. Row summaries (the usage matcher) decide which rows a word is in: counts, filters, candidates. Aligned spans say what happened at each place: the raw transcript and the final text (snippet expansions blanked by as many spaces, `maskExpansions`, so offsets stay the real text's) are tokenized as `usageTokens` does with offsets (`offsetTokens`), aligned word by word (common start and end set aside, then an LCS of the rest, capped at `ALIGNMENT_MAX_CELLS` 4M cells like `utils/textDiff.ts`; over it the row has no appearances, `aligned: false`), and each place a word's token sequence occurs is an appearance: `ok` (raw and final at aligned positions; case and punctuation alone are the same word, `ariaevent/view` = `AriaEvent.view`), `fix` (in final, the aligned raw span differs) or `chg` (in raw, the aligned final span differs). `fix` offsets are into the final text, `chg` into raw. `speechWrote` (a fix): the raw span from the first token's raw match (or the start of its changed run) to the last's, as written (case and punctuation kept), ≤ 4 tokens; null when a changed run reaches outside the word (ambiguous), speech wrote nothing there (an insertion), or longer; when the word is one changed run of 2–8 raw tokens, words merely dropped beside it are trimmed off (the contiguous part closest in letters, only when strictly closer and at most half its letters differ: "sean and" → "sean"; "ash varia", "work tree", "LittleBuddy" stay whole). A row's kinds are a set ("Little Buddy and Littbuddy": ok and fix). Row summaries and alignment agree except for mixed rows (the summary counts one "speech got it") and unaligned rows; a final-only row is all `fix` and a raw-only row all `chg` without aligning (only the cap is checked). **Attribution** (`by`, a fix only, `attributeFix`) only from this owner's recorded steps for the row: `glossary` when a glossary step's `detail.rulesFired` has a rule whose `to` is the word (`dictionaryWordKey`), else `cleanup` / `style` when that step's input (`stepChanges`) lacks the word's tokens and its output has them, else null; never from raw against final. **Ownership contract**: raw and final text follow History's account rule (the account in view's rows plus unattributed ones, `_historyScope()`), so snippets do too; the app, `by` and the assistant-row exclusion come only from the current owner's steps; the Dictionary's occurrences and variants are additionally gated by durable History for the owner (`usageAvailability`), as usage is. IPC (`ipcHandlers.js`; logs counts and codes only, never a word, a snippet or what speech wrote, whatever "Include dictated text" says): `dictionary-word-occurrences(word, { kind?, range?: { start, end }, cursor?, limit })` → `{ available, reason?, generation, items: [{ id, at, app, kinds, speechWrote, by, snippet: { kind, before, match, after } }], nextCursor, totals: { all, ok, fix, chg }, unaligned }`: the usage rows (`getDictionaryOccurrenceRows`: `getDictionaryUsageRows`' rows with id and History's sort key, in History's order `COALESCE(timestamp,'') DESC, id DESC`, the cursor `{ timestamp, id }` as `history-list`'s), limit 1–50 (20); one word's index (candidates and their kinds) is built once and cached (16 words) by owner, day, word, expansions, dictionary generation and History's content revision, aligning only rows with the word in both texts; a range filters by `created_at` in `[start, end)` and the totals follow it (a mixed row counts in each kind); a page re-reads its rows' texts (`getDictionaryOccurrenceTexts`, a row deleted meanwhile is skipped) and aligns them for the snippet (≤ 60 characters a side, cut at word boundaries, "…" within the limit; the first appearance of the requested kind, else fix, ok, chg; `chg` from raw) with the app and `by` from this owner's steps (`getHistoryStepRows`). `dictionary-speech-variants(word, { spelling? })` → `{ available, generation, variants: [{ spelling, count, keptInFinal }], requested? }`: the top 5 spellings speech wrote over the word's `fix` appearances (grouped by `spellingKey`, shown in their most common written form, `count` the dictations), `keptInFinal` the same rows whose final text (row summaries) still has that spelling; `requested` is one spelling's own numbers (History's card). Both re-check owner, policy and History's revision before answering: an owner or policy change meanwhile answers `{ available: false, reason }`, a History change reads again once. Main broadcasts `dictionary-history-changed` (`{ reason: "owner" | "policy" }`) from `_dictionaryOwnerMaybeChanged` and the retention sync, and invalidates the caches. **Speech writes it as** (`DictionarySpeechVariants`): each variant with its count and "“X” also stayed as written in N dictations" / "Never stayed as written"; **Make a glossary rule** (`utils/dictionaryGlossaryOffer.ts`, `GlossaryOfferNotice`) opens `GlossaryRuleDialog`: a rule that already has the spelling opens as it is, one that writes the word gets the spelling added, else a new rule with the word as canonical; the spelling's scope is `anywhere` only when `keptInFinal` is 0 and its lowercase tokens aren't all in `config/commonEnglishWords.json` (loaded with `loadCommonWords`; unknown counts as common), else `start` with a notice that says why (kept, everyday words, or unknown without History) and to pick apps or reconsider; a spelling an enabled rule already maps to the word shows "The glossary handles this" (`glossaryHandles`). Variants are read again when the glossary's revision changes. **Where it appeared** (`DictionaryWordOccurrences`): All / Fixed after speech / Speech got it / Changed with the totals, the week chip, rows (date, app, kinds, "by the glossary" / "by cleanup" / "by a style", the snippet with the match highlighted and what speech wrote struck through) that open in History (`requestOpenHistoryItem`), Load more, a note for unaligned dictations, and **Show in History** (no count; `requestOpenHistoryForWord` → History's `dictionaryWord` filter, 13d); unavailable says why. No proactive callout.
- **Usage** (was "heard vs written"; `helpers/dictionaryUsage.js`, ESM; IPC `dictionary-usage`; renderer `stores/dictionaryUsageStore.ts`): per word over the completed, non-deleted dictations of the last 90 days that History shows (`getDictionaryUsageRows`, `_historyScope()`: the account in view's rows plus unattributed ones; never translations, nor the owner's assistant rows by steps; the occurrence, evidence, Tidy up and cleanup-set queries read the same rows), `heard` (raw transcript has it), `written` (final text, with snippet expansions removed; the page's **used**), `bothRows` (raw and final both have it; **speech got it**), `missedRows` (paired per row: final has it, raw doesn't; **fixed after speech**); **changed after speech** is `heard − bothRows`. The matcher returns `heard`, `written`, `missed`, `both` and `changed` per row. A token index (lowercase word tokens, an ASCII fast path, multi-token words matched as sequences from their first token), about 50 ms for 5,000 rows × 250 words. Only while history is durable for the owner (`{ available: false, reason }` otherwise). Cached by owner, day, words and expansions plus `history_content_revision` (one counter bumped by triggers on every `transcriptions` insert, text/status/deletion update and delete, `_initHistoryContentRevision`), recomputed at most every 30 s while it changes; `invalidate()` on a retention-settings or owner change. `isSpeechMissed`: ≥ 2 missed rows, or ≥ 25% of the rows that wrote it. Counts only cross IPC; 0/0 is unknown. The speech-to-text hint list's evidence order reads the same counts (`getDictionaryHintScores`, derived from the store's words while `status` is ready: `hintScoreTuples`, section 13). Tidy up's texts and the similar words say used and speech got it too.
- **Cleanup word set** (`cleanupDictionaryWords`, section 5; `selectCleanupDictionary(settings, sources?, glossaryTerms?)` in `utils/snippets.ts` → `{ words, scope: { setting, applied, words }, groups? }`): `noEveryday` = every dictionary word whose effective category isn't Everyday words (`sources.categories`, the categories store's `getDictionaryCategoryLookup()`, null until ready); `typedPinned` = typed words plus pinned learned ones; `typedPinnedMissed` adds learned words speech often misses (usage store); `none` = no dictionary words; glossary canonical forms and snippet triggers always follow. The set filters only the user's own words: teammates' shared words (#2855) follow them for every set but `none`, capped at 200 words / 2,000 characters, and a teammate's copy never brings back a personal word the set left out; category groups hold only the user's own words, and the prompt names teammates' words in upstream's separate sentence (`config/prompts/index.ts`). OpenWhispr Cloud gets the split as `customDictionary` + `sharedDictionary` (`toCloudDictionaryFields`). Agent, translation, chat and notes use upstream's `getLlmDictionaryWords` / `getCloudLlmDictionaryFields`, which include the glossary. Until the sources load, without counts for `typedPinnedMissed`, or before the categories are ready for `noEveryday`, it applies `all` and the scope says so. With `cleanupDictionaryGrouping` on and the categories ready, `groups` (`[{ group: "people" | "work" | "everyday" | "other", words }]`, that order, empty ones left out, each in the flat list's order) holds the same flat words: dictionary words and glossary canonical forms by their effective category, unsorted words and snippet triggers under Other; grouping off (or not ready) adds no `groups` key at all, so the snapshot is the old one. The cleanup request carries it as `ReasoningConfig.cleanupDictionary` (`buildDictationCleanupConfig`, so ordinary cleanup, the translation chain's cleanup step, streaming, History's Re-run cleanup, Prompt Studio's cleanup Test and both style modes' cleanup share one snapshot per request; the prompt test set freezes it with `captureCleanupPromptInputs` → `promptInputs.groups`); `processText` strips it and, for a request without a `systemPrompt` or `promptInputs`, builds the prompt's dictionary (grouped when it has `groups`, 13c) and OpenWhispr Cloud's `customDictionary` (always the flat `words`) from it. Cloud cleanup paths in `audioManager.js` read the route's snapshot (`route.config.cleanupDictionary`, `_runCleanupStep`'s `cleanupConfig`; `selectCleanupDictionary(settings)` only when a route has none) and pass its flat `words`. Translation, the assistant, selection edits, chat and restyles (their own system prompt) keep the full list. The trace records `detail.dictionaryScope` on the `cleanup` step, or on the `style` step of a one-step style (`cleanupStepMeta`); `normalizeTrace` keeps it there only (enums, `noEveryday` included, and an integer 0–10,000), beside style detail and `failure`. View steps shows it ("all but everyday words"). Settings shows each choice's word count and estimated tokens (`estimateDictionaryTokens`, characters ÷ 4) (`CleanupDictionaryWordsSetting.tsx`, which loads the common-word list when it shows) and, below it, the grouping switch with each group's size (`CleanupDictionaryGroupingSetting.tsx`, "Not used with OpenWhispr Cloud").
- **Categories** (Dictionary v2 Stage C; People, Work terms, Everyday words, or unsorted):
  - **The rules** (`utils/dictionaryCategories.ts`, pure, `classifyDictionaryWord(word, commonWords)`, the **auto** category, first match wins): **work** when the word has a digit, one of `. _ / -` between two characters or a `#` after one (`C#`), a capital after a token's first letter (`SuperApp`, `IDEAs`, `AriaEvent.view`), or only capitals, two letters or more (`PR`, `CED`); **everyday** when every whitespace token, lowercased, is a letters-only word of the common-word list (`The`, `Ideas`); **people** when it starts with a capital and isn't everyday words (`Little Buddy`, `Dmitry`, and products the list doesn't know, `Kusto`, `Aria`: what the review is for); else **unsorted** (`kusto`, `groq`, `worktree`). Without the list only the work rule runs. Improving the rules re-sorts every automatic word without a migration. **Effective category** (`effectiveDictionaryCategory`): the stored choice, else the auto one, as `{ category, source: "user" | "model" | "auto" }`. `groupCleanupDictionaryWords` / `formatCleanupDictionaryGroups` build the grouped prompt (13c).
  - **Renderer** (`stores/dictionaryCategoriesStore.ts`, which never imports the settings store): main's stored choices (`dictionary-categories-get`, its broadcast, re-read on `dictionary-updated`) and the common-word list (`utils/commonWords.ts`'s one shared lazy load, also Tidy up's: loaded by the Dictionary page, the cleanup settings rows, or at startup when `noEveryday` or grouping is on, `initializeSettings`); `effectiveCategory(word)`, `ready` (both here), `getDictionaryCategoryLookup()` (null until ready, for `selectCleanupDictionary`), `setDictionaryCategories(items)`.
  - **The page**: a Category column with a pill (`DictionaryCategoryPill.tsx`: a dot and the name or "Unsorted", who decided it (auto, you, model), a Radix menu of People, Work terms, Everyday words (stored as the user's) and "Automatic (People)" (the stored choice removed); in rows not a tab stop (the listbox is the one): **C** on the focused row (no modifiers, not while composing; the C key of a non-Latin layout too) opens that row's menu anchored to its pill with the current choice focused, the list holding which row's menu is open (the pill's controlled `open` / `onOpenChange`; a click on a row's pill also makes that row the active one), and closing it (a pick, Esc, a click away) prevents Radix's `onCloseAutoFocus` and focuses the listbox, so `aria-activedescendant` still names the row; the row pill's tooltip ends "(C)" (`dictionary.categories.pill.titleRow`); typing in the portalled menu never reaches the list (its keys act only on the listbox itself); in the word panel's header a regular button; the row's accessible name ends with its category); a "Category" chip row (All, People, Work terms, Everyday words, Unsorted, with counts once ready; it combines with the other chips and search, and send order's pin sections need it on All); the bulk bar's **Set category** (one `setDictionaryCategories` call, the user's); the word panel's cleanup line says what's true for the current set ("No: “All words except everyday words” leaves out everyday words", "Yes: not an everyday word") and, grouped, "Listed under “People”"; "Where it goes" mirrors both settings and says "grouped by category".
  - **"🗂 Sort into categories"** (the toolbar, with how many words are unsorted; it replaces the list like Tidy up, Esc or "Back to the list" returns; `DictionaryCategories.tsx`): four cards with counts and one-line descriptions, the unsorted words each with a pick (the user's), **Ask a model**, and the notes (filter and bulk edit; the cleanup prompt's grouping, on or off, and the word set's saving, ≈ words and tokens, each with Open in Settings: `DictionaryView`'s `onOpenSettings("cleanup#dictation.cleanup.dictionaryGrouping")`; Tidy up unchanged; categories stay on this computer).
  - **Ask a model** (`services/dictionaryCategoryReview.ts` over Tidy up's `reviewModelOptions` / `runDictionaryReview`: the same models, reachability and Copilot rule, `maxTokens` 4096, temperature 0 except direct Gemini, `redactLogs`, `requireCompleteOutput`, `yieldToDictation`, a `requestId`; `utils/dictionaryCategoryReview.ts`; prompt `config/prompts/dictionaryCategoryReview.ts`, English, fixed, asking for at most 80 proposals with reasons in the UI language; state in `stores/dictionaryCategoryReviewStore.ts`): the request carries only the listed words, each with its effective category (or `unsorted`) and who set it (`["Kusto","people","auto"]`), never a count, History, a transcript, evidence or an app; the ≈ tokens show before sending. The reply (`{ "proposals": [{ "word", "category", "reason" }] }`) is read leniently (a code fence, the first balanced `{…}`) and validated strictly: over 200,000 characters or 300 proposals drops it all (oversized), no `proposals` array (malformed); a word not sent (matched exactly, or by key when only one sent word has it), a category other than the three, a reason with a line break or over 200 characters, or a word already named (the later one) is dropped; kept and dropped are counted. A reply is dropped whole when the dictionary or its categories changed while it ran (`dictionaryCategoryRevision`: every word, its effective category and source, taken at send); Cancel aborts a local model's request and discards any other. Proposals show current → proposed with the reason: equal ones as "No change", ones over a choice of yours as "Your choice kept" (Accept is still there), **Accept** stores one as the model's (`source: "model"`), **Accept all** the rest that differ and aren't yours, ✕ dismisses for the session; nothing is stored without a click; leaving the page cancels a review in flight.
- **Tidy up** (the Dictionary toolbar's "Tidy up", with how many suggestions start selected; it replaces the list in the page, Esc or "Back to the list" returns): checks that run on this computer, an optional model review, one-batch apply and a session Undo. Nothing changes without a click; nothing is logged but counts and codes.
  - **History's aggregates** (`dictionaryUsage.js` `countTidyInputs` / `computeDictionaryTidyInputs`, service `getTidyInputs`, IPC `dictionary-tidy-inputs` → `{ available, reason?, days, rows, frequentTokens: [{ token, rows }], adjacency: [{ a, b, together, rarerRows }] }`): one pass over the usage rows (`getDictionaryUsageRows`, final text with snippet expansions removed) with the same token index: the top 5,000 lowercase tokens by the rows they're in, and, for every pair of dictionary words that ever sit side by side (one's token sequence ends where the other's starts; two spellings of one sequence never pair), the rows with them side by side in either order (`a` the one usually first, at most 5,000 pairs) and the rows containing the rarer one. Words and counts only, by the usage rule (`usageAvailability`: this owner, durable History only; the owner's assistant rows and translations aren't read), cached like the totals (owner, day, words, expansions, content revision). About 40 ms for 5,000 rows × 250 words (budget 300 ms). The renderer mirrors it in `stores/dictionaryTidyStore.ts`, refreshed on `dictionary-updated` and History changes.
  - **The common-word list** (`src/config/commonEnglishWords.json`, loaded on first use as its own chunk through `utils/commonWords.ts`, one load shared by Tidy up, the glossary offer and the categories; `scripts/generate-common-words.js` regenerates it): SCOWL 2020.12.07's en_US and en_GB "words" lists at sizes 10 and 20 (`english-words`, `american-words`, `british-words`), lowercased, letters only, two letters or more, deduped: 11,111 words. Licence and sources in `commonEnglishWords.LICENSE.txt` (SCOWL's notice verbatim). Generated data: `src/.prettierignore` skips it.
  - **The checks** (`utils/dictionaryTidy.ts`, pure, `computeTidyGroups`): groups in this order, a word in one group at most (never heard aside), each capped at 200 items; only auto-learned, unpinned words start selected; glossary canonical forms and snippet triggers (by key) are never suggested for removal or merging away (a variant pair with one is locked to keep it). **Same word, two spellings** (`similarWordGroups` variants): keep one, by default the one written more often, else typed, else the newest; the rest merge into it. **Singular and plural**: the same, plus "Neither" (remove both, the default) when both are ordinary words. **Split phrases**: two dictionary words side by side in ≥ 80% of the ≥ 5 dictations with the rarer one: add "A B" typed (or use the dictionary's spelling of it when there is one) and remove both parts; it runs before ordinary words, so two everyday words that form a name ("Little" + "Buddy") are offered as the phrase. **Ordinary words**: in auto-learn's `COMMON_WORDS` (`utils/correctionCommonWords.js`, shared with `correctionLearner.js`), or in the common-word list with heard ≥ 3 and `missedRows` 0; without counts only the first part runs and the group says so (0/0 is never "speech gets it right"). **Likely typos**: auto-learned, not a common word, heard + written ≤ 1, within edit distance 1–2 (1 under five letters, a swap counts once) of a History token in ≥ 5 dictations (remove, or "Change to" it in the word's case) or of another dictionary word (respell into it: a merge). **Fragments**: `...`/`…`, `,`, `;` or `/` inside, digits only, a leading or trailing hyphen or dot, one character. **Never heard in 90 days**: auto-learned, heard 0 and written 0, added over 30 days ago; Keep or Remove, never selected.
