Typos, split phrases and never heard need this account's local History and say so ("Needs this account's local History", with why) instead of running on zeros. `tidyItemOps(item, choice)` turns an item and the user's choice into batch operations.
  - **Applying** (IPC `dictionary-tidy-apply(ops)`, `database.applyDictionaryTidy`; renderer `settingsStore.applyDictionaryTidy`, which adopts the stored list and pushes to OpenWhispr Cloud; `dictionaryTidyStore.applyTidy`): one transaction, each operation in its own savepoint, through the dictionary's own operations: remove (applyDictionaryChanges' delete path, `_deleteDictionaryRow` then the pins pruned, aimed at the word's own live row), merge (`mergeDictionaryWords`), respell (`renameDictionaryWord`, merging into the existing word on `EXISTS`), add-phrase (`applyDictionaryChanges` add as typed, then the parts removed). An item's buttons apply it alone; "Apply N selected" (a group's, or the footer's for every shown group and the model's) sends one batch. **Planned first** (`planTidyBatch(ops, liveWords)` in `utils/dictionaryTidy.ts`, pure, called by the page's apply for an item and for "Apply N selected"; main runs ops in the order given, so a removal before "respell typo → that word" would bring the word back, and a chain in the wrong order re-creates its middle): every word named gets one fate by **exact spelling** (main finds a word by exact spelling before key): removed (a removal, or an add-phrase's part), moved (respell, merge) or kept. A removal and a move of one word: the removal wins; duplicates collapse; two moves of one word to different places (destinations landing on the same word are one), and every move in a cycle (a→b, b→a, longer ones too), are conflicting choices: skipped, the words kept, counted. A move follows its destination's own fate to the end (the exact spelling first; else the one known word with its key, excluding the mover, as main's rename resolves it, unless that word moves to this very spelling too, so case variants respelled to one new spelling aren't a cycle); one that ends at a removed word becomes a removal of its source. A phrase whose spelling the batch removes or moves isn't added; its parts are still removed. Emitted: the phrases (adding removes their parts), then every surviving move as a `respell` from its exact source to its final destination (main renames, or merges on `EXISTS`, so no merge runs before its target exists), ordered by chain depth (the word a chain's last step renames first, the rest merge into it), then **one** `remove` with every other removal, so Undo restores them in one snapshot. Each applied operation returns its Undo steps with the full prior state; the footer then says what main applied ("Removed 8 · Merged 2 · Added 1 · Kept 1", a respell that merged counted as merged, a move turned removal as removed) and "N skipped: conflicting choices" when the plan skipped any (`TidySummary.skipped`; `applyTidy(ops, { skipped })`, which says so even when nothing is left to send). "Keep" hides an item for the session.
  - **Undo** (IPC `dictionary-tidy-undo(steps)`, `database.undoDictionaryTidy`): a stack in the renderer store, one entry per apply, kept until the app restarts; Undo (the last) and "Undo all (N)". Steps run last first in one transaction, all or none: `restoreDictionaryWords([{ word, source, createdAt, pinned, pinPosition, category, categorySource }], { into, expect, remove, unpin, repin, recategorize })` reactivates a tombstoned row the account can take back (the rows in view's rule; the same row and cloud identity, its date kept) or inserts a hard-deleted one again with its original `created_at`, stamped with the signed-in account like every insert, each with its source and pin, marked for sync (`pending`: a new row pushes as an add, a tombstone whose deletion wasn't pushed yet as an edit of its cloud entry); the snapshot (`_dictionaryWordState`) holds the pin's **place** (`pinPosition`), not only whether it was pinned, and a restored pin goes back to it (`_insertDictionaryPinAt`: a free place is taken as is, a taken one pushes that pin and every one after it down by one; restored highest first, so a reorder since doesn't scramble them; no place: last); a merge's step first unpins the word it went into when only the merge pinned it, or, when both were pinned and the merge moved `into` up to `from`'s place, puts it back at its own (`repin: [{ word, position }]`), then restores `from` at its place; the snapshot also holds the word's stored **category** and who chose it (`category`, `categorySource`, null for automatic): a restored word gets it back unless its key has one now (a case variant it was merged into keeps its own), and a merge that gave `into` `from`'s stronger category puts `into`'s own back first (`recategorize: [{ word, category, source }]`, null: automatic); a phrase's step removes the phrase Tidy up added; a rename's step renames back by exact spelling (`renameDictionaryWord(…, { exact: true })`). Refused, with the reason, when the word was changed since (`EDITED`), when another live word in view now has the spelling (`EXISTS`; the case variant it was merged into doesn't count, and neither do the rows this Undo already put back, `restoredIds`, which `undoDictionaryTidy` keeps across its steps: they were in the dictionary together before the action, e.g. a phrase's part and its case variant removed by two steps of one batch; two same-key spellings in one snapshot were already restored together, since the check reads the live rows before any insert) or when the word it was merged into is gone (`INTO_GONE`). Undo can't bring back a merged word's evidence (it comes from History) and doesn't demote a word a merge made typed (a demotion can't sync); the footer says the first.
  - **Ask a model** (`services/dictionaryReview.ts`, `utils/dictionaryReview.ts`, prompt `config/prompts/dictionaryReview.ts`, English and not in the prompt library): a select of the configured models that are reachable (the Cleanup model as a dictation's cleanup resolves it, the Chat model by `selectResolvedLLMConfig(…, "chatIntelligence")`, the Voice Assistant model by `resolveDictationAgentInference`, policy-effective settings), one per provider · model (and endpoint), GitHub Copilot only for scopes it serves (`copilotRequestRefusal`), Cleanup's first. Before sending it shows the word count and ≈ tokens (characters ÷ 4 of the system prompt and the message). The request (`ReasoningService.processText` with the review prompt as `systemPrompt`, the option's scope and provider, `maxTokens` 4096, temperature 0 except direct Gemini, `redactLogs`, `requireCompleteOutput`, `yieldToDictation`, a `requestId`) carries only the dictionary's words, each one's source (typed / auto) and pin, and heard/written/missed when counts are available (none at all otherwise): never a transcript, evidence, an app or a History token. The reply is read leniently (a code fence stripped, the first balanced `{…}`) and validated strictly: over 200,000 characters or over 300 suggestions drops it all (oversized), no `suggestions` array drops it all (malformed); a suggestion with an unknown action, a word not sent (matched exactly, or by key when only one word has it), a word already named, an `into` that isn't one line of 1–100 characters, a reason over 200 characters, or a change to a protected spelling is dropped; kept and dropped are counted. A reply is dropped whole when the dictionary (words, sources, pins: `dictionaryRevision`) changed while it ran. Cancel stops waiting (a local model's request is aborted with `cancelLocalReasoning`; any other reply is discarded). Errors show through `describeProviderError` (with Open Settings where it applies). The suggestions are the "Model suggestions" group, none selected: remove, respell, merge (into one of its words, into a dictionary word, or a new spelling: the first respelled, the rest merged into it) and keep (shown, nothing to apply); a suggestion whose words left the dictionary disappears.
- Tests: `test/helpers/dictionaryStateDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node: pins, suggestions, evidence lifecycle, content revision, usage rows), `test/helpers/dictionaryEditsDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node: rename, case-only, clash, a deleted row's spelling, merge, Mark typed, weekly counts, the cleanup-set comparison), `test/helpers/dictionaryTidyDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node: restore of a tombstoned and a hard-deleted row, a batch and its Undo, refusals and all-or-nothing, a legacy case variant, the tidy aggregates and their durability; planned batches in both input orders with what survives, what main applied and an Undo that brings every word back: the reported case (unplanned it resurrects the word), keep one plus a model respell, chains, 2- and 3-cycles, an existing phrase respelled with its split-phrase item, a move into a part, remove-and-move, two moves of one word, `CLAUDE`→`Claude` with `Claude` removed (pin and category back once), NFC-equivalent spellings, a phrase's part and its case variant across two steps), `ipcAutoLearnModes.test.js`, `dictionaryUsage.test.js` (also the tidy aggregates and their timing), `autoLearnSetting.test.js`, `audioManagerCleanupDictionary.test.js`, `stepHistory.test.js`, `sttHintParity.test.js` (the page's coverage against each captured retry request), `test/utils/cleanupDictionarySelection.test.js`, `test/utils/sttHintFields.test.js`, `test/utils/sttHintSpec.test.js`, `test/utils/dictionaryList.test.js`, `test/utils/dictionarySimilar.test.js`, `test/utils/dictionaryTidy.test.js` (every check on the measured patterns, priorities, preselection, the common-word list; `planTidyBatch`'s scenarios, each in both input orders), `test/utils/dictionaryReview.test.js` (the request, lenient parsing, strict validation, staleness), `test/components/dictionaryViewRender.test.js`, `test/components/dictionaryTidyRender.test.js` (groups, preselection, Apply as one planned batch, Undo and its refusal, conflicting choices skipped with "1 skipped: conflicting choices", History unavailable, Esc, the model request's canary, stale and cancelled replies), `test/components/dictionaryTokenPlurals.test.js` (the token estimate in every UI language with the real locale files: English 1 token / 2 tokens / compact 1.3K, Arabic's six forms, Russian 1, 2, 5, 21, million-scale compact figures, the form each count selects present in every locale so none falls back to English, every estimate sentence taking `{{tokensText}}`), `test/services/reasoningServiceCleanupDictionary.test.js`, `test/stores/dictionarySettings.test.js`, `test/utils/dictionaryHints.test.js`, `logTextCanary.test.js` (also the occurrence, variant and History dictionary handlers: no word, snippet or what speech wrote in any log). Stage A: `test/helpers/dictionaryOccurrences.test.js` (kinds and `speechWrote` on the measured patterns, mixed rows, insertions, ambiguous rewrites, case and punctuation, the cap, expansion-skipped offsets, row summaries vs alignment, snippets, attribution, the service's paging, totals, week bounds, variants, durability, an owner switch, policy off and a deletion mid-request, a counts-only canary, timing), `test/helpers/dictionaryOccurrencesDatabase.test.js` (Electron's Node with `REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1`: occurrences and paging, app and `by` only from the current owner's steps, durability, the generation's triggers, the SQL filters frozen per generation and a stale later page, the History card and badge, the 10,000-row × 250-word benchmark), `test/utils/dictionaryGlossaryOffer.test.js` (`keptInFinal` and everyday words → the scope prefill, existing rules, handled), `test/components/dictionaryOccurrencesRender.test.js` (the usage column and its explainer, the chip, the chart's tooltip and click-to-filter chip, the occurrence list and segments, Show in History, the variants and the rule prefill, glossary enable/edit/delete after reading, an owner change mid-request, Open in Dictionary), `test/components/historyDictionaryRender.test.js` (the History card, underlines, the cleanup step's marks, the chip and badge, the word chip, stale pages and dictionary/snippet changes over loaded pages). ### 14. GNOME Wayland Global Hotkeys

On GNOME Wayland, Electron's `globalShortcut` API doesn't work due to Wayland's security model. OpenWhispr uses native GNOME shortcuts:

**Architecture**:

1. `main.js` enables `GlobalShortcutsPortal` feature flag for Wayland
2. `hotkeyManager.js` detects GNOME + Wayland and initializes `GnomeShortcutManager`
3. `gnomeShortcut.js` creates D-Bus service at `com.openwhispr.App`
4. Shortcuts registered via `gsettings` as custom GNOME keybindings
5. GNOME triggers `dbus-send` command which calls the D-Bus `Toggle()` method

**Key Constants**:

- D-Bus service: `com.openwhispr.App`
- D-Bus path: `/com/openwhispr/App`
- gsettings path: `/org/gnome/settings-daemon/plugins/media-keys/custom-keybindings/openwhispr/`

**IPC Integration**:

- `get-hotkey-mode-info`: Returns `{ isUsingGnome, isUsingHyprland, isUsingNativeShortcut }` to renderer
- UI hides activation mode selector when `isUsingNativeShortcut` is true
- Forces tap-to-talk mode (push-to-talk not supported)

**Hotkey Format Conversion**:

- Electron format: `F8`, `CommandOrControl+Shift+Space`
- GNOME format: `F8`, `<Control><Shift>space`
- Backtick (`) → `grave` in GNOME keysym format
- F13-F24 bind the keysym the active keymap gives that key (`xkbcomp -xkb $DISPLAY -`, xkb keys `<FK13>`-`<FK24>`), for gsettings and the Hold portal alike: stock xkeyboard-config sends `XF86Launch7` for F16 and has no `F16` keysym, so GNOME can't grab `F16` (#1716). A key with no keysym (`keysym_missing`, e.g. stock F19/F24) or one gsd-media-keys already binds (`keysym_reserved`, e.g. F13 → `XF86Tools` opens Settings) is refused with a message. An unreadable keymap keeps the F-key name

### 15. Hyprland Wayland Global Hotkeys

On Hyprland (wlroots Wayland compositor), Electron's `globalShortcut` API and the `GlobalShortcutsPortal` feature don't work reliably. OpenWhispr uses native Hyprland keybindings:

**Architecture**:

1. `main.js` enables `GlobalShortcutsPortal` feature flag for Wayland (fallback)
2. `hotkeyManager.js` detects Hyprland + Wayland and initializes `HyprlandShortcutManager`
3. `hyprlandShortcut.js` creates D-Bus service at `com.openwhispr.App` (same as GNOME)
4. Shortcuts registered via `hyprctl keyword bind` (runtime keybinding)
5. Hyprland triggers `dbus-send` command which calls the D-Bus `Toggle()` method

**Detection**:

- Primary: `HYPRLAND_INSTANCE_SIGNATURE` environment variable (set by Hyprland)
- Fallback: `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` contains "hyprland"

**Hotkey Format Conversion**:

- Electron format: `Control+Super`, `CommandOrControl+Shift+Space`
- Hyprland format: `CTRL, Super_L`, `CTRL SHIFT, space`
- Modifier-only combos (e.g., `Control+Super`) → `CTRL, Super_L`

**Bind/Unbind Commands**:

- Register: `hyprctl keyword bind "ALT, R, exec, dbus-send --session ..."`
- Unregister: `hyprctl keyword unbind "ALT, R"`
- Bindings are ephemeral (don't survive Hyprland restart) but re-registered on app startup

**Limitations**:

- Push-to-talk not supported (Hyprland `bind` fires a single exec, not key-down/key-up)
- Requires `hyprctl` on PATH (ships with Hyprland)

### 16. Meeting Detection (Event-Driven)

Detects meetings via three independent sources, orchestrated by `MeetingDetectionEngine`:

**Architecture**:

- `MeetingDetectionEngine` listens to events from `MeetingProcessDetector` and `AudioActivityDetector`
- `CalendarReminderScheduler` provides calendar context (imminent events, active meetings) from the shared `calendar_events` table, fed by the Google/Microsoft/Apple calendar managers
- All three sources feed into a unified notification pipeline

**Process Detection** (known meeting apps — Zoom, Teams, Webex, FaceTime):

- macOS: `systemPreferences.subscribeWorkspaceNotification` — zero CPU, instant detection
- Windows/Linux: `processListCache` shared polling (30s interval, `ps-list` npm)
- Context-only: a running meeting app never prompts by itself (FaceTime idles in the background). It corroborates unattributed macOS device activity (below) and drives the auto-end process-exit fast path

**Microphone Detection** (unscheduled/browser meetings like Google Meet):

- macOS: `macos-mic-listener` binary — CoreAudio process-object input monitoring, excluding the background `com.apple.CoreSpeech` service (it runs input during ordinary playback). `CAPABILITY PID` transitions prompt on their own. `CAPABILITY AGGREGATE` device activity (no process objects — roughly macOS < 14.2 — or a transient snapshot failure) includes playback on combined input/output devices, so it prompts only while a known meeting app is running; the helper retries PID monitoring every 30s from its heartbeat and the detector accepts the later `CAPABILITY PID`. Capability is logged at info level; `sustained-audio-detected` carries `attributed`
- Windows: `windows-mic-listener.exe` — WASAPI `IAudioSessionManager2` session monitoring; self-mic exclusion happens in JavaScript using current OpenWhispr and capture-helper PIDs
- Linux: `pactl subscribe` — PulseAudio source-output events. Production captures (`media.role=production`, e.g. OBS) and sink-monitor captures (`stream.capture.sink=true`) still hold the mic for auto-end but don't prompt; the unfiltered fallbacks (a failed or JSON-less `pactl` listing, a failed excluded-pid lookup while polling, `pw-cli`) can't tell them apart
- Windows/Linux: Fall back to polling if the native binary/command is unavailable. macOS has no safe polling signal: a missing binary pauses audio prompts, a crashed listener is respawned with exponential backoff (5s → 60s), and meanwhile only calendar reminders prompt while auto-end uses its existing silence fallback

**Calendar Reminders** (scheduled meetings):

- `CalendarReminderScheduler` fires `meetingDetectionEngine.handleCalendarReminder(event)` 1 minute before the scheduled start (`MEETING_REMINDER_LEAD_MS`) — no native OS notifications; all meeting prompts use the in-app overlay so they survive Focus/DND and screen-share notification muting
- Calendar-sourced prompts show a Join primary action when the event has a meeting link (`getMeetingJoinUrl` in `src/helpers/meetingJoinUrl.js`, shared with the renderer's Upcoming Meetings join button) — Join opens the link and starts the note

**UX Rules**:

- All prompts render in one always-on-top overlay window (`MeetingNotificationCard`), content-protected so it never appears in screen shares
- Prompt copy is derived in the renderer from `{ variant, event, joinUrl }` (`meetingNotification.*` i18n keys); variants: `detected` (mic evidence), `starting` (calendar event not yet started), `underway` (event in progress)
- Per-source notification prefs: `notifyCalendarReminders` gates calendar prompts, `notifyMeetingDetection` gates mic/process prompts and, with `notificationsEnabled`, whether the mic and process detectors run at all (`meetingProcessDetection` additionally gates the process detector); nothing runs until the renderer has synced the saved snapshot
- During recording (tap-to-talk or push-to-talk): ALL notifications suppressed
- After recording: 2.5s cooldown before showing queued notifications
- Multiple signals coalesced: one overlay at a time; a newer prompt replaces the current one
- Calendar-aware: if an ongoing or imminent calendar event exists, the prompt shows the event name and links the note to the event
- Active meeting recording (meeting mode): all detections suppressed
- Workspace policy `meetings.transcriptionStart: "manual"` (desktop 1.11.1+; older builds ignore it) stops automatic starts: new note, meeting prompt and calendar join open the note without capturing, while Record and the hotkey, tray and pill still start. Requests carry a `noteSessionId`; `useMeetingAutoTranscriptionCheck` is read after every async step up to the first capture, and a request it suppresses isn't replayed. Under automatic, a recording policy already refuses isn't suppressed, so `startRecording` still explains why; under manual the note just opens, since nobody asked for that recording. A suppressed note releases meeting mode at once (`markMeetingNoteOnly`) so later meetings prompt, even with the note left open or hidden in the tray; until a calendar event ends, only that event's reminder and the first mic detection after joining stay quiet, queued ones included. A token the server hasn't refused (`hasUsableCredential`) isn't a guest, so a sign-in in progress or a stale offline profile waits for its policy. Recording from that note takes the hold back until Stop

**Binary Distribution**:

- macOS: Compiled from Swift source via `scripts/build-macos-mic-listener.js` during `compile:native`
- Windows: Compiled from C source via `scripts/build-windows-mic-listener.js` during `compile:native` (it releases its console when piped, so no `conhost.exe` beside it); the prebuilt binary (`scripts/download-windows-mic-listener.js`, pinned to `MIC_LISTENER_VERSION`) only when no compiler works
- CI workflow: `.github/workflows/build-windows-mic-listener.yml` auto-builds on push to main

**Calendar Sync Resilience**:

- 10s socket timeout on all Google Calendar API requests
- Exponential backoff on consecutive failures: 2min → 4min → 8min → cap 30min
- Reset to normal 2min interval on any successful sync

### 17. Voice Assistant Hotkey

A dedicated global hotkey that opens the assistant's own window (`?agent=true`, `AgentWindowManager`) and listens for a request — no wake word ("Hey [AgentName]") needed. Questions stream into that window. Editing requests over a captured selection replace that selection through the existing guarded editor; other replies are never pasted at the caret or copied automatically. The window is a pill, a typing layout (the composer above the pill), a card above the pill (`standalone`) or a modal (`agentWindowState.ts`), with voice and keyboard as input modes in each; the draft, the selected context and the running chat survive every layout change. It is transparent, frameless, always on top and content-protected (never in screen shares), created hidden once the normal app is up and kept alive while hidden, so it opens at once. Closing it starts a new chat; Minimize keeps the chat. Its surfaces are opaque: `agent-window.css` sets solid surface, muted and popover colors on the window root and its panels, so the desktop never shows through the modal, pill, cards, menus or toasts. The outer shadow margin remains transparent. In dark mode those surfaces are neutral near-blacks, the border too, and the primary and ring are the brand blue (`.dark:has(.agent-window)` reaches menus that portal out, like the chat switcher), with a modal shadow small enough for the window's 8 px margin. The modal's blue glow rises from its bottom, brightest at the two bottom corners: full on an empty chat (while the user speaks into it too), faint while a draft is typed into an empty chat or while it listens or works in a chat, and none at rest or while a card waits; light mode shows it as a paler wash. Its chat cards are a step larger than the same cards in the Control Panel and note chats, through the `agent:` Tailwind variant (`index.css`, the `.agent-window` scope).

**Flow**:

1. Hotkey pressed → `voiceAgent` slot callback in `main.js` → `windowManager.sendToggleVoiceAgent()` → `agentWindow.handleHotkey()`, which captures the press-time target before the window can take focus, shows the window bottom-center on that app's display (`agentWindowPlacement.js`) and sends `agent-window-hotkey`. During onboarding the press goes to the dictation window instead (the assistant demo below). The tray's and the pill menu's "Ask assistant" open the typing layout on the cursor's display
2. The renderer (`src/components/agent/AgentWindow.tsx`) decides what a press means: start a take, send the one recording, or, while a reply runs, only show the window. A press from another app reads that app's selection (`captureSelectedText`, waiting for the pending target PID on macOS) as Selected context marked as from another app (`source: "external"`, `selected_in_another_app` in the request) and, with screen context on, a screenshot
3. Voice is a batch take (`useAgentVoice` over `useVoiceDraft`): the user's transcription settings, snippets expanded, no live partials and no dictionary hints. One take at a time, and a chat switch, new chat or fork drops a take in progress. Main's voice state (`agent-window-voice-state`) keeps dictation and the agent off each other's microphone (the dictation window hears `agent-recording-changed` for its own starts) and counts as the user recording for meeting detection. Deepgram/AssemblyAI BYOK (`STREAMING_ONLY_PROVIDERS`) can't transcribe a take: the hotkey opens the typing layout with a hint and Mic stays off. While a take records, the agent pill (`AgentPill.tsx`) wears the floating icon's recording look: `useRecordingIndicator("assistant")` gives the style and the assistant's mode color (with "Color by mode" on), and `resolveAgentPillRecordingLook` (`recordingIndicator.js`) adds REC's blinking dot and the elapsed time (`hooks/useElapsedLabel.ts`) when the style is REC or "Show recording time" is on, which widens the pill window. A take that heard nothing follows "End empty dictations quietly" (section 2): shorter than it, the pill goes back to idle with no notice. Takes don't report the dictation lifecycle, so they show no edge strip, tray dot or recording reminders
4. Each reply has Regenerate (the latest only, in place, keeping the sends it made — `replaceMessage`; one stopped or failed before its first token restores the old reply), Copy, Fork and Read aloud (`speechSynthesis`, hidden without voices). Selecting answer text offers Follow up (Selected context in the same chat) and Fork from here (a fork that sends the text with its first request). A fork shows its own turns under its "Forked from" chip, which returns to the parent; the copied history stays in the fork and goes with every request, and shows only once the parent is gone (`shownMessages`). Until the fork has a turn of its own, a copied last reply whose card was left unanswered shows too, so the fork never holds a card the user can't see. A fork starts with its parent's title and takes its own from the first message the user writes in it, unless it was renamed first; an answer to a copied card, which the app sends on, never names it. A fork syncs with its origin: the parent's `client_conversation_id`, how many messages it copied (`fork_message_count`) and its text (`fork_context`). Another device links it to the parent once both are there, whichever arrives first, and keeps it a plain chat while the parent is missing or deleted (`upsertConversationFromCloud`); a cloud copy without them, from an older API, keeps the origin a device already has. The modal's `+` and chevron start a new chat or switch to another, forks marked
5. Requests run through the chat pipeline (`useAgentChat`: `useChatPersistence`, `useChatStreaming` and `useChatMessageSender` on the Voice Assistant scope, with connectors and interactions): chat tools (notes search/create/update, calendar, web search, clipboard, `get_snippet` — triggers listed in the tool description, body fetched on demand — and `update_dictionary` / `update_snippets`, which write through the settings store so the change syncs like a UI edit), RAG memory (library notes that match the request, sent with the message), and the custom dictionary plus snippet triggers injected into the system prompt. Conversations persist in the `agent_conversations` table and are browsable from the ControlPanel chat. The system prompt (`getAgentSystemPrompt`, appended after the user-editable base prompt, so it reaches custom prompts too) lists the offered tools grouped by capability, forbids claiming an ability a listed tool covers (web search is for public information the context doesn't already answer, never people the user knows), and names what is unavailable and how to enable it — for an unavailable connector the model still writes the email or message in its reply and ends with one line on how to have it sent (`resolveUnavailableCapabilities` in `src/config/agentCapabilities.ts`: signed out, org policy, paid plan, a Slack/Linear/GitHub connector not connected or needing a reconnect, no calendar, or a local model too small for tools; connectors are named only on surfaces that offer them, and the onboarding demo names nothing, `nameUnavailableCapabilities: false`). History (`toHistoryMessages` in `src/components/chat/historyMessages.ts`) replays each earlier call to a tool this turn offers as a tool-call and tool-result pair (`toolHistory: "parts"`: Cloud and hosted providers), and every other call as a `[Tools used: …]` note before the reply (a provider may refuse a call to a tool it doesn't offer, such as an earlier `look_at_screen` or a since-disconnected connector, and the send must stay known); local, LAN and custom servers get only the note (`"text"`), since their chat templates may not take tool messages. A reply with nothing left in it (a card-only turn noted nowhere, a stopped or failed one) is left out, since Anthropic refuses an empty assistant message, and the requests on either side of it go as one, with a line saying the first was stopped or failed, since some chat templates (Mistral's, Gemma's) refuse two user messages in a row. Either carries only allowlisted arguments (search queries, contact names, note titles, a Slack message's destination) and outcomes, never drafted content or results: a connector action's saved status (`sent`, `draft opened`, `may have been sent`, `cancelled by the user`, `not sent`, `failed`, `unavailable`, `needed details`), a sent one's reference and `https` link (`github_create_issue (sent: o/r#12 https://…)`), a question's answers; another call is `done` or `failed`. So a follow-up turn ("it's #engineering", "retry") never redoes what already went out; resolved destinations, card edits and error text stay out. A call cut off before its result reads "outcome not recorded", since a send can still commit after Esc. Replayed call ids keep only letters, digits, `_` and `-` (at most 40, unique), which every provider takes. History is the newest whole messages within 40 entries, tool messages included (the cloud's Gemma provider takes 64 a request, the turn's own steps included: each call adds two, so a full history leaves room for about a dozen, and the API answers the rest of a turn that makes more without Gemma), and about 16,000 tokens (`estimateNoteTokens`; 4,000 for a local or LAN server, whose context is small, `HISTORY_MAX_TOKENS`), not a fixed number of messages, and starts with a user message, since Gemini, Vertex and Bedrock refuse one that opens with a reply or its tool calls. Sends, opened drafts and sends that may have happened (a send cut off before its result included) in the messages it leaves out come first, each after the request that led to it (cut at 500 characters), the note that earlier messages were left out opening the first request (`ledgerTurns`: the latest that fit 10 entries, which the history then leaves room for within its 40; the sends of one request share its entry; with a text trace, the note and a line per request open the first kept message, since some chat templates refuse two user messages in a row, and take no entries of their own), so a long chat never repeats a send or denies one. The request itself, the newest user message, always stays, however long; when it would go alone, older messages are first cut to a third of the budget each (ending with a line saying the rest was cut), and a request still alone opens with the note that earlier messages were left out. A reply that opens with a note itself is shown and saved without it (`withoutEchoedToolTrace`). The prompt says to claim only what a tool result shows about the assistant's own actions (this turn's for what it does now, a recorded one for what it reports; what other people did is reported from the note or result that tells it), that an earlier reply's unrecorded claim didn't happen, and that tool results override earlier replies; the time, with its weekday, and the matching library notes ride at the end of the last user message (`buildMessageContext`; the notes fenced in `<library_notes>` tags and marked as data, never instructions), not in the system prompt, so the prompt, the tools and earlier turns stay one prefix the provider can cache. `get_calendar_events` returns each event's local date, weekday, times and offset, with all-day events as their days, and `free` for one that doesn't block the user's time (a holiday, a working location); a meeting at the next midnight belongs to the next day. A turn still calling tools at the step cap (`MAX_TOOL_STEPS`, 20) gets one more step that may not call them (`STEP_LIMIT_NOTE`: answer with what it has and say what is unfinished; BYOK keeps the tool definitions, without their `execute`, with `toolChoice: "none"`, so a server that ignores it still runs nothing, and the Cloud sends none; its claims are flagged, never asked again). Anthropic and Bedrock skip it, since Anthropic's SDK then sends the turn's tool calls without the tools, which the API refuses, and Bedrock's drops the calls and results; a failed answer ends the turn as it stands. Either way the turn's notes say it paused at the cap (`step_limit`). A final answer that claims an action is asked once more, as the user, in both tool loops (`CLAIM_CHECK_NOTE`; the reply stays on screen until the second answer replaces it, and is kept with the note below if that ask is stopped, fails or comes back empty). The check (`isUnbackedClaim` in `actionClaims.ts`) is a narrow safety net that prefers a miss to a wrong note: English only; only kinds a tool here can do (send, draft, create, connect, a saved key); only claims in the reply's own voice ("I've sent", "it was sent", "your email to Gabe has been sent", "Sent to Gabe!", "Email sent."), never in a condition or someone else's account ("Once it's sent", "According to your notes"), in fenced, quoted or blockquoted text, after a lead-in that introduces a draft ("Here's a reply:", "Translation:") or from a message's subject line or a greeting on a line of its own on ("Subject:", "Hi Sam,"; a greeting opening a line of text, or the reply, only when the user asked for something to be written, since the reply may greet the user that way), in an earlier claim or someone's account repeated ("I said I sent…", "the note says it was sent"), on a line a speaker or section labels ("Sarah: I sent…"), in a report with its own date ("it was sent on Monday") or one a lookup this turn may be repeating, or in words that are the user's request or selection or this turn's tool payload. A claim is backed by a result of its kind this turn (a send by `sent` or `unknown`; a connect also by a connector call whose own connect card answered connected); a report (or any claim answering a question about something done, such as "did you send it?") also by one recorded for an earlier turn that went to whoever it names. One that still claims it is kept, and the turn's notes say it wasn't done (`unbackedClaim`; chats without turn notes, like the note chat, get only the retry)
6. Wake word: a standalone "Hey [AgentName] …" command is transcribed by the dictation window and handed to the agent window (`submitAgentCommand` → `deliverCommand`), with any selection as external context. One that arrives while a reply runs waits in the composer; while a question waits it is held like a message written before the question, so the composer never answers the question with it
7. Selection edits: a wake word over highlighted text goes through the `dictationAgent` scope and replaces the text in place. The agent hotkey also edits highlighted text in place: `editSelectedText` reuses the same prompts and output validation, keeps the replacement session only in memory, and calls `replaceSelectedText`, which revalidates the target and original selection before pasting. A screenshot the model refuses is dropped for one more try, and an edit that still fails leaves the request in the composer. Closing, switching chats, forking or an account change abandons a pending edit. A hotkey over a selected body or subject in a pending email/Slack card edits that range through `updateApprovalDraft`; it never sends the card, and a changed draft or settled action rejects the late edit. When the editor judges the command a question about the selection ("explain this") rather than a change, it answers `SELECTION_QUESTION_MARKER` (`selectionEditing.js`, both the cloud and local prompts) and the command goes to the agent window with the selection as context, leaving the selection untouched
8. Connector tools (`email_draft`, `find_contact`, `slack_send_message`, the Linear tools, the GitHub tools; paid, policy-gated) run through `src/helpers/connectors`. Typed chat (`ChatView`), the agent window and every note's chat (`useEmbeddedChat`) opt in (`allowConnectors`); container chat and the onboarding demo don't get them. Note chat also gets a "Meeting attendees" block: the note chat sends its meeting as one request (`NoteAttendeesRequest`: note id, parsed participants, calendar event id, the user's OpenWhispr address), and main adds the speakers identified in the note (speaker profiles with an address) and the linked calendar event's organizer (Outlook and Apple leave them out of the attendees), keeps only valid addresses, strips control and invisible format characters, angle brackets and their look-alikes, and address-like words from names, and filters with `find_contact`'s exclusions plus the user's OpenWhispr address and the Gmail login's address (`connector-note-attendees`, `createNoteAttendeesLookup`), organizer included. The block is fenced in `<meeting_attendees>` tags and marked as data, and is added only when connector tools are registered; the note's title and text and the `search_notes` and `get_note` results lose the tag name (`withoutAttendeesFence`), so an invite's title can't fake a second list. A participant flagged `self` also excludes that address when it comes back as an identified speaker or the organizer. A participant's `self` flag marks whoever recorded the meeting, so the renderer honours it only on the user's own note (`ownsNote`, `attendeesForUser`; unknown ownership counts as someone else's). The note chat passes its note as `openNote`, which `getAgentSystemPrompt` puts under its own rule: for questions about what was said, decided or written, answer from this note, and when it doesn't cover the question, say so and offer to search the user's other notes; `search_notes` runs only when the user asks to look beyond the note, and the answer names the note used. Unlike the other chats it runs no up-front library search (RAG): other notes reach it only through `search_notes` and `get_note`, so a model too small for tools answers from the open note alone. The open note carries its transcript whole, as `Speaker: text` lines labelled exactly as `get_note` labels them (`noteChatTranscript`: saved speaker names, `Note taker` for whoever recorded it, numbered in-person speakers; after a line saying whether the note taker is the user, named on their own note), unless the model has `get_note` and the transcript is longer than it takes (`FULL_TRANSCRIPT_MAX_TOKENS` in `transcriptEvidence.ts`, counted with `estimateNoteTokens` since CJK text is about a token per character: 64,000 hosted, 4,000 on a local or LAN model); then it carries the first 500 characters (`transcript_preview`) and a rule to read further with `get_note` before saying the note doesn't cover something. Whole-note actions (`requestText`) always get the whole transcript. `get_note` returns the start of the transcript beside the editable fields; `transcript_query` (literal and case-insensitive, speaker names included; the person taking the notes is `Note taker`) or a `transcript_offset` past 0 returns one 500-character passage with the times it spans, paging metadata (offset 0 is an ordinary read, since models fill in defaults) and a revision naming the text read so far, so a recording that keeps appending can still be paged. Both tool loops keep the two newest passages, and every passage of the latest step even when it read more in parallel (`compactTranscriptHistory`); a query that matched nothing is never compacted. Its OpenWhispr Cloud requests are ordinary `/api/agent/stream` requests: the API keeps no assistant memory and adds nothing to the prompt, so the client's own system prompt is what the model sees (the `noteChat` flag went once openwhispr-api#243 removed memory). Unlike the main Chat view (section 21), New chat and switching conversations cancel the running turn, so a pending card can't hold the send lock or save into another conversation

**Cards that wait on the user** (questions, connect, access, API key, settings, and the answer cards; `src/components/chat/interactions/`, `ApprovalCard.tsx`):

- Where the user is in a card (the question on screen, every pick, an answer typed in the composer) lives in `agentInteractionStore`, so it survives every layout change. Questions go one at a time (‹ n/N ›); a radio pick shows for 200 ms and moves on, checkboxes have Continue, text and API keys a round ↑. A recommended option is only labelled. A typed key stays in its uncontrolled field (never in a store, React state or the `value` attribute) and leaves a "key saved" row; in the agent window every other settled card leaves one line saying what it came to ("Answered · Sam", "Connected Gmail", `InteractionOutcomeRow`), eased down from the card's height. A card shows where its call came in the reply's text (`textOffset`), so text written before it stays above it; replies saved before calls kept their place show their cards first. Card fields carry `input-inline`, which opts them out of the app's global input styles. Settings Done checks again before the task goes on; for the AI models target, in providers mode only, that the provider of the asking chat's scope has its key (the scope rides on the payload; Cloud, self-hosted and enterprise need none)
- A card that arrives while the window is compact (the pill or the typing layout) shows above the pill if it asks something (the `standalone` layout, sized to the card up to 400 px through `contentHeight`); an answer card waits behind the pill instead. From the typing layout (or a card above the pill with keyboard input), an answer card, or one that asks while a message is being written, opens in the modal instead, so the keyboard stays with the window and the message; a question that arrives while a message is being written isn't answered from the composer until the message is emptied. A reply opens the modal once it has text, an error, or a step that is doing more than asking (`INTERACTION_TOOL_NAMES`; a connector step gets 300 ms, once per reply, to show the connect card it asks with), so a spoken request that asks first stays compact, while an answer card usually opens the modal this way. A reply opens it at most once, and the user opening the modal counts. A card restored from a turn that ended (`restored`) never makes the pill wait or opens the modal; the modal's composer still answers it, the typing layout's (which doesn't show it) sends a new request. The pill window is sized to the widest narrow pill (Thinking… in German) plus its shadow margin, since on Windows and Linux its transparent sides take clicks; only the waiting pill and a notice widen it (`wide`), in place, a frame later, so a card arriving at the same moment fades in without a widen first
- The pill alone and above a card reads "Waiting for answer" for any waiting card; in the modal only for an interaction card (an answer card has its own Send). While an answer card waits or sends, the modal's voice pill reads as at rest (Mic, Keyboard, Close), and so does the pill alone while one sends; the typing layout's pill shows neither the label nor the cue. Outside the modal and the typing layout, Expand pulses blue (`agent-expand-cue`, steady under reduced motion) and after 4 s the modal opens to the card, once per card (`useAnswerNudge`; an interaction and the answer card after it on the same tool call are separate cards). The open is used up when it fires or the user acts on the card: Expand, Keyboard, moving the pointer over it above the pill (a `pointermove` with movement; a card laid out under a resting cursor gets a replayed `pointerenter` with none), or a click or key in it, in the modal too; focus alone doesn't count. The reply's own expand, a voice take and the window hiding only pause it, and minimizing again starts it over. Minimizing with a card waiting leaves the waiting pill; Keyboard reopens the card in the modal with the composer, Expand keeps voice
- While a question waits, the composer reads "Answer here…" and its text answers the question on screen (`answerWithText`), even a choice question; it never reaches a key card. In the modal, on a connect, access, key or settings card, it points at the card. Send stays off while an answer card waits. The composer keeps Stop beside a question, key or settings card, which have no dismiss control of their own (`CARDS_WITHOUT_DISMISS`, the set Esc dismisses), and offers none while a connect, access or answer card waits or an answer card sends; those cards end the wait themselves, and Esc and Close remain. A running reply's progress row says nothing while one of its cards waits or sends, since the card says what the step is doing, unless a step failed: that step shows, so it is seen before Send. "Checking answers…" shows again after Send or Cancel

**Esc, Close and focus**:

- Esc while listening or transcribing discards the take (and hides the window if the take opened it); while a reply runs it stops it and keeps the partial answer; otherwise it closes. Esc on a question, API-key or settings card dismisses that card (the turn goes on, and the model hears no); on any other card it stays in the card. Who gets the keyboard is decided in `AgentWindow` (the window, asked of main, and the composer) and in `useCardFocus` (inside a card). A move the window makes by itself (the timed open, a card moving the keyboard layouts to the modal) doesn't ask main for the keyboard unless the window already has it, until the user points at the window, the window gets focus, the hotkey is pressed or a new spoken command arrives, so it never takes the keyboard from the app the user went to; the composer's own focus doesn't wait on that, so a field the user clicks on coming back keeps the caret. The composer doesn't take it while a key card is shown (a restored one too), unless it holds a message being written. The keyboard stays in a card: when it appears in a focused window with nothing focused (the composer it replaced went away), or a key card with an empty composer focused, and no message being written; when a change on it (the next question, Done appearing, a connect starting) removes the focused control; or when the window gets the keyboard with nothing focused (a key card also with an empty composer focused and no message being written, so a user back by window focus alone pastes the key into its field): its field, first option or the card itself takes focus. A restored card never takes it on its own. While a take records, Esc is a global hotkey (`register-cancel-hotkey`), given back when its renderer reloads, crashes or closes
- Close, Esc on an idle window, and a close from the OS (Alt+F4, Cmd+W) cancel the take and the turn, start a new chat and hide the window; the old chat stays in the chat list. A command that arrives while the window hides goes to the new chat, though the window still ends hidden
- macOS: a non-activating panel, focusable from creation and never `focus()`ed; a click makes it key. Windows: `setFocusable(true)` and `focus()` while typing, for a card that needs typing, or after a click in the modal or on a card above the pill, released only from a focused window and after hiding. Linux: `setFocusable(true)` (Chromium never activates an unfocusable window, so no key would reach the page) and focus through the input-region helper, asked once more when the window isn't focused 250 ms later (a card resizing the window in the same moment can make Chromium miss the first); `setFocusable(false)` and a remap hand it back. The window stays override-redirect, out of Alt-Tab

**Storage & IPC**:

- Env var: `VOICE_AGENT_KEY` (persisted via `environment.js`), store key: `voiceAgentKey` (no default — user opt-in)
- IPC handlers: `update-voice-agent-hotkey`, `get-voice-agent-key`; the agent window's own channels (`agent-window-ready`, `agent-window-set-layout`, `agent-window-hide`, `agent-window-voice-state`) answer only that window, and `open-agent-window` / `agent-window-submit-command` only the dictation window
- Hotkey slot: `voiceAgent` (tap-to-toggle; GNOME-native slot via `ToggleVoiceAgent` D-Bus method, KDE via KGlobalAccel, otherwise `globalShortcut`)

**UI**:

- Settings → App & Controls → Hotkeys → "Voice Assistant Hotkey" (with cross-slot conflict validation)
- Onboarding: optional pair of steps (hotkey, then demo) after the Notes step, so the demo can suggest meeting times from a calendar connected there. The demo card is a mail thread; the reply is answered headlessly in the dictation window by `useOnboardingAssistantDemo` (the chat streaming pipeline, tools and screenshot included) and streamed back over `onboarding-demo-event` as `processing` (transcript) → `replying` (partial reply, `tool` while one runs) → `success`
- Agent window conversations and in-place selection edits both run on the Voice Assistant scope (Settings → Voice Assistant; `dictationAgent`/`dictationAgentVision`), resolved by `resolveChatStreamingInference`; the agent window falls back to the `chatIntelligence` scope while the Voice Assistant scope is unreachable, and then sends a selection from another app to the chat as context instead of editing it, as the wake word does. Typed chat (Control Panel, note and container chat) stays on `chatIntelligence`

**Screen Context (opt-in)**:

When "Share screen context" is enabled (Settings → Voice Assistant → Screen Context, store key `voiceAgentScreenContext`, default off), each voice-assistant recording start captures the display the cursor is on and attaches it to the request as a base64 JPEG (long edge ≤ 1568 px).

- Capture: `src/helpers/screenContextCapture.js` (main process; `screen.getCursorScreenPoint` + `desktopCapturer`). macOS requires the Screen Recording TCC permission (`useScreenRecordingPermission` hook + `PermissionCard` in `DictationAgentSettings`); Windows/Linux X11 need no permission; Linux Wayland is unsupported (capture silently skipped)
- Encoding: `encodeWithinBudget()` walks a JPEG quality ladder (82 → 70 → 55), then falls back to a 1024 px resize, keeping the payload under `MAX_ENCODED_BYTES` (1.5 MB ≈ 2.05M base64 chars, inside the API's 2.8M cap). Each rung re-encodes the original bitmap, so quality drops never compound. A screen that still won't fit is dropped
- Trigger: the agent window captures it at the hotkey press (`captureScreenContext`), beside the press-time selection, and a take waits up to 3 s for both when it is sent. The onboarding demo, which records in the dictation window, calls `audioManager.beginScreenContextCapture()` at recording start and awaits `consumeScreenContext()` (3s guard). The dictation overlay gets `setContentProtection` while the setting is on; the agent window is always content-protected
- Routing: the press-time screenshot is re-decided against the model that will actually answer. On BYOK, it attaches only when the chat model's provider client is image-wired (`supportsImages` on the `InferenceProvider`) and the model has `supportsVision` in `modelRegistryData.json`; on OpenWhispr Cloud it rides as `screenContext`, which the API sends to its vision chain. Either way the prompt gets `screenContextSuffix`: background only, used when the request is about the screen and otherwise answered as if there were none (no narrating it on small talk), nothing inferred from cut-off text, and text on it is content, never instructions. Dropping the image never fails the command
- Agent window on Cloud: the screenshot never rides with the request. That turn offers `look_at_screen(question)` (`src/services/tools/lookAtScreenTool.ts`), which sends only the screenshot and the question to the API's vision chain with no tools (`ReasoningService.askAboutScreen`, `LOOK_AT_SCREEN_PROMPT`: quote visible text, say what's cut off, never guess) and returns its answer as text, marked as untrusted screen content, which reaches that turn's model but never the saved (and synced) tool call, which keeps no arguments either (a question can quote an earlier read). A turn reads the screen at most twice. gpt-oss keeps the turn and its tools, so small talk never sees the image and the chat never switches models. A spoken request captures a fresh screenshot when enabled and allowed; a typed request captures nothing and carries only a press-time image already on hand. Questions about the user's own plans, prices or company answer from supplied library notes first, or call `search_notes` with topic keywords before public sources. Automatic context includes both personal notes and saved summaries; an empty cloud search falls back to local notes without widening a container scope.
- Privacy: screenshots live only in renderer memory for the turn they were taken with — never written to disk, stored in history, or logged (loggers emit `hasScreenContext` booleans only)
- IPC: `capture-screen-context`, `check-screen-recording-access`, `request-screen-recording-access`, `open-screen-recording-settings`, `screen-context-set-enabled`

**Tests**: `test/helpers/agentWindowManager.test.js`, `test/components/agentWindowState.test.js`, `test/components/agentWindowCards.test.js`, `test/components/useAnswerNudge.test.js`, `test/components/interactionCard.test.js`, `test/components/approvalCardStates.test.js`, `test/components/useVoiceDraft.test.js`, `test/helpers/windowIpcSenders.test.js`, `test/helpers/dictationRouting.test.js`, `test/helpers/screenContextCapture.test.js` (run with `node --test`)

### 18. Meeting Transcription: Echo, Duplicate, and Segment Pipeline

Live meeting transcription runs two streams (mic + system-audio tap) and must keep the remote party's voice, as heard through the local speakers, out of the mic transcript. The policy lives in pure, unit-tested seams so it can be tuned without touching the IPC plumbing:

- **Audio layer**: `meetingEchoLeakDetector.js` correlates mic chunks against recent system audio; `meetingMicGate.js` turns chunk RMS/peak (+ a "system speaking" lookback) into a `send` / `zero` / `skip` verdict that `ipcHandlers.js` applies in `dispatchMeetingAudioBuffer` (streaming) and `transcribeLocalMeetingChunk` (local)
- **Echo cancellation**: the WebRTC AEC3 helper (`meetingAecManager.js`, `native/meeting-aec-helper/`) replaces the mic channel only when Settings → Notes & Uploads → Live Recording → Echo cancellation for meetings is on (`meetingAecEnabled`, default off, sent as the `aecEnabled` start option). With headphones there is no echo, and AEC3 still cuts the user's own speech, worst in the first seconds (#1025)
- **Text layer**: `meetingMicHoldback.js` decides whether a mic final is risky (held back), a duplicate of recent system text (dropped), or racing an arriving system final (retracted); the `ipcHandlers.js` adapters (`shouldSkipDuplicateMicSegment`, `hasRiskyMicDuplicateProfile`, `removeRacingMicEntriesFor`, `removePendingMicFinalsFor`) apply the results to closure state and emit `meeting-transcription-segment` events (`partial` / `final` / `retract`)
- **Renderer**: `src/stores/meetingSegmentReducer.ts` is the pure `(state, event, deps) → reduction` transition for those events (timestamp-sorted insert, retract by exact match, per-source partial slots); `meetingRecordingStore.ts` supplies `mintSegmentId` / `decorateFinal` (speaker identifications, provisional speaker, locks — must not write to the store) and applies the reduction. Tests: `test/stores/meetingSegmentReducer.test.js`, `test/stores/meetingRecordingStoreImports.test.js`
- **Regression pins**: BYOK `session.update` payload and the disconnect-commit callback (`test/helpers/openaiRealtimeStreaming.test.js`, `tinfoilRealtimeStreaming.test.js`), token-endpoint wire bodies (`test/helpers/realtimeTokenProviders.test.js`). Tests named `characterization: …` pin known oddities on purpose — flip them deliberately when changing policy
- **Fixtures**: `test/helpers/harness/pcmFixtures.js` — deterministic 24 kHz PCM generators (`makeSine`, `makeSeededNoise`, `mix`, `delayBy`, `toInt16Buffer`, `chunkBuffer`, …) used by the gate and echo-detector tests

### 19. Note Templates and Actions

Rows in the `actions` table (built-ins seeded from `src/helpers/builtinActions.js`) are one of two kinds:

- **Templates** (`kind: 'template'`) write a note's AI summary (`enhanced_content`) from its notes, meeting context, and transcript, never from the summary they replace. A template is a context (`prompt`) plus ordered `sections` (`{heading, instruction}`); `compileTemplatePrompt` wraps sections in the shared notes rules. A template without sections keeps the request it always had after the `SOURCE_FIDELITY_RULE` prefix (the generic system prompt, or the material preamble for a standalone key). The note records the template in `enhancement_template_id` (its `client_id`), which syncs with the note, and `enhancement_prompt` the compiled system prompt
- **Actions** (`kind: 'action'`) work from the AI summary when the note has one, and from its notes and transcript otherwise (`buildNoteRunInput`). Only the built-in Make notes shorter (`shorten`) and Make notes longer (`lengthen`) actions edit that summary, selected by fixed built-in identity (`toActionItem`), never an editable name or stored `output`. A summary edit requires complete output, never splits into parts, and writes only `enhanced_content`; with no summary yet it writes one from the transcript and applies the action to it, run like a template (split into parts on a local model, the material hash recorded); every other built-in and custom action, including Write TL;DR (`addTldr`), runs in the note chat via `SendToAIOptions.requestText` (the chat shows a built-in's call to action where it has one, otherwise the action's name, and the preamble points the model at the summary or the transcript and has it answer in the chat as text in the note's language, using a tool to draft, send or post only when the action's instructions explicitly ask to send, post or file something, and leaving the note unchanged, with an asked-for edit written in the chat instead; its turn is never offered `update_note` (`keepNotesUnchanged`), so it can't change a note whatever the model decides; the note chat's other tools and attendee block otherwise apply as usual, and its transcript context names speakers only on notes known to be the user's own (`ownsNote`), never listing the unfiltered invitees)
- **The material picks the wrapper** (`runNoteAction`): speaker segments make a meeting (`isMeetingNote`: `MEETING_SYSTEM_PROMPT` or `MEETING_INPUT_PREAMBLE`, the `## Meeting Context` block and `Speaker: text` lines); a transcript in plain text (a recording that captured no segments, one made before 1.6.6, or an upload whose provider named no speakers: its text is stored as segments with no speaker or source (`speakerlessTranscriptText`; one untimed segment when it returned no segments), or as the note's content on uploads saved before the transcript held it) is its own case (`hasPlainTranscript`: `PLAIN_TRANSCRIPT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` or `PLAIN_TRANSCRIPT_INPUT_PREAMBLE`, #2666), with no Meeting Context since nothing shows who is speaking, a part prompt without speaker labels when a local model splits it, and action items as `- [ ] Action — Owner` lines with an owner or deadline only when the transcript or the user's notes state it, never a placeholder, and never a table unless the instructions ask for one; no transcript is a personal note (`BASE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` or `NOTE_INPUT_PREAMBLE`). Both transcript cases get the custom dictionary, and their known people (the user, the invitees, and a meeting's named speakers) turn matching owners into mentions (`tagActionItemOwners`)
- The default template is the built-in AI Summary (the Detailed Notes sections, stored under the old Generate Notes key `generateNotes`), listed first (launch re-applies the built-in order). Launch retires a stock Detailed Notes row (`LEGACY_DETAILED_NOTES`) unless AI Summary was customized; an edited one is kept and listed second, and saved template ids are never rewritten. Invariant: AI Summary, compiled from its sections, equals the flat 1.10.2 Detailed Notes prompt it replaced after the `SOURCE_FIDELITY_RULE` prefix used by templates and summary actions (`sourceFidelity.js`), but for the closing rule on material with nothing to summarize (`test/helpers/templatePrompts.test.js`); a built-in's `previousPrompts` only upgrades rows that are still flat. Chat actions get the rule once, from the chat system prompt (`getAgentSystemPrompt`), and the Cloud request leaves out the leading system message that repeats `systemPrompt`, which the API prepends itself
- For material with nothing substantive (only greetings, filler or recording checks), sectioned templates, AI Summary and a summary action writing a first summary ask the model to answer `NOTHING_TO_SUMMARIZE`; custom context-only templates and flat built-ins edited before this rule keep their own rules. A reply that is the marker (bare, fenced, emphasised or after a short sentence) outside a thinking block writes nothing; a summary that merely contains it is saved, and the runner shows `notes.actions.errors.nothingToSummarize` as a neutral notice (`notice` on the error event) instead of saving a placeholder summary
- UI: `TemplatePicker` opens from the chevron in the AI Summary tab, which names the note's template; the "Generate AI Summary" callout (runs the note's template, else the default), `ActionPicker` in the collapsed ask bar (runs the last action picked from it, in place of the send button), `ActionChips` once the in-view chat opens and in the docked chat (the first five actions in docked chat; the centered row omits Make notes longer without backfilling another chip; each has its action's icon, and All actions keeps every action; hovering a chip shows its icon, title and description without a destination badge; the in-view chat pins All actions at the end, and the docked chat shows them as filled pills in one tray with its composer: All actions, then the actions, scrolling together), a `/` menu in the note composers (`slashCommands` on `ChatInput`, each row with its action's icon, or a "/" for an icon this build doesn't know; it fills the chat above the composer until a command runs, the "/" is deleted, nothing matches or the composer loses focus), and `ActionManagerDialog` (Templates | Actions). The notes list and the docked chat widen or narrow from their edge facing the note (`useResizableWidth`, `PanelResizeHandle`), and keep their width across launches (localStorage `notesSidebarWidth`, `noteChatWidth`). Anything the ask bar sends (a message, or a chat action from the picker, a chip or the `/` menu) starts a new conversation in the docked chat, which holds the conversation and its history; the in-view chat is only the chips and the composer. Destinations are fixed and described in the editor; saving never asks a model to choose one. Legacy custom summary destinations read as chat (`toActionItem`), keeping the existing IPC fields, IDs and prompts. Summary generation stays in the AI Summary tab and callout. A run that writes the summary is undoable for 6 s instead of asking first: the runner queues the fields it overwrote (`appliedEvents`) and `BackgroundActionToastListener` shows the Undo toast

### 20. Tone & Style

Dictation cleanup writes in the tone the user picked for the kind of app they are dictating into: personal messages (formal, casual, very casual), work messages, email and everything else (formal, casual, excited). Off by default (cleanup keeps the speaker's own tone, the standard tone); turned on by finishing the setup dialog or the Tones & voices switch in Settings › Dictation › Tone & Style. The assistant and chats also write drafts (messages, emails, replies, posts) in the tone of the kind of text the request is for. Translation keeps its source's tone.

- **Detection** (main, at dictation start): `capture-dictation-app-category` reads the captured target through `selectionManager.getTargetAppContext()` and classifies it with `toneAppCategories.js`. macOS: `macos-text-monitor --app-context <pid>` prints the app name, bundle id, focused window title (accessibility, no Screen Recording needed) and, for a browser page, the URL host. Windows: `windows-fast-paste --detect-only` also prints `WINDOW_TITLE` (an older helper without it classifies by exe only). Linux: the window title is read on demand (`xdotool`/`kdotool getwindowname`) or taken from Hyprland's active window; an AT-SPI target's comes from `linux-fast-paste --atspi-target` (`WINDOW_TITLE_B64`, the active window's accessible name; an older helper prints none). Titles are kept beside the captured target (`_targetTitles`), never on it, since capture results reach the renderer. A browser (and a Windows UWP frame) is judged by its page or app title (its host, else the site name the title ends with; on a host not listed, only a site name the host carries, so a Notion page titled "Hey" stays `other`); native apps by whole-token app identity. Titles and hosts never leave the main process or reach the logs; only the category does
- **Cleanup**: `useAudioRecording` starts `audioManager.beginToneStyleCapture()` for every non-translation recording while Tone & Style is on; the cleanup route's `consumeToneStyle()` (1.5 s guard, unknown app reads as `other`) puts `toneStyle` on the reasoning config. Cloud cleanup sends `tone` + `appCategory` on `/api/reason`, and in the combined Orukeet cleanup's `pipelineOptions`: its prompt is fixed when the session is minted, so `startStreamingRecording` waits for the classification until 250 ms after the capture began (overlapping the mic opening, so the start cue isn't held back after it) before minting, returns without minting if the start was cancelled meanwhile, and holds the audio captured meanwhile until the session has started. An app still unknown then (a Wayland AT-SPI target takes about a second) gets no combined cleanup, and `/api/reason` cleans up in its tone at stop. Warmup mints for the app the last start learned in time (`lastToneAppCategory`), and without combined cleanup while none is known; BYOK and local append `appendToneStyleSuffix` (`src/config/prompts/toneStyle.ts`) after the dictionary suffix. **A dictation style wins** (13f): a styled recording (a style hotkey, or the dictation hotkey's style) captures no app tone and starts no combined Orukeet cleanup, so it gets no `toneStyle`; plain dictations get the tone. Where both reach one prompt (`getCleanupSystemPrompt(agentName, dictionary, language, uiLanguage, toneStyle, template, groups)`), the tone wraps the saved or overridden template and the style block comes after it; on Cloud the tone goes as `tone`/`appCategory` and the style in `customPrompt`. Its sentences are verbatim copies of openwhispr-api's `lib/prompts.ts` (English in every locale, as on the server); the mobile app's `composerPrompt.ts` copies the tone and priority sentences too, so change all three together
- **Assistant and chat**: `getAgentSystemPrompt`'s `writingStyle` option adds a WRITING STYLE block (`buildWritingStyleBlock`) to every chat surface's prompt while Tone & Style is on: the model works out from the request, the recipient and the tool (an `email_draft` body is an email) which kind of text it writes, and uses the user's tone for it; its own answers keep its voice, a tone the user asks for wins, and code is never restyled. Answers stay in the agent window and are never typed at the caret, so none is written for the app the user was in. Selection edits get one sentence (`selectionEditToneSentence`) that applies the selection's app style only to a prose rewrite asked for without a tone. The desktop builds every agent prompt itself, so the API needs nothing here
- **UI**: Home banner (`ToneStyleBanner`, cleanup-off copy turns cleanup on) and the setup dialog (`ToneStyleSetupDialog`: intro plus one step per category), offered once the user has dictated `TONE_STYLE_PROMPT_MIN_WORDS` (70, `src/config/toneStyle.ts`) words of local history; the dialog opens by itself once, the banner stays until set up or "Not now" (`useToneStylePrompt`). Settings › Dictation › Tone & Style (page `toneStyle`, after Cleanup; `SettingsPage` renders `settings/ToneStyleSection.tsx` with `hideHeader`, search anchors `dictation.toneStyle.enabled` and `dictation.toneStyle.tones`) has a Tones & voices switch (`toneStyleEnabled`); while it's off, the picks and Save Changes are grayed out. App badges live in `src/assets/icons/apps/`

### 21. Main Chat Background Turns

Control Panel → Chat answers outlive the view: leaving it, switching conversation or pressing New chat doesn't stop a turn, and conversations answer at the same time. Note, container and agent window chats still cancel when left. Turns don't survive quitting, a reload or a renderer crash.

- **Engine**: `components/chat/chatTurnEngine.ts` is the turn without React (context, tools, streaming, persistence, errors). `useChatStreaming` wraps one engine per surface and cancels on unmount, and replaces it on an account or credential change (`subscribeAuthBoundary`), so a turn from before writes nothing after it; the store creates one per send
- **Store**: `stores/chatTurnStore.ts` keeps one entry per live or unseen turn (`conversation:<id>`, or `submission:<id>` until its row exists), the selection, the drafts and New chat's skills. `startChatSend` reserves the entry synchronously, creates the row (`createAgentConversation`, never moving a newer selection) and writes through the bound id. Only the composer clears a draft; a starter card or skill draft leaves typed text alone, and a refused send adds its text back (`returnChatDraft`). New chat keeps its draft and clears its skill
- **Stop**: applies to the conversation on screen. The partial answer (text not yet flushed included) and its tool steps stay on screen (`settling` until the engine finishes, so the view takes them whole), are saved as a stopped reply (the store's engine runs the agent chat, `allowInteractions`, which saves the reply when its turn starts, so a turn stopped before it said anything keeps an empty stopped reply), and reach the next turn's history; a tool still running ends `stopped` and reads "outcome not recorded". A card that commits after the view took the turn still marks its call there (`takeCompletedChatTurn` hands that chat the turn's later writes), so a retry from that chat says it was sent earlier; after a newer turn in the same chat, or leaving and coming back first, the mark is only in the database until the conversation is opened again. Stop during creation returns the question to the composer and deletes the empty row. Deleting a conversation cancels its turn; archiving doesn't
- **View**: `useMainChatSession` validates every open through the conversation's claim (`claimAgentConversation`, else `getAgentConversation`): a deleted row picked from the list takes the user back to the chat they were in ("This chat is no longer available"), a restored deleted row lands on New chat, either cancels its turn, and a restored archived row lands on New chat and leaves it running. A row the store just created validates in the background (`createdSelectionToken`), so its message and the composer's focus stay. The composer is never disabled, only `sendDisabled`. A finished entry stays until its conversation opens, which keeps message ids (approval cards) and unsaved errors; clicking the open conversation does nothing. The hook selects only what its conversation shows, so another turn's chunks don't re-render the view
- **Other windows**: main lets one renderer at a time run a conversation's turn or answer its question (`claim-agent-conversation`; the agent window, a note's chat and the main chat each claim). The main chat claims for each turn (`claimChatConversation`, released when it ends) and while a restored question waits. A conversation another window holds opens as saved, its question answerable only there, and reloads once that window lets go (`agent-conversation-released`); a send to it is refused ("This chat is busy"), its text back in the composer. A send also takes the conversation as it is saved now, so turns another window ran meanwhile are in its history
- **Sidebar**: `ConversationItem` swaps the timestamp for a spinner while a turn runs. Chat shows a dot while one of its turns waits on the user's answer to a card and another view is open (`useChatNeedsAttention`). `listRevision` refreshes `ConversationList` silently once it has been shown
- **Notes**: a turn keeps the `openNote` of the render it was sent from (`useSourceNote`), so switching chats right after sending still sends the note. A conversation opened in a note's docked chat while answering shows live there (`useEmbeddedChat`) and can be stopped but can't start a second turn; once it ends, the docked chat takes its outcome from the store
- **Accounts and policy**: `resetForAccountChange` clears everything and fences late callbacks. It runs through `prepareChatAccountScope` before `setActiveAccountScope`, at the start of sign-out, account deletion, an invitation's account switch and Reset app data, and when main clears the scope (`subscribeChatAccountScope`; a guest's null scope is ignored). Chats are read per account (see `database.js`), so `ConversationList` also reloads on every scope broadcast: a launch that restored no scope learns its first one with no account generation change. The agent window stays open across an account or credential change (`subscribeAuthBoundary`) on a new chat, with its take, draft, picked skill and chat switcher list dropped. `subscribeChatPolicy` cancels every turn whenever `isAgentAllowed` is false, which includes `loading` and `error`
- **Local models**: chat talks to llama-server's port directly, so only `/slots` shows it's generating. A restart for another model or a bigger window waits while the server is busy, up to the caller's `busyWaitMs` (see `llamaServer.js`), and then throws `LOCAL_MODEL_BUSY`, leaving the answer running. A settings-driven stop waits with no limit (`stopWhenIdle`), and a later need cancels it

## Development Guidelines

### Internationalization (i18n) — REQUIRED

All user-facing strings **must** use the i18n system. Never hardcode UI text in components.

**Setup**: react-i18next (v15) with i18next (v25). Translation files in `src/locales/{lang}/translation.json`.

**Supported languages**: en, ar, es, fr, de, pt, it, ru, ja, zh-CN, zh-TW (`SUPPORTED_UI_LANGUAGES` in `src/i18n.ts`; Arabic is RTL, so layouts use logical properties such as `ms-`/`me-`/`start-`/`end-`)

**How to use**:

```tsx
import { useTranslation } from "react-i18next";

const { t } = useTranslation();
// Simple: t("notes.list.title")
// With interpolation: t("notes.upload.using", { model: "Whisper" })
```

**Rules**:

1. Every new UI string must have a translation key in `en/translation.json` and all other language files
2. Use `useTranslation()` hook in components and hooks
3. Keep `{{variable}}` interpolation syntax for dynamic values
4. Do NOT translate: brand names (OpenWhispr, Pro), technical terms (Markdown, Signal ID), format names (MP3, WAV), or the shared assistant `fullPrompt` in `prompts.json`. The other prompt keys (`cleanupPrompt`, `translatePrompt`, `dictionarySuffix`, `screenContextSuffix`) are translated per locale, and any change to a default prompt must update `CURRENT_DEFAULT_PROMPT_HASHES` in `src/config/retiredPrompts.js`
5. Group keys by feature area (e.g., `notes.editor.*`, `referral.toasts.*`)
6. Plurals: `key_one` / `key_other` selected by `count`, with every form the locale's CLDR rules select for 0–200 (Arabic zero/one/two/few/many/other, Russian one/few/many/other; Japanese and Chinese carry English's one/other alike). `npm run i18n:check` (`scripts/check-i18n.js`) requires every English key in every locale, those forms, and the count interpolated in any form that also renders other numbers: `{{count}}`, or the count through an i18next formatter, `{{count, number}}` / `{{count, number(notation: compact)}}`, which is also compared as a placeholder with its formatter (only `count` gets this). A key that can show whole millions (the Dictionary's token estimate) also gives French, Spanish, Italian and Portuguese `_many`, else i18next shows English there

### Image and Icon Assets — REQUIRED

Raster UI assets live in `src/assets/` (onboarding ones are named `onboarding-*`). Vector provider/brand marks live in `src/assets/icons/`.

UI icons come from `src/components/icons/` (vendored Nucleo core outline components behind lucide-style names, e.g. `import { Check, Loader2 } from "../icons"`). To add one, map a name to a Nucleo label in `src/components/icons/nucleo-map.json` and run `node scripts/sync-nucleo-icons.js`; never import from `lucide-react` or a machine-local Nucleo path. Icons outside that set — the status marks, the note toolbar's formatting glyphs, the note chat's rounded copy and "/" glyphs, the design's action icons (`ListEnd`, `FolderRounded`), media pause (`Pause`, beside Nucleo's play), the review's verdicts (`ThumbsUp`, `ThumbsDown`, Lucide's shapes), the agent window's and fork glyphs (`Maximize`, `Minimize`, `Volume`, `TextSelect`, `GitFork`, `SkillStack`), its progress row's `Lightbulb` and `MessageCircleQuestion`, and the question card's submit arrow (`ArrowUp`), which have no vendored Nucleo source yet — are drawn by hand in `src/components/icons/primitives.tsx`, which the generated `index.ts` re-exports.

**Typography**: `--font-family-sans` is Yowza (brand, Latin only) falling back to the bundled Noto Sans; `--font-family-display` is Yowza Soft for headings. The font files are licensed and never committed — `src/brandFonts.ts` registers whatever `scripts/download-brand-fonts.js` fetched at build time, and a build without them silently uses Noto Sans.

**Rules**:

1. **Always `import` the asset**; never write a literal path like `/assets/foo.webp`. Packaged builds load the renderer from a `file://` origin, so root-relative paths resolve to nothing. Importing lets Vite fingerprint the file and rewrite the URL:

   ```tsx
   import microphoneIcon from "@/assets/onboarding-permission-microphone.webp";
   <img src={microphoneIcon} ... />;
   ```

2. **WebP, lossless** for UI art with hard edges or alpha — `cwebp -lossless -z 9 -alpha_q 100 in.png -o out.webp` (roughly 40–55% smaller than PNG). Reserve lossy for photographic art.
3. **Author at 2x the CSS slot** and no larger (a 44px slot gets an 88px asset). Retina-sharp without paying for pixels that get downscaled away.
4. **Bake rounded corners into the artwork as transparency** rather than adding a CSS `rounded-*` on the `<img>` — CSS rounding on top of already-rounded art clips the corners twice.
